# Azion WebKit

> The Vue 3 design system of Azion (`@aziontech/webkit` 5.12.3): components, screen blocks with guidelines, and the design foundations, written for agents that build Azion product, marketing and docs screens.

Start every screen from a block (a reviewed, copyable screen), then fill gaps with components. Import each component from its own subpath (`@aziontech/webkit/<name>`), style only through tokens, and keep strings in the locale catalogs. These pages are generated from `catalog.json`; agents with MCP get the same data from `@aziontech/webkit-mcp` (`search_blocks`, `get_block`, `suggest_component`, `get_component`, `list_assets`). The names a component takes for its artwork (illustrations, diagrams, line figures, brand marks, icon classes) are listed in the asset foundation pages below; never guess one from a file in `src/`.

Installed copy: `node_modules/@aziontech/webkit/llms/`. Every component also has a JSON API next to its page (`components/<name>.json`).

## How this file is cut

- This file holds the components of the marketing and documentation sites (surface `site` and `docs`), cut like `llms-full.txt`: first example only, sub-component notes cut to their first sentence, related names without links, no right-to-left, internationalization and responsive notes.
- Foundations, blocks and the product (`app`) components are in `llms-full.txt`.
- Classes on a component tag: Only layout utilities (margin, flex and grid placement, width, order, self-alignment) go on the component tag. Colors, borders, padding, typography and every other look come from the component props; put anything else on a wrapper element or inside a slot. A component marked style seam says so on its own entry.

---

<!-- components/doc-accordion.md -->

# Doc Accordion

> One collapsible entry inside a `DocAccordionGroup`. It carries its title and its body only; the group owns the open-state model, the trigger and the panel. Rendered on its own it is a `display: contents` wrapper around its body.

- Import: `import DocAccordion from '@aziontech/webkit/doc-accordion'`
- Category: docs · Surface: docs · Structure: monolithic

## When to use

- As a direct child of `DocAccordionGroup`.

## Do

- Keep the title a question; keep the body prose.

## Don’t

- Outside a `DocAccordionGroup` — it renders no trigger of its own.

## Related

- `DocAccordionGroup` — the group that reads it.

## Props

| Prop | Type | Default | Required | Description |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `title` | `string` | `''` | false | The question the trigger shows; `DocAccordionGroup` reads it to build the row. |
| `defaultOpen` | `boolean` | `false` | false | Opens this entry on first render. |

## Slots

| Slot | Scope | Notes |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `default` | — | The answer shown when the entry is open. |

## States

- Visual states: `default` (the group owns open and closed).

## Accessibility

- No role of its own; the group renders the heading, the button and the region.

## Examples

### Default

```vue
<script setup>
  import DocAccordion from '@aziontech/webkit/doc-accordion'
  import DocAccordionGroup from '@aziontech/webkit/doc-accordion-group'
</script>

<template>
  <DocAccordionGroup>
    <DocAccordion
      title="Can I keep a stale object while the origin is down?"
      default-open
    >
      <p>
        Yes. Turn on stale cache and the last good copy is served until the origin answers again.
      </p>
    </DocAccordion>
  </DocAccordionGroup>
</template>
```

---

<!-- components/doc-accordion-group.md -->

# Doc Accordion Group

> The FAQ and "expand for detail" block of a documentation page. It reads the `title` of each `DocAccordion` child to build the triggers and renders the child body in the panel, so an author writes only titles and prose. It always ships as a card (surface, outer rule, radius) so a run of rows reads as one closed group in an open prose column. Built on `Accordion` at `size="large"`.

- Import: `import DocAccordionGroup from '@aziontech/webkit/doc-accordion-group'`
- Category: docs · Surface: docs · Structure: monolithic

## When to use

- Use DocAccordionGroup only inside documentation pages (Markdown AccordionGroup); product UI uses Accordion.
- A list of questions with answers at the end of a guide.
- Optional detail that would interrupt the main path of a page (edge cases, troubleshooting).

## Do

- Write each title as the full question the reader would ask.
- Keep answers short; link out for anything longer than a few paragraphs.
- Open at most one entry by default.

## Don’t

- For steps the reader must follow in order — use `DocSteps`.
- For alternative paths through the same instruction — use `DocTabs`.
- Inside an application screen — use `Accordion` directly.

## Related

- `DocAccordion` — the entry it reads.
- `Accordion` — the design-system surface it renders on.
- `Faq` — the marketing FAQ band.

## Props

| Prop | Type | Default | Required | Description |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `type` | `'single' \| 'multiple'` | `'single'` | false | Whether one or several entries can be open at once. |

## Slots

| Slot | Scope | Notes |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `default` | — | The `DocAccordion` children, in order. Each child's `title` and `defaultOpen` are read; its body is rendered inside the panel. |

## States

- Each row mirrors `Accordion` `data-state=open|closed`.
- The last row drops its own bottom rule while closed so it does not double the card edge.
- `data-doc-block` puts the group on the block rung of `DocProse`.

## Accessibility

- Each trigger is a button inside an `h3`, with `aria-expanded` and `aria-controls` from `Accordion`.
- Keyboard: `Tab` between triggers, `Enter`/`Space` toggles (from `Accordion`).
- The focus ring is drawn inside the button (`ring-inset`) so the card clipping does not cut it.
- Contrast: `--text-default` on `--bg-surface` in both themes.

## Examples

### Default

```vue
<script setup>
  import DocAccordion from '@aziontech/webkit/doc-accordion'
  import DocAccordionGroup from '@aziontech/webkit/doc-accordion-group'
</script>

<template>
  <DocAccordionGroup>
    <DocAccordion
      title="Why does my first request miss the cache?"
      default-open
    >
      <p>Each location warms up on its own first request. Warm-up is per location, not global.</p>
    </DocAccordion>
    <DocAccordion title="Can I purge a single path?">
      <p>Yes. Purge by URL for one object, or by wildcard for a folder.</p>
    </DocAccordion>
    <DocAccordion title="How long does a purge take to reach every location?">
      <p>Usually a few seconds. The purge request returns once every location has confirmed it.</p>
    </DocAccordion>
  </DocAccordionGroup>
</template>
```

1 more example(s) in [components/doc-accordion-group.md](components/doc-accordion-group.md).

<!-- components/doc-agent-card.md -->

# Doc Agent Card

> The "Your AI agent, fluent in Azion" card that opens the first section of a documentation home. Two columns in one framed card: on the left the title, one or two sentences on what the prompt gives the agent, and the inline `CopyPromptButton` with the agent marks; on the right, behind a vertical divider, the question "Prefer the guided route?" over an orange `Agent setup` button, set on an orange halftone panel that fades in from the bottom corner. Below the card's `@3xl` container size the columns stack and the divider turns horizontal. All four strings default to the catalog, so `<DocAgentCard :prompt="…" />` is the whole reference card.

- Import: `import DocAgentCard from '@aziontech/webkit/doc-agent-card'`
- Category: docs · Surface: docs · Structure: monolithic

## When to use

- The first block under "Start by interface" (or the hero) of a documentation home, to hand the reader's coding agent the platform in one prompt and offer the guided setup next to it.

## Do

- Pass the exact prompt in `prompt`; leave the strings to the catalog unless the product differs.
- Point `action-href` at the agent setup guide.
- Follow it with a three-column `DocCardGroup` (Console, CLI, API) in the same section.
- Do not add an overline or an eyebrow above the title; the card has none.

## Don’t

- To show the prompt text itself → use `DocPrompt`.
- As a generic two-column banner → use `DocCard`, `DocCallToAction` or a marketing `CallToAction` (`variant="announcement"`).
- On an article page → put `CopyPromptButton` in `DocPageHeader`'s meta actions instead.

## Related

- `CopyPromptButton` — the left column's action.
- `DocHero` — the masthead above it on a docs home.
- `DocCardGroup` — the Console / CLI / API cards that follow it; both share `RegistrationFrame`'s corner marks.
- `Texture` — the `dither` halftone behind the guided action.

## Props

| Prop | Type | Default | Required | Description |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `prompt` | `string` | `—` | true | The prompt `CopyPromptButton` copies. |
| `title` | `string` | `''` | false | Card heading (an `h3`); empty uses the catalog's `Your AI agent, fluent in Azion`. |
| `description` | `string` | `''` | false | One or two sentences on what the prompt gives the agent; empty uses the catalog's. |
| `agents` | `CopyPromptButtonAgent[]` | `undefined` | false | The agents whose marks the copy button shows; unset keeps the button's default list. |
| `asideTitle` | `string` | `''` | false | Question over the guided action; empty uses the catalog's `Prefer the guided route?`. |
| `actionLabel` | `string` | `''` | false | Label of the guided action; empty uses the catalog's `Agent setup`. |
| `actionHref` | `string` | `''` | false | Destination of the guided action; empty renders it as a button that only emits `action`. |

## Events

| Event | Payload | Notes |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `copy` | `(event: MouseEvent, value: string)` | Re-emitted from `CopyPromptButton` after a successful copy. |
| `action` | `MouseEvent` | The guided action was activated. |

## Slots

| Slot | Scope | Notes |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `actions` |  | Replaces the copy button in the left column. |

## States

- Wide (container ≥ `@3xl`): two columns, 7 : 3, vertical divider. Narrow: one column, horizontal divider.
- The copy button carries its own `idle` / `copied` / `disabled` states.
- Not applicable: `loading`, `empty`, `error`, `open`/`closed`.

## Accessibility

- A `<section>` named by its title (`aria-label`), with the title as an `h3` (it sits under the section's `h2`).
- The halftone is `aria-hidden` and never sits under text that needs contrast: the question and the button keep ≥ 4.5:1 on the surface.
- The actions keep their own roles, names and focus rings; reading order is left column, then right.

## Examples

### Default

```vue
<script setup>
  import DocAgentCard from '@aziontech/webkit/doc-agent-card'

  const prompt =
    'Set up the Azion CLI, connect the Azion docs over MCP and link this project to my Azion account.'
</script>

<template>
  <DocAgentCard
    :prompt="prompt"
    action-href="#"
  />
</template>
```

1 more example(s) in [components/doc-agent-card.md](components/doc-agent-card.md).

<!-- components/doc-call-to-action.md -->

# Doc Call To Action

> The closing offer of a documentation page, parked at the foot of the "On this page" rail after the outline. It is a docs card built like every other card on the page: a `RegistrationFrame` with corner marks, a flat `--bg-surface` face, the wordmark at its small size, a headline, a subtitle and full-width stacked actions. Set on the rail scale (14 px headline, 12 px copy) so it never out-shouts the page outline. Every string is content passed by the page. Its actions are `{ label, href }` objects, the same shape as the marketing bands' `primaryAction` / `secondaryAction`.

- Import: `import DocCallToAction from '@aziontech/webkit/doc-call-to-action'`
- Category: docs · Surface: docs · Structure: monolithic

## When to use

- Use DocCallToAction only in the docs 'On this page' rail; marketing bands use CallToAction.
- Once per documentation page, last in the right rail.

## Do

- One primary action; a secondary only when it is a real alternative (talk to sales).
- A one-line headline and a one-line subtitle.

## Don’t

- In the prose column — use `CallToAction` on marketing pages or a `DocCard` link in docs.
- More than once per page.

## Related

- `DocOnThisPage` — the rail it closes.
- `RegistrationFrame` — the frame.
- `CallToAction` — the marketing closing band.

## Props

| Prop | Type | Default | Required | Description |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `title` | `string` | `''` | false | The offer's headline; names the complementary landmark. |
| `label` | `string` | `''` | false | Fallback copy when the default slot is empty. |
| `primaryAction` | `SectionTitleAction` | `undefined` | false | The primary action, `{ label, href }`; unset or an empty label renders none. |
| `secondaryAction` | `SectionTitleAction` | `undefined` | false | The outlined secondary action, `{ label, href }`; unset renders none. |

## Slots

| Slot | Scope | Notes |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `default` | — | The offer's copy, set as the headline's subtitle. |

## States

- Visual states: `default`.

## Accessibility

- `role="complementary"` named by the visible headline (`aria-labelledby`).
- The wordmark is the `Brand` image with its own accessible name.
- Buttons come from `Button` with visible focus.

## Examples

### Default

```vue
<script setup>
  import DocCallToAction from '@aziontech/webkit/doc-call-to-action'
</script>

<template>
  <div class="max-w-56">
    <DocCallToAction
      title="Ready to build?"
      :primary-action="{ label: 'Start for free', href: '/signup' }"
    >
      Deploy your first application in minutes.
    </DocCallToAction>
  </div>
</template>
```

1 more example(s) in [components/doc-call-to-action.md](components/doc-call-to-action.md).

<!-- components/doc-callout.md -->

# Doc Callout

> The admonition a documentation page interrupts itself with — Note, Info, Tip, Check, Warning, Danger — rendered on the webkit `Alert` surface, so the severity colour, the border, the icon and the radius come from the design system rather than from this layer. Its anatomy is a glyph and a column of prose — a sentence, or a few short paragraphs and a list — nothing else: no title row.

- Import: `import DocCallout from '@aziontech/webkit/doc-callout'`
- Category: docs · Surface: docs · Structure: monolithic

## When to use

- Use DocCallout only in documentation prose (Note, Tip, Warning); console pages use Alert.
- An aside the reader must not miss mid-prose: a caveat, a prerequisite, a warning about data loss.
- A short confirmation that a step worked (`check`), or a shortcut worth knowing (`tip`).

## Do

- Let the glyph and the tint carry the severity; do not restate it in the copy ("Warning: …").
- Keep the body short — a sentence or two, links and inline code. A list is fine when the aside enumerates actions; the callout lays it out itself, because `DocProse` stops at its edge.
- Reach for `tip` when the aside claims nothing about stakes. Borrowing `check` for a friendly shortcut paints a shortcut the same green as a confirmed success.

## Don’t

- For a block that needs its own box — a fenced sample, a table, a card grid. Those belong outside the callout; its body is prose, at most a short list.
- For an application-screen banner or a form error — use `Alert` directly, which is what this composes.
- As a section heading. A callout is an interruption, not structure.

## Related

- `Alert` — the webkit surface this renders on; reach for it directly outside documentation prose.
- `DocProse` — the contract around it. A callout marks itself `data-doc-chrome`, so prose rules stop at its edge, and `data-doc-block`, so it takes the block rung of the ladder.

## Props

| Prop | Type | Default | Required | Description |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `variant` | `'note' \| 'info' \| 'tip' \| 'check' \| 'warning' \| 'danger'` | `'note'` | false | Which admonition this is; drives the severity color and the icon. |
| `label` | `string` | `''` | false | Fallback copy when the default slot is empty. |

## Slots

| Slot | Scope | Notes |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `default` | — | The callout copy: prose with links and inline code, or paragraphs and a list, laid out by the callout since `DocProse` stops at its edge. The copy region is a flow container, so server-rendered block markup stays inside the box. |

## States

- Visual states: `default` (the component is not interactive and holds no state)
- `data-variant` mirrors the `variant` prop, so a test or a consumer can target one flavour
- `data-neutral` is present only for `tip`, the one variant that carries no severity
- `data-doc-block` + `data-doc-chrome` are the `DocProse` contract (block rung; prose stops here)

## Accessibility

- Visible focus: the callout is not focusable; a link inside it inherits the prose focus ring.
- Keyboard map: none of its own — `Tab` reaches only a link in the copy.
- ARIA: the role comes from `Alert`; the glyph is decorative (`aria-hidden`), so the copy is the accessible content.
- Contrast ≥4.5:1: the copy is `--text-default` in every variant, measured 13.31:1–18.97:1 across the six fills in both themes. The inline-code chip keeps `--text-default` too, measured 10.05:1–14.43:1 — the severity ink on that chip drops to 4.03:1 in dark danger, which is why it is not used.
- `tip` deliberately does **not** speak in `--text-muted` (3.95:1 light, under AA).
- Touch target: not applicable; the component is not interactive.

## Examples

### Default

```vue
<script setup>
  import DocCallout from '@aziontech/webkit/doc-callout'
</script>

<template>
  <DocCallout variant="note">
    The first request to a location is a cache miss. Warm-up is per location, not global.
  </DocCallout>
</template>
```

2 more example(s) in [components/doc-callout.md](components/doc-callout.md).

<!-- components/doc-card.md -->

# Doc Card

> One cell of the grid a documentation page scans across: a leading glyph, an optional eyebrow, the title, the copy, and an optional call-to-action row at the foot. It renders as an anchor when it has an `href` and as a plain block when it does not, so a card is either a destination or a statement — never a dead link.

- Import: `import DocCard from '@aziontech/webkit/doc-card'`
- Category: docs · Surface: docs · Structure: monolithic

## When to use

- Use DocCard only inside DocCardGroup on documentation pages; product UI uses Card.
- A grid of peer destinations: "what you can build", the product tiles a landing section opens with.
- A set of short, scannable entries whose titles carry most of the meaning.

## Do

- Keep the title to a noun phrase and let the copy carry the verb.
- Use `eyebrow` only when the title alone does not say who or when; it is not a category label.
- Set `link-label` when the card should close on an explicit call to action; otherwise the whole tile being the link is enough.

## Don’t

- When each entry needs a sentence to be understood — use `DocItem` rows, one column, so every description is read at the page's own measure instead of squeezed into a cell.
- For a single call to action at the end of a page — that is a button, not a card.
- For an application surface — use `Card`, which is the console's carded surface.

## Related

- `DocCardGroup` — the frame and grid this is a cell of; a card outside one has no cell ring.
- `DocItem` — the row counterpart. A card claims a whole tile; a row claims one line.

## Props

| Prop | Type | Default | Required | Description |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `title` | `string` | `''` | false | Card heading. |
| `eyebrow` | `string` | `''` | false | Small muted line above the title — who makes the thing, when the title does not say. |
| `icon` | `string` | `''` | false | Azion icon class for the leading glyph. Ignored when the `icon` slot is filled. |
| `href` | `string` | `''` | false | Destination; when set the whole card becomes the link. |
| `target` | `'_self' \| '_blank'` | `'_self'` | false | Where the link opens. |
| `label` | `string` | `''` | false | Fallback copy when the default slot is empty. |
| `linkLabel` | `string` | `''` | false | Call-to-action text; when set the card closes on a link row. |

## Slots

| Slot | Scope | Notes |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `default` | — | Card copy. |
| `icon` | — | The leading mark, when a font glyph will not do. Replaces `icon`. |

## Assets

- Icons: every valid name is in icons.md (MCP `list_assets({ kind: "icons" })`). An unknown class renders nothing.

## States

- Visual states: `default`, `hover` and `focus-visible` — the last two only when the card is a link
- The root element switches on data, not on an `as` prop: an anchor when `href` is set, a plain block otherwise
- `data-doc-chrome` is the `DocProse` contract (prose rules stop at the card's edge)

## Accessibility

- Visible focus: a linked card takes `focus-visible:ring-2 focus-visible:ring-(--ring-color)` on the anchor root.
- Keyboard map: `Tab` reaches a linked card; `Enter` follows it. A card with no `href` is not focusable and not announced as interactive.
- ARIA: the accessible name is the card's own title text — the anchor wraps the content, so nothing is duplicated into an `aria-label`. The glyph is decorative (`aria-hidden`).
- A card that leaves the documentation (absolute URL, `mailto:`, or explicit `target="_blank"`) carries `rel="noreferrer"` and swaps the chevron for a diagonal arrow, so the destination change is visible and not colour-only.
- Contrast ≥4.5:1 (text) / ≥3:1 (large + icons).
- Touch target: the whole tile is the hit area, comfortably over 40×40 px.

## Examples

### Default

```vue
<script setup>
  import DocCard from '@aziontech/webkit/doc-card'
</script>

<template>
  <DocCard
    title="Edge Application"
    icon="az az-server"
    href="/docs/edge-application"
    link-label="Read the guide"
  >
    Serve and cache your application at the edge, close to the people using it.
  </DocCard>
</template>
```

1 more example(s) in [components/doc-card.md](components/doc-card.md).

<!-- components/doc-card-group.md -->

# Doc Card Group

> The framed, responsive grid a `DocCard` set sits in. The group is the frame, not each card: `RegistrationFrame` draws the perimeter and the four corner registration marks once around the whole set, the grid keeps 1px gaps, and every cell paints a 1px ring into them — so two neighbours share one hairline, an incomplete last row stays simply empty, and the set reads as one object the reader scans across rather than as loose tiles. `columns` names the widest column count; the grid always steps down toward one column on a phone unless a set of tiny cells opts into two with `mobileColumns`.

- Import: `import DocCardGroup from '@aziontech/webkit/doc-card-group'`
- Category: docs · Surface: docs · Structure: monolithic

## When to use

- Around every `DocCard` set — a card outside a group has no frame and no cell ring.
- The fan-out at the top of a landing or section-index page: product tiles, a framework grid, a "what you can build" band.

## Do

- Pick `columns` for the widest screen and let the breakpoints do the rest; do not wrap the group in another grid.
- Leave `mobileColumns` at 1 unless every cell is a mark plus one word — a card with copy needs the whole phone width.
- Let an incomplete last row be empty; do not stretch the last card to fill it.
- Inside a `section-module` with `padded` off, place the group directly in the body: the header's bottom rule and the column's side rules become the group's top and sides, the group drops its own top and side rules and top corner marks, and the cells' rings land on those rules. The group's bottom rule closes the module, so the module after it passes `divided` as `false` — every seam stays one hairline.

## Don’t

- When each entry needs a full-measure sentence to be understood — use `DocItem` rows, one column, instead of cells.
- As a general-purpose layout grid for application screens; it is documentation chrome, tied to the `DocProse` ladder.

## Related

- `DocCard` — the cell this frames; the group draws the perimeter and the cells paint the internal rules.
- `RegistrationFrame` — the layout primitive that draws the frame and the corner registration marks.
- `DocProse` — the contract around it; `data-doc-block` puts the group on the block rung of the prose ladder.

## Props

| Prop | Type | Default | Required | Description |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `columns` | `1 \| 2 \| 3 \| 4` | `2` | false | Column count at the large breakpoint. A Markdown `CardGroup cols` attribute maps here (through DocMarkdown). |
| `mobileColumns` | `1 \| 2` | `1` | false | Column count on a phone. Two only pays when the cells are a mark plus a word. |

## Slots

| Slot | Scope | Notes |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `default` | — | The `DocCard` children. |

## States

- Visual states: `default` (the component is not interactive and holds no state)
- `data-columns` and `data-mobile-columns` mirror `columns` and `mobileColumns`; Tailwind data-variants switch the grid's column count on them at the `sm` and `lg` breakpoints
- `data-doc-block` is the `DocProse` contract (the group takes the block rung of the ladder)

## Accessibility

- Visible focus: the group is not focusable; a linked `DocCard` inside it carries its own inset focus ring.
- Keyboard map: none of its own — `Tab` moves through the linked cards in DOM order, which follows the grid's reading order.
- ARIA: no role is added; the group is pure layout, so assistive tech reads the cards as a plain sequence of links.
- Contrast: the frame and the hairline rules are decorative; every readable surface belongs to the cells, which own their contrast.
- Touch target: not applicable; the component itself is not interactive.

## Examples

### Default

```vue
<script setup>
  import DocCard from '@aziontech/webkit/doc-card'
  import DocCardGroup from '@aziontech/webkit/doc-card-group'
</script>

<template>
  <DocCardGroup :columns="2">
    <DocCard
      title="Start from a template"
      icon="az az-layout-grid"
      href="#templates"
    >
      Ready-made projects that go live in a few clicks. No local tooling.
    </DocCard>
    <DocCard
      title="Import from GitHub"
      icon="az az-github"
      href="#github"
    >
      Connect a repository and let Azion build it on every push.
    </DocCard>
  </DocCardGroup>
</template>
```

1 more example(s) in [components/doc-card-group.md](components/doc-card-group.md).

<!-- components/doc-changelog-entry.md -->

# Doc Changelog Entry

> One entry in a changelog: what shipped, when, and under which version. The release's identity sits in a left column — the date, the version under it, the tags under that — the notes sit on the right, and a rule runs between them for the length of the entry and bridges the block gap to the next one, so a set of entries reads as one continuous timeline rather than a stack of boxes. The label is an `h2` and a link to its own id, because every entry is a URL somebody sends — the id is the label slugified, unless an explicit `anchor` overrides it. Given a `title`, the entry becomes a release: the label turns into a plain date beside a node on the rule, and the title takes the `h2`, the anchor and the tags, sized above every heading the notes carry.

- Import: `import DocChangelogEntry from '@aziontech/webkit/doc-changelog-entry'`
- Category: docs · Surface: docs · Structure: monolithic

## When to use

- A changelog or release-notes page: one `DocChangelogEntry` per release, newest first, inside `DocProse`.
- Any dated series a reader scans by its left edge — announcements, incident retrospectives, versioned API changes.

## Do

- Put the date (or release name) in `label` and the version in `description` — the left edge is what a reader scans, so the label carries the identity.
- Give each release its own entry with a `title` when one date ships several things — one title per entry, the date repeated, rather than several product headings inside one entry.
- Keep headings inside an entry one level below the heading: the label (or the `title`, when set) is the entry's `h2`.
- Set `anchor` only when two entries share a label; otherwise let the id derive from the label so shared links match what readers see.

## Don’t

- For a single dated aside inside a guide — that is a `DocCallout`, not a timeline entry.
- As a styled row inside another section. The label is an `h2`, so every entry claims a slot in the page outline; nesting one under a deeper heading breaks the outline.
- To generate a feed. Mintlify's `rss` prop is deliberately absent — this layer renders pages and generates nothing, so the prop would be inert.

## Related

- `DocProse` — the contract around it: `data-doc-block` takes the block rung of the spacing ladder, and `data-doc-chrome` keeps prose rules off the label column.
- `Tag` — renders the entry's tags row (`severity="neutral"`): small and rounded in the label column, medium with the default shape under a `title`.
- `use-heading-navigation` — the composable the label anchor calls. A page that owns its scroll container provides a scroller-aware handler with `provideHeadingNavigation`; rendered outside a provider, the injected default is a no-op — never a throw — and the browser's native hash navigation takes over.

## Props

| Prop | Type | Default | Required | Description |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `label` | `string` | `''` | false | The entry's name — a date, a release name. Also its anchor. |
| `title` | `string` | `''` | false | The release's heading, shown above the notes. The label becomes the date beside it. |
| `description` | `string` | `''` | false | Secondary line under the label; usually the version. |
| `tags` | `string[]` | `[]` | false | Short labels categorising the entry: a product, an area, a kind of change. |
| `anchor` | `string` | `''` | false | Anchor override; wins over the slug derived from the label. Set it when two entries share a label. |

## Slots

| Slot | Scope | Notes |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `default` | — | The release notes: prose, lists, code, or any documentation component. |

## States

- Visual states: `default`, plus `hover` / `focus-visible` on the label anchor — the label underlines and the chain glyph fades in
- Anchor derivation: an explicit `anchor` prop wins; otherwise the id is the `label` slugified (inline markup stripped, lowercased, kebab-cased), or the `label` and `title` slugified together when a `title` is set, so entries sharing a date stay distinct. The `h2` takes the same id with a `-label` suffix, and the section's `aria-labelledby` points at it
- Heading navigation degrades gracefully: the anchor calls the handler a page provided via `provideHeadingNavigation`; outside a provider the injected default is a no-op — activation never throws, and native hash navigation handles the jump
- `data-doc-block` + `data-doc-changelog-entry` mark the entry for `DocProse` block rhythm and let an entry that follows another bridge the rule through the block gap; `data-doc-chrome` keeps prose rules off the label column
- Below `md` the columns stack and the rule drops; from `md` up the label column sticks (`md:sticky`) while the notes scroll past
- Titled (`title` set, `data-titled` on the root and notes column): the label renders as a date, not a heading, centred on the title's first line; a decorative node sits on the rule at the same line (`md` up), centred on the rule in both directions (`-translate-x-1/2`, `rtl:translate-x-1/2`); the title is the `h2` and self-link, the tags move under it, and consecutive titled entries are padded apart below the notes

## Accessibility

- The label is a real `h2` inside a `section` whose `aria-labelledby` points at it, so every entry is a named region and joins the page outline; inside `DocLayout` it registers itself (through `useHeadingOutline`) as a level 2 entry of the "On this page" rail, named by its title, or its label without one. With a `title`, the title is that `h2` and the date is plain text, so the region is named by what shipped.
- The titled node on the rule is decorative (`aria-hidden`).
- Keyboard map: `Tab` reaches the heading anchor (the label, or the title when set); `Enter` follows it — through the page's provided heading-navigation handler, or native hash navigation without one. Nothing else in the entry's chrome is interactive.
- Visible focus: the anchor takes `focus-visible:outline-2 focus-visible:outline-offset-2` in the ring token.
- The chain glyph is decorative (`aria-hidden`) and revealed on `focus-visible` as well as hover, so the self-link affordance is not pointer-only.
- Contrast ≥4.5:1 (text) / ≥3:1 (large + icons).
- Touch target: the label anchor is a text link on its own line in the label column, with nothing else competing for the hit area.

## Examples

### Default

```vue
<script setup>
  import DocChangelogEntry from '@aziontech/webkit/doc-changelog-entry'
  import DocProse from '@aziontech/webkit/doc-prose'
</script>

<template>
  <DocProse>
    <DocChangelogEntry
      label="August 19, 2026"
      description="v2.4.0"
      :tags="['Console', 'Edge Functions']"
    >
      <p>
        Rules Engine conditions now match on request headers, so a rule can branch on anything the
        client sends without a function in the path.
      </p>
    </DocChangelogEntry>
  </DocProse>
</template>
```

2 more example(s) in [components/doc-changelog-entry.md](components/doc-changelog-entry.md).

<!-- components/doc-code-group.md -->

# Doc Code Group

> One or more code samples of the same thing in different languages or package managers, rendered on `CodeBlock` so the docs inherit its header, copy control and syntax surface. One sample renders a block titled by a label header (the sample label, else the language's readable name: Shell, JavaScript, JSON); several render the joined tab strip. A sample's `output` — what its command prints — renders as a separate muted "Output" block under it, with no copy control, so the command block holds only what the reader runs.

- Import: `import DocCodeGroup from '@aziontech/webkit/doc-code-group'`
- Category: docs · Surface: docs · Structure: monolithic

## When to use

- The same install command for npm, pnpm and yarn.
- The same request in cURL, JavaScript and Python.

## Do

- Label each sample with what differs (the language or the tool), not with "Example". With one sample, leave `label` empty: the header names the language.
- Keep the same order across a page.
- Do: set `language` on every sample, so the header reads "Shell" or "JavaScript" and the code is highlighted.
- Do: keep `code` to what the reader runs or pastes; the copy control copies exactly that. A leading `$ ` prompt is drawn but never copied.
- Do: put what the command prints in the sample's `output` (`{ language: 'bash', code: 'curl -I https://<your-azion-domain>', output: 'HTTP/2 200' }`), or say it in prose.
- Do: show a standalone printout (a deploy log) with `type="output"`.
- Don't: write output into `code` — never `curl -I https://example.com\n# HTTP/2 200`, never `$ azion version\nAzion CLI 4.23.0`. The lint `webkit/no-output-in-command` warns on it.
- Don't: turn the label header off on a docs page.

## Don’t

- For unrelated snippets — use one `CodeBlock` each.
- For prose that differs per platform — use `DocTabs`.

## Related

- `CodeBlock` — the surface it renders on.
- `DocTabs` — alternatives that are prose, not code.

## Props

| Prop | Type | Default | Required | Description |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `samples` | `DocCodeGroupSample[]` | `[]` | false | The samples, in tab order: `{ label?, language?, fileName?, code, output? }`. One renders a plain block, several render a joined tab strip; none renders nothing. |
| `showLineNumbers` | `boolean` | `false` | false | Shows the line-number gutter. |
| `showLabel` | `boolean` | `true` | false | Shows the label header (the sample label or the language name) on a single sample. |
| `type` | `'code' \| 'output'` | `'code'` | false | What the samples hold: `code` the reader runs, or `output` — what a command prints — drawn muted under an "Output" label, without a copy control. |

## States

- Visual states: from `CodeBlock` (tab selection, copied, label header, output type).
- Output: the active sample's `output` renders a second `CodeBlock` (`type="output"`, test id `${testId}__output`) under the code block (`${testId}__code`), `--spacing-xs` apart; switching tabs swaps it.
- `data-doc-block` and `data-doc-chrome` put it on the block rung and stop prose rules at its edge.

## Accessibility

- Tabs, copy button and announcements come from `CodeBlock`.
- A tab label falls back to the language's readable name, then to the sample position.

## Examples

### Default

```vue
<script setup>
  import DocCodeGroup from '@aziontech/webkit/doc-code-group'
</script>

<template>
  <DocCodeGroup :samples="[{ language: 'bash', code: 'azion link --preset vue' }]" />
</template>
```

2 more example(s) in [components/doc-code-group.md](components/doc-code-group.md).

<!-- components/doc-frame.md -->

# Doc Frame

> The bordered surface every screenshot, diagram and clip in a documentation page sits on, so no media floats loose on the canvas and all of it shares one border, radius and inset. Pass `src` and the frame decides from the extension whether it holds a still or a clip; compose the default slot when the framed thing is markup. A still opens full screen out of its own frame — the picture is the button — while a clip keeps its player controls (or, when it plays on its own, a pause control) and composed content has no source to open. The caption reads under the frame, centered, as part of the picture; the hint reads above it on the text's own left edge, as part of the sentence that led the reader here; each is a string prop for the plain case with a slot of the same name for rich content.

- Import: `import DocFrame from '@aziontech/webkit/doc-frame'`
- Category: docs · Surface: docs · Structure: monolithic

## When to use

- Use DocFrame for a screenshot or clip inside documentation; to run live components use BrowserFrame, for third-party iframe content use Embed.
- Any screenshot, diagram or screen recording inside documentation prose — every one of them, so the page's media all shares one border, radius and inset.
- A composed panel or mock the page wants presented as a figure rather than as live UI: fill the default slot.
- A clip that decorates a step (`autoplay`) or one the reader chooses to play (default, with controls).

## Do

- Always pass `alt` for a still or a clip: it labels the media, names the zoom trigger, and titles the full-screen dialog.
- Keep the caption to one line that belongs to the picture; a paragraph of context belongs in the prose above, or in `hint` when it leads directly into the frame.
- Use the `caption` / `hint` slots only when the sentence needs markup (a link, a bold run, a code chip); the string props cover the plain case in one attribute.
- Reach for `autoplay` only when the clip is decoration: it mutes, inlines and loops the clip and swaps its player controls for one pause control, on the prop path and for a clip written by hand in the slot alike. Under a reduced-motion preference it does not play until the reader presses play.

## Don’t

- For an application-screen media block outside documentation prose — use `RegistrationFrame` or compose the layout directly.
- For an image that is itself a link or a card — a frame is a figure, not a navigation target.
- For a gallery or carousel; the frame holds one piece of media at a time.

## Related

- `DocProse` — the page contract around it. The frame marks itself `data-doc-block`, so it takes the block rung of the prose ladder, and its caption and hint arrive unclassed for the page to style.
- `DocCallout` — the other block-level interruption; a callout carries words, a frame carries media.

## Props

| Prop | Type | Default | Required | Description |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `caption` | `string` | `''` | false | Caption under the frame, centered; plain-text fallback for the caption slot. |
| `hint` | `string` | `''` | false | Lead-in above the frame, left aligned; plain-text fallback for the hint slot. |
| `src` | `string` | `''` | false | Media source; omit to frame slot content instead. |
| `alt` | `string` | `''` | false | Alternative text for the framed media. |
| `autoplay` | `boolean` | `false` | false | Plays the clip on its own, muted, inline and looping, with a pause control instead of the player controls; off under reduced motion. |

## Slots

| Slot | Scope | Notes |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `default` | — | Framed content when no `src` is given. |
| `caption` | — | Rich caption content; the `caption` prop is its plain-text fallback inside the slot. |
| `hint` | — | Rich lead-in content; the `hint` prop is its plain-text fallback inside the slot. |

## States

- Visual states: `default`; `hover` and `focus-visible` on the zoom trigger of a still (the full-screen badge fades in on both, so the affordance is not pointer-only).
- The framed element is decided by data, not by a prop: a source matching a clip extension (`.mp4`, `.webm`, `.ogv`, `.ogg`, `.mov`, `.m4v`) renders a player, any other source renders a still, and no source renders the default slot.
- The lightbox contract: only a **still** opens full screen — the picture itself is the trigger. `Escape`, the backdrop, the close control and a click on the opened picture all close it, and focus returns to the thumbnail trigger. A **clip** does not zoom (it carries controls a click would fight, and a player has fullscreen of its own), and **composed slot content** does not zoom (there is no source to open).
- While open: the overlay carries `data-state="open"`, body scroll is locked, and the full-size copy carries `data-flip="start|armed"` — the attribute that arms the FLIP transition only after the starting transform has committed.
- `autoplay` is applied on both authoring paths: to the prop, and by rewriting a hand-written autoplaying clip in the default slot with the same muted/inline/loop attributes, no player controls and no native `autoplay` attribute. The frame starts the clip itself after mount, so it can honour the reader's preference: the clip carries `data-autoplay`, and a pause control (`IconButton`, `${testId}__pause`) sits on the frame's bottom end corner.
- Autoplay playback: `playing` → the clip loops and the control reads "Pause animation" (`common.pauseAnimation`, icon `az-pause`); `paused` → the clip holds its current frame and the control reads "Play animation" (`common.playAnimation`, icon `az-play`). Under `prefers-reduced-motion: reduce` the clip starts paused on its first frame; the reader's own choice then wins over the preference. If the browser refuses to play, the frame falls back to `paused`. When the clip has a text — `alt` on the prop path, the hand-written clip's `aria-label` or `title` in the slot — the label carries it, so several frames on one page have distinct names: "Pause animation: {alt}" (`docFrame.pauseNamed`) and "Play animation: {alt}" (`docFrame.playNamed`).
- `data-doc-block` is the `DocProse` contract (the frame takes the block rung of the prose ladder).

## Accessibility

- Visible focus: the zoom trigger is a real button with `focus-visible:outline-2 focus-visible:outline-(--ring-color)`; the close control carries the same ring. The full-screen badge fades in on keyboard focus as well as hover, so the zoom affordance is never pointer-only.
- Keyboard map: `Tab` reaches the zoom trigger of a still; `Enter` / `Space` opens the lightbox. On an autoplaying clip `Tab` reaches the pause control; `Enter` / `Space` pauses or plays the clip. While open, focus moves to the close control, `Tab` / `Shift+Tab` are trapped inside the dialog, and `Escape` closes it. On close — by any path — focus returns to the thumbnail trigger.
- ARIA: the trigger's `aria-label` names the picture ("View full screen: {alt}"). The lightbox is `role="dialog"` with `aria-modal="true"`, labelled from `alt`. The backdrop is `aria-hidden` — the pointer shortcut whose keyboard equivalents are `Escape` and the real close button. The badge is `aria-hidden` (the trigger already carries the name). A clip takes `aria-label` from `alt`; an autoplaying clip is muted so it never seizes a screen reader's audio. The pause control is a real button whose label switches between "Pause animation" and "Play animation", followed by the clip's text when it has one ("Pause animation: {alt}"), so two frames on a page never share a name (no `aria-pressed`, so the name and the state never contradict).
- Moving content (WCAG 2.2.2 Pause, Stop, Hide): an autoplaying clip loops for longer than five seconds, so it always ships the visible, keyboard-reachable pause control, and it does not start at all under a reduced-motion preference.
- The lightbox Teleports to `body` and locks body scroll while open, so assistive tech and the pointer agree on what is interactive.
- Contrast ≥4.5:1 (text) / ≥3:1 (icons) on all frame chrome tokens.
- Touch target: the zoom trigger is the whole picture; the close control is a padded button at the viewport corner.
- Teleported content: the teleported zoom dialog binds `dir` and `lang` from the nearest `ConfigProvider` (`usePortalLocale`), so it keeps the provider's direction and language although it renders under `body`. With no provider nothing is set and the document's own `dir` and `lang` apply.

## Examples

### Default

```vue
<script setup>
  import DocFrame from '@aziontech/webkit/doc-frame'
</script>

<template>
  <DocFrame caption="Console, + Create">
    <div
      class="w-full rounded-(--shape-control) border border-(--border-default) bg-(--bg-surface) p-(--spacing-md)"
    >
      <p class="text-heading-xs text-(--text-default)">Create a workload</p>
      <p class="pt-(--spacing-2xs) text-body-sm text-(--text-muted)">
        Pick a template, or start from an empty project.
      </p>
    </div>
  </DocFrame>
</template>
```

2 more example(s) in [components/doc-frame.md](components/doc-frame.md).

<!-- components/doc-heading.md -->

# Doc Heading

> A documentation section heading that is also the link to itself. The whole text links to its own `anchor` (the heading's `id`), so a reader can copy the URL to the section; a rule under the text and a chain glyph after it appear on hover and keyboard focus without taking layout. The click goes through `useHeadingNavigation`, so the page decides how to scroll; with no page it falls back to the native hash jump. MDX renderers and hand-composed pages both render headings through it, so the two authoring paths cannot drift. Inside `DocLayout`, a level 2 or 3 heading with an `anchor` registers itself (through `useHeadingOutline`) as an entry of the layout's "On this page" rail, with its rendered text and its level, once it mounts, and leaves the rail when it unmounts; the page never lists its headings anywhere else.

- Import: `import DocHeading from '@aziontech/webkit/doc-heading'`
- Category: docs · Surface: docs · Structure: monolithic

## When to use

- Use DocHeading only for h2/h3 inside documentation pages; a console page band uses SectionHeader.
- Every `h2`–`h4` on a docs page that the page rail lists.
- Section headings on a designed docs page composed in Vue.

## Do

- Give every heading a stable, slug-like `anchor`.
- Without an `anchor` the heading renders plain text with no anchor — a link to nowhere is never drawn — and stays out of the layout's outline; a page with no outline rail (the docs home) leaves its headings unanchored.

## Don’t

- For the page title — use `DocPageHeader`.
- On marketing pages — use `SectionTitle`.

## Related

- `DocOnThisPage` — the rail that points at the ids.
- `useHeadingNavigation` — the page-provided scroll handler.
- `DocProse` — skips `[data-doc-anchor]` in its link rules.

## Props

| Prop | Type | Default | Required | Description |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `level` | `1 \| 2 \| 3 \| 4` | `2` | false | Heading level; drives the tag and the prose type scale. |
| `anchor` | `string` | `''` | false | In-page anchor: the heading's id, what the page rail entry and the copied URL point at. Empty renders the heading without a link. |
| `label` | `string` | `''` | false | Fallback text when the default slot is empty. |

## Slots

| Slot | Scope | Notes |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `default` | — | The heading text. |

## States

- `data-doc-heading` mirrors `level` for the `DocProse` type scale.
- Hover and focus-visible reveal the underline and the link glyph.

## Accessibility

- A real `h1`–`h4`; the link name is the heading text (the glyph is `aria-hidden`).
- Keyboard: `Tab` reaches the link, `Enter` follows it; the focus outline is visible.
- The glyph meets 3:1 as a non-text indicator only when shown; the text keeps the heading colour.

## Examples

### Default

```vue
<script setup>
  import DocHeading from '@aziontech/webkit/doc-heading'
</script>

<template>
  <DocHeading
    anchor="purge-by-url"
    :level="2"
  >
    Purge by URL
  </DocHeading>
</template>
```

1 more example(s) in [components/doc-heading.md](components/doc-heading.md).

<!-- components/doc-hero.md -->

# Doc Hero

> The masthead of a documentation home: a display-size `h1`, a deck capped at a readable measure, an action row (the primary `Get started`, then `CopyPromptButton`) and one muted note under it that says what the prompt does. It carries the air the home needs — `--spacing-2xl` above and below, `--spacing-xl` between the deck and the actions — so the front door reads as a front door and not as one more article. An article page uses `DocPageHeader` instead.

- Import: `import DocHero from '@aziontech/webkit/doc-hero'`
- Category: docs · Surface: docs · Structure: monolithic

## When to use

- Use DocHero only on the documentation home; marketing page openings use HeroSection.
- The top of a documentation home or a docs section landing (`shell/docs`, variant `Landing`).

## Do

- Keep the title short (`Welcome to Azion Docs`) and the deck to one or two sentences.
- Put `Button variant="primary" size="large"` first and `CopyPromptButton` second in `#actions`; say what the prompt does in `#note`.
- Do not wrap it in `DocProse`; it is page chrome (`data-doc-chrome`).

## Don’t

- On an article or reference page → use `DocPageHeader` (breadcrumb, last updated, page actions).
- On the marketing site → use `Hero` or a page section.

## Related

- `DocPageHeader` — the article masthead.
- `CopyPromptButton` — the second action of the row.
- `DocAgentCard` — the agent card that usually opens the first section under the hero.

## Props

| Prop | Type | Default | Required | Description |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `title` | `string` | `''` | false | The page title, set at display size as the page's `h1`. |
| `description` | `string` | `''` | false | The deck under the title: what the docs cover, in one or two sentences. |

## Slots

| Slot | Scope | Notes |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `title` |  | Replaces the title text, for a title that is more than a string. |
| `default` |  | Replaces the description text. |
| `actions` |  | The action row: the primary `Button` first, then `CopyPromptButton`. |
| `note` |  | One muted line under the actions that says what the prompt does. |

## States

- Static; renders only the parts it receives (no deck, no action row, no note when empty).
- Not applicable: `hover`, `focus`, `disabled`, `loading`, `empty`, `error`, `open`/`closed`.

## Accessibility

- A `<header>` holding the page's single `h1`; the deck and note are paragraphs.
- No interactive parts of its own; the actions bring their own roles and focus.
- Contrast: muted deck and note ≥ 4.5:1 on the canvas in both themes.

## Examples

### Default

```vue
<script setup>
  import Button from '@aziontech/webkit/button'
  import CopyPromptButton from '@aziontech/webkit/copy-prompt-button'
  import DocHero from '@aziontech/webkit/doc-hero'

  const prompt =
    'Set up the Azion CLI, connect the Azion docs over MCP and link this project to my Azion account.'
</script>

<template>
  <DocHero
    title="Welcome to Azion Docs"
    description="We make every application fast and reliable. Deploy on a global network with enterprise-grade security and no cold starts."
  >
    <template #actions>
      <Button
        label="Get started"
        variant="primary"
        size="large"
        href="#"
      />
      <CopyPromptButton :value="prompt" />
    </template>
    <template #note>
      The prompt hands your coding agent the CLI, live docs over MCP, and this project linked.
    </template>
  </DocHero>
</template>
```

1 more example(s) in [components/doc-hero.md](components/doc-hero.md).

<!-- components/doc-item.md -->

# Doc Item

> One row of the documentation's related-content list: a leading glyph in a framed 32px tile, the row's name, and the sentence that says what the thing is. When it has an `href` the title becomes an anchor stretched over the whole row — one tab stop, the full row as the hit area — closed by a trailing chevron, or a diagonal arrow when the destination leaves the documentation. The shell keeps `role="listitem"`, so a set of rows inside an `ItemList` is a real list to assistive technology.

- Import: `import DocItem from '@aziontech/webkit/doc-item'`
- Category: docs · Surface: docs · Structure: monolithic

## When to use

- The "Related products" band a page ends on — peer destinations where each entry needs a sentence to be understood.
- A one-column list of concepts or next steps, read at the page's own measure, with or without links.

## Do

- Keep the title to a noun phrase and let the sentence carry the verb; inline code in the sentence renders as the chip.
- Give every row of one list the same shape — all with glyphs or none, all linked or none — so the column reads as one object.
- Compose rows inside a `RegistrationFrame` (with a solid surface) holding an `ItemList`: the frame draws the single box and the list draws full-width dividers, and the `role="list"` parent is what makes each row's `role="listitem"` valid.

## Don’t

- For a grid of scannable entries whose titles carry most of the meaning — use `DocCard` inside a `DocCardGroup`; a card claims a tile, a row claims one line.
- For an application resource list — compose `Item` and its parts directly; this row is documentation chrome.
- For a single call to action at the end of a page — that is a button, not a row.

## Related

- `DocCard` — the cell counterpart. A card claims a whole tile; this claims one line.
- `Item` / `ItemMedia` / `ItemContent` / `ItemTitle` / `ItemDescription` / `ItemActions` — the webkit anatomy this row composes.
- `RegistrationFrame` + `ItemList` — the documented group pattern: the page draws one framed surface holding an `ItemList`, and the list rules between the rows. There is no `DocItemGroup` in the package; the group is this composition.

## Props

| Prop | Type | Default | Required | Description |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `title` | `string` | `''` | false | The row's name — what the reader is choosing. |
| `icon` | `string` | `''` | false | Azion icon class for the leading glyph. |
| `href` | `string` | `''` | false | Destination; when set the whole row becomes the link. |
| `target` | `'_self' \| '_blank'` | `'_self'` | false | Where the link opens. |
| `label` | `string` | `''` | false | Fallback copy when the default slot is empty. |

## Slots

| Slot | Scope | Notes |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `default` | — | The row's copy: one or two sentences of inline prose. |

## Assets

- Icons: every valid name is in icons.md (MCP `list_assets({ kind: "icons" })`). An unknown class renders nothing.

## States

- Visual states: `default`, `hover` and `focus-visible` — the last two exist only when the row is a link, and both are driven off the single title anchor via the shell's `has-[a:hover]` / `has-[a:focus-visible]` variants; a row with no `href` is inert
- The anchor appears on data, not on a mode prop: the title wraps in an anchor when `href` is set, and the trailing glyph region renders only then
- An external destination (absolute URL, `mailto:`, or explicit `target="_blank"`) swaps the chevron for the diagonal arrow and, for `target="_blank"`, adds `rel="noreferrer"`
- `data-doc-chrome` is the `DocProse` contract (prose rules stop at the row's edge)

## Accessibility

- Visible focus: the shell draws `has-[a:focus-visible]:ring-2` with `var(--ring-color)`, inset, so the whole row lights when its one anchor is focused; the anchor itself suppresses its own outline.
- Keyboard map: `Tab` reaches a linked row's title anchor — one stop per row; `Enter` follows it. A row with no `href` is not focusable and not announced as interactive.
- ARIA: `role="listitem"` lives on the shell, never on the anchor (axe `aria-allowed-role` forbids it there), so rows inside an `ItemList` with `role="list"` form a valid list. The anchor wraps only the title text, so the accessible name is the row's name with no duplicated `aria-label`; the stretched after-pseudo makes the whole row the pointer hit area without changing the accessible structure. Both glyphs are decorative (`aria-hidden`).
- A row that leaves the documentation carries `rel="noreferrer"` on `target="_blank"` and swaps the chevron for a diagonal arrow, so the destination change is visible and not colour-only.
- Contrast ≥4.5:1 (text) / ≥3:1 (large + icons).
- Touch target: the full row is the hit area, comfortably over 40×40 px.

## Examples

### Default

```vue
<script setup>
  import DocItem from '@aziontech/webkit/doc-item'
  import ItemList from '@aziontech/webkit/item-list'
  import RegistrationFrame from '@aziontech/webkit/registration-frame'
</script>

<template>
  <RegistrationFrame class="w-full bg-(--bg-surface)">
    <ItemList>
      <DocItem
        title="Edge Functions"
        icon="az az-code"
        href="#edge-functions"
      >
        Build serverless applications on Azion's global network. Workloads integrate with functions
        natively, through a binding.
      </DocItem>
      <DocItem
        title="Edge SQL"
        icon="az az-database"
        href="#edge-sql"
      >
        Store relational data across the network. A workload reads it from the location that served
        the request.
      </DocItem>
      <DocItem
        title="Edge Cache"
        icon="az az-bolt"
        href="#edge-cache"
      >
        Decide what is cached, for how long, and by which key.
      </DocItem>
    </ItemList>
  </RegistrationFrame>
</template>
```

<!-- components/doc-layout.md -->

# Doc Layout

> The documentation chrome, composed once in the app: a sticky header (at the inline start the compact menu button, the Azion Docs mark and the section links; an empty `AppHeader.Center` spacer; at the inline end `SearchTrigger` and then the app's actions, the way most docs sites end their header with the search), the navigation rail (`Sidebar` + `SidebarMenu`) on the inline start, resizable by dragging its edge and collapsible to a thin edge that brings it back, the article in the layout's `<main>`, the "On this page" rail (`DocOnThisPage`) on the inline end with its own slot below it — filled from the page itself: every `DocHeading` of level 2 or 3 that carries an `anchor`, and every `DocChangelogEntry`, rendered anywhere in the default slot registers with the layout, and the rail lists them in document order, so a page composed once in the app needs no wiring to feed its outline — and the footer under the article. The **document** scrolls: the header and both rails are sticky, so a full-page capture shows the whole article, the browser's find and scroll restoration work, and the dark canvas is painted to the end. The outline follows the reading position, a jump clears the sticky header, and below `lg` the rail moves into a drawer opened from the header. The `Command` palette lists every navigation page. Doc pages are only their article (page bodies with no header, rail or footer of their own), rendered through the default slot.

- Import: `import DocLayout from '@aziontech/webkit/doc-layout'`
- Category: docs · Surface: docs · Structure: monolithic

## When to use

- Every documentation page (guide, reference, legal, changelog): put one `DocLayout` in the app's root (around `RouterView`) and render each page's article as its default slot.
- A docs site that needs the navigation rail, the outline and the search palette without hand-composing `AppHeader`, `Sidebar`, `SidebarMenu`, `DocOnThisPage`, `Drawer` and `Command`.

## Do

- Compose the layout once, in the app's root; each route renders only its article. Never put a header, a rail or an inner scroll area in a page.
- Give every navigation row an `id` and an `href` (an in-app path is followed with the router; never `to`); leave `activeId` empty so the current page follows the route.
- Never hand the outline to the layout: give each section heading a `DocHeading` with an `anchor` and the layout lists it, in document order, with the heading's own text, and tracks which one is in view. A heading without `anchor` stays out of the outline.
- Put the theme switch in `navigation-footer`, the GitHub and Console links in `actions`, a call to action (`DocCallToAction`) in `aside`, and the site footer (`Footer variant="content"`) in `footer`. The `aside` content shows only on pages with an outline, so a shared call to action never lands on the docs home.
- The page renders no `<main>` and no `ScrollArea` around the article: the document scrolls.
- Bind `v-model:navigation-open` and `v-model:navigation-width` to state the app persists (local storage, a cookie) so the rail keeps the reader's size and collapsed state across pages and reloads; leave them unbound and the rail resets on each mount.
- A drill row (`type: 'drill'`) opens its level in place; the rail's Back then reads `Back to Docs` at the first level. Set `rootLabel` when the tree is not the whole docs (`Back to API reference`); never add a hand-made Back row to `navigation`.

## Don’t

- The signed-in console → `ConsoleLayout`. Marketing pages → `SiteLayout`.
- Never as part of a page: a page body is only its article, so it never renders `DocLayout`. A docs home with no outline rail is still a page inside the one layout: its headings carry no `anchor`, so nothing registers and the end rail is not rendered.

## Related

- `DocOnThisPage` — the outline rail; the registered headings and `outlineGroups` are bound onto it.
- `DocHeading` — headings whose anchor links the layout scrolls to, clearing the sticky header; an anchored level 2 or 3 heading registers itself as an outline entry.
- `DocChangelogEntry` — each entry registers itself as a level 2 outline entry.
- `useHeadingOutline` (`@aziontech/webkit/use-heading-navigation`) — the registration a custom heading calls to join the outline.
- `DocPageHeader`, `DocProse`, `DocPagination` — the article parts rendered in the default slot.
- `Sidebar`, `SidebarMenu` — the navigation rail; `navigation` is mapped onto `SidebarMenu` groups.
- `SearchTrigger`, `Command` — the header search and the palette it opens.
- `shell/docs` — the block that shows this layout with a sample docs tree.

## Props

| Prop | Type | Default | Required | Description |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `navigation` | `DocLayoutNavGroup[]` | `[]` | false | The docs tree: groups of rows, each `{ id, label, icon?, href?, tagValue?, disabled?, kind?, defaultOpen?, children? }`; an in-app `href` is followed with the router. |
| `activeId` | `string` | `''` | false | Id of the current row. Empty matches the current route against each row's `href`. |
| `sections` | `DocLayoutSection[]` | `[]` | false | The header's section links (`Reference`, `Guides`), each `{ label, href? }`; shown from `lg`. |
| `home` | `string` | `'/'` | false | Where the Azion Docs mark links. |
| `outlineGroups` | `DocOnThisPageGroup[]` | `[]` | false | Complementary link groups under the outline (GitHub, Community). |
| `ariaLabel` | `string` | `''` | false | Accessible name of the navigation landmark. Empty uses the locale catalog (`Documentation`). |
| `rootLabel` | `string` | `''` | false | Names the top of the docs tree in the rail's Back button on the first drill level (`Back to Docs`); deeper levels name their parent row. Empty uses the locale catalog (`Docs`). |
| `searchOpen` | `boolean` | `false` | false | Two-way value (`v-model:searchOpen`). |
| `navigationOpen` | `boolean` | `true` | false | Two-way value (`v-model:navigationOpen`). |
| `navigationWidth` | `number \| null` | `null` | false | Two-way value (`v-model:navigationWidth`). |

## Events

| Event | Payload | Notes |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `navigate` | `(event: MouseEvent \| KeyboardEvent, item: DocLayoutNavItem)` | Fired when a navigation row or a palette page is chosen; the layout then follows its `href` (an in-app path with the router). Event first. |
| `search-select` | `(event: MouseEvent \| KeyboardEvent, value: string \| number)` | Fired when a palette item from the `search` slot is chosen. Event first. |

## Slots

| Slot | Scope | Notes |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `default` | — | The article, rendered inside the layout's `<main>`. |
| `actions` | — | The header's end actions after the search (GitHub, Console). |
| `navigation-footer` | — | The foot of the navigation rail and of the drawer (the theme switch). |
| `aside` | — | Content under the outline in the end rail (a `DocCallToAction`). |
| `footer` | — | The footer under the article (`Footer variant="content"`). |
| `search` | { query: string } | Extra `Command.Group`s listed before the navigation pages. |

## v-model

| Model | Type | Default | Emits | Notes |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `v-model:search-open` | `boolean` | `false` | `update:searchOpen` | Whether the search palette is open; the header search and ⌘K / Ctrl K set it. |
| `v-model:navigation-open` | `boolean` | `true` | `update:navigationOpen` | Whether the navigation rail is open from `lg`; `false` collapses it to the edge affordance that brings it back. No effect below `lg`, where the rail is a drawer. |
| `v-model:navigation-width` | `number \| null` | `null` | `update:navigationWidth` | The rail's width in px after the reader drags its edge; `null` uses the natural width (`var(--container-2xs)`). Clamped by `Sidebar` between `var(--container-3xs)` and `var(--container-sm)`. |

## States

- Visual states: `default`; from `lg` the rail is `expanded` or `collapsed` (`Sidebar` `data-collapsed`, the edge affordance shown) and `resizing` while dragged; below `lg` the rail is a drawer (`open` / `closed`) opened from the header; the end rail shows from `xl` when the page registered at least one heading (the `aside` slot renders under the outline, never on its own); the palette `open` / `closed`.
- Outline: built from the headings the page registers, in document order, updated as a page mounts, changes or leaves (a route change swaps the entries); no heading registers on the server, so the server render and the first client render carry no outline rail and it appears once the page mounts. The entry of the heading nearest the top of the viewport (under the header) carries `aria-current="location"`; choosing an entry or a heading anchor scrolls the heading under the header and updates the address hash.
- Not applicable: `loading`, `empty`, `error`, `disabled`, `readonly`, `invalid` (the article owns its states).

## Accessibility

- Landmarks: the header is `AppHeader` (`<header>` named by the catalog `Documentation sections` on its section links); the rail is the `Sidebar` `<nav>` named by `ariaLabel`; the article is inside the single `<main>`; the end rail is an `<aside>` holding the `DocOnThisPage` navigation landmarks. A page must not render a second `<main>`.
- Keyboard map: `Tab` runs header → rail → article → end rail → footer; the rail's resize handle is a focusable `separator` (`Resize navigation`) moved with `ArrowLeft` / `ArrowRight`, its collapse button (`Collapse navigation`) folds the rail and the edge affordance's button (`Expand navigation`) brings it back, all owned by `Sidebar`; `⌘K` / `Ctrl K` opens the palette (`Command`); the compact menu button opens the drawer, `Escape` closes it and returns focus. Choosing an outline entry moves focus to its heading (made focusable with `tabindex="-1"`).
- Names: the mark link (`Azion Docs home`), the compact menu button (`Open documentation navigation`) and the search (`Search docs`) come from the locale catalog.
- Visible focus: composed controls own their rings; the mark link uses `focus-visible:ring-2 focus-visible:ring-(--ring-color)`.

## Examples

### Default

```vue
<script setup lang="ts">
  import Button from '@aziontech/webkit/button'
  import DocCallToAction from '@aziontech/webkit/doc-call-to-action'
  import DocHeading from '@aziontech/webkit/doc-heading'
  import DocLayout, { type DocLayoutNavGroup } from '@aziontech/webkit/doc-layout'
  import DocPageHeader from '@aziontech/webkit/doc-page-header'
  import DocProse from '@aziontech/webkit/doc-prose'
  import ThemeSwitcher, { type ThemeSwitcherValue } from '@aziontech/webkit/theme-switcher'
  import { ref } from 'vue'

  const navigation: DocLayoutNavGroup[] = [
    {
      label: 'Fundamentals',
      items: [
        { id: 'overview', label: 'Overview', href: '/docs' },
        {
          id: 'get-started',
          label: 'Get started',
          defaultOpen: true,
          children: [
            { id: 'create-account', label: 'Create an account', href: '/docs/create-account' },
            { id: 'first-deploy', label: 'First deploy', href: '/docs/first-deploy' }
          ]
        }
      ]
    }
  ]

  const theme = ref<ThemeSwitcherValue>('system')
</script>

<template>
  <DocLayout
    :navigation="navigation"
    :sections="[
      { label: 'Guides', href: '/docs' },
      { label: 'Reference', href: '/docs/reference' }

  >

        label="Console"
        variant="secondary"

            { label: 'Get started', href: '/docs/get-started' },

          ]"
        />

          <DocHeading
            anchor="before-you-start"
            :level="2"
            >Before you start</DocHeading
          >
          <p>You need an Azion account and a GitHub account.</p>
          <DocHeading
            anchor="deploy-with-cli"
            :level="2"
            >Deploy with Azion CLI</DocHeading
          >
          <p>Install the CLI, log in and run the deploy command from your project folder.</p>
          <DocHeading
            anchor="next-steps"
            :level="2"
            >Next steps</DocHeading
          >
          <p>Add a domain and turn on the cache for your static assets.</p>
        </DocProse>

<!-- components/doc-markdown.md -->

# Doc Markdown

> Renders a documentation page written in Markdown with Mintlify-style MDX component tags into the documentation components. Prose becomes plain semantic HTML (headings, paragraphs, lists, block quotes, rules, tables, inline code, links, bold, italic) that the surrounding `DocProse` styles, and the component tags (`<Note>`, `<Steps>`, `<CardGroup>`, `<Tabs>`, `<CodeGroup>`, `<Update>`, `<Tooltip>` …) mount `DocCallout`, `DocSteps`, `DocCardGroup`, `DocTabs`, `DocCodeGroup`, `DocChangelogEntry`, `HoverCard` and the rest. Headings render as `DocHeading` with unique slug ids, and the named export `parseDocMarkdown` returns the same headings (for a standalone `DocOnThisPage`; inside `DocLayout` the rendered `DocHeading`s fill the outline on their own) and the frontmatter (for `DocPageHeader`). It is a small in-house reader of a fixed MDX subset, not an MDX compiler: no JSX expressions, no imports, no script, and no `v-html`, so a page's source can never run code.

- Import: `import DocMarkdown from '@aziontech/webkit/doc-markdown'`
- Category: docs · Surface: docs · Structure: monolithic

## When to use

- Use DocMarkdown for a whole documentation page written in MDX; model Markdown that arrives while it renders uses StreamingResponse.
- A docs page whose content is authored as `.mdx` / Markdown text (a content folder, a CMS field, a string in data) instead of hand-composed Doc components.
- Copy held in data that carries inline markup (a sentence with code and a link in a block's fixture) and should render the same as a page paragraph.

## Do

- Wrap it in `DocProse`; inside `DocLayout` the headings it renders register with the layout's outline, so the page passes nothing. Read the frontmatter (and, for a standalone `DocOnThisPage`, the headings) from the same source: `const { headings, frontmatter } = parseDocMarkdown(source)`.
- Write one component tag per line block; a one-line callout (`<Note>Copy.</Note>`) is fine. Attribute values are strings, numbers, booleans or a string list (`tags={["API", "CLI"]}`); anything else stays literal text.
- Repeated headings are fine: the second `Test it` gets the id `test-it-2`, so anchors and the outline stay unique.
- Use `components` to swap the component a tag mounts (your own `Card`) or to add a block tag your docs use; never fork the parser.
- Write in-app links as root-relative paths (`/docs/domains`): they follow the app's router. `javascript:`, protocol-relative and other unsafe URLs render as their link text.

## Don’t

- A page composed by hand from `DocHeading`, `DocCallout`, `DocSteps` … → compose them directly inside `DocProse`; there is nothing to parse.
- Chat or AI responses streamed as Markdown → `StreamingResponse` / `ChatMessage`.
- Arbitrary MDX with JSX expressions, imports or custom script → compile it with an MDX toolchain in the app; this component reads a fixed subset only.
- Code with no prose around it → `CodeBlock` or `DocCodeGroup`.

## Related

- `DocProse` — wrap this component in it; it owns the type scale, the block rhythm and the copyable inline code chips.
- `DocHeading` — every Markdown heading renders as one, with the slug id the outline points at.
- `DocLayout` — its outline lists the rendered `DocHeading`s with no wiring. `DocOnThisPage` — takes `parseDocMarkdown(source).headings` as `items` when used without the layout.
- `DocPageHeader` — takes the frontmatter's `title` and `description`.
- `DocCallout`, `DocSteps`, `DocStep`, `DocCardGroup`, `DocCard`, `DocItem`, `DocAccordionGroup`, `DocAccordion`, `DocTabs`, `DocTab`, `DocCodeGroup`, `DocFrame`, `DocPrompt`, `DocChangelogEntry` — what the tags mount.
- `HoverCard` — an inline `<Tooltip tip title linkLabel href>` tag renders as a HoverCard: the term is a dotted-underlined button, the card shows the title, the definition and an optional link.
- `RegistrationFrame`, `ItemList` — the frame and list an `<ItemGroup>` renders its `<Item>` rows in.
- `StreamingResponse` — renders chat Markdown with the AI layer's reader; this component reuses that reader's inline tokenizer and `safeHref` and adds the MDX block tags, document heading levels and anchors a docs page needs, which a chat bubble must not have.

## Props

| Prop | Type | Default | Required | Description |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `source` | `string` | `''` | false | The page source: optional frontmatter, then Markdown with MDX component tags. |
| `components` | `Record<string, Component>` | `{}` | false | Replaces the component a block tag mounts, or adds a block tag, keyed by tag name (`Card`, `Note`). The tag's attributes become props. |

## States

- Visual states: `default` only; the component renders what the source holds.
- Lazy tags: a code group, tabs, an accordion, a frame, an item row or a prompt renders once its async chunk loads (in place on the server and on hydration).
- Empty: an empty `source` renders the empty root (no placeholder; the page owns its empty state).
- Unknown or malformed input: an unknown capitalized tag, an unclosed tag or an unsupported attribute expression stays literal text rather than disappearing; an unclosed tag runs to the end of the source.
- Links: a root-relative `href` is followed with the app's router (`useAppNavigation`); an `http(s)` link opens in a new tab with `rel="noreferrer"` and an announced suffix; a URL that is not `http(s)`, `mailto`, root-relative or a `#fragment` renders as plain text (the shared `safeHref` of the AI layer's Markdown reader).
- Not applicable: `loading`, `error`, `disabled`, `readonly`, `invalid` (static content).

## Accessibility

- Semantics: headings are real `h1`–`h4` (`DocHeading`) in source order with unique ids; lists are `ul` / `ol`; a table is a real `table` with `th scope="col"` header cells, wrapped in a horizontally scrollable region; a rule is `hr`; a quote is `blockquote`.
- Keyboard map: no keyboard handling of its own; links, heading anchors, tabs, accordions and code copy buttons are the mounted components' and follow their maps.
- Links: an external link announces `(opens in a new tab)` through a visually hidden suffix; link text is the author's.
- Visible focus: owned by `DocProse` (links) and the mounted components.
- Contrast: table header text `var(--text-muted)` and body text `var(--text-default)` on `var(--bg-surface)` meet 4.5:1 in both themes; no disabled state.
- Pattern: native document semantics; no WAI-ARIA APG widget of its own.
- Inside a callout, an item row or a prompt, paragraphs render as block spans so no `p` nests inside the component's own paragraph.

## Examples

### Default

````vue
<script setup lang="ts">
  import DocMarkdown from '@aziontech/webkit/doc-markdown'
  import DocProse from '@aziontech/webkit/doc-prose'

  const source = [
    '---',
    'title: First deploy',
    'description: Deploy a template and see it served from the edge.',
    '---',
    '',
    '## Before you start',
    '',
    'You need an Acme account. Log in with `acme login`, then read the [CLI reference](https://example.com/docs/cli).',
    '',
    '- A project folder with a `package.json`',
    '- Node.js 22 or later',
    '',
    '<Note>A first deploy takes about a minute.</Note>',
    '',
    '## Deploy with the CLI',
    '',
    '<Steps>',
    '  <Step title="Install the CLI">',
    '    <CodeGroup>',
    '    ```bash npm',
    '    npm install -g acme-cli',
    '    ```',
    '    ```bash pnpm',
    '    pnpm add -g acme-cli',
    '    ```',
    '    </CodeGroup>',
    '  </Step>',
    '  <Step title="Deploy">Run `acme deploy` from the project folder.</Step>',
    '</Steps>',
    '',
    '## Limits',
    '',
    '| Plan | Builds per day |',
    '| :--- | ---: |',
    '| Free | 100 |',
    '| Pro | 1,000 |',
    '',
    '## Next steps',
    '',
    '<CardGroup cols={2}>',
    '  <Card title="Domains" icon="az az-globe" href="/docs/domains">Point your own domain at the deploy.</Card>',
    '  <Card title="Cache" icon="az az-bolt" href="/docs/cache">Cache static assets at the edge.</Card>',
    '</CardGroup>'
  ].join('\n')
</script>

<template>
  <DocProse>
    <DocMarkdown :source="source" />
  </DocProse>
</template>
````
