<!-- components/doc-on-this-page.md -->

# Doc On This Page

> The "On this page" rail: the page's own headings, the active one lit, nested headings indented under their parent — followed by the rail's complementary groups (the repository, the community). The rail is a single path that bends inward as the outline nests, and the active marker is a dash cut from that same path, so moving between headings slides the marker **along** the line, through the bends, rather than jumping a separate bar between two positions.

- Import: `import DocOnThisPage from '@aziontech/webkit/doc-on-this-page'`
- Category: docs · Surface: docs · Structure: monolithic

## When to use

- Beside the body of a long documentation page, as in-page navigation over its `h2`/`h3` outline.
- To carry a page's peer links (repository, community) under the outline, each as its own named group.

## Do

- Feed it depth 2 and 3 only; the rail indents each level by 8px and a third level reads as noise.
- Own the active heading in the page (a scroll-spy) and pass it down; do not expect the rail to guess.
- Keep the complementary groups to peers of the page, never sections of it — a reader must not read "Join us on Discord" as part of what they are reading.

## Don’t

- Expecting it to track scroll. It is presentation only: `activeId` is a prop, because the page owns the scroll container and therefore owns which heading is active.
- For navigation _between_ pages — that is `DocPreviousNext` at the foot, or the sidebar.
- For an outline deeper than a couple of levels; the indent is legible for two, not for five.

## Related

- `DocPageHeader` — the masthead above the body, whose `h1` is chrome, so the outline starts at `h2`.
- `DocPreviousNext` — navigation between documents, where this is navigation within one.
- `use-heading-navigation` — the composable a page uses to receive a rail activation and scroll its own container.

## Props

| Prop | Type | Default | Required | Description |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `items` | `DocOnThisPageItem[]` | `[]` | false | The headings, in document order. |
| `activeId` | `string` | `''` | false | The id of the heading currently in view. |
| `title` | `string` | `''` | false | The rail's own heading. Empty uses the locale catalog (`On this page`). |
| `groups` | `DocOnThisPageGroup[]` | `[]` | false | Complementary groups rendered below the outline, in order. |

## Events

| Event | Payload | Notes |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `select` | `(event: MouseEvent, item: DocOnThisPageItem)` | Fired when a rail entry is activated; the page decides what scrolling means. Event first, per `event-payloads.md`. |

## States

- Visual states: `default`, `hover` (a morphing highlight follows the pointer), and the active entry
- The active entry carries `aria-current="location"`; the marker is a dash cut from the rail path, positioned by measured length rather than by an index
- The complementary groups are deliberately drawn as peers: no rail, no indent, no active marker

## Accessibility

- Visible focus: each entry takes the focus ring on `focus-visible`.
- Keyboard map: `Tab` moves through the entries and then through each group's links; `Enter` activates. The rail owns no arrow-key model — it is a list of links, not a widget.
- ARIA: the outline is a `nav` named by `title`, and **each complementary group is its own named `nav`**, so the three read as three landmarks rather than one long list. Entries are `li` children of a `ul` (the axe `list` rule requires that), and the active entry carries `aria-current="location"`. The rail path and the group glyphs are decorative (`aria-hidden`). Under `dir="rtl"` the rail svg is mirrored (`rtl:-scale-x-100`), so its depth bends step toward the inline end like the entries they follow.
- The marker is never the only signal: the active entry also changes ink and carries `aria-current`, so the state does not depend on seeing the dash.
- Contrast ≥4.5:1 (text) / ≥3:1 (large + icons).
- Touch target: entries are padded rows; the rail itself is not interactive.

## Examples

### Default

```vue
<script setup>
  import DocOnThisPage from '@aziontech/webkit/doc-on-this-page'
</script>

<template>
  <DocOnThisPage
    :items="[
      { id: 'deploy', text: 'Deploy an application', depth: 2 },
      { id: 'what-you-get', text: 'What you get', depth: 3 },
      { id: 'build-command', text: 'Build command', depth: 3 },
      { id: 'bind-a-domain', text: 'Bind a domain', depth: 2 }
    ]"
    active-id="what-you-get"
    :groups="[
      {
        label: 'Repository',
        links: [
          { label: 'View source', href: 'https://github.com/aziontech', icon: 'az az-github' }
        ]
      }
    ]"
    @select="(event, item) => scrollToHeading(item.id)"
  />
</template>
```

1 more example(s) in [components/doc-on-this-page.md](components/doc-on-this-page.md).

<!-- components/doc-page-header.md -->

# Doc Page Header

> The masthead of a documentation page: where the reader is (the breadcrumb), what the page is (the title), what they can do with it (a Copy Page control that hands the page to an AI tool — or, through the `actions` slot, the consumer's own menu; the trail is likewise slottable, for an application whose crumbs route rather than navigate), the deck that says what they will have by the end, and when the content last changed. It closes on a rule spanning the column, which is what gives every `h2` below it something to be subordinate to.

- Import: `import DocPageHeader from '@aziontech/webkit/doc-page-header'`
- Category: docs · Surface: docs · Structure: monolithic

## When to use

- Use DocPageHeader only on documentation pages; console pages use PageHeader.
- At the top of any documentation or changelog page, above `DocProse`.

## Do

- Give every `metaActions` entry a `tip`: two or three words name a control, and only a sentence says what it does.
- Give an entry an `href` when it has a destination, so it is a real link — then prevent the default in `action-click` if the app routes in-page.
- Let `lastUpdated` come from the page's own frontmatter. It is the author's claim that the content changed, not the file's mtime.
- Put the current page last in `breadcrumbs`; it is marked `active` for you.
- Use `title` and `details` for a page ABOUT something (a tool, a product): its mark and maker are a title, and its facts belong between the deck and the meta line.

## Don’t

- For an application screen's title — that is `PageHeader`, which carries console actions rather than a Copy Page control.
- For a section heading inside a page — the content's own `h2` does that, and the masthead's `h1` is chrome.
- The documentation home → use `DocHero` (display title, `Get started` and `CopyPromptButton`), then `DocAgentCard`.

## Related

- `DocProse` — the body below it. The masthead's `h1` is chrome, so authored content starts at `h2`.
- `DocOnThisPage` — the rail beside the body, which lists those `h2`s.
- `Button` / `Tooltip` / `Separator` — the meta line's entries: a quiet `text` button (an anchor when the entry has an `href`), the sentence its label has no room for, and the rule between two of them.

## Props

| Prop | Type | Default | Required | Description |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `title` | `string` | `''` | false | The page title. |
| `description` | `string` | `''` | false | The deck under the title. |
| `breadcrumbs` | `BreadcrumbItemData[]` | `[]` | false | Ancestor trail, current page last. |
| `lastUpdated` | `string` | `''` | false | When the page's content last changed. ISO date, or a ready-made string. |
| `metaActions` | `DocPageHeaderAction[]` | `[]` | false | The controls on the meta line, in reading order. |

## Events

| Event | Payload | Notes |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `action-click` | `(event: MouseEvent, item: DocPageHeaderAction)` | Fired when a meta-line control is activated; `item` is the entry that was picked. Event first, per `event-payloads.md`. A link's default navigation is the consumer's to keep or to prevent. |

## Slots

| Slot | Scope | Notes |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `breadcrumbs` | — | The trail; replaces the built-in `Breadcrumb` fed by the `breadcrumbs` prop. |
| `title` | — | The page's name; replaces the built-in `h1`, for a title that is more than a string. |
| `actions` | — | A control on the title's line. Empty by default; the meta line carries the page's actions. |
| `details` | — | Rows between the deck and the meta line — a page's own facts, tags or references. |

## States

- Visual states: `default`. The masthead holds no interactive state of its own; each meta-line entry owns its own hover, focus and tooltip.
- Each region renders only when it has content: no breadcrumb, deck, or meta line appears empty. An unfilled slot with a `v-if`-ed fallback renders no element, so a masthead with no trail pays no gap for the region; an empty `metaActions` with no date renders no meta line at all.
- `data-undated` on the meta line when there is no date: the line then starts with a `text` button, whose label sits one spacing step inside its own box, so the row is pulled left by exactly that padding to keep the ink on the column.
- The rules between entries render from `md` up only. Below that the line wraps per entry, and a rule at the end of a wrapped line has nothing after it.
- Below `sm` a passed `actions` control leaves the title's line and stacks under it at its natural width; from `sm` up the two share one row.

## Accessibility

- Visible focus: inherited from `Breadcrumb`, `Button` and `Tooltip`; the masthead adds no focusable element of its own.
- Keyboard map: `Tab` reaches the breadcrumb links and then each meta-line entry in reading order; a `tip` opens on focus, not on hover alone, so a definition is never pointer-only. The separators are `aria-hidden` — three announced separators on the way to three controls is noise.
- ARIA: the root is a `header`. The title is the page's single `h1`, so authored content must start at `h2`. The "last updated" glyph is decorative (`aria-hidden`) because the words "Last updated" sit beside it, and the date is wrapped in `<time datetime>` so the machine-readable value survives formatting.
- The ISO date is parsed and rendered in **UTC** on purpose: read as local time, a bare `2026-06-30` becomes the 29th for every reader west of Greenwich.
- Contrast ≥4.5:1 (text) / ≥3:1 (large + icons).
- Touch target: each meta-line entry is a standard `small` button (28px) with its own hover surface; nothing else is interactive.

## Examples

### Default

```vue
<script setup>
  import DocPageHeader from '@aziontech/webkit/doc-page-header'
</script>

<template>
  <DocPageHeader
    title="Deploy an application"
    description="Go from a template to a live edge application in a few clicks."
    :breadcrumbs="[{ label: 'Docs', href: '/docs' }, { label: 'Deploy an application' }]"
    last-updated="2026-06-30"
    :meta-actions="[
      {
        value: 'copy',
        label: 'Copy as Markdown',
        icon: 'az az-copy',
        tip: 'Copy this page as Markdown, ready to paste into an assistant.'
      },
      {
        value: 'markdown',
        label: 'View as Markdown',
        icon: 'az az-eye',
        tip: 'Open this page as plain Markdown in a new tab.'
      },
      {
        value: 'agent-setup',
        label: 'Agent setup',
        icon: 'az az-microchip-ai',
        href: '/docs/agent-setup',
        tip: 'Set up your coding agent to build on Azion.'
      }
    ]"
  />
</template>
```

2 more example(s) in [components/doc-page-header.md](components/doc-page-header.md).

<!-- components/doc-previous-next.md -->

# Doc Previous Next

> The previous / next pair that closes a documentation page. Each side is a link carrying an eyebrow and the destination's title; a side with no neighbour leaves its half of the row empty, so the remaining link stays anchored to its own edge rather than drifting to the centre.

- Import: `import DocPreviousNext from '@aziontech/webkit/doc-previous-next'`
- Category: docs · Surface: docs · Structure: monolithic

## When to use

- Use DocPreviousNext only at the foot of a documentation page to link the previous and next documents.
- At the foot of a page that sits in a reading order — a guide, a tutorial, a numbered series.
- Whenever "what do I read next" has a single obvious answer in each direction.

## Do

- Pass the destination's real title, not "Next page". The title is what tells the reader whether they want to go.
- Leave a side `null` at the ends of a series rather than looping around.
- Handle `navigate` when the app routes client-side; the anchor's `href` is what makes the link real for everyone else.

## Don’t

- To page through a data set — that is `Pagination`, which walks pages of rows and owns a page number. This links two neighbouring documents and knows nothing about a total.
- On a page with no reading order (a reference index, a landing page): a next link that points somewhere arbitrary is worse than none.

## Related

- `Pagination` — the data counterpart; numbered pages over a collection, not two named documents.
- `DocOnThisPage` — navigation _within_ the page, where this is navigation _between_ pages.

## Props

| Prop | Type | Default | Required | Description |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `previous` | `DocPreviousNextLink \| null` | `null` | false | The page before this one. |
| `next` | `DocPreviousNextLink \| null` | `null` | false | The page after this one. |
| `previousLabel` | `string` | `''` | false | Eyebrow over the previous link. Empty uses the locale catalog (`Previous`). |
| `nextLabel` | `string` | `''` | false | Eyebrow over the next link. Empty uses the locale catalog (`Next`). |

## Events

| Event | Payload | Notes |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `navigate` | `(event: MouseEvent, page: DocPreviousNextLink)` | Fired when either neighbour is activated; `page` is the side that was clicked. Event first, per `event-payloads.md`. |

## States

- Visual states: `default`, `hover` (the chevron travels outward), `focus-visible`
- One side absent renders an empty placeholder so the other stays edge-anchored; there is no `data-*` state, because the component holds none

## Accessibility

- Visible focus: `focus-visible:outline-2 focus-visible:outline-offset-2 focus-visible:outline-(--ring-color)` on each link.
- Keyboard map: `Tab` reaches each present side; `Enter` follows it. Nothing else — the component owns no keyboard model of its own.
- ARIA: the root is a `nav` labelled "Page navigation", so a screen reader announces it as a landmark distinct from the page's own nav. That name is **overridable** by passing `aria-label` — two nav landmarks may not share an accessible name (axe `landmark-unique`), so a page carrying two paginations must distinguish them. Each link's accessible name is its eyebrow plus the destination title; the chevrons are decorative (`aria-hidden`).
- Contrast ≥4.5:1 (text) / ≥3:1 (large + icons).
- Touch target: each side is a padded block, comfortably over 40×40 px.

## Examples

### Default

```vue
<script setup>
  import DocPreviousNext from '@aziontech/webkit/doc-previous-next'
</script>

<template>
  <DocPreviousNext
    :previous="{ label: 'Deploy an application', href: '/docs/deploy' }"
    :next="{ label: 'Bind a domain', href: '/docs/domains' }"
    @navigate="(event, page) => router.push(page.href)"
  />
</template>
```

1 more example(s) in [components/doc-previous-next.md](components/doc-previous-next.md).

<!-- components/doc-prompt.md -->

# Doc Prompt

> DocPrompt renders text the reader is meant to **run, not read** — the block a documentation page uses when the next step is "say this to your agent". It is the one component in this layer whose payload is addressed to a machine, so it is the one that ships an affordance for handing it to one: a copy control wired to the exact string on screen. It is deliberately **not** a code block — a prompt is a sentence, so it carries no language, no gutter, and no syntax highlighting, and it is set in the mono face only to say "this is the literal text, copy it as it stands".

- Import: `import DocPrompt from '@aziontech/webkit/doc-prompt'`
- Category: docs · Surface: docs · Structure: monolithic

## When to use

- Use DocPrompt to show a prompt the reader pastes into an agent, with its copy control.
- A documentation step, onboarding screen, or empty state whose next action is "paste this into your agent".
- A short list of example prompts a reader can try, under a heading that has already said what they are.
- Anywhere a literal string must be copied verbatim and the reader is the one who decides where it goes.

## Do

- Write the prompt once, in the default slot. It is both the visible text and the copied string, so the two cannot drift.
- Give `title` only when the surrounding page has not already said what the block is. Three one-line prompts under a "Prompts to try" heading want the bare shape.
- Use `variant="line"` for a single instruction and `variant="block"` for a priming paragraph. A one-sentence prompt broken across three lines reads as three instructions.
- Keep the prompt in the imperative and free of placeholders the reader must fill silently — if a value must be substituted, name it in the prose above.

## Don’t

- The payload is source code, a shell command, or configuration → use a code viewer, which owns languages, gutters, and highlighting; a prompt has none of them.
- The payload is a short inline token (a key, a flag, a variable name) → use `kbd` or inline code in prose.
- The block needs to run the prompt itself, or route it to a named vendor → out of scope; this component only puts the text on the clipboard.
- The "Copy prompt" action of a docs home hero or agent card, where the prompt is copied and not shown → use `CopyPromptButton` (inside `DocHero` or `DocAgentCard`).

## Related

- `copy-button` — the control DocPrompt composes. Reach for it directly when the value being copied has no block of its own.
- `DocCallout` — draws attention to prose the reader should _read_; DocPrompt is for text the reader should _use_.
- `DocProse` — the contract around it. The prompt marks itself `data-doc-chrome`, so prose rules stop at its edge, and `data-doc-block`, so it takes the block rung of the ladder.

## Props

| Prop | Type | Default | Required | Description |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `variant` | `'block' \| 'line'` | `'block'` | false | Wrapping paragraph capped at four lines, or one line that scrolls sideways. |
| `title` | `string` | `''` | false | What the block is, in a word or two. Renders as a titled row above the prompt. |
| `icon` | `string` | `'az az-sparkles'` | false | Azion icon class for the glyph beside the title. |
| `label` | `string` | `''` | false | Fallback prompt text when the default slot is empty. |

## Slots

| Slot | Scope | Notes |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `default` | — | The prompt itself: one or more sentences of literal text. Also the string the copy control carries. |

## Assets

- Icons: every valid name is in icons.md (MCP `list_assets({ kind: "icons" })`). An unknown class renders nothing.

## States

- Visual states: `default`, `focus-visible` (on the `line` shape, which is a scroll container)
- `data-variant` mirrors the `variant` prop: `block` | `line`
- `data-state` — `open` | `closed` on the prompt text of a capped `block`: `open` once the reader has opened it
- `data-capped` — present on the root when a `block` prompt is taller than its four-line cap; absent when it fits, so a prompt that fits earns no disclosure control
- `data-overflow` — on the `line` shape: `start` | `end` | `both`, whichever edge has text scrolled behind it; absent when the line fits
- `data-single-line` — on the prompt row when the prompt occupies exactly one line (`line` always; a `block` whose sentence fits its column). The row then centres the prompt against the copy control instead of top-aligning it — the control is 28px and one line of the prompt face is 19.5px, so a top-aligned pair leaves the sentence 4.25px above the control's middle. Absent the moment the prompt wraps.
- `data-doc-block` + `data-doc-chrome` are the `DocProse` contract (block rung; prose stops here)
- Empty: an empty slot with an empty `label` renders the shell with no prompt text and no copy control

## Accessibility

- Visible focus: `focus-visible:outline-2 focus-visible:outline-offset-2 focus-visible:outline-(--ring-color)` on the `line` shape
- Keyboard map: the `line` shape takes a tab stop because it is a scroll container — a scroll container holding no focusable child is unreachable by keyboard (and an axe `scrollable-region-focusable` violation); arrow keys then scroll it. The `block` shape scrolls nothing, so it takes no tab stop. `Tab` reaches the disclosure control and the copy control; `Enter` / `Space` activate them.
- ARIA: the full prompt text is in the DOM in every state, so the cap hides nothing from a screen reader — the disclosure is a visual convenience and the state it reports is the change in its own label (`Show more` / `Show less`). Every fade gradient is `aria-hidden="true"`; the title glyph is `aria-hidden="true"`. The copy control carries its own accessible name.
- Contrast ≥4.5:1 (text) / ≥3:1 (large + icons) — the prompt is `--text-default`, not the muted body ink, because the prompt IS the payload and everything around it is chrome
- `motion-reduce:transition-none` on both animated states; the disclosure move is skipped rather than shortened
- Touch target ≥40×40 px on both controls (both are `size="small"` webkit controls, which meet the floor)

## Examples

### Default

```vue
<script setup>
  import DocPrompt from '@aziontech/webkit/doc-prompt'
</script>

<template>
  <DocPrompt title="AI Assistant">
    Deploy this repository to Azion and show me the edge URL when it is live.
  </DocPrompt>
</template>
```

2 more example(s) in [components/doc-prompt.md](components/doc-prompt.md).

<!-- components/doc-prose.md -->

# Doc Prose

> The typography contract every documentation page inherits. A markdown or MDX pipeline compiles text to plain semantic HTML — `h2`, `p`, `ul`, `table`, `a`, `code` — and this container is what gives that HTML the Azion type scale, the semantic colors and the vertical rhythm, so an author writes markdown and never a class. It styles **descendants** rather than itself, so it works the same over any renderer's output, a slot of hand-written markup, or raw HTML from a CMS.

- Import: `import DocProse from '@aziontech/webkit/doc-prose'`
- Category: docs · Surface: docs · Structure: monolithic

## When to use

- Wrapping the body of a documentation or changelog page, whatever produced its HTML.
- Giving authored prose inside a documentation block (a step's body, a tab's panel) the same type contract as the page around it.

## Do

- Mark a documentation component's root `data-doc-chrome` so prose rules stop there, and `data-doc-block` so it takes the block rung of the ladder. A callout is both.
- Do not add per-page overrides. If a rung is wrong, it is wrong for every page and belongs in this contract.
- Keep the consumer's column cap outside this component; it is typography, not layout.

## Don’t

- To cap the reading column — `DocProse` has no measure of its own. The consumer sets the width with `.layout-column-content` (`--layout-measure-content`, 876px).
- To style a webkit component's internals. Those are chrome and are excluded by design; restyle nothing from here.
- For a single run of body copy in an application screen — use the `text-body-*` tokens directly.

## Related

- `DocPageHeader` — the page masthead above this container; its `h1` is chrome, so authored content starts at `h2`.
- `DocCallout` / `DocCard` / `DocSteps` — blocks that sit inside prose and mark themselves `data-doc-chrome` so the contract stops at their edge.

## Slots

| Slot | Scope | Notes |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `default` | — | The document body: markdown-rendered HTML or hand-written markup. |

## States

- Visual states: `default`, plus `hover` and `focus-visible` on descendant links, and `hover` on an authored inline `code` chip (a ring, and a tooltip offering the copy)
- No `data-state`: the container is static and holds no state of its own
- Reflow: an inline `code` chip wraps at any character (`wrap-anywhere`, with `box-decoration-clone` so each line keeps the chip's border and padding), so a long path or URL never widens the column at 320px or at 200% zoom. Code inside `pre` keeps its lines
- Clicking an authored inline `code` chip copies its text (a chip inside a link is the link: no copy ring, no tooltip, the click follows the link); the tooltip reads `Copy`, then `Copied`, and falls back to `Press ⌘C to copy` on macOS (`Press Ctrl+C to copy` elsewhere, the modifier from the `kbd` catalog) with the chip selected when the clipboard is refused. A chip inside `pre`, inside a link, or generated by a component (it carries a `data-testid`, or holds child elements) is excluded
- Reads two descendant contracts: `data-doc-chrome` (every prose rule stops there) and `data-doc-block` (takes the block rung of the ladder)

## Accessibility

- Visible focus: descendant links take `focus-visible:outline-2 focus-visible:outline-offset-2 focus-visible:outline-(--ring-color)`.
- Keyboard map: `Tab` moves through descendant links; the container itself is not focusable.
- ARIA: none — the container adds no role. The semantics are the author's HTML (`h2`, `ul`, `blockquote`), which is the point of the contract.
- Contrast: headings, `strong` and inline `code` are `--text-default` (≥14.7:1). **Body copy and list items are `--text-muted`, which is 3.78:1 on canvas and 3.95:1 on surface in light mode — under the 4.5:1 AA needs for body text** (dark is fine at 5.32:1 / 5.01:1). The contract carries that gap knowingly and in one place; closing it means a documentation body ink one step darker (`#737373` reads the same and clears AA at 4.54:1), not a per-page override.
- The chip copy adds **no tab stops**: it is a pointer shortcut over text that stays selectable the ordinary way, so a page of thirty chips does not become thirty stops. The tooltip is `role="tooltip"` and `pointer-events-none`.
- `motion-reduce:transition-none` on the descendant link transition.
- Touch target: not applicable; the container is not interactive.
- Teleported content: the teleported copy tip binds `dir` and `lang` from the nearest `ConfigProvider` (`usePortalLocale`), so it keeps the provider's direction and language although it renders under `body`. With no provider nothing is set and the document's own `dir` and `lang` apply.

## Examples

### Default

```vue
<script setup>
  import DocProse from '@aziontech/webkit/doc-prose'
</script>

<template>
  <DocProse>
    <h2>Deploy an application</h2>
    <p>
      Templates are ready-made projects that go live in a few clicks. You don't need any local
      tooling, and the <a href="https://www.azion.com/en/documentation/">template's guide</a> lists
      what each one needs.
    </p>
    <h3>What you get</h3>
    <ul>
      <li>A <strong>workload</strong> bound to its own Azion domain</li>
      <li>An edge application built from your <code>azion.config.js</code></li>
      <li>A certificate issued and renewed for you</li>
    </ul>
    <ol>
      <li>Pick a template</li>
      <li>Fill in the fields it asks for</li>
      <li>Deploy</li>
    </ol>
    <blockquote>
      <p>The first request to a location is a cache miss. Warm-up is per location, not global.</p>
    </blockquote>
    <h4>Build command</h4>
    <p>
      An h4 is a label inside a section rather than a new section, so it shares the base gap of the
      prose around it and carries its level on weight instead.
    </p>
    <hr />
    <p>A section break above resets the reader before the next part of the page.</p>
  </DocProse>
</template>
```

1 more example(s) in [components/doc-prose.md](components/doc-prose.md).

<!-- components/doc-steps.md -->

# Doc Steps

> The ordered walkthrough from the docs frame: a column of `DocSteps.Step` children joined by a rail. Each step registers itself through the context this root provides and reads its number back, so the numbering is a fact of the document order — an author writes titles, never numbers, and reordering the page renumbers it. The final registered step is marked last, which is what drops its connector and trailing space.

- Import: `import DocStepsRoot from '@aziontech/webkit/doc-steps-root'`
- Compound import (dot notation, not tree-shakeable): `import DocSteps from '@aziontech/webkit/doc-steps'`
- Category: docs · Surface: docs · Structure: composition

## When to use

- Use DocSteps only inside documentation prose for numbered instructions read top to bottom.
- A procedure the reader performs in order: install, link, deploy.
- Any "follow these steps" band in documentation prose, however short.

## Do

- Start each step title with the verb; keep the how in the step's body.
- Let the numbering be the document order — never encode a number into a title.
- Give a step a body only when the heading alone does not carry it; the rail stretches to cover whatever the body holds.

## Don’t

- For a wizard or form flow inside the application — that is `Stepper` (or `Wizard`), not documentation chrome.
- For an unordered list of options or features — order is the whole claim this component makes; a list that has none is a plain list or `DocItem` rows.
- For a single instruction — one step is a sentence, not a walkthrough.

## Related

- `Stepper` — the app's interactive step rail. Both number their steps with the same shared step registry (`useStepRegistry`), which reads the order from the page, so a step inserted between two others takes its place in the count.
- `DocProse` — the contract around it. The column marks itself `data-doc-block`, so it takes the block rung of the prose ladder.

## Slots

| Slot | Scope | Notes |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `default` | — | The `DocSteps.Step` children, in the order they should be numbered. |

## Sub-components

- `doc-steps.vue` — the root column; provides the numbering context.
- `doc-steps-step.vue` — `DocSteps.Step`, one numbered step: the circled index, the rail that continues to the next step, the step heading (`title` prop, `''` default) and an optional body in its default slot.

## States

- Visual states: `default` (the column is not interactive and holds no visual state of its own)
- Provides the numbering context (the shared step registry): each `DocSteps.Step` registers in setup, hands over its element on mount and deregisters on unmount; `index` is the 1-based position on the page; the final step's connector is suppressed by a last-child CSS variant
- `data-doc-block` is the `DocProse` contract (the column takes the block rung of the ladder)

## Accessibility

- Visible focus: the column is not focusable; `Tab` reaches only interactive content nested inside a step's body.
- Keyboard map: none of its own.
- ARIA: the column is a plain block. The circled indices and the rail are decorative (`aria-hidden`, owned by `DocSteps.Step`), so assistive tech reads the step headings and bodies in document order — the same order the numbers claim.
- Contrast: inherited from `DocSteps.Step`; the column paints nothing.
- Touch target: not applicable; the component is not interactive.

## Examples

### Default

```vue
<script setup>
  import DocSteps from '@aziontech/webkit/doc-steps'
</script>

<template>
  <DocSteps>
    <DocSteps.Step title="Access Azion Console." />
    <DocSteps.Step title="Click + Create on the homepage." />
    <DocSteps.Step title="Select your preferred template to start your project." />
    <DocSteps.Step title="Fill in the fields the template asks for." />
    <DocSteps.Step title="Customize the elements using the sidebar tools." />
  </DocSteps>
</template>
```

# Doc Tabs

> Alternative paths through the same instruction ("macOS / Linux / Windows", "Console / CLI"). It reads the `title` of each `DocTab` child to build the strip and renders each child body in its panel, on `Tabs`, so the strip, the indicator and the keyboard model are the design system's.

- Import: `import DocTabs from '@aziontech/webkit/doc-tabs'`
- Category: docs · Surface: docs · Structure: monolithic

## When to use

- Use DocTabs only in documentation prose for alternative paths through one instruction (macOS / Linux); product UI uses Tabs.
- The same procedure that differs per platform or per tool.

## Do

- Two to five tabs; put the most common path first.
- Keep the panels parallel so switching compares like with like.

## Don’t

- For code-only alternatives — use `DocCodeGroup`.
- For page navigation — use links or `DocCardGroup`.

## Related

- `DocTab` — the panel it reads.
- `Tabs` — the surface it renders on.
- `DocCodeGroup`.

## Slots

| Slot | Scope | Notes |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `default` | — | The `DocTab` children, in strip order. A child without a `title` is labelled by its position. |

## States

- The first tab is selected on render; selection state comes from `Tabs`.
- `data-doc-block` on the root; the strip carries `data-doc-chrome`.

## Accessibility

- `tablist`, `tab` and `tabpanel` roles and the arrow-key model come from `Tabs`.
- Panels have no padding of their own; the first block in a panel carries the prose rhythm.

<!-- components/registration-frame.md -->

# Registration Frame

> The registration frame: a hairline box with a small square set inside each corner and an optional linear hatch texture behind its content. Every side and every corner is addressable on its own, so frames stack and abut without doubling a rule or a mark. It is the container every framed page section is built from — a section title, the gap between two sections, a cell of a divider grid — so a page's rules and corner marks come from one component instead of from per-screen borders.

- Import: `import RegistrationFrame from '@aziontech/webkit/registration-frame'`
- Category: layout · Surface: site · Structure: monolithic

## When to use

- Wrapping a page section, band, or grid cell that should read as a framed module.
- Building a page whose vertical rhythm is drawn by rules rather than by margins: stack frames and let `flush` collapse each junction to a single hairline.
- Adding the corner registration marks (and optionally the hatch texture) to an area that already has its rules drawn by a surrounding grid — set `borders="none"` and keep `marks`.

## Do

- Stack or abut frames with `flush` so a shared edge reads as one hairline instead of two. It subtracts the named sides from this frame's own set, so it works vertically (`flush`, i.e. `top`) and horizontally (`flush="left"`) with the same mechanic.
- Use `borders` rather than overriding the border utilities from the call site: `y` for a full-bleed band that keeps only its top and bottom rules, `none` for a cell of a `gap-px` divider grid whose edges are already drawn by the grid's seams.
- Keep `marks` on: the corner squares are the frame's identity, and they cost nothing when the rules are handed over to a grid. On a shared edge, pair `flush` with the opposite `marks` keyword (`flush` + `marks="bottom"`, `flush="left"` + `marks="right"`) so the junction carries one rule and one mark per corner.
- Never double a line. A frame placed directly in a box whose top and side edges its container already draws (a `section-module` body with `padded` off, marked `data-frame-edges`) drops its own top and side rules and its two top corner marks: the header's bottom rule and the column's side rules are its edge there, so each seam stays one hairline at every width. Its bottom rule and bottom marks stay; the module that follows it passes `divided` as `false`.
- Reach for `hatch` sparingly — one hatched frame per view reads as texture; several read as noise. For a ruled ground, compose `texture` at its `lines` kind inside the frame instead: it is the system's texture layer, and its ink and pitch are tunable from the call site.

## Don’t

- For a card-shaped surface with padding, a header and a footer → use `card` instead.
- For a single separating line between two blocks → use `separator` instead.
- For the empty framed band that separates two sections → use `section-gap`, which is this frame configured for that one job.
- Built to be personalized (style seam): any class is accepted on the tag.

## Related

- `section-gap` — an empty `registration-frame` that holds the air between two sections.
- `section-title` — a `registration-frame` around a centered section header.
- `card` — a padded surface with header/content/footer regions; a card, not a frame.
- `separator` — one hairline with optional centered label; no corners, no content.

## Props

| Prop | Type | Default | Required | Description |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `borders` | `'all' \| 'none' \| 'x' \| 'y' \| Side \| Side[]` | `'all'` | false | Which of the frame's own rules to draw. Takes a keyword, one side, or a list of sides. |
| `marks` | `'all' \| 'none' \| Edge \| Corner \| Corner[]` | `'all'` | false | Which corner registration squares to draw. Takes a keyword (`all`, `none`, `top`, `bottom`, `left`, `right`), one corner, or a list of corners. |
| `hatch` | `boolean` | `false` | false | Show the frame's own linear hatch texture behind the content, faded toward the edges. Prefer `texture`'s `lines` kind for a ruled ground; `section-gap` composes that instead. |
| `flush` | `boolean \| Side \| Side[]` | `false` | false | Which sides a neighbouring frame already draws, so this one does not draw them again. `true` is shorthand for `top`; use `left` for a horizontal row, or a list for a grid cell. |

## Slots

| Slot | Scope | Notes |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `default` | — | Frame content; rendered above the hatch texture and stretched to the frame height. |

## States

- Visual states: `default`
- `data-borders` carries the RESOLVED, space-separated side list (`flush` already subtracted), or `none`
- `data-marks` carries the RESOLVED, space-separated corner list, or `none`
- `data-hatch` present when the hatch texture is drawn, or when a consumer sets it — a component that paints its own texture inside the frame keeps the attribute on this root
- `data-flush` carries the space-separated list of sides a neighbour draws; absent when none do
- Directly inside an element marked `data-frame-edges`, the frame's top and side rules and its two top corner marks are dropped, whatever `borders` and `marks` resolve to; the bottom rule and the bottom marks follow `borders` and `marks` as usual

## Accessibility

- Visible focus: not applicable — the frame is a container and holds no interactive surface of its own; focusable content composed into it keeps its own `focus-visible:ring-2 focus-visible:ring-(--ring-color)` ring.
- Keyboard map: none — the frame is not focusable and traps nothing; `Tab` order is decided entirely by the slotted content.
- ARIA: the hatch texture and the four corner squares are decorative and carry `aria-hidden="true"`; the root adds no role, so the slotted content's semantics reach the accessibility tree unchanged.
- Contrast ≥4.5:1 (text) / ≥3:1 (large + icons) — the rules and marks are non-informational decoration, and the slotted content owns its own contrast.
- `motion-reduce:transition-none motion-reduce:transform-none` — not applicable, the component is static.
- Touch target ≥40×40 px — not applicable, no interactive control.

## Examples

### Default

```vue
<script setup>
  import RegistrationFrame from '@aziontech/webkit/registration-frame'
</script>

<template>
  <RegistrationFrame>
    <div class="p-(--spacing-xl) text-center text-body-md text-(--text-muted)">Framed content</div>
  </RegistrationFrame>
</template>
```

2 more example(s) in [components/registration-frame.md](components/registration-frame.md).

<!-- components/site-layout.md -->

# Site Layout

> The marketing site's chrome, composed once in the app: `SiteHeaderSection` on top, the page's sections in the layout's `<main>`, `SiteFooterSection` at the foot, and the `Command` search palette wired to the bar's search button and to ⌘K / Ctrl K. The navigation and the footer are data (the `SiteHeaderSection` and `SiteFooterSection` props), the canvas is painted with the theme tokens so a dark site never shows an unpainted page below the fold, and the palette lists every destination of the navigation by default. Site pages are only their sections, rendered through the default slot; they never repeat the bar or the footer.

- Import: `import SiteLayout from '@aziontech/webkit/site-layout'`
- Category: layout · Surface: site · Structure: monolithic

## When to use

- Use SiteLayout once in the marketing app for the site chrome.
- Every public marketing page (home, product, solution, company, legal): put one `SiteLayout` in the app's root (around `RouterView`) and render each page's sections as its default slot.
- A site that needs the Azion navigation, footer and search palette without hand-composing `AppHeader`, `NavigationMenu`, `Footer`, `SearchTrigger` and `Command`.

## Do

- Compose the layout once, in the app's root; each route renders only its sections. Never add `SiteHeaderSection`, `SiteFooterSection`, `AppHeader` or `Footer` to a page.
- The site is dark: set `data-theme="dark"` on `<html>` (in `index.html`), so the layout, the teleported menus and the palette all resolve the dark tokens.
- Bind the real navigation and footer data once (`navigation`, `footer`); give every navigation item an `href` so it also appears in the search palette.
- Add extra palette groups (articles, docs results) in the `search` slot; listen to `search-select` for them.
- The page renders no `<main>` of its own: the layout owns the landmark.

## Don’t

- The signed-in console → `ConsoleLayout`. Documentation → `DocLayout`.
- A one-off landing page with no site navigation (a campaign form) → render the sections on their own.

## Related

- `SiteHeaderSection` — the bar and its mega-menus; `navigation` is bound onto it.
- `SiteFooterSection` — the footer; `footer` is bound onto it. In `variant="site"` it spans the full width like the bar, with no side rules, and opens on a plain full-width rule, the same clean line as the bar's, with no corner marks.
- `SectionContainer`, `SectionGap` — the frame the page's sections stack in, inside the slot.
- `Command` — the search palette the bar's search button opens.
- `shell/site` — the block that shows this layout with the azion.com navigation and footer.

## Props

| Prop | Type | Default | Required | Description |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `navigation` | `SiteLayoutNavigation` | `—` | true | The bar, bound onto `SiteHeaderSection`: `{ menus, home?, links?, contact?, primaryAction?, secondaryAction?, secondaryActionOnPhones? }`. |
| `footer` | `SiteLayoutFooter` | `undefined` | false | The footer, bound onto `SiteFooterSection`: `{ variant?, home?, columns?, socials?, statusLabel?, languages? }`; `variant` defaults to `site`. No footer when absent. |
| `searchPlaceholder` | `string` | `''` | false | Placeholder of the palette's input. Empty uses the locale catalog (`Search the site`). |
| `navOverlay` | `boolean` | `false` | false | Floats the bar over the page's first band instead of stacking it above; the bar stays sticky over the whole page. |
| `searchOpen` | `boolean` | `false` | false | Two-way value (`v-model:searchOpen`). |

## Events

| Event | Payload | Notes |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `navigate` | `(event: MouseEvent \| KeyboardEvent, page: SiteLayoutPage)` | Fired when a navigation destination is chosen in the palette (`{ label, href }`); the layout then follows it (with the app's router for an in-site path). Event first. |
| `search-select` | `(event: MouseEvent \| KeyboardEvent, value: string \| number)` | Fired when a palette item from the `search` slot is chosen. Event first. |

## Slots

| Slot | Scope | Notes |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `default` | — | The page's sections, rendered inside the layout's `<main>`. |
| `search` | { query: string } | Extra `Command.Group`s listed before the navigation destinations. |

## v-model

| Model | Type | Default | Emits | Notes |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `v-model:search-open` | `boolean` | `false` | `update:searchOpen` | Whether the search palette is open; the bar's search button and ⌘K / Ctrl K set it. |

## States

- Visual states: `default`; the palette `open` / `closed`; the bar's sheet and mega-menus own their states (`SiteHeaderSection`).
- `navOverlay` (mirrored on `data-nav-overlay`): the root becomes a one-column grid where the bar and the main landmark share the first row, so the first band runs under the bar (a home page whose hero art fills the top); the bar stays sticky to the end of the page and the footer takes the second row.
- Not applicable: `loading`, `empty`, `error`, `disabled`, `readonly`, `invalid` (the sections own their states).

## Accessibility

- Landmarks: the bar is the `SiteHeaderSection` banner named "Azion"; the sections are inside the layout's single `<main>`; the footer is the `SiteFooterSection` `contentinfo`. A page must not render a second `<main>`.
- Keyboard map: `Tab` runs bar → page → footer; `⌘K` / `Ctrl K` opens the palette (owned by `Command`); in the palette `ArrowUp` / `ArrowDown` move, `Enter` chooses, `Escape` closes and returns focus. The bar's menus and sheet own their keyboard models.
- Visible focus and contrast: owned by the composed components; the canvas tokens keep text ≥ 4.5:1 in light and dark.

## Examples

### Default

```vue
<script setup lang="ts">
  import HeroSection from '@aziontech/webkit/hero-section'
  import SectionContainer from '@aziontech/webkit/section-container'
  import SectionModule from '@aziontech/webkit/section-module'
  import SiteLayout, {
    type SiteLayoutFooter,
    type SiteLayoutNavigation
  } from '@aziontech/webkit/site-layout'

  const navigation: SiteLayoutNavigation = {
    home: '/en/',
    menus: [
      {
        value: 'products',
        label: 'Products',
        columns: [
          [
            {
              label: 'Build',
              items: [
                {
                  label: 'Functions',
                  description: 'Run serverless code close to your users',
                  href: '/en/products/functions/'
                },
                {
                  label: 'Applications',
                  description: 'Deliver and accelerate your applications',
                  href: '/en/products/applications/'
                }
              ]
            }
          ],
          [
            {
              label: 'Secure',
              items: [
                {
                  label: 'Firewall',
                  description: 'Stop attacks before they reach your origin',
                  href: '/en/products/firewall/'
                }
              ]
            }
          ]
        ]
      }
    ],
    links: [{ label: 'Pricing', href: '/en/pricing/' }],
    contact: { label: 'Contact', href: '/en/contact/' },
    secondaryAction: { label: 'Login', href: 'https://console.azion.com/login' },
    primaryAction: { label: 'Start for free', href: 'https://console.azion.com/signup' }
  }

  const footer: SiteLayoutFooter = {
    home: '/en/',
    columns: [
      {
        title: 'Products',
        links: [
          { label: 'Functions', href: '/en/products/functions/' },
          { label: 'Firewall', href: '/en/products/firewall/' }
        ]
      },
      {
        title: 'Company',
        links: [
          { label: 'About us', href: '/en/about-us/' },
          { label: 'Careers', href: '/en/careers/' }
        ]
      }
    ],

    ],

<template>
  <SiteLayout
    :navigation="navigation"
    :footer="footer"
  >
    <HeroSection
      variant="center"

    />
    <SectionContainer max-width="site">
      <SectionModule
        :divided="false"
        title="Deploy from the CLI or from Git"
        description="Push a change and it goes live on every edge location in seconds."
      />
    </SectionContainer>
  </SiteLayout>
</template>
```

<!-- components/backdrop.md -->

# Backdrop

> The decorative backdrop a site, hub or docs band stands on, for a `Hero` `background` slot or any band with `relative overflow-hidden`: `globe` hangs half a globe past the page's inline-end rule over the dot lattice, `map` insets the world map beside the copy, `network` lays the map full bleed with its PoPs lit, the sample's `MapMesh` traffic crossing between them (`NetworkMap` `traffic`: links and packets, the links alone as the still frame under reduced motion) and a scrim over the copy column. From the design sample's `shared/ui/banners` (GlobeBanner, MapBanner, NetworkBanner), drawn with WebKit's own `SpinningGlobe` and `NetworkMap` so there is one map and one globe in the system. The inventory's "`Banner` `art` variants" became this sibling component, `backdrop`: `Banner` is an announcement band with its own surfaces, and a backdrop layer is a different thing.

- Import: `import Backdrop from '@aziontech/webkit/backdrop'`
- Category: marketing · Surface: site · Structure: monolithic

## When to use

- Behind the opening band of a landing, the hub or the docs home.

## Do

- One backdrop per page.
- Keep the copy at the inline start: every art is washed toward it.

## Don’t

- As content: it is `aria-hidden` and says nothing.
- Behind dense text or a form.
- For a plain texture — use `Texture`.

## Related

- `Hero` — the `background` slot it fills.
- `NetworkMap`, `SpinningGlobe` — what it draws.
- `Texture` — the dot lattice.
- `Banner` — the announcement band (unrelated surface).

## Props

| Prop | Type | Default | Required | Description |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `variant` | `BackdropVariant` | `'map'` | false | Which backdrop to draw: `globe`, `map` or `network`. |
| `animated` | `boolean` | `true` | false | Spins the globe or pulses the PoPs; a still frame when false or under reduced motion. |

## States

- `data-variant` mirrors the backdrop drawn.

## Accessibility

- `aria-hidden="true"` and `pointer-events-none`: never announced, never in the way of a click.
- The washes keep the copy column at canvas contrast.

## Examples

### Default

```vue
<script setup>
  import Backdrop from '@aziontech/webkit/backdrop'
  import Hero from '@aziontech/webkit/hero-root'
  import HeroTitle from '@aziontech/webkit/hero-title'
</script>

<template>
  <Hero
    variant="band"
    size="large"
    max-width="site"
  >
    <template #background>
      <Backdrop variant="map" />
    </template>
    <HeroTitle
      eyebrow="Network"
      eyebrow-prefix="//"
      title="Close to every user, by default"
      description="Requests land on the nearest location and stay there."
      size="large"
    />
  </Hero>
</template>
```

1 more example(s) in [components/backdrop.md](components/backdrop.md).

<!-- components/bento-grid.md -->

# Bento Grid

> A mosaic of unequal cells on one set of shared rules: each cell claims a number of columns and rows, and the rules between them are drawn once so the whole block reads as a single framed surface. It is the layout a marketing page uses when several claims deserve different amounts of room — the alternative to a row of identical tiles.

- Import: `import BentoGridRoot from '@aziontech/webkit/bento-grid-root'`
- Compound import (dot notation, not tree-shakeable): `import BentoGrid from '@aziontech/webkit/bento-grid'`
- Category: marketing · Surface: site · Structure: composition

## When to use

- When a set of claims is genuinely unequal and one or two deserve more room than the rest.
- As the framed body of a product or platform section, under a `section-title`.
- For a logo wall, a client-story band, or any set where a few members carry a headline and the rest carry only a mark.
- Whenever the cells hold composed content — copy, media, a control — rather than one repeated shape.

## Do

- Size the mosaic so the cells fill the rectangle: count the tracks (`columns` × rows) and check the spans add up, or the grid ends on a gap of bare canvas.
- Keep cells in reading order — the grid auto-places in DOM order, so that order is what a screen reader and a keyboard follow, and it is what puts a cell in the track you meant.
- Compose real elements inside a cell rather than passing a config array; the cell is a slot, and that is the point of it.
- Let the grid draw the rules. Do not add borders inside a cell, or the shared edges double.
- Give a cell `variant="none"` + `padded="false"` when its own content paints the cell — a media layer, a brand fill, or a link that must be the whole tile.
- Inside a framed page column, set `flush` so the column's rules stay one hairline each.

## Don’t

- When every claim carries the same weight → use `card-grid`.
- For a row of measured figures → use `stats`, which already draws this grid for numbers.
- For a single copy-and-media pairing → use `media-split`.
- For a list of questions → use `faq`.

## Related

- `card-grid` — the equal-cell grid with no spans, in the gap or divider register.
- `stats` — the same collapsed-rule grid, specialised for figures.
- `media-split` — the two-column band when there is one claim, not several.
- `registration-frame` — the frame each cell is drawn with.

## Props

| Prop | Type | Default | Required | Description |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `ariaLabel` | `string` | `''` | false | Accessible name for the grid, when the surrounding section does not already name it. |
| `columns` | `BentoGridColumns` | `2` | false | How many columns the mosaic lays out from the medium breakpoint up. |
| `mobileColumns` | `BentoGridMobileColumns` | `1` | false | How many columns the mosaic holds below the medium breakpoint, where every cell claims one of them. |
| `flush` | `boolean` | `false` | false | The surrounding frame already draws the grid's outer rules: the grid drops its own top and left ones and lays its right and bottom ones onto the frame's, so a framed column keeps one hairline per edge. |

## Slots

| Slot | Scope | Notes |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `default` | — | The grid's cells, composed as `BentoGrid.Cell` elements in reading order. |

## Sub-component imports

- `@aziontech/webkit/bento-grid-cell`

## Sub-components

- `bento-grid-cell/bento-grid-cell.vue` — one cell of the mosaic; owns its own frame, and takes the tracks it claims (`span`, `rows`), the fill it carries (`variant`) and whether it pads its content (`padded`).

Cell props: `span` (`'1' | '2' | '3' | '4' | 'full'`, default `'1'`) — columns claimed from the medium breakpoint up; `rows` (`'1' | '2'`, default `'1'`) — rows claimed from the medium breakpoint up; `variant` (`'surface' | 'canvas' | 'none'`, default `'surface'`) — the cell's fill, where `none` leaves the surface to the composed content; `padded` (`boolean`, default `true`) — off for a cell whose content reaches its edges.

The compound `index.ts` annotates the `Object.assign` result with an explicit `typeof BentoGrid & { Cell: typeof BentoGridCell }` type. Without it, declaration emit expands the root's private `Props` interface and `vue-tsc` fails with TS4082 ("default export of the module has or is using private name"). Do not simplify it back to a bare `Object.assign`.

## States

- Visual states: `default`
- `data-columns` and `data-mobile-columns` on the grid mirror the track counts it lays out at each breakpoint
- `data-flush` on the grid mirrors `flush`, and is what withholds its own outer rules
- `data-span` and `data-rows` on each cell mirror its props and drive the tracks it claims from `md` up
- `data-variant` and `data-padded` on the cell's content box mirror its fill and its padding
- Below `md` every cell claims one of the mobile columns — only `span="full"` still takes the whole row — and no cell claims a second row
- Empty: a grid with no cells renders no rules, so an unfilled grid leaves no empty frame on the page

## Accessibility

- Visible focus: the grid and its cells are containers and are not focusable; controls composed inside a cell keep their own `focus-visible:ring-2 focus-visible:ring-(--ring-color) focus-visible:ring-offset-2 focus-visible:ring-offset-(--bg-canvas)` ring.
- Keyboard map: none of its own — `Tab` follows DOM order through whatever the cells contain. The grid is not a composite widget, so it declares no arrow-key model.
- ARIA: the grid is a plain container named by `ariaLabel` when set; cells add no role, so headings composed inside them keep the document outline the page intends.
- Contrast ≥4.5:1 (text) / ≥3:1 (large + icons): the cells provide `var(--bg-surface)` or `var(--bg-canvas)`; a `variant="none"` cell hands both the fill and its contrast to the composed content.
- `motion-reduce:transition-none motion-reduce:transform-none` — not applicable, the grid is static.
- Touch target ≥40×40 px — not applicable; the grid holds no interactive target of its own.

## Examples

### Default

```vue
<script setup>

  import BentoGridRoot from '@aziontech/webkit/bento-grid-root'
</script>

<template>
  <BentoGridRoot aria-label="Platform capabilities">
    <BentoGridCell>
      <h3 class="m-0 text-heading-sm text-(--text-default)">Deploy in seconds</h3>
      <p class="m-0 mt-(--spacing-sm) text-body-sm text-(--text-muted)">
        Push to a repository and the application is live at every location, with no pipeline to
        assemble first.
      </p>
    </BentoGridCell>
    <BentoGridCell>
      <h3 class="m-0 text-heading-sm text-(--text-default)">Scale without capacity planning</h3>

        Traffic is absorbed where it arrives, so there is no region to size and nothing to provision
```

<!-- components/call-to-action.md -->

# Call To Action

> The closing ask of a marketing page: a headline, a supporting sentence and the actions that follow from them, set apart from the surrounding copy by its own surface. It is a panel, not a section header — `section-title` opens a section, `call-to-action` closes one. Four registers. `panel` sets the copy and its controls on one surface, as a row from `md` up. `split` divides the band in two at a 7fr/3fr ratio: a raised lead cell carrying the headline and the primary action, and an aside carrying the supporting line and the secondary one — each cell pushing its control to the band's floor, so the two actions share a baseline at any height. `split` is the register a product page closes on, where the ask has a primary and a secondary path and the headline turns on a second, muted line. `lead` is that lead cell standing alone: the same raised surface, the same two-tone headline, with the supporting line moved under it and every control in one row on the band's floor. It is the register for a close that has no second, quieter path to describe — the band states the ask once and offers its actions together, rather than dividing them across two cells of unequal weight. `announcement` is the loud mid-page interruption: the announcement, its eyebrow and its actions in a wide leading column on the surface, and a short supporting note set large on a raised trailing column (a third of the band from `md` up, stacked under it below). The note is the `description`, or the default slot when it carries a link. `primary` is the loud close: one centred ask standing alone as a billboard, on the page canvas lit from above by the brand. The texture composed into the band — a pixelated layer whose two waves travel across it in the primary colour — is lit by its waves alone and carries the band's colour, so the ground stays the canvas and the copy keeps its ordinary ink. The texture is composed in through the `background` slot and the strip along the floor through `bottom`, so the band owns the light and the page owns what is drawn on it.

- Import: `import CallToAction from '@aziontech/webkit/call-to-action'`
- Category: marketing · Surface: site · Structure: monolithic

## When to use

- Use CallToAction (primitive) for an ask panel inside a hand-built SFC, mid-page or closing; set `variant="announcement"` for a loud mid-page announcement.
- Rendering page JSON, or you need a whole band with no custom content → the section (`closing-call-to-action-section`). Building an SFC by hand, needing slots, or nesting inside another band → this primitive.
- At the end of a marketing page or section, to carry the reader into signup, docs or sales.
- Whenever a page needs one visually separated block whose whole job is the action.
- Between two sections, as a mid-page ask that should not read as a new section header.
- In `split`, when the ask has two paths of unequal weight — a primary one to start and a secondary one to talk to someone.
- In `primary`, when the page ends on a single ask and the close should be the most lit band on the page, with benefits or proof running along its floor.
- In `lead`, when the band's actions are peers and belong in one row, so an aside cell would have nothing of its own to carry.
- In `announcement`, to announce a launch, programme or offer part-way down a marketing page, with its actions in a wide leading column and a short supporting note on a raised trailing column. Once per page.

## Do

- `framed` decides who draws the rule. On, the band draws its own registration frame — vertical rules handed back to the column, a tick at its floor. Off, the rule and the corner marks belong to the `section-module` or `registration-frame` the page assembles it into, so a band placed in an already-framed column never lands a second hairline on the column's own.
- Keep `title` to one line and one ask. A panel that poses two questions gets neither answered.
- Put the controls in the `actions` slot; the panel lays them out as a row beside the copy from `md` up and stacks them full-width below it.
- Lead with the primary action and keep secondary ones `outline` or `text`, so the panel has one obvious target.
- Use `eyebrow` only when the panel needs naming out of context; the headline usually carries the ask on its own.
- In `split`, `title` states the claim and `title-muted` states its consequence — one sentence in two tones, not two headlines. The band renders them as two spans of a single `h2`, so a screen reader reads one heading.
- In `split`, put the primary control in `actions` and the secondary one in `aside`; each cell floors its own control, which is what gives the two a shared baseline. `actions` stretches its child full-width below `md`; `aside` stretches its child at every width, so a control drawn to size to its own content goes in a wrapper there.
- In `lead`, every control goes in `actions` — they lay out as one row from `md` up and stack full-width below it. The copy column is capped at `--container-2xl` so the headline keeps a readable measure however wide the band runs.
- `lead` sets the copy-to-actions gap at `--spacing-xl`, not the `--spacing-2xl` the other two use. In `split` that larger number is the _floor_ of a stretched cell — what pushes the control onto the band's bottom edge — and in `panel` the controls sit beside the copy from `md` up. In `lead` nothing stretches and nothing sits beside, so `--spacing-2xl` stops reading as a floor and starts reading as a hole.
- In `primary`, the band is centred and carries `title` and `description` only; `eyebrow` and `titleMuted` are not drawn, so the headline stands alone over the texture.
- In `primary`, compose one `texture` into `background` — `pixelate` at `small` with `fade="bottom"` — and a `marquee` into `bottom`. The band moves the pixelate's resting pools out of frame and runs its two waves at triple gain and double pace, so the travelling bands carry the light instead of a still glow; keep the headline clear of the densest cells by leaving the band's own padding alone.
- In `primary`, give the marquee enough claims that the loop never repeats inside one screen, and let the pass derive itself: an item pass runs three times slower per claim than a mark pass, because a claim is read.
- Leave `framed` on when the band stands in a section column of its own, and off when the page already wraps it in a `registration-frame` — two frames draw two hairlines on the same edge.

## Don’t

- To open a section with a headline and optional actions → use `section-title`, which draws a header rule rather than a panel.
- For the page's leading statement and primary CTAs → use `hero`, whose `Hero.Title` owns the `h1`.
- For a dismissible or time-bound notice inside the product → use `alert`.
- For a close that sits quietly in the column → use `split` or `panel`; `primary` fills a whole brand-coloured band and should appear once per page.
- For a plan's call to action inside pricing → use `pricing-card`, which owns its own action row.

## Related

- `section-title` — opens a section with a header rule; this closes one with a panel.
- `hero` — the page's leading band; its `Hero.Title` owns the statement and the `h1`.
- `registration-frame` — the registration frame the band draws when `framed` is on, and the one the page supplies around it when off.
- `button` — the controls composed into the `actions` and `aside-actions` slots.
- `eyebrow` — the `//`-prefixed eyebrow the band renders above the headline.
- `texture` — the pixelated, waving texture composed into `background` in `primary`.
- `marquee` — the strip of benefits composed into `bottom` in `primary`.

## Props

| Prop | Type | Default | Required | Description |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `title` | `string` | `—` | true | Headline of the band, rendered as its `h2`. |
| `titleMuted` | `string` | `''` | false | Second line of the headline, set in muted ink; rendered as a second span of the same `h2`. |
| `description` | `string` | `''` | false | Supporting sentence — under the headline in `panel` and `lead`, in the aside cell in `split`, on the raised trailing column in `announcement`; overridden by the default slot. |
| `eyebrow` | `string` | `''` | false | Short uppercase overline rendered above the headline. |
| `variant` | `CallToActionVariant` | `'panel'` | false | Layout of the band: `panel` sets the copy and its controls on one surface, `split` divides it into a raised lead cell and an aside that each floor their own control, `lead` is that lead cell standing alone with every control in it, `primary` is a centred ask on the canvas lit by a pixelated, waving primary texture, with a floor, `announcement` is a mid-page announcement with its actions in a wide leading column and the supporting note on a raised trailing column. |
| `framed` | `boolean` | `false` | false | Draw the band's own registration frame. Turn it off when the page already wraps the band in a frame. |

## Slots

| Slot | Scope | Notes |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `default` | — | Description body; replaces the `description` prop when provided. |
| `actions` | — | The controls the band exists to offer; in `split`, the lead cell's primary control. |
| `aside` | — | The aside cell's control in `split`; ignored in `panel`, `lead`, `primary` and `announcement`, where every control belongs in `actions`. |
| `background` | — | Decoration drawn behind the copy in `primary`, such as a `texture`; ignored in the other registers. |
| `bottom` | — | The strip that floors the band in `primary`, such as a `marquee`, under a hairline; ignored in the other registers. |

## States

- Visual states: `default`
- Register: `data-variant="panel" | "split" | "lead" | "primary" | "announcement"` on the root
- The band holds no state of its own; controls composed into `actions` / `aside` keep theirs

## Accessibility

- Visible focus: not applicable to the panel itself; controls composed into `actions` keep their own `focus-visible:ring-2 focus-visible:ring-(--ring-color) focus-visible:ring-offset-2 focus-visible:ring-offset-(--bg-canvas)` ring.
- Keyboard map: none of its own — `Tab` reaches only the controls placed in the `actions` slot, in DOM order.
- ARIA: the headline is a real `h2` — rendered by the composed `section-title` in `panel`, and by the band itself in `split` and `lead`, where `title` and `title-muted` are two spans of that one heading so it is announced as a single string. The band is a `<section>` named by its `title`, reachable as a landmark with a name; no `role` is added.
- Contrast ≥4.5:1 (text) / ≥3:1 (large + icons): in `primary` the copy is ordinary ink on `var(--bg-canvas)`, so the pair is the same as `panel`'s. The headline is `var(--text-default)` on `var(--bg-surface)`; the description uses `var(--text-muted)`, which clears AA on the dark canvas and is a known theme-level shortfall on the light one (see `stats`).
- `motion-reduce:transition-none motion-reduce:transform-none` — not applicable, the panel is static.
- Touch target ≥40×40 px — both action slots stretch their children to full width below the breakpoint, so slotted buttons keep their own target size.

## Examples

### Default

```vue
<script setup>
  import Button from '@aziontech/webkit/button'
  import CallToAction from '@aziontech/webkit/call-to-action'
</script>

<template>
  <CallToAction
    title="Run your first application at the edge today."
    description="Deploy once and it runs in every location on the network — no servers to size, no credit card to start."
  >
    <template #actions>
      <Button label="Start for free" />
      <Button
        variant="outline"
        label="Talk to sales"
      />
    </template>
  </CallToAction>
</template>
```

2 more example(s) in [components/call-to-action.md](components/call-to-action.md).
