<!-- components/card-grid.md -->

# Card Grid

> The responsive cell grid of the page language, in three registers: spaced cards separated by real gutters, a hairline box grid whose internal rules are the gaps themselves, and a framed grid whose cells are real `registration-frame` elements. The `frame` register is the one that composes: each `CardGrid.Cell` draws its own rules and corner marks and carries its own fill, so the grid needs no background trick and nests flush inside a column that already owns the outer edges.

- Import: `import CardGridRoot from '@aziontech/webkit/card-grid-root'`
- Compound import (dot notation, not tree-shakeable): `import CardGrid from '@aziontech/webkit/card-grid'`
- Category: marketing · Surface: site · Structure: composition

## When to use

- For a row or block of equal cells that should fan out from one column on a phone to several on a wide screen.
- With `variant` set to `frame` whenever the cells are real content — a `feature-item`, a claim, a figure — and should carry the page's frame language, corner marks included.
- For the hairline cell grid a `section-module` body holds, with `variant` set to `divider`.
- For a row of self-contained cards with gutters between them, with the default `gap`.

## Do

- **Do** hand the grid however many cells the content has. The default `balanced` layout takes the fewest rows `columns` allows and the narrowest track count that keeps them, so a count that splits evenly fills every row; a short last row keeps the track width and starts at the start edge, its trailing tracks left empty, as azion.com lays out its grids (compliance badges, the use-case grid). Under `columns: 3` that is 1 → one full-width cell, 2 → halves, 3 → thirds, 4 → 2 × 2, 5 → 3 + 2, 6 → 3 + 3, 7 → 3 + 3 + 1; under `columns: 4`, 4 → four across, 5 → 3 + 2, 6 → 3 + 3, 7 → 4 + 3; under `columns: 5`, 5 → five across. Below `lg` the same rule runs against two columns (`sm`) and against `mobileColumns` (phone), so 4 cells are 2 × 2 on a tablet and four across from `lg`.
- **Don't** pick `columns` from the item count or pad the list with placeholder cells. `columns` is the most a row may hold, not how many it must; the track count already drops to the even split (2 → halves, 4 → 2 × 2), and a short last row is never stretched across the empty tracks — a stretched last cell reads as a different, wider card.
- **Don't** reach for `layout="fixed"` to fix a count: keep it for tiles whose width must not change from row to row — square logo tiles, a cell that spans rows (`customer-use-cases-section`) or columns (`subject-grid-section`'s closing cell), a floor that keeps its track count whatever it holds (`network-section` benefits), a wall that overrides the track count. Anything else that wants unequal cells belongs in `bento-grid`.
- Prefer `frame` over `divider` for new work. The hairline register depends on every child filling its own background; the framed register draws real rules, so a cell that forgets its fill still reads correctly, and the corner marks come with it.
- Give every child of a `divider` grid its own background fill, or the gap trick has nothing to show through and the whole band goes the colour of the rule.
- Leave the perimeter to the container: the `divider` register draws no outer border, and a `frame` grid set to `flush` withholds its own top and left rules so a surrounding `registration-frame` keeps one hairline per edge.
- Let the grid draw the rules. Do not add borders inside a `frame` cell, or the shared edges double.
- Give a cell `variant="none"` and `:padded="false"` when its own content paints the cell — a media layer, a brand fill, or a link that must be the whole tile.
- Reach for `mobileColumns` of `2` only for short, glyph-led cells; a cell carrying a description reads better one-up on a phone.

## Don’t

- For a mosaic whose cells claim different numbers of columns and rows → use `bento-grid`, which is this grid with spans.
- For a single framed box → use `registration-frame`.
- For one card with a header, content and footer → use `card`.
- For the band that wraps the grid → use `section-module`.
- For tabular data a reader scans row by row → use `table`.
- Built to be personalized (style seam): any class is accepted on the tag.

## Related

- `bento-grid` — the same framed grid with unequal cells; reach for it when one claim deserves more room than the rest.
- `feature-item` — the glyph, headline and sentence a `frame` cell usually holds.
- `section-module` — the band whose body holds this grid.
- `section-container` — the column whose vertical rules the `divider` and flush `frame` grids deliberately do not repeat.
- `registration-frame` — the frame each `frame` cell is drawn with, and the framed box a `gap` cell may be built from.
- `card` — a padded card surface, the usual child of a `gap` grid.

## Props

| Prop | Type | Default | Required | Description |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `variant` | `CardGridVariant` | `'gap'` | false | Register of the grid: gutters between self-contained cards, hairline rules drawn by the gaps, or framed cells that draw their own rules and marks. |
| `columns` | `CardGridColumns` | `3` | false | How many columns the grid fans out to at the large breakpoint. |
| `mobileColumns` | `CardGridMobileColumns` | `1` | false | How many columns the grid holds below the small breakpoint. |
| `dividerColor` | `CardGridDividerColor` | `'default'` | false | Weight of the hairline rules in the `divider` register. |
| `flush` | `boolean` | `false` | false | In the `frame` register, the surrounding frame already draws the grid's outer rules: the grid drops its own top and left ones and lays its right and bottom ones onto the frame's, so a framed column keeps one hairline per edge. |
| `layout` | `CardGridLayout` | `'balanced'` | false | How the cells fill the rows: `balanced` picks the narrowest track count that keeps the fewest rows `columns` allows, so a count that splits evenly fills every row (2 → halves, 4 → 2 × 2), and a short last row keeps the track width with its trailing tracks left empty, as azion.com does (5 → 3 + 2, 7 → 3 + 3 + 1); `fixed` keeps a strict `columns`-wide grid whatever the count. |

## Slots

| Slot | Scope | Notes |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `default` | — | The cells, in reading order. In the `frame` register they are `CardGrid.Cell` elements; in the `divider` register each cell fills its own background. |

## Sub-component imports

- `@aziontech/webkit/card-grid-cell`

## Sub-components

- `card-grid-cell/card-grid-cell.vue` — one cell of the `frame` register; wraps `registration-frame` so the cell owns its own rules and corner marks, and takes the fill it carries (`variant`) and whether it pads its con…

Cell props: `variant` (`'surface' | 'canvas' | 'none'`, default `'surface'`) — the cell's fill, where `none` leaves the surface to the composed content; `padded` (`boolean`, default `true`) — off for a cell whose content reaches its edges.

The cell's `registration-frame` is set `flush` on its top and left edges, so it draws only its right and bottom rules; the grid root draws the matching top and left ones. That is what gives the block one hairline per edge instead of two at every junction.

The compound `index.ts` annotates the `Object.assign` result with an explicit `typeof CardGrid & { Cell: typeof CardGridCell }` type. Without it, declaration emit expands the root's private `Props` interface and `vue-tsc` fails with TS4082 ("default export of the module has or is using private name"). Do not simplify it back to a bare `Object.assign`.

## States

- Visual states: `default`
- `data-variant` carries the register
- `data-columns` carries the large-breakpoint column count
- `data-mobile-columns` carries the small-breakpoint column count
- `data-divider-color` carries the hairline weight
- `data-flush` mirrors `flush`, and is what withholds the `frame` grid's own outer rules
- `data-layout` mirrors `layout`; `data-count` carries how many cells render (comments and empty text are not cells)
- The root is a CSS grid of equal tracks; it sets `--card-grid-columns-base`, `--card-grid-columns-sm` and `--card-grid-columns-lg` to the track count each breakpoint resolves to (`balanced`: the narrowest count for the fewest rows; `fixed`: `mobileColumns`, two, `columns`); every direct child is set to `height: auto` and stretches to its row; the counts come from the render, so the server HTML and the hydrated page lay out the same
- `data-fill-trailing` marks a `balanced` `divider` grid: its `::after` fills the trailing empty tracks of a short last row with `--bg-canvas` (spanning `--card-grid-rest`, hidden when the row is full), so the rule colour the gaps show through never shows as a block
- `data-filled` mirrors whether any cell renders, so an empty `frame` grid draws no stray rules
- `data-variant` and `data-padded` on each cell's content box mirror its fill and its padding
- Empty: a `frame` grid with no cells renders no rules, so an unfilled grid leaves no empty frame on the page

## Accessibility

- Visible focus: not applicable — the grid and its cells are containers; controls composed into a cell keep their own `focus-visible:ring-2 focus-visible:ring-(--ring-color)` ring.
- Keyboard map: none — the grid is not focusable; `Tab` order follows the cells in DOM order, which is reading order at every breakpoint.
- ARIA: the root adds no role, so a list composed into it keeps its own semantics and the cells reach the accessibility tree unchanged; a cell adds no role either, so headings composed inside it keep the document outline the page intends.
- Contrast ≥4.5:1 (text) / ≥3:1 (large + icons) — the rules are non-informational decoration; a `frame` cell provides `var(--bg-surface)` or `var(--bg-canvas)`, and a `variant="none"` cell hands both the fill and its contrast to the composed content.
- `motion-reduce:transition-none motion-reduce:transform-none` — not applicable, the component is static.
- Touch target ≥40×40 px — not applicable, no interactive control of its own.

## Examples

### Default

```vue
<script setup>
  import CardGridRoot from '@aziontech/webkit/card-grid-root'
</script>

<template>
  <CardGridRoot :columns="3">
    <div class="bg-(--bg-canvas) p-(--spacing-xl) text-body-md text-(--text-muted)">One</div>
    <div class="bg-(--bg-canvas) p-(--spacing-xl) text-body-md text-(--text-muted)">Two</div>
    <div class="bg-(--bg-canvas) p-(--spacing-xl) text-body-md text-(--text-muted)">Three</div>
  </CardGridRoot>
</template>
```

2 more example(s) in [components/card-grid.md](components/card-grid.md).

<!-- components/carousel.md -->

# Carousel

> A horizontally scrollable track of cards with snap points and a pair of step controls. It is built on native scrolling and CSS scroll snap rather than a carousel runtime, so it is draggable, swipeable and keyboard-scrollable by default, and it never moves on its own. The unit is Carousel, then a `Carousel.Item` card, then its content — usually a `quote` or a `feature-item`. Two layouts, set on the root with `variant`: `strip` (the default) sets the cards edge to edge with no gutter — each draws its own hairline and the next one overlaps it by a pixel, so the row reads as one ruled strip; `cards` separates them with a gutter and rounds each one, so the row reads as a set of standalone cards. Each card takes its width from its own `size`, never from a class. The track fades at whichever end still has cards past it. An optional `header` slot shares the row the step controls sit in: the band's title goes at the start and the controls move to the end, so a titled carousel needs no hand-built row around it.

- Import: `import CarouselRoot from '@aziontech/webkit/carousel-root'`
- Compound import (dot notation, not tree-shakeable): `import Carousel from '@aziontech/webkit/carousel'`
- Category: marketing · Surface: site · Structure: composition

## When to use

- Use Carousel when the reader steps through cards themself.
- When a set of cards, quotes, logos or figures is longer than the row it sits in and the reader chooses how far to go.
- For a peek-and-swipe row on narrow screens where a grid would stack into a very long column.
- Whenever the content is browsable rather than essential — a reader must never have to scroll a carousel to reach something they need.

## Do

- Never put essential content in a carousel. A slide past the first is content a reader may never see.
- Size the cards with `size` on `Carousel.Item` (`small` `var(--container-2xs)`, `medium` `var(--container-sm)`, `large` `var(--container-md)`; each capped at 85% of the viewport on phones). Never put width classes on the item: they fight its own width and the `no-style-override` lint rejects them.
- Put content straight into `Carousel.Item`, not inside another framed box. The item is already the card (border, surface, padding), and a second frame inside it draws two rules.
- Pick the layout with `variant`: `strip` for a ruled row of quotes or figures that reads as one surface, `cards` for standalone cards with a gutter (use-case cards, articles). Never add margins to the items to fake a gutter.
- Put the band's title in the `header` slot when the controls belong on the title row; the controls then sit at the row's end. For a whole titled band of overline/title/text cards use the `card-carousel-section` section instead of composing one.
- Keep a partial slide visible at the trailing edge on narrow screens; a peek is what tells a reader the track scrolls at all.
- The track is dragged with the pointer as well as scrolled. A mouse gets a grab cursor and drags the row directly; touch and pen keep native scrolling and its momentum, which is already better than anything a handler would impose.
- Always name the carousel with `ariaLabel`. "Customer stories" tells a screen-reader user what they are about to scroll through; an unnamed scroll region does not.
- The track never advances on its own, and it must not be made to. Auto-advancing content needs a pause control under WCAG 2.2.2 and steals reading time from everyone.

## Don’t

- For content the reader must see → lay it out as a grid; anything past the first slide can be missed.
- For a vertical list → use `item` with `ItemGroup`.
- For a framed grid of unequal cells → use `bento-grid`.
- For a set of figures that fits the row → use `stats` or `logo-wall`, which wrap instead of scrolling.

## Related

- `bento-grid` — the framed grid, when everything should be visible at once.
- `logo-wall` — a wrapping grid of marks; reach for the carousel only when the wall is too long.
- `feature-item` — the usual slide content.
- `scroll-area` — the console's styled scroll container; this one owns snap points and step controls.

## Props

| Prop | Type | Default | Required | Description |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `ariaLabel` | `string` | `''` | false | Accessible name for the scrollable track, announced before its contents. |
| `variant` | `CarouselVariant` | `'strip'` | false | Layout of the row: `strip` sets the cards edge to edge as one ruled strip, `cards` separates them with a gutter and rounds each card. |

## Slots

| Slot | Scope | Notes |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `default` | — | The slides, composed as `Carousel.Item` elements in reading order. |
| `controls` | — | The step controls, composed as `Carousel.Previous` and `Carousel.Next`; rendered above the track, at the row's end when a header is set. |
| `header` | — | Content at the start of the controls row, usually the band's title; the controls move to the row's end. |

## Sub-component imports

- `@aziontech/webkit/carousel-item`
- `@aziontech/webkit/carousel-previous`
- `@aziontech/webkit/carousel-next`

## Sub-components

- `carousel-item/carousel-item.vue` — one card; a bordered, padded surface that is a snap point in the track, does not shrink and stretches its content to the row's tallest card.
- `carousel-previous/carousel-previous.vue` — context-aware control that steps the track back; disables itself at the start.
- `carousel-next/carousel-next.vue` — context-aware control that steps the track forward; disables itself at the end.

## States

- Visual states: `default`, plus `hover` / `focus-visible` / `disabled` on each step control
- `data-variant` (`strip` | `cards`) is on the root, the track and every item; under `cards` the track gaps its items by `var(--spacing-md)` and each item is rounded
- The row above the track renders only when `header` or `controls` is filled; with a header it spreads the header to the start and the controls to the end, without one the controls sit at the start
- `data-scrollable` is present on the root while the track overflows its row; a track that fits renders no controls to press
- `data-can-scroll-prev` / `data-can-scroll-next` are on the root and the track while the track has content past its start / end edge. The track fades only the edge that continues, over 12% of its own width, so the row at rest fades on the right alone
- The fade stands down while the track has keyboard focus, so the focus ring it would otherwise mask is fully visible
- The track carries `data-scrollable` too, which is what gives it the grab cursor; a track that fits shows the ordinary pointer, because there is nothing to drag
- `data-dragging` is on the track for the length of a mouse drag: the cursor closes, snapping and smooth scrolling stand down so the row tracks the pointer exactly, and text selection is suppressed
- A drag that travels more than a few pixels swallows the click it ends on, so releasing over a link inside a slide does not follow it
- Each control carries `disabled` at its end of the track, so a reader is never offered a step that does nothing
- The track is focusable and shows the focus ring, because a scrollable region must be reachable by keyboard

## Accessibility

- Visible focus: the track and both controls carry `focus-visible:ring-2 focus-visible:ring-(--ring-color) focus-visible:ring-offset-2 focus-visible:ring-offset-(--bg-canvas)`.
- Keyboard map: `Tab` reaches the controls and the track itself; with the track focused the arrow keys scroll it natively, and `Enter` / `Space` on a control steps one slide. No custom key handler replaces native scrolling. Pointer dragging is an addition for mice, never the only way to move the track — the keyboard, the wheel, touch and the step controls all still reach every slide.
- ARIA: the root is a `<section>` with `aria-roledescription="carousel"` named by `ariaLabel`; the track is a `<ul>` of `<li>` slides carrying `tabindex="0"` so the scrollable region is keyboard-reachable (WCAG 2.1.1); each control is a real `<button>` with its own `ariaLabel` and the native `disabled` state at its end of the track.
- Contrast ≥4.5:1 (text) / ≥3:1 (large + icons): the control glyph is `var(--text-muted)` on `var(--bg-surface)` and darkens to `var(--text-default)` on hover; slide content carries its own colours.
- `motion-reduce:scroll-auto` on the track and `motion-reduce:transition-none` on the controls. The track never auto-advances, so WCAG 2.2.2 (pause, stop, hide) does not apply by construction.
- Touch target ≥40×40 px — each step control is a 40×40 button.

## Examples

### Default

```vue
<script setup>
  import Carousel from '@aziontech/webkit/carousel'
  import Quote from '@aziontech/webkit/quote'
</script>

<template>
  <Carousel aria-label="Customer testimonials">
    <template #controls>
      <Carousel.Previous />
      <Carousel.Next />
    </template>
    <Carousel.Item size="large">
      <Quote
        text="We moved checkout to the edge in a single sprint. Pages now answer in under 50 ms in every region, and nobody on the team manages a server."
        name="Jane Doe"
        job-title="VP of Engineering, Acme Inc."
      />
    </Carousel.Item>
    <Carousel.Item size="large">
      <Quote
        text="Launch week triples our traffic. The platform absorbs it without a capacity conversation, and we ship changes to production in minutes."
        name="John Smith"
```

<!-- components/diagram.md -->

# Diagram

> A node-and-connector diagram for marketing pages: the request path of an architecture, a routing policy, a product's place in the platform. The consumer names one of the official diagrams with `name` — the closed library of 28 drawings in webkit's assets, one starter architecture per Azion product plus the page diagrams the product pages tab through — or passes a one-off `spec`: the nodes, how they are arranged, and the edges between them. The component lays the nodes out with real CSS (rows, branches, grids and framed groups), then measures the laid-out nodes and draws every connector, port and edge label on top. Nothing is drawn on a fixed canvas: the layout reflows below its container breakpoint into a stacked column — centred on one axis, or an indented tree where the row branches — and the connectors are re-measured, so a diagram stays legible from a phone to a wide band.

- Import: `import Diagram from '@aziontech/webkit/diagram'`
- Category: marketing · Surface: site · Structure: monolithic

## When to use

- Use Diagram for one of the official marketing architecture drawings.
- Showing how a request travels through Azion — client, application, the product the page is about, and its outcomes.
- Showing a routing or failover policy as nodes and labelled edges, with the path a request takes highlighted.
- Filling the media cell of a marketing band (`media-tabs`, `media-split`) with an architecture instead of a picture.

## Do

- Keep a diagram to about four columns in the wide layout so it fits a band's media cell; split a larger story into several diagrams.
- Light exactly one node with `lit` — the product the diagram is about — and mark the request path with `active` on its nodes and edges.
- Use `gap: 'wide'` on a row whose edges carry labels, so the chips have room between the nodes.
- Name an official diagram instead of rebuilding its `spec`, so the same architecture is the same drawing on every page. Reach for `spec` only for a diagram the library does not have.
- Every named diagram carries its own one-sentence description. Give a `spec` diagram an `ariaLabel` (in the prop or in the spec) that states what it shows; the drawing itself is hidden from assistive technology.
- Do: for a vertical flow in a docs column (a command → the CLI → the API → the resources), use one `row`. In the column it stacks centred, every connector runs through the horizontal centre of both nodes and an edge label sits centred on its line.
- Don't: nudge nodes or labels with wrappers, margins or spacer nodes to centre a flow; the layout does it.
- Reach for `card` for platform pieces, `item` for models and providers, `pill` for a client, `tile` for an icon-only grid cell, and `app` for the consumer's own application.

## Don’t

- For decorative product art → use `illustration`.
- For a world map of points of presence → use `network-map`.
- For a sequence of steps the reader follows in order → use `stepper` or an ordered list.
- For live data or anything the reader operates — a diagram is a figure, not a control.

## Related

- `illustration` — the drawn scene library; a diagram is generated from data, an illustration is authored art. Both pick an official drawing by `name`.
- `media-tabs` — hosts one diagram per tab in its media column; key each `Diagram` by its tab so it re-measures.
- `registration-frame` — the registration frame a diagram's groups and tiles are drawn in.
- `tag` — the status chip a card or item node carries.

## Props

| Prop | Type | Default | Required | Description |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `name` | `'ai-gateway' \| 'ai-gateway-latency-routing' \| 'ai-gateway-model-routing' \| 'ai-gateway-provider-failover' \| 'ai-gateway-response-cache' \| 'ai-inference' \| 'application-accelerator' \| 'applications' \| 'bot-manager' \| 'cache' \| 'data-stream' \| 'ddos-protection' \| 'edge-dns' \| 'firewall' \| 'functions' \| 'image-processor' \| 'kv-store' \| 'load-balancer' \| 'network-shield' \| 'object-storage' \| 'real-time-events' \| 'real-time-metrics' \| 'sql-database' \| 'web-application-firewall' \| 'workloads' \| 'workloads-gradual-rollout' \| 'workloads-isolate-per-request' \| 'workloads-security'` | `undefined` | false | Name of an official diagram in the asset library; ignored when spec is set. |
| `spec` | `DiagramSpec` | `undefined` | false | The nodes, their layout and the edges between them, for a diagram the library does not have. |
| `ariaLabel` | `string` | `''` | false | Accessible description of the whole diagram; empty uses the description the spec carries. |

## Assets

- Diagrams: every valid name is in diagrams.md (MCP `list_assets({ kind: "diagrams" })`). An unknown name draws nothing.

## States

- **Layout.** The root is an inline-size container. At the `5xl` container width (1192 px) and up, `row` lays its items side by side and `branches` stacks its items beside the node before it; below that width every row stacks. A row with no `branches` in it stacks **centred** (`data-stack="centre"`): its nodes share one vertical axis. A row that holds `branches`, and any row nested inside a tree (a branch that is itself a row), stays an **indented tree** (`data-stack="tree"`, nodes at the start edge) and `branches` indents under the node it leaves, so the tree reads top to bottom. `grid` keeps its 2 or 3 columns at every width.
- **Connectors.** After layout, the component measures every node and group (each carries `data-diagram-node` with its key) and draws each edge whose two ends exist: a curve from the source's right edge into the target's left edge, centre to centre on the vertical axis, when the target sits to the right of the source; when the target sits below, a connector from the **bottom centre** of the source to the **top centre** of the target — a straight line when the two centres align, otherwise an orthogonal path that turns at the gap's midline with rounded corners — with its label chip centred on the line (at the midpoint of the gap). Inside an indented tree (both ends in a `tree` stack) the tree rail is kept instead: an elbow from the source's bottom edge, one inset in from its start, into the target's start edge when the target is indented to the right, and a straight drop along that inset otherwise. Each end gets a port; ports at the same point merge. Edges whose ends are missing are skipped. The drawing is re-measured on every resize of the canvas or of any node, when the web fonts settle, and when the edges change.
- **Named.** A `name` loads its spec on demand from the library; the root keeps its width and draws nothing until the spec arrives, and a `name` change clears the current drawing at once. A `name` outside the library (from untyped code), or a spec that fails to load, warns in development only and leaves the root empty; it never throws. With neither `name` nor `spec` the root is empty.
- **Label.** The root is announced as one image named by `ariaLabel`, or, when that is empty, by the description the spec carries; with neither it is a plain figure.
- **Ready.** The canvas sets `data-ready` once the first measure lands and fades in from transparent, so a diagram never shows unconnected nodes.
- **Active.** An `active` edge, its ports and its label chip are drawn in the accent colour and painted above the rest; an `active` node is part of the highlighted path.
- **Lit.** A `lit` card, item or pill gets a primary edge and a primary-and-accent glow; a `lit` tile gets a primary edge.
- **Groups.** A `group` draws its content inside a registration frame with a small label above it; `variant: 'dashed'` dashes its edge and `fill` tints it with the primary or secondary mask.
- **Node types.** `card` — header row (mark, logo or icon, eyebrow, status tag) over the title, clamped to two lines. `item` — a leading mark, logo or icon, the title with the eyebrow in a mono line under it, and an optional trailing status tag. `pill` — a rounded chip with an optional icon. `tile` — an icon-only cell inside a registration frame, its title shown in a tooltip. `app` — a quiet window placeholder (three dots, the title, placeholder bars) that fades out at the bottom.
- **Test ids.** Root fallback `diagram` (consumer override wins); `${testId}__canvas` on the measured canvas.

## Accessibility

- Visible focus: the diagram has no focusable parts.
- Keyboard map: none — the diagram is a figure and takes no input.
- ARIA: the root is a `figure`; with `ariaLabel` set it is `role="img"` named by it. The canvas is `aria-hidden="true"`, so the nodes, connectors and chips are never read one by one — the label carries the meaning.
- Contrast ≥4.5:1 (text) / ≥3:1 (large + icons): node and chip text use `var(--text-default)` / `var(--text-muted)` on `var(--bg-surface)`; connectors meet 3:1 as non-text graphics against `var(--bg-canvas)`.
- `motion-reduce:transition-none` on the canvas fade; nothing else moves.
- Touch target: not applicable — nothing in the diagram is interactive.

## Examples

### Default

```vue
<script setup>
  import Diagram from '@aziontech/webkit/diagram'
</script>

<template>
  <Diagram name="ai-gateway-model-routing" />
</template>
```

2 more example(s) in [components/diagram.md](components/diagram.md).

<!-- components/embed.md -->

# Embed

> A responsive `iframe` for third-party content — a demo, a player, a status page, a form — that keeps its aspect ratio at every width and always carries the accessible name an `iframe` needs. It is the one place external content enters a page, so the attributes that are easy to forget are not optional.

- Import: `import Embed from '@aziontech/webkit/embed'`
- Category: marketing · Surface: site · Structure: monolithic

## When to use

- Use Embed for third-party iframe content; for a screenshot in documentation use DocFrame, to run live components use BrowserFrame.
- To embed a video player, live demo, status page or third-party form in a marketing page.
- Whenever content comes from another origin and must sit inside the page's own frame.
- When the embedded thing has a natural aspect ratio the page should preserve while it scales.

## Do

- The band draws no frame of its own: the rule and the corner marks belong to the `section-module` or `registration-frame` that the page assembles it into, so a band placed in a framed column never lands a second hairline on the column's own.
- Always write a `title` that says what the embedded thing is. It is the frame's accessible name, and a screen-reader user meets it before the content.
- Pick the `ratio` that matches the source. A `video` ratio around a square form leaves bands of empty frame.
- Prefer `loading="lazy"` behaviour for anything below the fold — it is the default here, and an embed is usually the heaviest thing on a marketing page.
- Do not embed something essential. A blocked or failed third-party frame leaves a hole the page cannot fill.

## Don’t

- For a screenshot or a clip hosted by us inside documentation → use `doc-frame`, which frames stills and clips and opens them full screen.
- For an image → use a plain `img` inside `registration-frame`; an `iframe` for a picture costs a document load.
- For content we own and could render directly — an embed is a last resort, not a layout tool.

## Related

- `doc-frame` — the documentation frame for stills and clips, not third-party documents.
- `registration-frame` — the frame drawn around the embedded document by whatever assembles the page; the embed draws none of its own.
- `media-split` — when the embed is one half of a copy-and-media band.

## Props

| Prop | Type | Default | Required | Description |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `src` | `string` | `—` | true | URL of the document to embed. |
| `title` | `string` | `—` | true | Accessible name for the embedded document, announced before its content. |
| `ratio` | `'video' \| 'square' \| 'wide'` | `'video'` | false | Aspect ratio the frame holds while it scales. |

## States

- Visual states: `default`
- `data-ratio` mirrors the `ratio` prop and drives the frame's aspect ratio
- The frame reserves its aspect ratio before the document loads, so the page does not shift when it arrives

## Accessibility

- Visible focus: the embedded document manages focus inside itself; the frame adds no focusable element of its own.
- Keyboard map: `Tab` moves into the embedded document, which owns its own keyboard model. The frame intercepts nothing.
- ARIA: `title` is required and renders as the `iframe`'s `title` attribute — an `iframe` without one is announced as an unnamed frame, which is why the prop is not optional here.
- Contrast ≥4.5:1 (text) / ≥3:1 (large + icons): the embedded document carries its own contrast; the frame contributes only its border.
- `motion-reduce:transition-none motion-reduce:transform-none` — not applicable, the frame is static.
- Touch target ≥40×40 px — not applicable; any control belongs to the embedded document.

## Examples

### Default

```vue
<script setup>
  import Embed from '@aziontech/webkit/embed'
</script>

<template>
  <Embed
    src="data:text/html,<body style='margin:0;height:100vh;display:grid;place-items:center;font:1rem system-ui;color:gray'>Placeholder embed</body>"
    title="A placeholder document standing in for an embedded demo"
    ratio="video"
  />
</template>
```

1 more example(s) in [components/embed.md](components/embed.md).

<!-- components/faq.md -->

# Faq

> The frequently-asked-questions band of a marketing page: a two-column panel with the section's heading on the left and the answers as a disclosure list on the right. It is the page-level band, not the disclosure primitive — it composes `accordion`, which remains the right choice anywhere a bare expandable list is wanted. The band is drawn in the hairline register: the two columns and every row are separated by a one-pixel gap over a rule fill, so the seams are the grid's own gaps rather than borders on the cells. The rule under a row is held at the item's edge and drawn only while that item is open, which keeps exactly one rule per row in either state; the last row draws none, because it sits on the band's own floor. A single `--accordion-inset` hook, re-declared per breakpoint, puts the heading, every question and every answer on one content column.

- Import: `import Faq from '@aziontech/webkit/faq'`
- Category: marketing · Surface: site · Structure: monolithic

## When to use

- Use Faq in a hand-built SFC that needs rich answers in the `#answer` slot.
- Rendering page JSON, or you need a whole band with no custom content → the section (`faq-section`). Building an SFC by hand, needing slots, or nesting inside another band → the primitive (`faq`).
- As the FAQ band near the end of a marketing or pricing page.
- Whenever a page must answer a known set of objections without sending the reader to docs.
- When the answers are short enough to read in place rather than link out to.

## Do

- `framed` decides who draws the rule. On, the band draws its own registration frame. Off, the rule and the corner marks belong to the `section-module` or `registration-frame` the page assembles it into, so a band placed in an already-framed column never lands a second hairline on the column's own.
- Keep the band to the questions a reader actually asks before buying; a FAQ is objection handling, not a manual.
- Write each `question` as the reader would ask it, in their words rather than the product's.
- Keep answers to a couple of sentences and link out for the rest — use the `answer` slot when an answer needs a link or richer content.
- Give every item a stable `value`. It is the accordion's open-state key, so a reordered or re-keyed list loses whatever the reader had open.
- Leave the first item closed. An open answer on load pushes the rest of the list down and hides the range of questions.

## Don’t

- For a bare expandable list anywhere else → use `accordion` directly.
- For long-form reference content that belongs in documentation → use `doc-prose` and link to it.
- For a single expandable aside inside a section → use `accordion` with one item.

## Related

- `accordion` — the disclosure primitive this band renders its answers through.
- `section-title` — the header for a section whose body is not a question list.
- `registration-frame` — the frame the band is drawn with, applied by whatever assembles the page; the band draws none of its own.

## Props

| Prop | Type | Default | Required | Description |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `title` | `string` | `''` | false | Headline of the band, rendered as its `h2` in the left column. |
| `items` | `FaqItem[]` | `[]` | false | The questions and their answers, in order; each item is `{ value, question, answer }` where `value` is the item's stable key. |
| `framed` | `boolean` | `false` | false | Draw the band's own registration frame. Turn it off when the page already wraps the band in a frame. |

## Slots

| Slot | Scope | Notes |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `answer` | { item: FaqItem } | Replaces the rendered answer for every item, so an answer can carry a link or richer content. |

## States

- Visual states: `default`
- Each answer is closed on load; open state is owned by the composed `accordion`
- Empty: when `items` is empty the band renders no answer list and no frame

## Accessibility

- Visible focus: owned by the composed `accordion`, whose triggers carry the `focus-visible:ring-2 focus-visible:ring-(--ring-color) focus-visible:ring-offset-2 focus-visible:ring-offset-(--bg-canvas)` ring.
- Keyboard map: delegated to `accordion` — `Tab` moves between questions, `Enter` / `Space` opens and closes the focused one.
- ARIA: the headline is a real `h2` and the band is a `<section>` labelled by it; the disclosure semantics (`aria-expanded`, the trigger/panel association) belong to `accordion` and are not re-declared here.
- Contrast ≥4.5:1 (text) / ≥3:1 (large + icons): the headline is `var(--text-default)`; the question and answer colours are the composed `accordion`'s.
- `motion-reduce:transition-none motion-reduce:transform-none` — the open/close transition belongs to `accordion`, which carries its own reduced-motion fallback; this band adds no motion.
- Touch target ≥40×40 px — the accordion trigger spans the column width and keeps its own target height.

## Examples

### Default

```vue
<script setup>
  import Faq from '@aziontech/webkit/faq'
</script>

<template>
  <Faq
    title="Frequently asked questions"
    :items="[
      {
        value: 'pricing',
        question: 'How does pricing work?',
        answer:
          'You pay for what you use — requests, data transfer and compute time — with no seats and no upfront commitment. Usage is metered and billed monthly.'
      },
      {
        value: 'free-tier',
        question: 'Is there a free tier?',
        answer:
          'Yes. Every core product has a monthly free allowance, and billing starts only once you pass it.'
      },
      {
        value: 'migration',
        question: 'How much work is it to migrate?',
        answer:
          'Most teams move a first workload in a day. Point the domain at us, keep the origin where it is, and cut traffic over once the numbers look right.'
      },
      {
        value: 'compliance',
        question: 'Where does my data run, and are you compliant?',
        answer:
          'You choose the regions a workload and its data are allowed in. Current audit reports and certifications are available on request.'
      },
      {
        value: 'support',
        question: 'What support do I get?',
        answer:
          'Every account has documentation, community and email support. Paid plans add a 24/7 incident channel and a named engineer.'
      }
    ]"
  />
</template>
```

<!-- components/feature-item.md -->

# Feature Item

> One claim stated in three parts: a glyph, a short headline it shares a line with, and a sentence under them that explains it. It is the repeated unit of a marketing claim grid — the content of a cell, not the cell itself, so it carries no surface, no padding and no rules of its own and reads the same dropped into a `card-grid` cell, a `bento-grid` cell, or a column the page lays out by hand. Given an `href` the whole claim becomes the link, and its headline closes on a trailing arrow, so a band of claims needs no separate row of "learn more" controls.

- Import: `import FeatureItem from '@aziontech/webkit/feature-item'`
- Category: marketing · Surface: site · Structure: monolithic

## When to use

- Use FeatureItem for one icon + headline + sentence claim inside a grid cell.
- For each cell of a "what you get" band: a row of equal claims under a section's headline.
- Inside `CardGrid.Cell` or `BentoGrid.Cell`, where the grid owns the frame and the fill and the topic owns only the copy.
- Whenever a claim is glyph-led and one sentence long, repeated three to six times across a band.

## Do

- Let the cell own the surface. A topic paints no background and adds no padding, so put it inside `CardGrid.Cell` / `BentoGrid.Cell` (or a padded cell the page owns) rather than giving the topic a fill.
- Keep `headingLevel` in step with the page outline: `2` when the band has no headline of its own and these are the first sub-headings under the `h1`, `3` when a `section-title` has already opened the section. The level is the outline; the size is fixed at `heading-xs`.
- Keep the description to one sentence. The glyph carries the recognition, the headline carries the claim, and a second sentence turns a scannable grid into a wall of prose.
- Drop `icon` for a band whose claims are not glyph-led rather than reaching for a decorative glyph that says nothing.
- Give the claim an `href` rather than composing a button under it. A grid of six claims that each close on their own "Learn more" reads as six calls to action instead of six claims, and the button is a second target for the destination the claim already points at.
- A linked claim stretches its link over the nearest positioned ancestor, so the whole cell is the hover and click target, not just the copy. `CardGrid.Cell` and `BentoGrid.Cell` are positioned; a cell the page builds by hand needs `relative`, or every claim in the grid covers the band.

## Don’t

- For a tile with its own border and surface → keep the topic and put it in a `CardGrid.Cell`, which draws the frame and the fill.
- For a claim that carries an eyebrow, a media region or a button → use `media-split`.
- For the headline that opens the whole section → use `section-title`.
- For a whole band of claims or links placed from page JSON → use `capability-grid-section`, which lays topics out for you.
- For a measured figure with a unit → use `stats`.
- For a console list row with media and actions → use `item`.

## Related

- `card-grid` — the equal-cell grid a topic is usually repeated in; its `frame` register gives each topic a real `registration-frame` cell.
- `bento-grid` — the unequal-cell mosaic, when one claim deserves more room than the rest.
- `section-title` — the headline that opens the band a row of topics sits in.

## Props

| Prop | Type | Default | Required | Description |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `title` | `string` | `—` | true | The claim, rendered as the topic's heading. |
| `description` | `string` | `''` | false | One sentence explaining the claim; overridden by the default slot. |
| `icon` | `string` | `''` | false | Icon class for the glyph that leads the headline. |
| `href` | `string` | `''` | false | When set, the whole claim renders as an anchor link to this URL and its headline closes on a trailing arrow. |
| `headingLevel` | `FeatureItemHeadingLevel` | `2` | false | Level of the heading element. Keep `2` when the band has no headline of its own; drop it to `3` when a `section-title` has already opened the section, so the document outline stays in order. |

## Slots

| Slot | Scope | Notes |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `default` | — | Description body; replaces the `description` prop when provided. |

## Assets

- Icons: every valid name is in icons.md (MCP `list_assets({ kind: "icons" })`). An unknown class renders nothing.

## States

- Visual states: `default`, and `hover` / `focus-visible` when `href` is set
- `data-icon` mirrors whether a glyph is rendered, so a band can style the glyph-less case
- `data-described` mirrors whether a description is rendered, from either the prop or the slot
- `data-linked` present when the claim carries an `href`
- The root element switches between `<a>` and `<div>` on the `href` data prop, never on an `as` string — so the whole claim is the target, not a control composed under it
- A linked root lays an `::after` over its positioned cell, so hovering anywhere in the cell hovers the claim
- The heading element follows `headingLevel`; its size does not change with it

## Accessibility

- Visible focus: the linked root draws an inset `focus-visible` ring in `var(--ring-color)` on its `::after`, so the ring outlines the whole cell it covers. Without an `href` the topic holds no interactive control of its own.
- Keyboard map: `Tab` reaches the linked claim and `Enter` follows it — the anchor's own behaviour, no handler of our own. Unlinked, the topic is static content and `Tab` passes through whatever the description slot contains.
- ARIA: both glyphs are decorative and carry `aria-hidden="true"` — the leading icon and the trailing arrow — so the link's accessible name is the headline and the sentence, the copy a reader sees. `headingLevel` is what keeps the document outline in order (axe `heading-order`); the heading stays a heading inside the anchor.
- Contrast ≥4.5:1 (text) / ≥3:1 (large + icons): `var(--text-default)` for the heading, `var(--text-muted)` for the description, `var(--primary)` for the glyph and `var(--text-primary)` for the hovered headline — all against the fill the surrounding cell provides.
- `motion-reduce:transition-none` on both transitions a linked claim carries.
- Touch target ≥40×40 px — the whole claim is the target, so it is the cell's own height; a topic is never smaller than its heading plus its sentence.

## Examples

### Default

```vue
<script setup>
  import FeatureItem from '@aziontech/webkit/feature-item'
</script>

<template>
  <div class="relative">
    <FeatureItem
      icon="az az-edge-nodes"
      title="Consistent global speed"
      description="Serve content and run web apps across hundreds of locations with median latency under 30 ms. No infra to manage."
    />
  </div>
</template>
```

2 more example(s) in [components/feature-item.md](components/feature-item.md).

<!-- components/hero.md -->

# Hero

> The opening band of a page: a full-bleed section spanning the whole viewport width whose copy is centered in a capped column, optionally filling one screen and optionally carrying a decorative backdrop behind it. It is the top layer of the page language and the owner of the page's first rule — the bottom hairline every column below it hangs from. `Hero.Title` is the copy block it centers.

- Import: `import HeroRoot from '@aziontech/webkit/hero-root'`
- Compound import (dot notation, not tree-shakeable): `import Hero from '@aziontech/webkit/hero'`
- Category: marketing · Surface: site · Structure: composition

## When to use

- Use the Hero primitive only in a hand-built SFC that needs slots HeroSection does not offer.
- Rendering page JSON, or you need a whole band with no custom content → the section (`hero-section`). Building an SFC by hand, needing slots, or nesting inside another band → the primitive (`hero`).
- As the opening band of a marketing, hub or documentation page.
- Whenever a band must span the window while its copy stays on the page's measure.
- For any band that carries a decorative backdrop behind its content.
- With `variant="screen"` when the opening should measure exactly one viewport.

## Do

- One `Hero` per page. It owns the `h1` through `Hero.Title`, and a second breaks the document outline.
- Pass `variant="screen"` for an opening band that should measure one screen, and declare the sticky bar's height as `--hero-banner-offset` so the band still measures exactly one screen beneath it.
- Put the backdrop in the `background` slot rather than on the band's own class: the slot renders beneath the content layer and is already marked decorative.
- Turn `padded` off when the slotted content owns its block rhythm; the inline inset is not optional, because it is the page boundary the bar above the band also reads.
- Set the band's air with `size`, never with a padded wrapper around the copy: `large` is the opening a page leads with, and the rhythm is the same above and below so the copy and the media sit on the band's vertical centre.
- Keep `titleHighlight` to the opening phrase of the headline — it is the same sentence, only painted differently.
- Put the calls to action in `Hero.Title`'s `actions` slot; it stacks them full-width on narrow screens, which is what makes a hero button a comfortable thumb target.
- Set `mediaAlign="end"` when the media should close on the same vertical line as the frames below the band; leave it `center` when the media is a self-contained object that reads best balanced in its column.
- Reach for `floorTexture` rather than wiring a texture into the `bottom` slot by hand: the window is a fixed cell, so the band already carries the ink and the horizontal falloff fitted to it, and a hand-fitted copy of that drifts.
- Leave the `media` slot out entirely rather than filling it with decoration — a band with one column and a strong statement beats one with a stock image.

## Don’t

- For a marketing page opening placed from page JSON → use `hero-section` (or `hero-form-section` for a lead form).
- For the framed column of bands below the opening → use `section-container`.
- For one band inside that column → use `section-module`.
- For a section header further down the page → use `section-title`, which renders an `h2`.
- For a mid-page announcement → use `call-to-action` with `variant="announcement"`; for the closing ask → use `call-to-action`.
- For a copy-and-media pairing that is not the page opening → use `media-split`.

## Related

- `section-container` — the framed column below it; this band's `border-b` is that column's top edge.
- `section-module` — the brick stacked inside that column.
- `texture` — the backdrop the `texture` and `floorTexture` props paint, at the pitch `textureSize` and `floorTextureSize` set; reach for the `background` slot only when the layer needs more than variant, size and fade.
- `media-split` — the same copy-beside-media shape at section scale, rendering an `h2`.
- `call-to-action` — the mid-page announcement band (`variant="announcement"`) and the closing ask.

## Props

| Prop | Type | Default | Required | Description |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `variant` | `HeroVariant` | `'band'` | false | Height of the band; `screen` fills one viewport minus `--hero-banner-offset`. |
| `maxWidth` | `HeroWidth` | `'7xl'` | false | Width the inner column is capped at; `site` is the marketing measure, `full` keeps only the inset. |
| `bordered` | `boolean` | `true` | false | Draw the band's bottom rule, which is the top edge of whatever follows it. |
| `padded` | `boolean` | `true` | false | Apply the band's own vertical rhythm. The inline inset is always applied. |
| `size` | `HeroSize` | `'medium'` | false | Vertical rhythm the band applies when `padded`, equal above and below; `large` is the opening a page leads with. |
| `texture` | `TextureVariant` | `'none'` | false | Paint this texture behind the band's content; `none` leaves the backdrop to the `background` slot. |
| `textureSize` | `TextureSize` | `'medium'` | false | Pitch of the tiling the `texture` prop paints — how far apart its cells sit. |
| `textureFade` | `TextureFade` | `'none'` | false | Fade applied to the layer the `texture` prop paints. |
| `floorTexture` | `TextureVariant` | `'none'` | false | Paint this texture standing on the band's floor, filling the `bottom` window under the brand strip. |
| `floorTextureSize` | `TextureSize` | `'medium'` | false | Pitch of the tiling the `floorTexture` prop paints — how far apart its cells sit. |
| `align` | `HeroAlign` | `'center'` | false | Where the content column sits vertically when the band fills a screen (`start` is the top, `end` the bottom). |
| `mediaAlign` | `HeroMediaAlign` | `'center'` | false | Where the `media` slot sits in its column: `center` balances it in the column, `end` sets its end edge on the container boundary, past the inline inset, from `md` up. |
| `offset` | `string` | `''` | false | Height of the fixed chrome above the band, subtracted from the viewport when `variant` is `screen`; any CSS length. |
| `bottomHeight` | `string` | `''` | false | Height of the window under the content, where the brand strip and the floor texture stand; any CSS length. |
| `marquee` | `boolean` | `false` | false | Stand the brand marquee on the band's floor, under the `bottom` window. |
| `marqueeMarks` | `string[]` | `[]` | false | Registry names of the marks the marquee shows, in order. |
| `marqueeLabel` | `string` | `''` | false | Eyebrow above the brand marquee, stating the claim the marks make. |

## Slots

| Slot | Scope | Notes |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `default` | — | The band's copy, centered in the capped column above the backdrop; usually one `Hero.Title`. |
| `media` | — | Screenshot, diagram or form set beside the copy from `md` up. When filled, the content column becomes two equal halves from `md` up and the copy keeps the leading one; the `Hero.Title` `maxWidth` caps the copy inside its half. |
| `background` | — | Decorative backdrop rendered full-bleed beneath the content; marked `aria-hidden`. |
| `top` | — | Decorative asset window overlaying the band's top edge; clips its child, which may be larger than the window and offset inside it. It claims no space, so content still centres on the band. |
| `bottom` | — | Asset window standing on the band's floor; clips its child, which may be larger than the window and offset inside it. It is real content, so it **reserves its height** and `align` centres the copy in what is left. It shares the floor with the `marquee` strip, which stands under it. `floorTexture` fills this same window when the asset is one of the system's textures. |

## Assets

- Brand marks: every valid name is in brand-marks.md (MCP `list_assets({ kind: "brandMarks" })`). An unknown name is not drawn as a mark: `Marquee` prints it as a typographic wordmark, the quote components show no mark.

## Sub-component imports

- `@aziontech/webkit/hero-title`

## Sub-components

The root `Hero` owns the band — the full-bleed section, the capped column, the bottom rule, the backdrop and asset windows, and the one-screen height. `Hero.Title` owns the copy block placed in its default slot. There is no shared state between them: the band is a layout shell and the copy block is self-contained, so nothing is provided or injected and the consumer wires nothing. Member names mirror this component's anatomy; the band has no `data-state="open|closed"`, so it has no `Trigger` or `Content`.

- `hero-title/hero-title.vue` — the page's leading statement: overline, `h1` with an optionally accented opening phrase, supporting paragraph and the actions row.
  - Props: `title` (`string`, required) — headline of the page, rendered as the page's `h1`; `titleHighlight` (`string`, `''`) — opening phrase of the headline, painted in the brand accent, reading as one sentence with `title`; `description` (`string`, `''`) — supporting sentence under the headline, overridden by the default slot, running to the block's own width and set one step further from the headline than the rest of the block; `eyebrow` (`string`, `''`) — short uppercase overline rendered above the headline, always closed by the blinking cursor, wrapping to the block's width instead of overflowing it; `eyebrowPrefix` (`HeroTitleEyebrowPrefix` = `'' | '//' | '<>' | '</>'`, `''`) — mark set before the eyebrow label, empty renders the label alone; `align` (`HeroTitleAlign` = `'start' | 'center'`, `'start'`) — where the whole block aligns, at every width. `mobileAlign` (`HeroTitleAlign`, `undefined`) — where the whole block aligns below `md` only, where a band with `media` stacks the copy above it; unset, it follows `align`. `size` (`HeroTitleSize` = `'medium' | 'large'`, `'medium'`) — headline scale, medium steps one rung below large. `maxWidth` (`HeroTitleWidth` = `'lg' | 'xl' | '2xl' | '3xl' | '4xl'`, `'4xl'`) — width the whole block (headline, paragraph and actions) is capped at; beside a `media` slot the block is capped inside its half of the band. `sticky` (`boolean`, `false`) — keep the block pinned beneath the fixed chrome while the column beside it scrolls, from the large breakpoint up.
  - Events: _none_.
  - Slots: `default` — description body, replacing the `description` prop when provided; `actions` — the page's leading calls to action, stacked full-width while the title's container is narrower than `@2xs` (300 px, so a 390 px phone keeps the row and a 320 px one stacks) and laid out in a row from it.

## States

- Visual states: `default`
- `data-variant` carries the band's height mode (`band` or `screen`)
- `data-width` carries the resolved width key
- `data-bordered` present when the bottom rule is drawn
- `data-padded` present when the band applies its own vertical rhythm
- `data-size` carries the rhythm key (`medium` or `large`); it takes effect only with `data-padded`, and the same step applies above and below the content, so the copy and the media sit on the band's vertical centre
- `data-media` present when the `media` slot is filled and the content column is split into two equal halves from `md` up, the copy leading and the media trailing; stacked below `md`
- `data-media-align` carries where the `media` slot sits in its column (`center` or `end`); it takes effect only with `data-media`. With `end`, from `md` up, the media cell extends through the end-side inline inset so its end edge lands on the container boundary — the same line the frames of the section below draw — and media narrower than the cell is set against that edge. Stacked below `md`, the media keeps the inset
- `data-align` carries where the content column sits vertically in a `screen` band. The column takes the band's leftover height, (`start`, `center` or `end`), so `center` is the centre of the space actually available — a `bottom` asset reserves its height and the copy centres above it, while a `top` asset overlays and claims none
- The band is an `isolate` stacking context, so its layer order never leaks into the page. Layer tokens set that order: `--hero-banner-z-background` (0), `--hero-banner-z-top` / `--hero-banner-z-bottom` (1), `--hero-banner-z-content` (10)
- Each asset window clips its slotted child, so an asset larger than the band shows only the part the window frames. Custom properties place it: `--hero-banner-top-height` / `--hero-banner-bottom-height` size the window, and `--hero-banner-top-x` / `-y`, `--hero-banner-bottom-x` / `-y`, `--hero-banner-background-x` / `-y` move the asset inside it
- The backdrop sets `--texture-fade-lead: transparent`, so a `top` / `bottom` fade on the `texture` layer — or on a `texture` in the `background` slot — ramps in from zero at its leading edge as well as fading out: the opening band sits under the site header, and the texture must clear it. Outside a hero the same fades stay single
- `data-floor` present when `floorTexture` paints the floor window. The window then takes a fluid default height (`clamp(9rem, 30dvh, 34rem)`) so a `screen` band still measures one screen on a laptop and caps at the design's field where there is room; with no floor texture it stays `auto` and is sized by its slotted child. `--hero-banner-bottom-height` overrides either, and `--hero-banner-floor-ink` (0.6) sets how strongly the texture reads
- The floor holds the `bottom` window and the `marquee` strip in one block, so a single ground covers both: `--hero-banner-floor-bg` (transparent by default) paints it, which is how a design plinths the floor a shade off the band's own canvas
- The `marquee` strip stands on `--bg-canvas` edge to edge, so a `texture` backdrop or `background` slot never shows through the marks; a `--hero-banner-floor-bg` plinth replaces that ground so the floor still reads as one block
- `marquee` loads the strip on demand, so a band without one pulls in neither the strip nor its mark registry
- `marqueeLabel` names what the strip is evidence of; left empty the marks stand alone, which is the right register when the band's copy has already said it

## Accessibility

- Visible focus: not applicable to the band — it is a container; controls composed into it keep their own `focus-visible:ring-2 focus-visible:ring-(--ring-color)` ring.
- Keyboard map: none — the band is not focusable; `Tab` order is decided entirely by the slotted content.
- ARIA: the root renders a `section` and adds no role; the backdrop and the top asset window are wrapped `aria-hidden="true"` so decoration never reaches the accessibility tree. `Hero.Title` renders a `header` holding the page's single `h1`.
- Contrast ≥4.5:1 (text) / ≥3:1 (large + icons) — the backdrop is decoration and the slotted copy owns its own contrast against the canvas.
- `motion-reduce:transition-none motion-reduce:transform-none` — not applicable, the component is static.
- Touch target ≥40×40 px — not applicable to the band; the actions row in `Hero.Title` stacks full-width below `@2xs` (300 px) so each control clears the target.

## Examples

### Default

```vue
<script setup>
  import Hero from '@aziontech/webkit/hero'
</script>

<template>
  <Hero max-width="7xl">
    <Hero.Title
      align="center"
      eyebrow="Infrastructure"
      eyebrow-prefix="//"
      title="A global network built for fast applications"
      description="Run closer to users with low-latency delivery and stable routing."
    />
  </Hero>
</template>
```

2 more example(s) in [components/hero.md](components/hero.md).

<!-- components/link-directory.md -->

# Link Directory

> A directory of destinations laid out in columns on one set of shared hairlines: each column carries an overline heading over a rule, then its rows — a glyph, the destination's name, and one line of what it is. It is the page-level navigation band a marketing page closes a section with, so a reader who got that far can leave for anything the platform holds without going back to the bar.

- Import: `import LinkDirectoryRoot from '@aziontech/webkit/link-directory-root'`
- Compound import (dot notation, not tree-shakeable): `import LinkDirectory from '@aziontech/webkit/link-directory'`
- Category: marketing · Surface: site · Structure: composition

## When to use

- Use LinkDirectory for a columned directory of destinations inside a marketing band.
- As the "everything on the platform" band of a product, solutions or pricing page, under a `section-title` and inside the page's frame.
- When a set of destinations is grouped and the group's name carries as much as the destinations under it.
- When each destination needs a glyph and one line of what it is, not just a label.
- When the same directory appears on several pages and has to read identically on every one.

## Do

- Name the block with `ariaLabel`. It is a `nav` landmark, and a page carrying more than one needs every one of them named.
- Let the grid draw the rules: a column paints the page canvas and the 1px gaps between columns are the hairlines, so a border added inside a column doubles them.
- Keep the column count and the number of groups in step — four groups in a three-column block wrap the fourth onto a row of its own, beside two tracks of bare canvas.
- Give every row in a column a `description`, or give none of them one; a single row without its line reads as a mistake rather than as brevity.
- Leave `href` empty for a destination that does not exist yet. The row still renders and still reads, and it does not answer the pointer or take focus.
- Inside a framed page column, set the surrounding `registration-frame` to `flush` so the column's rules stay one hairline each.

## Don’t

- For the header's mega-menu → use `navigation-menu`, which owns the trigger, the panel, and the open/closed state this block deliberately has none of.
- For a set of claims rather than destinations → use `card-grid` with `feature-item`, or `bento-grid` when the claims are unequal.
- For the site-wide link directory at the foot of the page → use `footer`, which already lays its columns out.
- For a trail back up the current hierarchy → use `breadcrumb`.
- For the headings of the page currently being read → use `doc-on-this-page`.

## Related

- `card-grid` — the equal-cell grid whose `divider` register draws its rules the same way; reach for it when the cells are cards rather than columns of links.
- `bento-grid` — unequal cells on the same shared rules.
- `navigation-menu` — the same anatomy inside an overlay, with a trigger and a panel.
- `footer` — the other place a directory of links is laid out in columns.
- `section-module` / `registration-frame` — the frame this block is normally dropped into.

## Props

| Prop | Type | Default | Required | Description |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `ariaLabel` | `string` | `''` | false | Accessible name for the navigation landmark; a page carrying more than one needs each of them named. |
| `columns` | `LinkDirectoryColumns` | `4` | false | How many columns the directory fans out to at the large breakpoint. |
| `mobileColumns` | `LinkDirectoryMobileColumns` | `1` | false | How many columns the directory holds below the small breakpoint. |

## Slots

| Slot | Scope | Notes |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `default` | — | The columns, composed as `LinkDirectory.Column` elements in reading order. |

## Sub-component imports

- `@aziontech/webkit/link-directory-column`
- `@aziontech/webkit/link-directory-item`

## Sub-components

- `link-directory-column/link-directory-column.vue` — one column of the directory: its overline heading over a rule, then the rows it holds, as a list that heading names.
- `link-directory-item/link-directory-item.vue` — one destination row: a glyph, the destination's name, and one line of what it is.

Column props: `title` (`string`, default `''`) — the column's overline heading, which also names its list for a screen reader. Column slot: `default` — the column's rows, composed as `LinkDirectory.Item` elements.

Item props: `icon` (`string`, default `''`) — icon-font class for the glyph, e.g. `az az-workloads`; `title` (`string`, default `''`) — the destination's name, the row's first line; `description` (`string`, default `''`) — one line of what the destination is, omitted when empty; `href` (`string`, default `''`) — the destination, where empty renders a row that is not a link and not focusable. Item event: `click` — `(event: MouseEvent, item: LinkDirectoryItemTarget)`, fired only by a row that has an `href`, with `item` carrying `{ title, href }` so a consuming app can `preventDefault()` and route the destination itself instead of reloading.

The compound `index.ts` annotates the `Object.assign` result with an explicit `typeof LinkDirectory & { Column: typeof LinkDirectoryColumn; Item: typeof LinkDirectoryItem }` type. Without it, declaration emit expands the root's private `Props` interface and `vue-tsc` fails with TS4082 ("default export of the module has or is using private name"). Do not simplify it back to a bare `Object.assign`.

## States

- Visual states: `default`, `hover`, `focus-visible`
- `data-columns` and `data-mobile-columns` on the block mirror the track counts it lays out at each breakpoint
- `data-linked` on a row mirrors a non-empty `href`; it is what gates the row's hover plate and its brand glyph, so a row with nowhere to go never answers the pointer
- Below the small breakpoint the block holds `mobileColumns` tracks and every column claims one of them
- Empty: a block with no columns draws no rules, so an unfilled block leaves no hairline on the page

## Accessibility

- Visible focus: a row with an `href` carries `focus-visible:ring-2 focus-visible:ring-(--ring-color) focus-visible:ring-offset-2 focus-visible:ring-offset-(--bg-canvas)`; a row with nowhere to go is not a link and is not focusable.
- Keyboard map: `Tab` moves through the rows in DOM order and `Enter` follows one. The block is a set of links, not a composite widget, so it declares no arrow-key model and traps nothing.
- ARIA: the block is a `nav` landmark named by `ariaLabel`; each column's rows are a list named by that column's heading through `aria-labelledby` (id from `useId`), so the group and its length are announced before the rows; the glyph is `aria-hidden`.
- Contrast ≥4.5:1 (text) / ≥3:1 (large + icons): the row's title is `var(--text-default)` and its description `var(--text-muted)` on `var(--bg-canvas)`, and the hover plate keeps both on `var(--bg-hover)`.
- `motion-reduce:transition-none` on the row's hover transition, on the same class string.
- Touch target ≥40×40 px: a row is at least 44 px tall and spans the column's full width, and its hover plate is pulled past the text on both sides so the target is the row, not the words.

## Examples

### Default

```vue

  import LinkDirectoryItem from '@aziontech/webkit/link-directory-item'
  import LinkDirectoryRoot from '@aziontech/webkit/link-directory-root'
</script>

<template>
  <LinkDirectoryRoot
    aria-label="Platform"
    :columns="4"
  >
    <LinkDirectoryColumn title="Compute">
```
