<!-- components/section-container.md -->

# Section Container

> The framed content column: a centered, width-capped block carrying only its two vertical rules, which every section below a hero stacks inside. It is the middle layer of the page language — the hero above it owns the page's top rule, the footer below it owns the bottom, and this column owns the sides — so a page's frame is drawn once, by three components, instead of by every band that happens to need an edge.

- Import: `import SectionContainer from '@aziontech/webkit/section-container'`
- Category: marketing · Surface: site · Structure: monolithic

## When to use

- Use SectionContainer once per marketing page as the framed column below the hero.
- As the container for every band below a `hero`, on a marketing, hub or documentation page.
- Whenever a stack of `section-module` bricks needs one continuous frame around it.
- For a plain prose column that should sit on the page's own measure, with `padded`.

## Do

- Leave `padded` off for a stack of modules: each brick owns its own padding, and column padding would double it and pull the bricks off the frame.
- Match `maxWidth` to the `hero` above it so the band's content and the column's content open on one vertical.
- Turn `bordered` off only for a column nested inside another frame, where the outer frame already draws the sides.

## Don’t

- For the full-bleed band at the top of a page → use `hero`, which is the hero rule.
- For one band inside the column → use `section-module`, the brick this container stacks.
- For a hairline grid of cells → use `card-grid`, which draws its rules as gaps.
- For a bordered box around arbitrary content → use `registration-frame`.
- Built to be personalized (style seam): any class is accepted on the tag.

## Related

- `hero` — the full-bleed opening band above this column; its `border-b` is this column's top edge.
- `section-module` — the brick stacked inside this column.
- `card-grid` — the hairline cell grid a module's body holds.
- `registration-frame` — the registration frame a module wraps its body in.

## Props

| Prop | Type | Default | Required | Description |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `maxWidth` | `SectionContainerWidth` | `'7xl'` | false | Width the column is capped at; `site` is the marketing measure every band of that page frame shares. |
| `bordered` | `boolean` | `true` | false | Draw the column's two vertical rules. |
| `padded` | `boolean` | `false` | false | Pad the column itself. Leave off for a stack of modules that own their padding. |

## Slots

| Slot | Scope | Notes |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `default` | — | The bands stacked inside the column, in reading order. |

## States

- Visual states: `default`
- `data-width` carries the resolved width key
- `data-bordered` present when the vertical rules are drawn
- `data-padded` present when the column pads its own content

## Accessibility

- Visible focus: not applicable — the column is a container and holds no interactive surface of its own.
- Keyboard map: none — the column is not focusable; `Tab` order is decided entirely by the slotted content.
- ARIA: the root adds no role, so the slotted content's semantics reach the accessibility tree unchanged.
- Contrast ≥4.5:1 (text) / ≥3:1 (large + icons) — the rules are non-informational decoration and the slotted content owns its own contrast.
- `motion-reduce:transition-none motion-reduce:transform-none` — not applicable, the component is static.
- Touch target ≥40×40 px — not applicable, no interactive control.

## Examples

### Default

```vue
<script setup>
  import RegistrationFrame from '@aziontech/webkit/registration-frame'
  import SectionContainer from '@aziontech/webkit/section-container'
</script>

<template>
  <SectionContainer max-width="7xl">
    <RegistrationFrame borders="y">
      <div class="p-(--spacing-xl) text-center text-body-md text-(--text-muted)">First band</div>
    </RegistrationFrame>
    <RegistrationFrame
      flush
      borders="y"
      marks="bottom"
    >
      <div class="p-(--spacing-xl) text-center text-body-md text-(--text-muted)">Second band</div>
    </RegistrationFrame>
  </SectionContainer>
</template>
```

<!-- components/section-gap.md -->

# Section Gap

> The empty registration frame that divides two page sections. On a page with no margins between sections, this frame _is_ the gap: it holds the vertical air, and its own two rules are what separate the sections it sits between — which is why it is a frame and not a spacer `div`. `size` picks how much air, on a three-step scale built from the theme's own spacing tokens.

- Import: `import SectionGap from '@aziontech/webkit/section-gap'`
- Category: marketing · Surface: site · Structure: monolithic

## When to use

- Use SectionGap only between marketing page sections inside SectionContainer; in product UI use Separator.
- Between two stacked page sections on a full-bleed, framed layout.
- Wherever a page needs breathing room that still reads as part of the frame instead of as empty margin.
- To change the weight of one break without touching its neighbours — a `large` gap before a new part of the page, `small` between two bands that belong together.

## Do

- Pick the step by what the break means, not by how the page happens to look: `medium` is the ordinary break between sections, `large` opens a new part of the page, `small` keeps two related bands close.
- On the Blog, the gap right after the `title-band` hero (`size="medium"`) is `small`, so the article highlight reads as part of the opening.
- Do not override the height from the call site. The steps are multiples of `--spacing-2xl`, so they already scale with the viewport; a hand-set height freezes one break out of the page rhythm.
- Keep one step per break — stacking two gaps to reach a bigger one draws an extra pair of rules.
- The gap draws its own bottom rule, so the `section-module` that follows it passes `divided` as `false`: two rules never share one seam.
- Let it carry the shared rule: it renders `flush`, so its top rule is subtracted and the section above supplies it instead of doubling it. It still ticks every corner (`marks="all"`): the shared rule is given away, the registration mark is not.
- Reach for `hatch` here rather than on a section header: the gap is the one band with no copy of its own, so the texture gives the break identity without sitting behind a headline.
- Leave the ruling unmasked. Each line is one solid ink edge to edge and its transparency is the ink's own, so the field reads as ruled page material rather than as a fill that fades out; a vignette on a band this short reads as a smear.
- Do not wrap it in a padded container; the gap is the padding.

## Don’t

- To space two elements inside a section → use the layout's own `gap-(--spacing-*)` utility.
- To draw a single separating line with no air → use `separator` instead.
- When the gap must hold content (a headline, a CTA) → use `section-title`, or a `registration-frame` you fill yourself.

## Related

- `registration-frame` — the frame this component configures; reach for it directly when the gap needs content or different rules.
- `texture` — the layer that paints the ruling, composed here at its `lines` kind.
- `section-title` — the framed header that usually follows a gap; it owns no vertical air of its own beyond its padding, so the gaps around it are what set the page's rhythm.
- `separator` — a hairline with no vertical air.

## Props

| Prop | Type | Default | Required | Description |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `size` | `'small' \| 'medium' \| 'large'` | `'medium'` | false | How much vertical air the gap holds, as a multiple of `--spacing-2xl`: `small` is 1× (keeps two related bands close), `medium` 2× (the ordinary break), `large` 3× (opens a new part of the page). The token is responsive, so every step scales with the viewport. |
| `hatch` | `boolean` | `false` | false | Draw the gap's ruled line texture — fine vertical rules at a fixed pitch. |

## States

- Visual states: `default`
- `data-size` mirrors the `size` prop: `small` | `medium` | `large`
- `data-hatch` present when the hatch texture is drawn; the texture layer itself is addressable at `section-gap__hatch`

## Accessibility

- Visible focus: not applicable — the gap holds no content and no interactive surface.
- Keyboard map: none — the gap is not focusable and is skipped by `Tab`.
- ARIA: no role and no accessible name; the frame's rules and corner marks are decoration inherited from `registration-frame`, which marks them `aria-hidden="true"`.
- Contrast ≥4.5:1 (text) / ≥3:1 (large + icons) — no text; the rules are non-informational decoration.
- `motion-reduce:transition-none motion-reduce:transform-none` — not applicable, the component is static.
- Touch target ≥40×40 px — not applicable, no interactive control.

## Examples

### Default

```vue
<script setup>
  import RegistrationFrame from '@aziontech/webkit/registration-frame'
  import SectionGap from '@aziontech/webkit/section-gap'
</script>

<template>
  <div>
    <RegistrationFrame borders="y">
      <div class="p-(--spacing-xl) text-center text-body-md text-(--text-muted)">Section above</div>
    </RegistrationFrame>
    <SectionGap size="medium" />
    <RegistrationFrame
      flush
      marks="bottom"
      borders="y"
    >
      <div class="p-(--spacing-xl) text-center text-body-md text-(--text-muted)">Section below</div>
    </RegistrationFrame>
  </div>
</template>
```

2 more example(s) in [components/section-gap.md](components/section-gap.md).

<!-- components/section-module.md -->

# Section Module

> The brick of the page column: a section whose header row is divided from its body by a hairline and which is divided from the module above it by another. Stacked inside a `section-container`, a run of modules reads as one continuous frame, because each module draws only its own top rule and hands its sides to the column. It is where a band's header and a band's content are joined, so no page has to re-draw that join.

- Import: `import SectionModule from '@aziontech/webkit/section-module'`
- Category: marketing · Surface: site · Structure: monolithic

## When to use

- Use SectionModule for each band inside SectionContainer.
- For every band stacked inside a `section-container`.
- Whenever a band needs a header row divided from its content by a rule.
- For a body-only band, by passing no title and no header slot.

## Do

- Pass `divided` as `false` on the first module in a column: its top edge is already the hero's bottom rule, and drawing its own would put two hairlines on one pixel.
- Pass `padded` as `false` when the body is an edge-to-edge `card-grid` or `doc-card-group`, so the grid's rules meet the frame with no gutter. The unpadded body's top and side edges are drawn for it, by the header's bottom rule and the column's two side rules, so a framed part placed directly in it (a `registration-frame`, and the `doc-card-group` built on one) drops its own top and side rules and its top corner marks instead of drawing a second hairline beside them. Its bottom rule stays and closes the body, so pass `divided` as `false` on the module that follows — two modules never stack two rules on one seam.
- Use `title` and `eyebrow` for the ordinary header, and reach for the `header` slot only when the row needs markup the default header cannot express.

## Don’t

- For the full-bleed band at the top of a page → use `hero`.
- For the column the modules stack inside → use `section-container`.
- For the header alone, with no body → use `section-title`, which this module composes.
- For a hairline grid of cells → use `card-grid` inside this module's body.
- Built to be personalized (style seam): any class is accepted on the tag.

## Related

- `section-container` — the framed column this module stacks inside.
- `section-title` — the header row this module renders by default.
- `card-grid` — the edge-to-edge cell grid a module's body commonly holds.
- `registration-frame` — the registration frame a body wraps itself in.

## Props

| Prop | Type | Default | Required | Description |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `title` | `string` | `''` | false | Headline of the module's header row, rendered as its `h2`. |
| `description` | `string` | `''` | false | Supporting sentence under the headline. |
| `eyebrow` | `string` | `''` | false | Short uppercase overline rendered above the headline. |
| `align` | `SectionModuleAlign` | `'start'` | false | Layout of the default header row. |
| `divided` | `boolean` | `true` | false | Draw the top rule that divides this module from the one above it. |
| `padded` | `boolean` | `true` | false | Pad the module's body. Leave off for an edge-to-edge grid that owns its cell padding. |

## Slots

| Slot | Scope | Notes |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `default` | — | The module's body. |
| `header` | — | Replaces the default header row entirely. |
| `actions` | — | Trailing controls inside the default header row. |

## States

- Visual states: `default`
- `data-align` carries the header layout
- `data-divided` present when the top rule is drawn
- `data-padded` present when the body is padded
- `data-frame-edges` present on the body when it is not padded: the header and the column draw the body's top and side edges, so a `registration-frame` placed directly in it drops its own top and side rules and its top corner marks

## Accessibility

- Visible focus: not applicable — the module is a container; controls composed into it keep their own `focus-visible:ring-2 focus-visible:ring-(--ring-color)` ring.
- Keyboard map: none — the module is not focusable; `Tab` order is decided entirely by the slotted content.
- ARIA: the root renders a `section` and adds no role; the header's heading level comes from `section-title`, so a page's headings stay in order.
- Contrast ≥4.5:1 (text) / ≥3:1 (large + icons) — the rules are non-informational decoration and the header and body own their own contrast.
- `motion-reduce:transition-none motion-reduce:transform-none` — not applicable, the component is static.
- Touch target ≥40×40 px — not applicable, no interactive control of its own.

## Examples

### Default

```vue
<script setup>
  import SectionModule from '@aziontech/webkit/section-module'
</script>

<template>
  <SectionModule
    eyebrow="Platform"
    title="Build, run, and protect applications"
    description="One sentence naming what the band is about."
  >
    <p class="m-0 text-body-md text-(--text-muted)">Everything the band has to say.</p>
  </SectionModule>
</template>
```

2 more example(s) in [components/section-module.md](components/section-module.md).

<!-- components/section-stack.md -->

# Section Stack

> A run of framed bands that follow one another down the page, each one drawn as two registration frames — its left half and its right half — sharing a hairline with the next. With `sticky` on, each band pins under the site header a step lower than the band before it, so the run piles up as the reader scrolls and every band already read stays visible as a ledge above the one being read. The component owns the band rules, the shared hairlines and the pin offsets, and by default every band is split into two frames: a `media-split` composed into the stack frames its copy cell and its media cell as two `registration-frame`es, left and right, each with its four marks. The bands themselves are whatever the consumer composes into the default slot — each direct child becomes one band. It carries no copy, heading or media of its own.

- Import: `import SectionStack from '@aziontech/webkit/section-stack'`
- Category: marketing · Surface: site · Structure: monolithic

## When to use

- Use SectionStack (primitive) for several full bands in sequence; `sticky` piles them under the header.
- For a run of peer bands — three to six `media-split`s, one per solution or capability — that should read as one sequence.
- With `sticky`, when the run is the page's centrepiece and the reader should feel each band land on the last.
- Without `sticky`, to frame a run of bands with one shared hairline between neighbours, with no pinning.

## Do

- Keep the run to three to six bands. With `sticky` every band read stays pinned as a ledge, and a long run fills the viewport with ledges.
- Give every band the same height class (`media-split` `size="large"`, same `align`), so the ledges line up as the pile grows.
- Pass `flush` when the element directly above the stack already draws a rule (a `section-gap`, another frame), so the first band does not draw a second one.
- Give each band an opaque fill (`media-split` paints its own cells). A pinned band covers the one before it, and a transparent band shows the one underneath through it.
- Compose `media-split`s as the bands. The two frames are the band's own cells, so a child that is not a `media-split` carries no marks unless it frames itself — wrap it in a `registration-frame` with `borders="none"`.
- Render the bands with `v-for` directly in the default slot; each direct child is one band, so a wrapper element around them collapses the run into a single band.

## Don’t

- For a run where the scroll position should decide which claim is open beside one media pane → use `sticky-stack`.
- For peer claims a reader jumps between at will → use `media-tabs`.
- For a single band → frame it with `registration-frame` directly, or set `framed` on the `media-split`.
- For a grid of cards → use `card-grid`.

## Related

- `media-split` — the band this stack holds, typically at `size="large"`. Inside the stack it frames its own two cells, so leave its `framed` off.
- `sticky-stack` — the scroll-driven sibling: one pinned frame whose open claim follows the scroll, instead of bands that pile up.
- `registration-frame` — the frame each band is drawn in.
- `section-gap` — the spacer usually above the stack; pass `flush` so the first band does not draw a second rule under it.

## Props

| Prop | Type | Default | Required | Description |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `sticky` | `boolean` | `false` | false | Pin each band under the site header from `lg` up, a step lower than the band before it, so the run piles up as the page scrolls. |
| `flush` | `boolean` | `false` | false | Drop the first band's top rule, for a stack sitting directly under an element that already draws one. |

## Slots

| Slot | Scope | Notes |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `default` | — | The bands. Each direct child — including every child a `v-for` renders — is wrapped in its own frame and becomes one band; comment nodes are skipped. |

## States

- Visual states: `default`
- `data-sticky` mirrors the `sticky` prop and is what pins the bands: from `lg` up each band is `position: sticky`, its `top` the site header's height plus one `var(--spacing-md)` step per band before it. Below `lg` the bands stack in normal flow with no pinning, because a pinned pile of full-width bands leaves a phone no room to read
- `data-flush` mirrors the `flush` prop; the first band's frame then draws no top rule
- Each band is a `registration-frame` with its top and bottom rules and no marks of its own. The stack provides a context that tells a `media-split` band to frame its two cells, left and right, each with all four registration marks, so every band reads as two frames and both sides of its seam are ticked. Every band after the first pulls up one pixel so neighbouring rules overlap into a single hairline, while each band still owns its top rule — the rule a pinned band shows when it covers the one before it
- The pin offset is owned by the component, not the page: the stack pins below the site header's `3.5rem` bar, and a page sets nothing to get it
- Later bands paint over earlier ones in DOM order, so the pile grows downward with no `z-index` of its own

## Accessibility

- Visible focus: not applicable to the stack; controls inside each band keep their own `focus-visible` ring.
- Keyboard map: none of its own — `Tab` moves through each band's controls in DOM order, band by band.
- ARIA: the stack is a plain `div` with no role; each band keeps its own semantics (`media-split` is a `section` named by its heading).
- Contrast ≥4.5:1 (text) / ≥3:1 (large + icons): not applicable — the stack draws only rules; each band owns its text contrast.
- `motion-reduce:transition-none motion-reduce:transform-none` — not applicable, the stack declares no motion. Pinning is scroll position, not animation.
- Touch target — not applicable; the stack composes no control of its own.

## Examples

### Default

```vue
<script setup>
  import Illustration from '@aziontech/webkit/illustration'
  import MediaSplit from '@aziontech/webkit/media-split'
  import SectionStack from '@aziontech/webkit/section-stack'
</script>

<template>
  <SectionStack>
    <MediaSplit
      title="Build and Run Applications"
      description="Deploy applications and static sites straight from Git."
      size="large"
      align="center"
      :heading-level="3"
    >
      <template #media>
        <Illustration name="ai-applications" />
      </template>
    </MediaSplit>
    <MediaSplit
      title="Secure Applications and Networks"
      description="Stop DDoS attacks, bots and exploits before they reach your origin."
      size="large"
      align="center"
      :heading-level="3"
    >
      <template #media>
        <Illustration name="deploy-secure-mcp-server" />
      </template>
    </MediaSplit>
    <MediaSplit
      title="Observe Every Request"
      description="Follow logs, metrics and events from every location as they happen."
      size="large"
      align="center"
      :heading-level="3"
    >
      <template #media>
        <Illustration name="live-debugging" />
      </template>
    </MediaSplit>
  </SectionStack>
</template>
```

1 more example(s) in [components/section-stack.md](components/section-stack.md).

<!-- components/section-title.md -->

# Section Title

> The framed header row that opens a page section: an optional overline, the section's `h2`, an optional description and an optional row of actions, held inside a registration frame whose bottom rule is what divides the header from the section body. `variant` picks the layout — `center` for a symmetric opener, `start` for a reading-order one, and `split` for a wide row that sets the headline against its description.

- Import: `import SectionTitle from '@aziontech/webkit/section-title'`
- Category: marketing · Surface: site · Structure: monolithic

## When to use

- Use SectionTitle as the header row of a marketing band; console pages use SectionHeader.
- Opening a marketing or landing page section that needs a framed header.
- Whenever the header itself should carry the rule that separates it from the content below, with no extra margin.
- For a wide band whose headline and supporting sentence should sit side by side rather than stacked — `align="split"`.

## Do

- Leave `framed` on when the header is a brick of its own in a page column; turn it off when a band composes the header inside a frame it already draws, so the rule and the padding are not drawn twice.
- `framed` defaults to `true` here, unlike `call-to-action`, `faq` and `media-split`, where it defaults to `false`: a section title most often opens a page section as its own brick, while those components are most often composed inside a band's frame. The default stays as it is because flipping it would change every existing page; pass `framed` explicitly when composing.
- Keep one `section-title` per section, and let it own the section's `h2` so the page keeps one document outline. `size` changes the headline's step on the type scale, never its heading level — a page with a `large` and a `small` header still reads as two `h2`s.
- Leave `size` at `medium` for an ordinary section opener. Reach for `large` only where the headline is the band's whole statement, and for `small` where the header opens a subsection inside a band that already has one.
- Write the eyebrow as one or two words: it is set uppercase and prefixed with `//`, so a sentence in it reads as noise.
- Reach for `split` when the description is long enough to earn its own column — on a narrow viewport it stacks back under the headline.
- In `split`, set `--section-title-headline-span` to `2` from the consumer's class to run the headline across the middle column too, when it would otherwise wrap to three lines; the description keeps the third column, and the default `1` leaves that column as air. `--section-title-headline-max` caps the headline's width inside its span (`none` by default), so a headline that would fit on one line across two columns still breaks into two.
- In `split`, set `descriptionWidth="wide"` when the description runs to a paragraph and would stack six or more lines in the third column alone; it then spans the middle and third columns, capped at `--container-xl` and aligned to the right edge so it ends where the narrow description ends. It takes the column a `--section-title-headline-span` of `2` would claim, so use one or the other. Outside `split` the prop has no effect.
- Put the section's CTAs in the `actions` slot rather than in the body; the slot already stacks them fluid below `sm` and aligns them with the chosen `align` above it.
- The vertical rhythm around the header belongs to the `section-gap` frames on either side, not to this component.

## Don’t

- For the page's leading band and its `h1` → use `hero` instead.
- For the top bar of an application shell → use `app-header` instead.
- For a plain framed container with arbitrary content → use `registration-frame` instead.

## Related

- `hero` — the opening band; its `Hero.Title` renders the page's `h1` at hero scale.
- `registration-frame` — the frame this component composes.
- `section-gap` — the empty frame that sets the air before and after a section header; this component holds no vertical air of its own beyond its padding.
- `eyebrow` — the eyebrow treatment rendered above the headline.

## Props

| Prop | Type | Default | Required | Description |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `title` | `string` | `—` | true | Headline of the section, rendered as the section's `h2`. |
| `description` | `string` | `''` | false | Supporting sentence under the headline; overridden by the default slot. |
| `eyebrow` | `string` | `''` | false | Short uppercase overline rendered above the headline. |
| `align` | `'start' \| 'center' \| 'split'` | `'center'` | false | Layout of the header: `center` stacks and centers the copy, `start` stacks it at the start edge, `split` sets the headline and its description in two columns. |
| `size` | `'small' \| 'medium' \| 'large'` | `'medium'` | false | Step of the headline on the heading scale: `small` for a subsection inside a band, `medium` for an ordinary section opener, `large` for a band whose headline is the statement. |
| `framed` | `boolean` | `true` | false | Draw the header's own frame and padding. Turn it off when the header is composed inside a band that already owns both. |
| `padding` | `'small' \| 'medium'` | `'medium'` | false | Vertical padding of the framed header: `medium` for a section opener, `small` for a header that sits tight over its band. |
| `descriptionWidth` | `'narrow' \| 'wide'` | `'narrow'` | false | Width of the description in the horizontal layout: `narrow` keeps it to the third column, `wide` runs it across the middle column too. |

## Slots

| Slot | Scope | Notes |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `default` | — | Description body; replaces the `description` prop when provided. |
| `actions` | — | Call-to-action row under the copy, aligned with the chosen `align`. |

## States

- Visual states: `default`
- `data-align` mirrors the `align` prop: `start` | `center` | `split`
- `data-size` mirrors the `size` prop: `small` | `medium` | `large`
- `data-framed` present when the header draws its own frame and padding
- `data-padding` mirrors the `padding` prop: `small` | `medium`
- `data-description-width` mirrors the `descriptionWidth` prop: `narrow` | `wide`

## Accessibility

- Visible focus: not applicable to the header itself; controls composed into the `actions` slot keep their own `focus-visible:ring-2 focus-visible:ring-(--ring-color) focus-visible:ring-offset-2 focus-visible:ring-offset-(--bg-canvas)` ring.
- Keyboard map: none of its own — `Tab` reaches only the controls placed in the `actions` slot, in DOM order.
- ARIA: the headline is a real `h2` (the page's `h1` belongs to `Hero.Title`), so no `role` or `aria-label` is added; the frame's rules and corner marks stay `aria-hidden="true"`. In `split` the headline precedes its description in DOM order, so the reading order matches the visual one.
- Contrast ≥4.5:1 (text) / ≥3:1 (large + icons): headline on `var(--text-default)`, description on `var(--text-muted)`, both over the page canvas.
- `motion-reduce:transition-none motion-reduce:transform-none` — not applicable, the component is static.
- Touch target ≥40×40 px — the `actions` row stretches its children to full width below `sm`, so slotted buttons keep their own target size.

## Examples

### Default

```vue
<script setup>
  import SectionTitle from '@aziontech/webkit/section-title'
</script>

<template>
  <SectionTitle
    eyebrow="Platform"
    title="Everything runs at the edge"
    description="One platform for applications, security and observability."
  />
</template>
```

2 more example(s) in [components/section-title.md](components/section-title.md).

<!-- components/site-page.md -->

# Site Page

> The page renderer of the marketing site: it takes a page as data — the page JSON `{ hero, sections }` whose entries are `{ _id, props, scenes?, gap?, submission? }` — and renders each entry with the page section published at `@aziontech/webkit/<_id>`, binding `props` onto it. Openings (`hero`) run full bleed; the column (`sections`) sits in one `SectionContainer max-width="site"` with a hatched `SectionGap` before each band and one more after the last, the hatched break a page closes on before the footer, as the design sample's `SitePage` lays a page out. A page that ends on a closing band (`closing-call-to-action-section`, `newsletter-section`, or a `template-gallery-section` with `closing: true`) closes on that band's own closing frame instead, so no trailing gap is drawn; two closing bands in a row share one frame (the upper one is bound with `hatch: false`); and a `closing-call-to-action-section` of `variant: 'frame'` takes no gap before it. A scene named in `scenes` is resolved by name through the scene registry and drawn in the section's slot of that name (`hero-section` `top` / `bottom`, `console-showcase-section` `scene`). Every section and scene is loaded on demand, so a page pulls in only the sections it lists. An `_id` or scene name the renderer does not know is skipped with a development warning naming it, never silently. It reverses the earlier rule in `.claude/docs/SECTIONS.md` ("the package ships no registry and no dispatcher"): the owner decided to ship the renderer (ROADMAP M2.10 row 44), and that doc is updated with it. **Section registry.** `src/components/marketing/site-page/site-page-sections.ts`, beside the component, maps every folder in `src/components/section/` except `site-header-section` and `site-footer-section` (the frame, which `shell/site` owns) to a `() => import('../../section/<name>/<name>.vue')` thunk, keyed by the folder name, which is the `_id`. The component test fails when a section folder has no entry. **Retired `_id` aliases.** Page JSON lives outside this repo, so an `_id` retired by a 6.0 merge keeps rendering: `sitePageSectionAliases` in the same file maps each retired id to the section that absorbed it and an optional transform of its props (`'hero-copy-on-top-art'` and `'hero-with-opening'` → `hero-section`; `'closing-call-to-action-agents'` → `closing-call-to-action-section` `variant: 'primary'`; `'closing-call-to-action-templates'` → `template-gallery-section` `closing: true`; `'customer-spotlight'` → `client-story-columns-section` `reveal: 'hover'` (`items` → `cells`, `description` → `story`); `'sticky-carousel'` → `process-scroll-section` `pinned` (`items` → `steps`); `'sticky-scroll-code'` → `feature-scroll-section`; `'compare-support-tiers'` → `plan-comparison-section` (`tiers` → `plans`); `'article-index-blog'` / `'article-index-resource-hub'` → `article-index-section` `filters: 'category-search' | 'facets'`; `'use-case-links'` / `'resource-grid'` → `capability-grid-section`; `'code-split'` → `media-split-section`). `resolveSectionEntry(id, props)` applies it before the section is resolved and before the closing rules read the entry, and no warning is raised; the entry handed back on `submit` is the page's own. Renamed props travel the same way: before any alias transform, `resolveSectionEntry` translates the section's old prop names and values from the 6.0 rename map (`src/cli/migrations/v6-props.json`, copied into the same file and kept in step by the component test), for every entry whether its `_id` is retired or current, so `{ _id: 'hero-band', props: { kind: 'centered' } }` renders `hero-section` with `variant: 'center'`; an old name is dropped only when its new name is not already set. Layouts and media removed in 6.0 are translated after the renames: a hero `variant` (or v5 `kind`) `pixel-floor` renders as `title-band`; a closing band's `split` renders as `panel`, with `titleMuted` joined onto `title`; a media stack drops its old `variant` and each band's `diagram`; and a media band (`media-split-section`, or a retired `'code-split'` or `'media-split-band'` entry) with no picture is routed to the section that now shows its media, so code (`codeTabs`) renders as a `feature-scroll-section` with one step per file and a request-flow `diagram` as a `console-showcase-section` with `variant: 'diagram'` and the diagram in its `scene` slot (`resolveSectionEntry` hands back that `scenes` entry, and a scene the page JSON sets for the slot wins); a band that has a picture keeps it and drops the retired media props. This is the only alias the package keeps: no retired export or prop is re-exported. **Scene registry.** `src/assets/scene-registry.ts` exports `sceneAssets` (scene name to a `() => import(...)` thunk), the `SceneName` union of its keys and `resolveScene(name)`, which returns one cached async component per name or `null` for a name it does not hold. It is seeded with `BrandGlobeScene` and `SpinningGlobe` (the two scenes `media-split-section` drew from a private map) and with `Diagram`, `ConsoleMockup` and `Illustration`, the components a scene slot already takes. `media-split-section` moves from its private `SCENES` constant onto it in the same change, so the system has one scene map, and a scene ported later (`ConsoleApplicationScene`, `ConsoleTraceScene`, `FunctionsHeroCanvas`) becomes drawable on every page by one entry there. **Root.** A plain `div` with no role, carrying `v-bind="$attrs"` and the `data-testid` fallback `site-page`.

- Import: `import SitePage from '@aziontech/webkit/site-page'`
- Category: marketing · Surface: site · Structure: monolithic

## When to use

- Use SitePage inside SiteLayout to render a page from its JSON.
- Rendering a marketing page stored as data (a CMS entry, a JSON file, the site's page modules) on WebKit sections.
- A campaign landing built from existing sections with no page-specific code.

## Do

- Write `_id` as the section's public subpath (`customer-use-cases-section` for `@aziontech/webkit/customer-use-cases-section`); the `props` object is exactly what the section's own `site-*.json` example carries.
- Put openings (`hero-section`, `hero-form-section`, `article-body-section`, `pricing-plans-section`) in `hero` and every band after them in `sections`.
- Keep scenes as names (`{ component: 'BrandGlobeScene' }`), never as code, so the page stays plain JSON.
- Use `gap` only to change the default break: `none` for a band that follows its neighbour with no break, `small` for two bands that belong together.
- End a page on a closing band, or on `{ _id: 'closing-call-to-action-section', props: { variant: 'frame' } }` when its last band already carries its actions; never set `hatch` yourself on stacked closings, the renderer does.
- Keep transport details of a form (`endpoint`, `hiddenFields`) in `submission`, next to `props`; the renderer passes the entry back on `submit` and the site sends.
- Watch the development console after changing a page: an unknown `_id` or scene is named there.
- Keep one opening per page: every opening renders the page `h1`, so two openings put two `h1` on the page.
- Render the page on the server when layout shift matters: a section still loading on the client reserves no space.

## Don’t

- A page that composes marketing primitives by hand (`Hero`, `MediaSplit`, `CardGrid`) — write the SFC with `SectionContainer` / `SectionGap` instead.
- A console screen or a docs page — use `shell/console` or `shell/docs`.
- One section on its own — import that section (`@aziontech/webkit/<name>`) and bind its props.

## Related

- `section-container` — the column the renderer lays `sections` in.
- `section-gap` — the hatched break it draws before each band.
- The page sections (`hero-section`, `customer-use-cases-section`, `faq-section`, …) — what each `_id` names.
- `shell/site` — the nav and footer frame a page renders inside.

## Props

| Prop | Type | Default | Required | Description |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `hero` | `SitePageSection[]` | `() => []` | false | Opening sections, rendered full bleed above the column in order; each is `{ _id, props?, scenes?, gap?, submission? }`. |
| `sections` | `SitePageSection[]` | `() => []` | false | Column sections, rendered in order inside the site column with a hatched gap before each and after the last; each is `{ _id, props?, scenes?, gap?, submission? }`. |

## Events

| Event | Payload | Notes |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `submit` | `(event: SubmitEvent, payload: SitePageSubmit)` | A section's form passed its own validation (`hero-form-section`, `newsletter-section`); the site sends it and feeds `loading` / `submitted` / `error` back through that entry's `props`. |

## Assets

- Illustrations: every valid name is in illustrations.md (MCP `list_assets({ kind: "illustrations" })`). An unknown name renders a "PLACE DESIGN ASSET" placeholder and warns in the console.
- Diagrams: every valid name is in diagrams.md (MCP `list_assets({ kind: "diagrams" })`). An unknown name draws nothing.
- Brand marks: every valid name is in brand-marks.md (MCP `list_assets({ kind: "brandMarks" })`). An unknown name is not drawn as a mark: `Marquee` prints it as a typographic wordmark, the quote components show no mark.

## States

- No state of its own; each section owns its states (`loading`, `submitted`, `error` on a form band come from its `props`).
- An unknown `_id` or scene name renders nothing in its place and warns once per name in development.
- A retired `_id` renders with its successor through `sitePageSectionAliases`, with the props transform applied.
- An old prop name or value of a section (`kind: 'centered'` on a hero) renders as its 6.0 successor (`variant: 'center'`).
- Closing bands: a closing band followed directly by another is bound with `hatch: false` (an explicit `hatch` in its `props` is overridden), so stacked closings share the lower band's closing frame; a `closing-call-to-action-section` with `variant: 'frame'` takes no gap before it; when the last column entry is a closing band that draws its frame, the trailing gap is omitted.
- A section or scene still loading renders nothing in its place until its chunk arrives.
- A section or scene whose chunk fails to load (network failure, deploy skew) or takes longer than 10 s renders nothing in its place, the rest of the page keeps rendering, and a development warning names the `_id` or scene; it does not retry (`defineAsyncComponent` with `timeout` and `onError`).

## Accessibility

- The renderer adds no landmark, heading or focusable element of its own; each section owns its landmark (`<section>` named by its title) and its keyboard model.
- Openings render before the column, so the page `h1` (in the opening) comes first in reading order.
- The breaks are decorative (`SectionGap` is `aria-hidden`).
- Keyboard map: none of its own; focus moves through the sections in page order.

## Examples

### Default

```vue
<script setup lang="ts">
  import SitePage, { type SitePageSection } from '@aziontech/webkit/site-page'

  const hero: SitePageSection[] = [
    {
      _id: 'hero-section',
      props: {
        variant: 'title-band',
        size: 'large',
        eyebrow: 'SQL Database',
        title: 'Relational data, next to every user',
        description:
          'Query a distributed SQL database from your functions, with reads served from the nearest location.',
        primaryAction: { label: 'Start for free', href: '/signup' },
        secondaryAction: { label: 'Docs', href: '/docs', trailing: true }
      }
    }
  ]

  const sections: SitePageSection[] = [
    {
      _id: 'customer-use-cases-section',
      props: {
        eyebrow: 'SQL Database',
        title: 'What teams use SQL Database for',
        description: 'Teams use it to:',
        primaryAction: { label: 'Docs', href: '/docs', trailing: true },
        items: [
          {
            icon: 'az az-comments',
            title: 'RAG applications',
            description: 'Build topic-specific chatbots on data stored in SQL Database.'
          },
          {
            icon: 'az az-search',
            title: 'Search applications',

          },
          {
            icon: 'az az-sync',

            description: 'Give AI applications relevant context from vector embeddings.'
          },
          {
            icon: 'az az-globe',

          }
        ]

    },
    {
      _id: 'faq-section',
      props: {
        anchor: 'faq',

          {
            question: 'Which SQL dialect does it speak?',
            answer: 'SQLite-compatible SQL, so existing queries and tools work unchanged.'
          },

        variant: 'primary',
        anchor: 'contact',
```

<!-- components/stats.md -->

# Stats

> A band of headline figures — throughput, uptime, points of presence — set in the display face at big-number scale, each in its own cell of a hairline `card-grid` with a short caption underneath. It is the proof band of a marketing page: the one place a page states its numbers, between a `section-title` and the section body.

- Import: `import Stats from '@aziontech/webkit/stats'`
- Category: marketing · Surface: site · Structure: monolithic

## When to use

- Use Stats inside another band or a hand-built SFC; for product KPIs use StatTile.
- Rendering page JSON, or you need a whole band with no custom content → the section (`stats-section`). Building an SFC by hand, needing slots, or nesting inside another band → the primitive (`stats`).
- To state a handful of measured facts (2–6) as the proof band of a marketing or landing page.
- Whenever the figure itself is the message and the caption only names what was measured.
- Under a `section-title`, as the opening evidence of a section.

## Do

- The band draws no frame of its own: the rule and the corner marks belong to the `section-module` or `registration-frame` that the page assembles it into, so a band placed in a framed column never lands a second hairline on the column's own.
- Keep the band to 2–6 figures: it reads as a row, and past six the cells stop being scannable.
- Put the unit in `suffix` and the qualifier in `prefix`, so the figure itself stays the largest, highest-contrast thing in the cell.
- Keep `label` to a few words — it is set at overline scale and is a caption, not a sentence.
- Round the figures. A band is a claim, not a readout; `99.99%` belongs here, `99.9873%` does not.
- Leave `size` at `medium` unless the band is the page's hero statement (`large`) or sits inside a denser section (`small`).

## Don’t

- For a single monetary amount → use `currency-amount`, which owns the amount ladder and its symbol placement.
- For a plan's headline price inside a pricing card → use `pricing-card`.
- For tabular or comparable figures a reader will scan row by row → use `table`.
- For a label-and-value pair at body scale inside a card or list → use `item`.

## Related

- `section-title` — the framed section header this band usually sits under.
- `card-grid` — the hairline grid the cells sit in; its `gap-px` seams are the rules between them.
- `currency-amount` — a monetary amount at three reading distances, with symbol handling.
- `pricing-card` — a plan card, where the headline figure is a price.

## Props

| Prop | Type | Default | Required | Description |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `items` | `StatsItem[]` | `[]` | false | The figures rendered as cells, in order; each item is `{ value, label, prefix?, suffix? }` where `value` is the figure, `label` its caption, and `prefix` / `suffix` the qualifier and unit set beside it. |
| `size` | `'small' \| 'medium' \| 'large'` | `'medium'` | false | Size token; picks the big-number scale the figures are set at. |

## States

- Visual states: `default`
- `data-size` mirrors the `size` prop and drives the figure's type scale
- Empty: when `items` is empty the band renders no cells and draws no frame — a marketing proof band with nothing to prove is absent from the page, not an `empty-state`

## Accessibility

- Visible focus: not applicable — the band is static copy and holds no interactive control.
- Keyboard map: none of its own; the band contains no focusable element and is skipped by `Tab`.
- ARIA: each cell is a `<figure>` whose `<figcaption>` carries the caption, so the figure and what it measures are one unit for a screen reader; `prefix` and `suffix` are plain spans inside the figure's text, so the announced value is the whole expression.
- Contrast ≥4.5:1 (text) / ≥3:1 (large + icons): the figure is `var(--text-default)` (17.65:1 light, 20.12:1 dark) and the accent prefix `var(--text-primary)` (`primary-700` in light, `primary-500` in dark) is large text (5.5:1 or more light, 6.71:1 dark). The caption is `var(--text-muted)` at overline scale — 5.32:1 on the dark canvas, but **3.78:1 on the light canvas, under AA for normal text**. This is a system-wide property of `--text-muted`, not of this component; it is carried as a theme-level follow-up and is not worked around here with an off-token colour.
- `motion-reduce:transition-none motion-reduce:transform-none` — not applicable, the component is static.
- Touch target ≥40×40 px — not applicable, no interactive target.
- Reflow: the figure row wraps (`flex-wrap`), so the prefix and the suffix move under the value when a cell is narrow, and the value itself breaks (`wrap-anywhere`) rather than overflow at 200% zoom.

## Examples

### Default

```vue
<script setup>
  import Stats from '@aziontech/webkit/stats'
</script>

<template>
  <Stats
    :items="[
      { value: '120', suffix: '+', label: 'Points of presence' },
      { value: '99.99', suffix: '%', label: 'Availability' },
      { prefix: '<', value: '15', suffix: 'ms', label: 'Median latency' },
      { value: '4.5', suffix: 'Tbps', label: 'Network capacity' }
    ]"
  />
</template>
```

1 more example(s) in [components/stats.md](components/stats.md).

<!-- components/sticky-stack.md -->

# Sticky Stack

> A band that pins to the viewport and is read by scrolling through it: a stack of claims on one side, one piece of media on the other, and the scroll position — not a pointer — deciding which claim is open. Each claim expands in turn while the ones already read collapse and stack above it, so the column keeps one height and the reader always sees where they are in the run. The media beside it is replaced, not dimmed: the outgoing scene leaves and the incoming one mounts, so a scene that animates plays its entrance every time it is reached.

- Import: `import StickyStack from '@aziontech/webkit/sticky-stack'`
- Category: marketing · Surface: site · Structure: monolithic

## When to use

- Use StickyStack (primitive) for claims that open one by one as the reader scrolls past pinned media.
- For a sequence of claims a reader should take in one at a time, each with a medium worth looking at — five ways a platform is used, each with the file you would actually write.
- When the media is the payload and the copy is the caption: a code sample, a console shot, an official scene.
- As the one band on a page that rewards scrolling slowly, placed where a reader has already committed to the page.

## Do

- Keep the run to three to six claims. The band holds the page for `dwell` of a screen per claim, and a reader who cannot see the end of it reads it as a page that will not let go.
- Write each claim to stand alone. A reader can land mid-band from a deep link or a focus jump, so no claim may depend on the one above it.
- Keep a title to one line. The collapsed rows set the column's height, and a title that wraps changes it for every claim at once.
- Keep a description to two or three lines. Only the open claim shows one, and a long one pushes the stack past the pinned frame.
- Give every claim a medium. The frame holds one at a time and mounts it fresh, so a claim with nothing to show reads as a band that broke rather than one that is quiet.
- Use media of the same shape. The frame does not resize between claims, and a scene that changes the frame's height un-pins the band mid-read.
- Set `--sticky-stack-top` to the height of any sticky header above the band, or the first claim opens underneath it. Set it on a wrapper around the band rather than on the band's own tag: custom properties inherit, and the band is not a style seam.
- Leave `showProgress` on. It is the only thing telling a reader how much of the pin is left.
- Raise `dwell` for claims that carry code a reader must actually read; lower it for scenes that are grasped at a glance.

## Don’t

- For peer claims a reader should be able to jump between at will → use `media-tabs`, which selects on hover, click and a timer without taking the scroll.
- For one claim beside one picture → use `media-split`.
- For questions and answers, or any run longer than about six items → use `faq`. A pinned band holds the page for its whole run, and a long run holds it too long.
- On a page that is already a single short screen: there is no scroll to spend.
- For the product-page walkthrough placed from page JSON → use `feature-scroll-section`.

## Related

- `media-tabs` — the pointer-driven sibling. Same two-column anatomy; the selection moves on hover, click and a timer, and the band does not pin.
- `media-split` — one claim, one medium, no selection and no pin.
- `code-block` — the medium this band most often holds, one sample per claim.
- `illustration` — the official scene a claim's `media` slot otherwise holds.
- `section-title` — the heading above the band; this component carries no title of its own.

## Props

| Prop | Type | Default | Required | Description |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `items` | `StickyStackItem[]` | `[]` | false | The claims, in reading order; each item is a title, the sentence that opens with it, and the image shown while it is open. |
| `size` | `'small' \| 'medium' \| 'large'` | `'medium'` | false | Height the open claim is held to, and so the height of the whole stack beside the media. |
| `dwell` | `number` | `0.7` | false | Screen-heights of scrolling each claim holds while the band is pinned; the band's total scroll is one screen plus this much per claim. |
| `showProgress` | `boolean` | `true` | false | Fills a hairline along the open claim's bottom edge as its share of the scroll is spent. |

## Events

| Event | Payload | Notes |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `active-index-change` | `number` | The open claim changed; the payload is the index now open. Fires on the scroll that moved it and on a reader selecting a claim. |

## Slots

| Slot | Scope | Notes |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `media` | { item: StickyStackItem, index: number, active: boolean } | The claim's medium; replaces the image built from the item's own source. Mounted fresh on every change, so an entrance animation inside it replays. |
| `actions` | { item: StickyStackItem, index: number, active: boolean } | The claim's own controls, pinned to the bottom edge of the open claim. From `lg` up they show only while the claim is open and are out of the tab order while it is collapsed; below `lg` every claim is open and carries its own. Nothing renders when the slot is empty. |
| `backdrop` | — | Fills the media frame behind every claim's medium, from `lg` up. Mounted once and never swapped, so a texture or an animated surface placed here holds still while the media change over it. Nothing renders when the slot is empty. |

## States

- Visual states: `default`, `hover`, `focus-visible` on each claim's title control
- `data-active` marks the open claim; it holds the plain text tone and shows its description
- `data-completed` marks every claim already read; they collapse to their title and rest at a muted tone, further dimmed so the read part of the run is legible at a glance, stacked above the open one
- `data-size` carries the size token, which is what sets the open claim's height and so the stack's height
- `data-progress` is present on the root while `showProgress` is on
- The media frame shows exactly one medium: the outgoing one leaves as the incoming one mounts
- The backdrop is not part of the medium: it stays mounted across every change, under the medium that is leaving and the one arriving
- A claim's description is held to `var(--container-sm)` so the measure stays short however wide the column is
- Below `lg` the band does not pin: every claim is open, each with its own medium under it, and nothing moves
- Empty: when `items` is empty the band renders nothing

## Accessibility

- Visible focus: `focus-visible:ring-2 focus-visible:ring-(--ring-color) focus-visible:ring-offset-2 focus-visible:ring-offset-(--bg-canvas)` on every claim's title control
- Keyboard map: `Tab` reaches each claim's title control in reading order; focusing or activating one scrolls the band to that claim's share of the scroll, so a keyboard reader moves the band exactly as a pointer reader does. There is no key that scrolls the page other than the reader's own — the band never takes the scroll it is given.
- ARIA: the claim list is an `<ol>` (the run is ordered); the open claim's control carries `aria-current="true"`; the progress hairline is `aria-hidden="true"`
- Every claim's description stays in the accessibility tree while collapsed (clipped, never `display: none`), so the whole run is readable without scrolling through it
- Contrast ≥4.5:1 (text) / ≥3:1 (large + icons), including the muted tone a read claim rests at
- `motion-reduce:transition-none` / `motion-reduce:animate-none` on every animated state; the pin itself is not motion and stays
- Touch target ≥40×40 px: each claim's control spans its whole row

## Examples

### Default

```vue
<script setup>
  import Illustration from '@aziontech/webkit/illustration'
  import StickyStack from '@aziontech/webkit/sticky-stack'

  const scenes = ['ai-applications', 'live-debugging', 'deploy-secure-mcp-server']

  const items = [
    {
      title: 'Automated deployment via Git or CLI',
      description:
        'Push to a branch or run one command; the platform builds the project and puts the result in every location, with the build before it a rollback away.'
    },
    {
      title: 'Infrastructure as code with Terraform',
      description:
        'Declare workloads, rules and domains in the provider and apply them the same way as the rest of your estate, with the same review and the same plan.'
    },
    {
      title: 'Metrics and events via GraphQL API',
      description:
        'Query the same numbers the console charts, from one endpoint, so a dashboard you already run can carry the platform alongside everything else.'
    }
  ]
</script>

<template>
  <StickyStack :items="items">
    <template #media="{ index }">
      <Illustration :name="scenes[index]" />
    </template>
  </StickyStack>
</template>
```

1 more example(s) in [components/sticky-stack.md](components/sticky-stack.md).

<!-- components/texture.md -->

# Texture

> The one decorative surface every band in the system is painted on: a full-bleed, non-interactive layer that tiles a texture behind whatever sits above it. It is a material, not a container — it takes no content and claims no space, so a band composes it into its own backdrop slot and keeps ownership of its layout.

- Import: `import Texture from '@aziontech/webkit/texture'`
- Category: marketing · Surface: site · Structure: monolithic

## When to use

- In a band's `background` slot (`hero`), where the opening band needs the site's own lattice under its copy.
- Behind a panel, dropzone or slide that would read as empty at a flat fill.
- Anywhere a surface should look like a technical drawing ground without shipping an image.

## Do

- Give the layer a positioned ancestor (`relative`) that also clips it; it fills that box and nothing else.
- Keep the texture behind copy, never over it. `dots` is the quiet default; `lines` is quieter still and reads as ruled paper; `dither` and `pixelate` are loud enough to need their ink dropped or their dense end steered away from the words.
- Do not stack two layers at different pitches — that reads as a moiré, not as a texture.
- Quiet a texture with `--texture-ink` rather than with `opacity`, so the ground keeps showing through at full strength.
- Leave it out when the band already sits inside a `registration-frame`; rules plus texture is usually one layer too many.

## Don’t

- To draw the rules between real content cells → use `registration-frame`, whose rules are structural.
- For the ruled divider between sections → use `section-gap`, which composes this layer's `lines` variant and owns that treatment.
- For a meaningful image or diagram → use `illustration`.
- As a wrapper around content. The layer is absolutely positioned and paints only itself; content is a sibling above it, inside the positioned ancestor.

## Related

- `hero` — the band whose `background` slot this is composed into.
- `registration-frame` — structural rules and corner marks, not decoration.
- `section-gap` — the hatched rhythm band between sections.
- `hero` — the marketing band that paints this texture behind its own copy.

## Props

| Prop | Type | Default | Required | Description |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `variant` | `'dots' \| 'grid' \| 'lines' \| 'dither' \| 'pixelate' \| 'none'` | `'dots'` | false | Which texture to paint; `none` renders the layer with no texture at all. |
| `size` | `'small' \| 'medium' \| 'large'` | `'medium'` | false | Pitch of the tiling — how far apart the cells sit. |
| `fade` | `'none' \| 'top' \| 'bottom' \| 'left' \| 'right' \| 'edges' \| 'vignette'` | `'none'` | false | Fades the layer out along an axis so it meets content without a hard edge. |

## States

- Visual states: `default`
- `data-variant`, `data-size` and `data-fade` mirror their props and drive which texture paints, at what pitch, and how it fades out
- `variant="none"` paints nothing, so a band can switch its texture off without unmounting the layer
- `fade="none"` leaves the layer unmasked; every other value masks it so the ink reaches zero before the layer's own edge; `top` and `bottom` are a single fade — full ink at the leading edge, zero at `--texture-fade-end`; `left` and `right` fade out toward the named edge in the same way, full ink at the opposite edge, which is how a band keeps its texture behind the media and clears it under the copy. Setting `--texture-fade-lead: transparent` adds a second ramp in from zero at the leading edge, so the layer also clears whatever overlaps that edge; `hero` sets it on its backdrop because its opening band sits under the site header, and no other band needs it
- Custom properties tune a texture in place, set from the consumer's class: `--texture-ink` (every variant), `--texture-fade-start` / `--texture-fade-end` (where the fade holds full ink and where it reaches zero), `--texture-fade-lead` (the ink at the leading edge of `top` / `bottom`; `black` by default, `transparent` for the double fade), `--texture-from` / `--texture-to` / `--texture-direction` (the `dither` ramp's density span and axis), `--texture-pool-x` (where both `pixelate` light pools sit on the x axis), `--texture-pool-a` / `--texture-pool-b` (each pool's own `x y` position, so the two can sit on opposite edges; either falls back to `--texture-pool-x`), `--texture-wave-gain` (multiplies the ink of both `pixelate` waves, 1 by default, capped at full ink, so a band can make the motion read over the pools), `--texture-wave-pace` (a whole number of wave periods each loop travels, 1 by default; 2 doubles the speed without changing the animation's duration token)

## Accessibility

- Visible focus: not applicable — the layer is decorative and holds no interactive control.
- Keyboard map: none; the layer is `pointer-events-none` and is never in the tab order.
- ARIA: the root carries `aria-hidden="true"`, so no texture is announced and the layer adds no role.
- Contrast ≥4.5:1 (text) / ≥3:1 (large + icons): the ink is mixed down from `var(--text-default)` to a surface step, deliberately below text contrast — content above the layer keeps its own colours and its own contrast against the page canvas.
- `motion-reduce:animate-none` on both pixelate waves; every other variant is static.
- Touch target ≥40×40 px — not applicable, no interactive target.

## Examples

### Default

```vue
<script setup>
  import Texture from '@aziontech/webkit/texture'
</script>

<template>
  <div class="relative h-80 w-full overflow-hidden bg-(--bg-canvas)">
    <Texture
      variant="dots"
      size="medium"
      fade="none"
    />
  </div>
</template>
```

2 more example(s) in [components/texture.md](components/texture.md).

<!-- components/architecture-tabs-section.md -->

# Architecture Tabs Section

> The architecture band of a product page: a horizontal section title with one large button, over a row of up to five tabs whose active tab draws its named diagram the full width of the frame. It is a page section — the site places it from a `{ _id: 'architecture-tabs-section', props }` entry and binds `props` straight onto it — and it composes `section-module`, `section-title`, `button`, `registration-frame`, `media-tabs` and `diagram`. The tabs advance on a timer that pauses under the pointer or keyboard focus and never runs under reduced motion; that state is owned by `media-tabs`.

- Import: `import ArchitectureTabsSection from '@aziontech/webkit/architecture-tabs-section'`
- Category: section · Surface: site · Structure: monolithic

## When to use

- Use ArchitectureTabsSection only when every tab shows a full-width official diagram (up to five tabs).
- To plot several architectures of one product, or one request across the platform, one diagram at a time.

## Do

- Keep to five tabs with `tabsSide` `top` or `bottom`, three with `start` or `end`; tabs past the limit are not rendered.
- Give every tab a `diagram` from the diagram library and a stable `value`.

## Don’t

- For features shown by an illustration each → use `feature-tabs-section`.
- For a scroll-driven walkthrough → use `feature-scroll-section`.

## Related

- `media-tabs` — the timed tab row this section renders through.
- `diagram` — the named architecture diagram each tab draws.

## Props

| Prop | Type | Default | Required | Description |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `anchor` | `string` | `''` | false | In-page id set on the band so a `#hash` link can scroll to it. |
| `eyebrow` | `string` | `''` | false | Eyebrow above the section title. |
| `title` | `string` | `''` | false | Headline of the band, rendered by the horizontal section title as its `h2`; the band renders no header when empty. |
| `description` | `string` | `''` | false | Supporting sentence beside the headline. |
| `tabsSide` | `ArchitectureTabsSectionSide` | `'top'` | false | Side of the diagram the tabs sit on: `top`, `bottom`, `start` or `end`; start and end hold up to three tabs. |
| `tabs` | `ArchitectureTabsSectionTab[]` | `[]` | false | The architectures, in order; each is `{ value, title, description, diagram, diagramAriaLabel? }` where `diagram` is the name of the diagram drawn while the tab is active and `diagramAriaLabel` replaces the description that diagram ships. |
| `autoplay` | `boolean` | `true` | false | Opens the next tab on a timer, with the composed tabs' visible pause control. |
| `autoplayInterval` | `number` | `6000` | false | Milliseconds each tab holds before the next one opens. |
| `primaryAction` | `SectionTitleAction` | `undefined` | false | Large button in the section title; `{ label, href, trailing?, target? }`. Omitted when absent. |

## Assets

- Diagrams: every valid name is in diagrams.md (MCP `list_assets({ kind: "diagrams" })`). An unknown name draws nothing.

## States

- Visual states: `default`; the active tab, its progress and the timer are owned by `media-tabs`
- Empty: when `tabs` is empty the band renders nothing — not the header, not the frame
- The header renders only when `title` is set
- The timer holds each tab for `autoplayInterval` milliseconds (owned by `media-tabs`)

## Accessibility

- Visible focus: owned by `media-tabs` (the tabs) and `button`.
- Keyboard map: `Tab` reaches the button and the tab row; the tab row's keys are owned by `media-tabs`.
- ARIA: the band is a `<section>` named by its title (`aria-label`) when one is set; the headline is the section title's `h2`; each diagram carries the description its spec ships, or the tab's `diagramAriaLabel` when one is set.
- Contrast ≥4.5:1 (text) / ≥3:1 (large + icons): owned by `section-title`, `media-tabs` and `diagram`.
- `motion-reduce:transition-none motion-reduce:transform-none` — the band adds no motion; the timer never runs under reduced motion (owned by `media-tabs`).
- Touch target ≥40×40 px: the large button and the tab rows.

## Examples

### Default

```vue
<script setup>
  import ArchitectureTabsSection from '@aziontech/webkit/architecture-tabs-section'

  const tabs = [
    {
      value: 'request-path',
      title: 'Request path',
      description:
        'A call crosses Firewall, the application and the function before it reaches a model.',
      diagram: 'ai-gateway'
    },
    {
      value: 'failover',
      title: 'Provider failover',
      description: 'When a provider degrades, the gateway moves to the next model on the alias.',
      diagram: 'ai-gateway-provider-failover',
      diagramAriaLabel:
        'A request falls back from a degraded model provider to the next model on the alias'
    },
    {
      value: 'costs',
      title: 'Cost routing',
      description: 'Premium tokens go only where they pay off; everyone else gets a lighter model.',
      diagram: 'ai-gateway-model-routing'
    },
    {
      value: 'latency',
      title: 'Latency routing',
      description: 'Real-time tasks run on AI Inference, close to the user, in under 30 ms.',
      diagram: 'ai-gateway-latency-routing'
    },
    {
      value: 'cache',
      title: 'Response cache',
      description:
        'A repeated prompt is answered from cache, with no model call and no tokens spent.',
      diagram: 'ai-gateway-response-cache'
    }
  ]

  const primaryAction = {
    label: 'Docs',
    href: '/product-preview',
    trailing: true
  }
</script>

<template>
  <ArchitectureTabsSection
    eyebrow="Routing"
    title="Every AI request through one control point"
    description="Every client calls one endpoint. Firewall stops abuse, then a function checks the key, routes the call, and logs it."
    tabs-side="bottom"
    :autoplay-interval="8000"
    :tabs="tabs"
    :primary-action="primaryAction"
  />
</template>
```

<!-- components/article-body-section.md -->

# Article Body Section

> The reading page of an article. A brand banner sits over a three-column grid: the share, community and copy-page rail on the left; the breadcrumb, headline, byline and prose down the middle at a reading measure; and the "On this page" outline on the right. Both rails stick under the header and fold away below `lg`, leaving the column alone. The section opens the page itself, so it carries its own `section-container` instead of sitting in the page column. The prose arrives as markdown: `##` / `###` headings (each with an id slugged from its text, feeding the outline), fenced code blocks, `-` and numbered lists and paragraphs, with inline links, `**strong**`, `*emphasis*` and `` `code` `` inside paragraphs and list items. The outline follows the reader: the heading last scrolled past is active, and choosing an entry scrolls to it (smoothly unless reduced motion is set) and writes its hash to the address. The byline can carry the writers' names and their photos as overlapping avatars. It is a page section — the site places it from a `{ _id: 'article-body-section', props }` entry and binds `props` straight onto it — and it composes `section-container`, `section-module`, `registration-frame`, `breadcrumb`, `hero-title`, `divider`, `doc-prose`, `tooltip`, `icon-button`, `split-button`, `avatar` and `doc-on-this-page`; copying to the clipboard is local behaviour, confirmed by a toast.

- Import: `import ArticleBodySection from '@aziontech/webkit/article-body-section'`
- Category: section · Surface: site · Structure: monolithic

## When to use

- To open every blog post and every success story page.

## Do

- Write the body as plain blocks — headings, paragraphs and `-` lists separated by blank lines; inline markdown is rendered as text.
- Mark the last breadcrumb entry `active` (it renders `aria-current="page"`).

## Don’t

- For a list of articles → use `article-index-section`.
- For a closing row of related articles → use `article-cards-section`.

## Related

- `doc-prose` — the reading-measure typography the body renders in.
- `doc-on-this-page` — the outline rail fed by the body's headings.

## Props

| Prop | Type | Default | Required | Description |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `eyebrow` | `string` | `''` | false | Eyebrow above the headline. |
| `article` | `ArticleBodySectionArticle` | `—` | true | The piece being read: its `title` (the page h1), `description`, `date`, `readTime`, canonical `href`, and optionally `author` (the writers' names) and `authors` (each a `name` and an `avatar` photo URL). |
| `trail` | `BreadcrumbItemData[]` | `[]` | false | The breadcrumb over the headline, each a `label` and an `href`, the last one marked `active`. |
| `body` | `string` | `''` | false | The article as markdown; its `##` and `###` headings feed the outline. |
| `banner` | `string` | `''` | false | URL of the image over the masthead; the banner frame is omitted when empty. |
| `share` | `ArticleBodySectionShare` | `undefined` | false | Labels of the share group: its `title`, `copyLink`, `shareX` and `shareLinkedIn`, optional `copyFeed` with the `feed` URL it copies, and optional toast messages `copiedLink`, `copiedFeed` and `copiedPage`; the group is omitted when absent. |
| `community` | `ArticleBodySectionCommunity` | `undefined` | false | The community group: a `title` and `links`, each a `label`, an `href` and an `icon`; omitted when absent. |
| `copyPage` | `ArticleBodySectionCopyPage` | `undefined` | false | The copy-page split button: its `label` and a `menu` of entries, each a `value`, a `label` and an `icon`; omitted when absent. |
| `assistants` | `Record<string, string>` | `{}` | false | URL templates of the assistants the copy-page menu opens, keyed by menu `value`; `{prompt}` in a template is replaced by the encoded prompt to read the article. |
| `anchor` | `string` | `''` | false | In-page id set on the band so a hash link can target it. |

## States

- Visual states: `default`, `hover` and `focus-visible` on every link and button
- The outline lists the body's headings; it is omitted when the body has none
- Scroll-spy: the first heading is active on the server and on load; after mount the active heading is the last one whose top has scrolled above the header line (120 px), updated on scroll; choosing an outline entry scrolls its heading into view (`smooth`, or instant under reduced motion), makes it active and replaces the address hash
- The left rail renders only the groups that are set, and is omitted when none is
- Copying: the copy-link button and the `link` menu entry copy the current page URL (`article.href` when there is none); the copy-feed button (when `share.copyFeed` and `share.feed` are set) copies `share.feed`; the split button's main action copies the page as markdown (headline, deck, body); each success raises a success toast (`share.copiedLink` / `copiedFeed` / `copiedPage`, or the locale default), and a failed clipboard write raises an error toast. Toasts render where the page mounts a `Toaster`
- Copy-page menu: the `link` entry copies the URL; the `markdown` entry opens the page as plain-text markdown in a new tab; an entry whose `value` is a key of `assistants` opens that template in a new tab with `{prompt}` replaced by the encoded prompt `Read <article.href> so I can ask questions about it.`
- Byline: with `authors`, overlapping large avatars (photo, or initials without one) lead the byline; with `author`, the names sit above the date and read time
- Prose: fenced code blocks render as `pre`/`code`; numbered lists as `ol`; a full `http` link opens in a new tab
- The eyebrow is plain text with no `//` prefix: that prefix belongs to the hero and the pricing sections only.

## Accessibility

- Visible focus: every link and button shows a `focus-visible` ring.
- Feedback: copy results are announced by the `Toaster`'s live region; the outline's active entry carries `aria-current` (owned by `doc-on-this-page`).
- Keyboard map: `Tab` moves through the breadcrumb, the rails and the prose links; the split button owns its menu keyboard map.
- ARIA: the root is a plain `div` so the page keeps its own landmarks; the headline is the page `h1` (from `hero-title`); the prose is an `article`; the two rails are `aside` landmarks, the left one named by the share title, the right one by the outline's own navigation; the share and community groups are named `section`s; icon-only share buttons are named by their labels and carry a tooltip; the banner is decorative (`aria-hidden`, `alt=""`); heading ids are slugged from their text.
- Contrast ≥4.5:1 (text) / ≥3:1 (large + icons): text uses `--text-default` / `--text-muted`.
- `motion-reduce:transition-none` on every transition.
- Touch target ≥40×40 px — owned by `icon-button` and `split-button`.

## Examples

### Default

```vue
<script setup>
  import ArticleBodySection from '@aziontech/webkit/article-body-section'
  import placeholderImage from '@aziontech/webkit/assets/placeholder-image.svg'
</script>

<template>
  <ArticleBodySection
    eyebrow="Blog"
    :banner="placeholderImage"
    body="## Challenge&#10;&#10;Magalu is the most innovative retail company in LATAM.&#10;&#10;## Solution&#10;&#10;Magalu counts on Azion’s platform to evolve its security posture.&#10;&#10;## Results and Impact&#10;&#10;Magalu simplified security operations by blocking millions of threats per year."
    :article="{
      title: 'Magalu Ensures High Availability at Scale with Azion',
      description:
        'Explore how Magalu enhances cybersecurity with Azion’s Firewall and WAF, improving threat intelligence and bot management in retail.',
      date: 'FEB 4, 2022',
      readTime: '5 min read',
      href: 'https://www.azion.com/en/success-case/magalu/',
      author: 'Jane Doe',
      authors: [{ name: 'Jane Doe' }]
    }"
    :trail="[
      { label: 'Success cases', href: '/success-cases' },
      { label: 'Retail', href: '/success-cases?industry=Retail' },
      { label: 'Magalu Ensures High Availability at Scale with Azion', active: true }
    ]"
    :share="{
      title: 'Share this article',
      copyLink: 'Copy link',
      copyFeed: 'Copy RSS feed',
      feed: 'https://example.com/blog/feed.xml',
      shareX: 'Share on X',
      shareLinkedIn: 'Share on LinkedIn'
    }"
    :community="{
      title: 'Community',
      links: [
        {
          label: 'Join us on Discord',
          href: 'https://discord.com/invite/Yp9N7RMVZy',
          icon: 'az az-discord'
        },
        { label: 'Read our blog posts', href: '/blog', icon: 'az az-comment' },
        { label: 'Follow us on X', href: 'https://twitter.com/aziontech', icon: 'az az-x-brand' }
      ]
    }"
    :copy-page="{
      label: 'Copy page',
      menu: [
        { value: 'link', label: 'Get page link', icon: 'az az-link' },

  />
```
