<!-- components/article-cards-section.md -->

# Article Cards Section

> A titled row of three linked article cards on one hairline frame grid with no gutter, stacking to one column on mobile. Each card is a single link: a 16:9 media cell holding the article's cover — or, when it has none, the client's mark in one flat ink that follows the theme — then the headline, the deck and a `meta` overline pinned to the foot. It is a page section — the site places it from a `{ _id: 'article-cards-section', props }` entry and binds `props` straight onto it — and it composes `section-module`, `section-title`, `registration-frame` and `card-grid`. `variant` places the heading only: `start` at the start (an article's closing row), `center` centred over the cards under an overline (a landing-page band); the cards keep the same shape in both.

- Import: `import ArticleCardsSection from '@aziontech/webkit/article-cards-section'`
- Category: section · Surface: site · Structure: monolithic

## When to use

- To close a blog post with its related articles, or a success story with its related stories.
- To show three stories as a landing-page band under a centred heading.

## Do

- Give a card either `image` or `mark`; the mark is drawn only when there is no cover.
- Keep the band to three cards so the frame row stays full.
- Set `target: '_blank'` on a card whose article lives on another site; it opens in a new tab and says so to assistive technology.

## Don’t

- For the full, filterable list of articles → use `article-index-section`.
- For one featured article selected from a wall → use `article-highlight-section`.

## Related

- `card-grid` — the frame grid the cards sit on.
- `article-highlight-section` — the blog's opening band.

## Props

| Prop | Type | Default | Required | Description |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `align` | `ArticleCardsSectionAlign` | `'start'` | false | Placement of the heading: `start` sets it at the start, `center` centres it over the cards. |
| `anchor` | `string` | `''` | false | Id set on the band so an in-page `#hash` link can target it. |
| `eyebrow` | `string` | `''` | false | Eyebrow above the heading. |
| `title` | `string` | `''` | false | Headline of the band; the band renders no header when empty. |
| `items` | `ArticleCardsSectionItem[]` | `[]` | false | The cards, in order; each is `{ id, href, title, description, meta, image?, mark?, target? }`; a card whose `target` is `_blank` opens in a new tab. |
| `ariaLabel` | `string` | `''` | false | Accessible name of the card grid. |

## States

- Visual states: `default`, `hover` (the card lifts to the raised surface and its media scales up), `focus-visible` (inset ring)
- `data-align` mirrors the `align` prop and places the heading
- Empty: when `items` is empty the band renders nothing — not the header, not the frame
- The header renders only when `title` is set
- `anchor` sets the band's `id`; a card whose `target` is `_blank` links with `rel="noopener"`
- A card title clamps to two lines (`line-clamp-2`); the clamp is visual only, so the full title stays in the accessibility tree, with no `title` attribute.

## Accessibility

- Visible focus: each card is one link with an inset `focus-visible` ring.
- Keyboard map: `Tab` moves card to card; `Enter` follows the link.
- ARIA: the band is a `<section>` named by its title (`aria-label`) when one is set; the grid is a `group` named by `ariaLabel` when set; the headline is the section title's `h2` and each card's headline an `h3`; a cover is decorative (`alt=""`), a client mark is named by the client; a `_blank` card appends the visually hidden `(opens in a new tab)` to its name.
- Contrast ≥4.5:1 (text) / ≥3:1 (large + icons): text uses `--text-default` / `--text-muted` on the canvas.
- `motion-reduce:transition-none` on every transition.
- Touch target ≥40×40 px — the whole card is the target.

## Examples

### Default

```vue
<script setup>
  import ArticleCardsSection from '@aziontech/webkit/article-cards-section'
  import placeholderImage from '@aziontech/webkit/assets/placeholder-image.svg'
</script>

<template>
  <ArticleCardsSection
    align="start"
    title="Related Articles"
    aria-label="Related Articles"
    :items="[
      {
        id: 'how-to-reduce-ai-agent-latency',
        href: '/blog/how-to-reduce-ai-agent-latency',
        title: 'How Do You Reduce AI Agent Latency?',
        description:
          'Learn where latency builds up in AI agents and how to reduce delays across inference, tools, memory, and state with distributed architecture, caching, and observability.',
        meta: 'SEP 23, 2026 • 8 min read',
        image: placeholderImage
      },
      {
        id: 'cloudflare-alternatives-and-competitors',
        href: '/blog/cloudflare-alternatives-and-competitors',
        title: 'Best Cloudflare Alternatives and Competitors',
        description:
          'Compare six Cloudflare alternatives for CDN delivery, security, and application hosting. Assess trade-offs, operating costs, and migration paths with Azion.',
        meta: 'SEP 22, 2026 • 13 min read',
        image: placeholderImage
      },
      {
        id: 'which-azion-plan-is-right-for-your-project',
        href: '/blog/which-azion-plan-is-right-for-your-project',
        title: 'Which Azion Plan Is Right for Your Project?',
        description:
          'Compare Azion’s Hobby, Pro, and Enterprise plans for personal projects, production applications, and operations that need customized terms.',
        meta: 'SEP 22, 2026 • 7 min read',
        image: placeholderImage
      }
    ]"
  />
</template>
```

<!-- components/article-highlight-section.md -->

# Article Highlight Section

> The band that opens a blog under its hero. One article is shown large — its cover beside its overline, headline, deck, read time and a button into it — over a wall of the highlight articles that acts as its selector. The wall is a tablist and the large article its panel; selecting a title on the wall swaps the panel with a short slide in the direction of travel, and every article is drawn invisibly under the panel so the band keeps the height of its tallest article and never jumps. With four or more articles the wall runs four across. It is a page section — the site places it from a `{ _id: 'article-highlight-section', props }` entry and binds `props` straight onto it — and it composes `section-module`, `registration-frame`, `card-grid` and `button`; the selection is local state. With `autoplay` the band advances on its own every `autoplayInterval`, drawing a primary progress line at the panel's foot, and pauses while the pointer is over it or keyboard focus is inside it; it never autoplays under reduced motion or with one article. With `fill`, from `lg` the band fills the first viewport under the sticky site header.

- Import: `import ArticleHighlightSection from '@aziontech/webkit/article-highlight-section'`
- Category: section · Surface: site · Structure: monolithic

## When to use

- To open a blog or a resource page with its newest or most important articles.

## Do

- Feature three to five articles; the wall sets one column per article from `lg`, so five sit in one row of five as on azion.com's blog.
- Give a `highlightImage` cropped for the panel when the cover does not read at that size.
- Bind `v-model` (the selected index) to open on an article other than the first; the band never advances on its own, so it holds whatever the reader or the page picked.

## Don’t

- For a closing row of related articles → use `article-cards-section`.
- For the full list of articles → use `article-index-section`.

## Related

- `article-cards-section` — the closing row of related articles.
- `card-grid` — the frame grid the wall of titles sits on.

## Props

| Prop | Type | Default | Required | Description |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `anchor` | `string` | `''` | false | In-page id set on the band so a `#hash` link can scroll to it. |
| `items` | `ArticleHighlightSectionItem[]` | `[]` | false | The highlight articles in wall order, the one at the bound index (the first by default) selected on load; each is `{ key, href, image, highlightImage?, meta, title, description, readTime }`. |
| `actionLabel` | `string` | `''` | false | Label of the button into the selected article; the button is omitted when empty. |
| `ariaLabel` | `string` | `''` | false | Accessible name of the wall of titles. |
| `autoplay` | `boolean` | `false` | false | Advances to the next article every `autoplayInterval`, with a visible pause control, pausing under hover and keyboard focus; off under reduced motion and with one article. |
| `autoplayInterval` | `number` | `6000` | false | Milliseconds each article is held before the band advances. |
| `fill` | `boolean` | `false` | false | From `lg` up, the band fills the first viewport under the sticky site header. |
| `modelValue` | `number` | `0` | false | Two-way value (`v-model`). |

## v-model

| Model | Type | Default | Emits | Notes |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `v-model` | `number` | `0` | `update:modelValue` | Index of the selected article; left unbound the band starts on the first one. A click or an arrow key writes the new index back; an index past the ends is clamped to the wall. |

## States

- Visual states: `default`, `hover` (a wall title lights, the panel cover scales up), `focus-visible` (inset ring), `selected` (`data-active` on the selected wall title)
- `data-columns` on the wall: one column per article from `lg`, three at least and five at most
- Empty: when `items` is empty the band renders nothing
- Without `autoplay` the band has no timer: the selected article changes only on a click, an arrow key or a bound `modelValue`
- Autoplay: with `autoplay`, more than one article and no reduced-motion preference, the band advances (wrapping to the first) every `autoplayInterval` ms after mount and writes the index back through `v-model`; `data-autoplay` is set while it runs; an outlined `IconButton` at the band's top end pauses and resumes it, and `data-paused` marks the band while the reader holds it; a progress line (`var(--primary)`, `aria-hidden`) grows along the panel's foot from the inline start; pointer hover over the band or a `:focus-visible` element inside it pauses it, and leaving resumes it; any selection restarts the count
- Direction: `data-direction` on the panel is `forward` or `back` (from the arrow key, the autoplay step, or the index change) and drives an out-in slide of the panel's article, mirrored under `rtl`
- Fill: `data-fill` is set; from `lg` the band's height is the viewport minus `var(--layout-header-height)` and the section gaps, the panel takes the free height and its cover drops its fixed aspect; under 50 rem of viewport height the copy tightens and the deck hides
- Reflow: the hidden layout sizer is `min-w-0`, so the panel never grows past a 320 px box.
- A wall title clamps to two lines (`line-clamp-2`); the clamp is visual only, so the full title stays the tab's accessible name, with no `title` attribute.

## Accessibility

- Autoplay (WCAG 2.2.2): off by default; when on, a visible pause control ("Pause rotation" / "Resume rotation") stops and resumes it for touch and screen-reader users, and it also pauses while the pointer is over the band or keyboard focus is inside it, never runs under `prefers-reduced-motion: reduce`, and the panel is not a live region, so an advance is never announced.
- Visible focus: the panel headline link and every wall title show an inset `focus-visible` ring.
- Keyboard map: on the wall, `ArrowRight` / `ArrowDown` select the next title, `ArrowLeft` / `ArrowUp` the previous one (wrapping), `Home` the first and `End` the last; selection follows focus and only the selected title is in the tab order (roving `tabindex`).
- ARIA: the wall is a `tablist` named by `ariaLabel`, each title a `tab` with `aria-selected` and `aria-controls`; the panel is a `tabpanel` labelled by the selected tab; the panel headline is an `h2` whose link covers the article; the invisible sizer is `aria-hidden` and `inert`; covers are decorative (`alt=""`). Ids come from `useId`.
- Contrast ≥4.5:1 (text) / ≥3:1 (large + icons): text uses `--text-default` / `--text-muted` on the canvas.
- `motion-reduce:transition-none` on every transition.
- Touch target ≥40×40 px — every wall title is a full-cell button.

## Examples

### Default

```vue

        description: 'Compare Azion’s Hobby, Pro, and Enterprise plans.',
        readTime: '7 min read'
      }
    ]"
  />
</template>
```

<!-- components/article-index-section.md -->

# Article Index Section

> Every article or resource of a blog or a resource hub as one ruled list, with the controls to narrow it on one row. `filters` picks the filter bar: `category-search` draws a category picker and a search field (the blog), `facets` draws one dropdown per facet — content type, category, industry — that wraps when it runs out of room (a resource hub). Each entry is a row — its cover on the left, and on the right its overline, headline, a deck and, when it has them, its writers with the byline; the row is one link named by its headline, and from `lg` up a read cue rises into its end on hover or keyboard focus. The filters combine; a facet dropdown with more than seven options gains an option search; a load-more button reveals the next page, and when nothing matches an empty state offers to clear every filter. It is a page section — the site places it from a `{ _id: 'article-index-section', props }` entry and binds `props` straight onto it — and it composes `section-module`, `registration-frame`, `toggle-group`, `select`, `input`, `avatar`, `empty-state` and `button`; the selected category, the search term, the facet selections, each dropdown's option search and the page count are local state. A row without a cover draws its client's mark in one flat ink that follows the theme.

- Import: `import ArticleIndexSection from '@aziontech/webkit/article-index-section'`
- Category: section · Surface: site · Structure: monolithic

## When to use

- Use ArticleIndexSection for the full filterable list of articles or resources; choose the filter bar with `filters`: `category-search` for the blog (category picker and search field), `facets` for a resource hub (one dropdown per facet).

## Do

- With `filters="category-search"`, list the articles newest first and file every article under at least one of `categories`; keep the category list short enough to scroll on one row.
- With `filters="facets"`, give every item a `facets` entry per facet `id` it can be filtered by (an item without one never matches a selection on that facet), and keep each facet's `options` to values some item carries.
- Set `emptyTitle` (and `emptyActionLabel`) so a reader who filters everything away gets a way back; without it the list just disappears.
- Set `leading` to the number of entries a highlight band above already shows, so they are not listed twice until the reader filters.
- Leave `rowVariant` unset to get the layout that fits the filter bar: `byline` for `category-search`, `center` for `facets`.

## Don’t

- For a card grid of customer stories with search and facets → use `story-index-section`.
- For a filterable list of non-article rows (jobs) → use `listing-index-section`.
- For a closing row of related articles → use `article-cards-section`.

## Related

- `article-highlight-section` — the band that opens the blog above this list.
- `story-index-section` — the card-grid library with search and facets.
- `listing-index-section` — the filterable list of job rows.

## Props

| Prop | Type | Default | Required | Description |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `anchor` | `string` | `''` | false | In-page id set on the band so a `#hash` link can scroll to it. |
| `title` | `string` | `''` | false | Headline beside the controls, rendered as the band's `h2`; it also names the band. |
| `filters` | `ArticleIndexSectionFilters` | `'category-search'` | false | Filter bar: `category-search` draws the category picker and the search field, `facets` draws one dropdown per facet. |
| `items` | `ArticleIndexSectionItem[]` | `[]` | false | The entries, in order; each is `{ id, href, image?, mark?, meta, title, description?, authors?, byline?, categories?, facets?, target? }`. |
| `categories` | `string[]` | `[]` | false | Segments of the category picker after the show-all segment (`category-search`); the picker is omitted when empty. |
| `allLabel` | `string` | `''` | false | Label of the first category segment, which shows every category (`category-search`). |
| `categoryAriaLabel` | `string` | `''` | false | Accessible name of the category picker (`category-search`). |
| `searchPlaceholder` | `string` | `''` | false | Placeholder of the search field (`category-search`); the field is omitted when this and `searchAriaLabel` are both empty. |
| `searchAriaLabel` | `string` | `''` | false | Accessible name of the search field (`category-search`). |
| `facets` | `ArticleIndexSectionFacet[]` | `[]` | false | The facet dropdowns, in order (`facets`); each is `{ id, label, allLabel, options }`. |
| `rowLabel` | `string` | `''` | false | Read cue revealed at the end of a row under hover or focus. |
| `moreLabel` | `string` | `''` | false | Label of the load-more button under the list; without it every match is listed and no button renders. |
| `pageSize` | `number` | `12` | false | Rows listed per page when `moreLabel` is set; the load-more button reveals the next page. |
| `leading` | `number` | `0` | false | Leading entries left out until a filter applies, because a band above already shows them. |
| `emptyTitle` | `string` | `''` | false | Headline of the state when nothing matches; the state renders only with it. |
| `emptyDescription` | `string` | `''` | false | Sentence under the empty-state headline. |
| `emptyActionLabel` | `string` | `''` | false | Label of the empty-state button that clears every filter; no button renders without it. |
| `rowVariant` | `ArticleIndexSectionRowVariant` | `undefined` | false | Row layout: `byline` pins the writers to the foot of the copy, `center` centres the copy; unset, `byline` for `category-search` and `center` for `facets`. |
| `categoryParam` | `string` | `''` | false | Name of a URL query parameter that preselects a category on load and scrolls the band into view (`category-search`); off when empty. |
| `ariaLabel` | `string` | `''` | false | Accessible name of the list. |

## States

- Visual states: `default`, `hover` (a row lights and its read cue rises in), `focus-visible` (inset ring on the row, the read cue rises in)
- `data-filters` on the list mirrors `filters`; `category-search` clamps the deck to two lines in `text-body-sm`, `facets` sets it in full in `text-body-lg`
- Filtered (`category-search`): the selected category keeps the entries whose `categories` hold it, the show-all segment keeps every one; the search matches the title and the description; both combine
- Filtered (`facets`): each facet keeps the entries whose `facets[id]` holds the selected value; its `allLabel` option keeps every one; the facets combine
- Option search (`facets`): a dropdown whose `options` number more than seven renders a search field above its options; shorter lists render none
- Paged: with `moreLabel` set, the first `pageSize` matches render and the load-more button, shown while more remain, adds the next `pageSize`; changing a filter returns to the first page
- Leading: until a filter applies, the first `leading` entries are skipped
- Empty: when no entry matches, the list and the load-more button are not rendered; with `emptyTitle` set, an `EmptyState` (search icon) renders `emptyTitle` and `emptyDescription`, and an `emptyActionLabel` button clears every filter
- `data-variant` on the list mirrors the row layout: `byline` spreads the copy so the byline sits at the foot, `center` centres it
- A row with no `image` draws its `mark` (the client's logo, flattened to one ink that inverts in the dark theme); an author with an `avatar` URL shows the photo, otherwise the initials
- A row whose `target` is `_blank` opens in a new tab (with `rel="noopener"`)
- `categoryParam` (`category-search`): on mount (client only) a query value that matches one of `categories` selects it and scrolls the band into view

## Accessibility

- Visible focus: the headline link draws an inset ring over the whole row; the picker, field, dropdowns and button own theirs.
- Keyboard map: `Tab` moves through the filter controls, each row and the load-more button; the category picker owns its arrow-key map and each dropdown owns its keyboard map.
- ARIA: the band is a `<section>` named by `title` (`aria-label`) when one is set, with the title as its `h2`; the toolbar is a `search` landmark; the category picker is a radio group named by `categoryAriaLabel`, the field named by `searchAriaLabel`, each dropdown trigger named by its facet `label`; the list is named by `ariaLabel`; each row is an `article` whose headline (`h3`) link covers it; the cover is decorative (`alt=""`), a client mark is named by the client, and the read cue `aria-hidden`; a `_blank` row appends the visually hidden `(opens in a new tab)` to its link; the empty state is a `status`; an option search with no match announces itself as a `status`.
- Contrast ≥4.5:1 (text) / ≥3:1 (large + icons): text uses `--text-default` / `--text-muted`.
- `motion-reduce:transition-none` on every transition.
- Touch target ≥40×40 px — every row is a full-row link.

## Examples

### Default

```vue
</script>

    anchor="posts"
    all-label="All articles"

    :categories="['Developers', 'Security', 'Company news', 'Serverless']"
    search-placeholder="Search articles"
    search-aria-label="Search articles"
    :items="[
```

<!-- components/capability-grid-section.md -->

# Capability Grid Section

> A framed grid of claims or links under an optional section title: each cell is a `FeatureItem` (optional glyph, heading, sentence) with optional bullet points, three or four to a row, and an item with an `href` turns its whole cell into a link to that page. It is the one marketing grid of claims or links: it replaces `'use-case-links'`, `'resource-grid'` and `'content-columns'`. From the design sample's site `CapabilityGridSection`. Use CapabilityGridSection for any grid of claims or links on a marketing page. It has three layouts, set by the items rather than a prop: benefits (every item has an `icon`), resources (every item has an `href` and no icon) and text columns (no icon, no link). Per-item links are optional; icons are all or none.

- Import: `import CapabilityGridSection from '@aziontech/webkit/capability-grid-section'`
- Category: section · Surface: site · Structure: monolithic

## When to use

- A product page listing what the product does, three or six at a time.
- Use cases or resources that each link to their own page, on a hub or landing page: give each item an `href` (and no `icon` for a resource list).
- A bare grid (no title) under a band that already has its heading.

## Do

- Three or six items at three columns, four or eight at four, so rows stay full.
- Keep points to three short lines.
- A benefits grid gives every item an `icon`; a resource list or text columns give none. A grid where only some items have an icon is a benefits grid with a gap: it logs a development warning, and the site-composition check (`test/standards/site-composition.test.mjs`) fails it in the shipped page JSON.
- Give either every item an `href` or none, so the grid reads as one kind of cell; do not combine `points` with an `href` (the whole cell is the link).
- Set `titleAlign: 'start'` for a band whose header sits at the start edge (the Partners page's "Why you should be a partner"); the grid keeps its title, so each capability stays an `h3`. Never wrap a bare grid in `SectionModule` to move the title: the capability headings rise to `h2` and the outline breaks.

## Don’t

- For capabilities with art — use `FeatureTilesSection` or `BentoGrid`.
- For use cases whose band title sits in the first grid cell with one action — use `CustomerUseCasesSection`.
- For the use cases of a product page — not on product pages; use `CustomerUseCasesSection` with exactly four items. The linked use-case grid belongs on hub and landing pages.
- For cells that each carry their own action or copy button — use `FeatureWidgetsSection`.

## Related

- `FeatureItem`, `CardGrid`, `RegistrationFrame`, `SectionTitle` — the parts.
- `FeatureTilesSection` — tiles with illustrations.

## Props

| Prop | Type | Default | Required | Description |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `eyebrow` | `string` | `''` | false | Eyebrow above the title. |
| `title` | `string` | `''` | false | Headline over the grid, rendered as the band `h2`; without it the grid is bare and each capability heading is an `h2`. |
| `description` | `string` | `''` | false | One or two sentences under the title. |
| `items` | `CapabilityGridSectionItem[]` | `[]` | false | The capabilities, in order; each is `{ title, description, icon?, points?, href? }`; an item with `href` links its whole cell to that page. The band renders nothing when empty. |
| `columns` | `CapabilityGridSectionColumns` | `3` | false | Capabilities per row from the large breakpoint up. |
| `primaryAction` | `CapabilityGridSectionAction` | `undefined` | false | First button under the section title, `secondary`; `{ label, href, trailing?, target? }`. Shown only with a label and a link. |
| `secondaryAction` | `CapabilityGridSectionAction` | `undefined` | false | Second button under the section title, `outline`; same shape. Shown only with a label and a link. |
| `anchor` | `string` | `''` | false | In-page id set on the band so a hash link can target it. |
| `titleAlign` | `SectionTitleAlign` | `'center'` | false | Layout of the section title: `center` stacks and centers it, `start` stacks it at the start edge, `split` sets the headline and its description in two columns. |

## States

- Renders nothing without items.
- Heading level drops to `h3` under a title and rises to `h2` without one.
- An item with `href` renders its heading as a link that covers the cell.
- Items that mix an `icon` with none log one development warning naming how many lack it; nothing changes in the render.
- `titleAlign` is passed to the section title as its `align`; it has no effect on a bare grid.

## Accessibility

- A `section` named by its title; headings nest under it.
- Points are a real list.
- Glyphs are decorative (from `FeatureItem`).
- A linked cell is one link named by its heading (from `FeatureItem`), with a visible focus ring.

## Examples

### Default

```vue
<script setup>
  import CapabilityGridSection from '@aziontech/webkit/capability-grid-section'

  const items = [
    {
      icon: 'az az-bolt',
      title: 'Functions',
      description: 'Run JavaScript and WebAssembly close to every user.'
    },
    {
      icon: 'az az-database',
      title: 'Object Storage',
      description: 'Keep files where they are read, with no egress to plan for.'
    },
    {
      icon: 'az az-shield',
      title: 'Firewall',
      description: 'Stop bots and attacks before they reach your origin.'
    }
  ]
</script>

<template>
  <CapabilityGridSection
    eyebrow="Platform"
    title="Everything an application needs, in one place"
    description="Compute, storage and security that deploy together."
    :items="items"
    :primary-action="{ label: 'Explore the platform', href: '/platform', trailing: true }"
  />
</template>
```

2 more example(s) in [components/capability-grid-section.md](components/capability-grid-section.md).

<!-- components/card-carousel-section.md -->

# Card Carousel Section

> The titled card-carousel-section band of a product or solution page: a section title on the start of the row with the previous/next controls at its end, over a horizontally scrolling track of standalone cards. Each card is an optional overline (the category), a heading, a sentence and, when it links somewhere, a mono link label closed by an arrow ("See example", "See solution"). The band answers "see how to use", "industries", "orchestration with control" on azion.com product pages, where the cases outnumber the row. It is a page section — the site places it from a `{ _id: 'card-carousel-section', props }` entry and binds `props` straight onto it — so its whole API is the props below. It composes `carousel` (`variant="cards"`, its `header` slot, `size="medium"` items and its step controls), `section-title` and `eyebrow`, and owns only the card's inner layout.

- Import: `import CardCarouselSection from '@aziontech/webkit/card-carousel-section'`
- Category: section · Surface: site · Structure: monolithic

## When to use

- Use CardCarouselSection for four or more marketing cards the reader steps through.
- Four or more cases, industries or examples of equal weight that do not all need to be seen: the reader steps through them.
- When each case is a short card (overline, heading, one or two sentences) that may link to its own page or example.

## Do

- Give the band a `title`; its arrows sit on the title row, and the title names the row for assistive technology.
- Keep each card to a heading and one or two sentences, so the row height stays even.
- Use `eyebrow` on every card or on none; a row where some cards carry an overline reads as ragged.
- Give every card a `linkLabel` when every card has an `href`, and none when none does. The label is a visual cue; the card heading is the link.
- Pass as many cards as the content has; the track scrolls, so the count never has to fill the row.

## Don’t

- When every case must be visible, or there are three or six of equal weight → use `capability-grid-section` (linked grid) or `customer-use-cases-section` (grid with one action).
- For a quote or testimonial row → use `client-quotes-section`.
- For numbered principles pinned while the page scrolls → use `process-scroll-section` with `pinned`.
- For articles with images → use `article-cards-section`.

## Related

- `carousel` — the primitive this band composes; reach for it directly only for a non-card row.
- `capability-grid-section` — the same content as a static, fully visible grid.
- `process-scroll-section` — numbered dark cards, pinned on scroll with `pinned`.

## Props

| Prop | Type | Default | Required | Description |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `anchor` | `string` | `''` | false | In-page id set on the band so a hash link can target it. |
| `eyebrow` | `string` | `''` | false | Eyebrow above the band title. |
| `title` | `string` | `''` | false | Headline of the band, rendered as its `h2` at the start of the row the step controls end; the band renders no title when empty. |
| `description` | `string` | `''` | false | Supporting sentence under the headline. |
| `items` | `CardCarouselSectionItem[]` | `[]` | false | The cards, in order; each is `{ eyebrow?, title, description, href?, linkLabel? }`, and a card with `href` links to it. |
| `ariaLabel` | `string` | `''` | false | Accessible name of the card row; defaults to the title. |

## States

- Visual states: `default`; each linked card has `hover` (the arrow nudges toward the end edge, the label brightens) and `focus-visible` (an inset ring on the whole card).
- Empty: with no `items` the band renders nothing — no title, no controls.
- Untitled: with no `title` the controls sit alone at the start of the row (the carousel's own layout) and the row is named by `ariaLabel` only.
- Unlinked card: without `href` the card is a plain card with no hover state and no link label.
- The step controls carry `disabled` at each end of the track, from the carousel; a row that fits shows both disabled.
- `loading`, `error`, `disabled`, `readonly`, `invalid`, `open`: the band cannot be in these states; it renders static content from props.

## Accessibility

- Pattern: the WAI-ARIA APG carousel (`aria-roledescription="carousel"`, named region) from `carousel`; the band root is a plain container so the row is the one named region, named by `ariaLabel` or the title.
- Outline: the title is the section title's `h2`; each card heading is an `h3`.
- Links: on a linked card the `h3` holds the anchor, so the link's name is the card title; a stretched `::after` makes the whole card its hit area. The link label and its arrow are `aria-hidden` decoration.
- Keyboard map: `Tab` reaches the previous control, the next control, the track (arrow keys scroll it natively) and each card link in order; `Enter` / `Space` on a control steps one card; `Enter` on a card link follows it. A card that scrolls into focus is brought into view by the browser.
- Visible focus: card links draw `focus-visible:after:ring-2 focus-visible:after:ring-inset focus-visible:after:ring-(--ring-color)` around the whole card; the controls and track carry the carousel's ring.
- Contrast ≥4.5:1: copy is `var(--text-muted)` on `var(--bg-surface)`; the link label is `var(--text-muted)` at rest; the arrow is `var(--text-primary)` (≥3:1 as a graphic).
- `motion-reduce:transition-none` on every transition; the track never auto-advances (WCAG 2.2.2 does not apply).
- Touch target ≥40×40 px: the controls are 40×40; a card link spans its whole card.

## Examples

### Default

```vue

    {
      eyebrow: 'E-commerce',
      title: 'E-commerce product image optimization',
      description:
        'Optimize product images for faster page loads and better conversion rates. Automatically deliver responsive images at the right size and format for each device.',
      href: '/docs/products/build/applications/image-processor',
      linkLabel: 'See example'
    },
    {
      eyebrow: 'Media',
      title: 'News and media site responsive images',
      description:
        'Deliver optimized images for news articles and media content that load instantly on any device. Automatic format conversion ensures the best experience.',
```

<!-- components/cli-terminal-section.md -->

# Cli Terminal Section

> The Azion CLI band of a product page: a horizontal section title with one outlined button, over a terminal on the pixelate texture that runs off the frame's bottom rule. The terminal is either a recorded Azion CLI session typed live by a `console-mockup` (`script`), in a fixed-height stage that rises in when it scrolls into view, or a static session whose lines enter one after another (`session`). It is a page section — the site places it from a `{ _id: 'cli-terminal-section', props }` entry and binds `props` straight onto it — and it composes `section-module`, `section-title`, `button`, `registration-frame`, `texture`, `console-mockup` and `code-block`.

- Import: `import CliTerminalSection from '@aziontech/webkit/cli-terminal-section'`
- Category: section · Surface: site · Structure: monolithic

## When to use

- Use CliTerminalSection only on marketing pages for a full-width terminal session.
- To close a product argument on how little it takes to ship from the terminal, like Functions and Workloads.

## Do

- Keep the session to a few commands and their output; comment the output lines so the copied text still runs.
- Name the copy button with `copyAriaLabel` so a screen reader says what it copies.
- Prefer `script` on a product page: `start` types the first-project session, `deploy` the deploy session. Use `session` for a command set the recordings do not cover.
- Set `anchor` when a hero button scrolls to this band.

## Don’t

- For a config file or handler beside the claim → use `feature-scroll-section` with a `code` step.
- For several capabilities, each with its own file → use `feature-scroll-section` with code items.

## Related

- `code-block` — the terminal session the band shows.
- `feature-scroll-section` — the walkthrough whose steps can each pin a code sample (`code`).

## Props

| Prop | Type | Default | Required | Description |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `anchor` | `string` | `''` | false | In-page id set on the band so a `#hash` link can scroll to it. |
| `eyebrow` | `string` | `''` | false | Eyebrow above the section title. |
| `title` | `string` | `''` | false | Headline of the band, rendered by the horizontal section title as its `h2`; the band renders no header when empty. |
| `description` | `string` | `''` | false | Supporting sentence beside the headline. |
| `script` | `CliTerminalSectionScript` | `undefined` | false | Recorded Azion CLI session the terminal types live: `start` or `deploy`; wins over `session`. |
| `session` | `string` | `''` | false | The terminal session shown under the header when no `script` is set, commands and their output verbatim, highlighted as bash; the band renders nothing when neither is set. |
| `copyAriaLabel` | `string` | `''` | false | Accessible name of the session's copy button; empty keeps the code block's own. |
| `primaryAction` | `SectionTitleAction` | `undefined` | false | Outlined button in the section title; `{ label, href, trailing?, target? }`. Omitted when absent. |

## States

- Visual states: `default`; the copy button and the button own their hover and focus states
- Empty: when neither `script` nor `session` is set the band renders nothing — not the header, not the frame
- `script` draws the `console-mockup` `cli-terminal-start` or `cli-terminal-deploy` in a stage 18rem tall (24rem from `md` up); `data-entered` is set on the stage the first time a fifth of it is on screen, and the stage rises, grows and fades in. The widget types its session while on screen and shows its finished state under reduced motion (owned by `console-mockup`). Without `script` the `session` renders as a `code-block`
- The header renders only when `title` is set

## Accessibility

- Visible focus: owned by `code-block` (copy button) and `button`; the `script` terminal has no focusable parts.
- Keyboard map: `Tab` reaches the button and the copy button; `Enter` follows the button.
- ARIA: the band is a `<section>` named by its title (`aria-label`) when one is set; the headline is the section title's `h2`; the copy button is named by `copyAriaLabel`; the `script` terminal is an `aria-hidden` picture of the console (owned by `console-mockup`), so the headline carries the meaning.
- Contrast ≥4.5:1 (text) / ≥3:1 (large + icons): owned by `section-title`, `code-block` and `button`.
- `motion-reduce:transition-none` — the `script` stage's entrance is skipped under reduced motion and the stage is drawn at rest; the session's line entrance is owned by `code-block` and draws at once under reduced motion.
- Touch target ≥40×40 px: the large button.

## Examples

### Default

```vue
<script setup>
  import CliTerminalSection from '@aziontech/webkit/cli-terminal-section'

  const session =
    'azion link --preset javascript --name auth-handler --auto\n# Project successfully configured\n\nazion deploy --auto\n# Upload completed successfully!\n# Your Application was deployed successfully\n# Domain: https://k7q2fmx4ze.map.azionedge.net\n\ncurl https://k7q2fmx4ze.map.azionedge.net/\n# Hello World'

  const primaryAction = {
    label: 'Install the CLI',
    href: 'https://www.azion.com/en/documentation/products/azion-cli/overview/',
    trailing: true
  }
</script>

<template>
  <CliTerminalSection
    eyebrow="Azion CLI"
    title="Deploy your first function in two commands"
    description="Link a project, deploy it, and curl the domain the CLI hands back. Builds run on your machine with --local, or on Azion."
    :session="session"
    copy-aria-label="Copy the deploy session"
    :primary-action="primaryAction"
  />
</template>
```

<!-- components/client-mosaic-section.md -->

# Client Mosaic Section

> The client mosaic: a framed bento grid of customer cells where a story cell carries a photograph, the client's mark, one line of its result and a "read the case study" link, and a mark cell carries only the client's linked logo on a chosen fill. With `stories` and `logos` the layout is derived from the data: the first story spans two columns and two rows, every other story one column and two rows, every mark one cell. With `cells` the page places story and mark cells in one ordered list, each with its own column and row span, so stories and marks interleave as on the home page. It is a page section — the site places it from a `{ _id: 'client-mosaic-section', props }` entry and binds `props` straight onto it — and it composes `section-module`, `registration-frame`, `bento-grid`, `logo` and `link`.

- Import: `import ClientMosaicSection from '@aziontech/webkit/client-mosaic-section'`
- Category: section · Surface: site · Structure: monolithic

## When to use

- On the home page, to show the best customer stories and a wall of marks in one band.

## Do

- Two stories and six marks fill the four-column grid exactly.
- Use `cells` when the order matters (a story between marks, a tall story at the end of a row): give each cell its `span` and `rows` (`'1'` or `'2'`) and make the spans add up to full rows of four.
- Pick `fill` and `logoTreatment` together: a `knockout` mark reads on `white` or `primary`, a `monochrome` one on `canvas` or `surface`. Left unset, a mark on `white` or `primary` takes `knockout` and every other mark `monochrome`.
- Set `stacked` on a cell whose mark is a stacked lockup (squarer than a wordmark) so it draws one step taller.
- A story without a `photo` sits on its fill; keep photos for the stories that have one.

## Don’t

- For three equal story columns → use `client-story-columns-section`.
- For a plain wall with one quote → use `logo-wall-section`.

## Related

- `bento-grid` — the grid the cells are laid in.
- `client-story-columns-section` — the three-column sibling with brand-coloured faces.
- `logo` — draws every client mark in the ink its fill needs.

## Props

| Prop | Type | Default | Required | Description |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `ariaLabel` | `string` | `''` | false | Accessible name of the grid. |
| `linkLabel` | `string` | `''` | false | Label of the link under every story. |
| `anchor` | `string` | `''` | false | In-page id set on the band so a hash link can target it. |
| `stories` | `ClientMosaicSectionStory[]` | `[]` | false | The story cells, in order; each is `{ logo, logoAlt, photo?, text, href, fill?, logoTreatment? }`. The first spans two columns and two rows, the rest one column and two rows. |
| `logos` | `ClientMosaicSectionLogo[]` | `[]` | false | The mark cells, in order; each is `{ logo, logoAlt, href, fill?, logoTreatment? }` where `fill` is `'canvas'`, `'surface'`, `'white'` or `'primary'` and `logoTreatment` is `'color'`, `'monochrome'` or `'knockout'` (the shared `LogoTreatment`). |
| `cells` | `ClientMosaicSectionCell[]` | `[]` | false | The cells in placement order, story and mark cells interleaved; each is `{ logo, logoAlt, href, text?, photo?, span?, rows?, fill?, logoTreatment?, stacked? }` where a cell with `text` is a story cell, `span` and `rows` are `'1'` (default) or `'2'`, and `fill` defaults to `canvas`. When set it replaces `stories` and `logos`. |

## States

- Visual states: `default`, `hover` and `focus-visible` on each cell
- `data-cell` (`story` or `logo`), `data-span` and `data-rows` on every grid cell; `data-fill` on every cell face drives its fill; every mark is a `Logo` with `data-treatment` and `data-variant` for its ink and `data-stacked` for a stacked lockup; `data-lead` sets the larger text of the first story (from `stories`) or of a story spanning two columns (from `cells`)
- Empty: when `cells`, `stories` and `logos` are all empty the band renders nothing
- Not applicable: `loading`, `error`, `disabled`, `readonly`, `invalid`, `open`/`closed`

## Accessibility

- Visible focus: every mark cell is a link with `focus-visible:ring-2 focus-visible:ring-(--ring-color) focus-visible:ring-inset`; the story link is a `link` with its own ring.
- Keyboard map: `Tab` moves between mark links and story links; `Enter` follows.
- ARIA: the band is a `<section>` named by `ariaLabel`; the photo of a story is decorative (`alt=""`, `aria-hidden`); every mark is a `Logo` (`role="img"`) named by `logoAlt`; the story text is a paragraph and its link is named by `linkLabel`.
- Contrast ≥4.5:1 (text) / ≥3:1 (large + icons): story text `var(--text-default)` over the canvas gradient; marks are inked to their fill through `logoTreatment`.
- `motion-reduce:transition-none` on the fill transition.
- Touch target ≥40×40 px — each mark cell is a full grid cell.

## Examples

### Default

<!-- components/client-quotes-section.md -->

# Client Quotes Section

> The client-stories band: a framed `quote-tabs` carousel of customer quotes, each signed by its author and marked by the client's logo, with one call to action under it. It is a page section — the site places it from a `{ _id: 'client-quotes-section', props }` entry and binds `props` straight onto it — and it composes `section-module`, `registration-frame`, `quote-tabs` and `button`.

- Import: `import ClientQuotesSection from '@aziontech/webkit/client-quotes-section'`
- Category: section · Surface: site · Structure: monolithic

## When to use

- Rendering page JSON, or you need a whole band with no custom content → the section (`client-quotes-section`). Building an SFC by hand, needing slots, or nesting inside another band → the primitive (`quote-tabs`).
- Use ClientQuotesSection for a band of customer quotes the reader browses by client.
- To let several customers speak in turn, one at a time, near the end of a page's argument.

## Do

- Three to six items; the tabs need room for each client's mark.
- Give each item either a registry `mark` or a `logo` URL with `clientName`, not both.
- On azion.com the client wall is in each brand's colours, not one ink: give each client its logo file from the package, `import gpa from '@aziontech/webkit/assets/gpa-extended-color.svg'`, then `logo: gpa`. On the dark site use `-extended-color.svg` where the colour logo reads on black (GPA, Zoop, HeroSpark, Magalu) and `-extended-reversed.svg` where the colour logo has dark lettering (Axur, Contabilizei, Dafiti, Global Fashion Group as `gfg`); on a light page use `-extended-color.svg`. A registry `mark` (one ink, follows the theme) is for a client that ships no logo file. `catalog.json` lists every file under `imports` with `"kind": "asset"`.
- Every item needs its quotation (`text`): a card is a tab that features it, and an item without one is skipped. Do not invent a quotation to fill the wall; list the clients you have quotes for, or use `logo-wall-section` for one quotation beside a logo wall.
- The band advances on a timer like azion.com's. Set `:autoplay="false"` when the page must hold one quote (a still capture, a page with other moving content), and bind `v-model` (the selected index) to open on a quote other than the first; never wrap or re-time the band yourself.

## Don’t

- For one quote beside a logo wall → use `logo-wall-section`.
- For a scrolling strip of analyst recognitions → use `recognition-marquee-section`.

## Related

- `quote-tabs` — the tabbed quote carousel this band frames.

## Props

| Prop | Type | Default | Required | Description |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `anchor` | `string` | `''` | false | In-page id set on the band so a hash link can target it. |
| `ariaLabel` | `string` | `''` | false | Accessible name of the quote carousel. |
| `items` | `ClientQuotesSectionItem[]` | `[]` | false | The quotes, in order; each is `{ text, name?, jobTitle?, mark?, logo?, clientName?, photo? }` where `mark` is a registry mark name and `logo` a mark URL named by `clientName`. |
| `primaryAction` | `SectionTitleAction` | `undefined` | false | Button under the quotes; `{ label, href, trailing? }`. Omitted when absent. |
| `autoplay` | `boolean` | `true` | false | Advances to the next quote on a timer, as `quote-tabs` does, with its visible pause control; pauses under the pointer or keyboard focus and never runs under reduced motion. |
| `autoplayInterval` | `number` | `5000` | false | Milliseconds each quote is held before the band advances. |
| `modelValue` | `number` | `0` | false | Two-way value (`v-model`). |

## Assets

- Brand marks: every valid name is in brand-marks.md (MCP `list_assets({ kind: "brandMarks" })`). An unknown name is not drawn as a mark: `Marquee` prints it as a typographic wordmark, the quote components show no mark.

## v-model

| Model | Type | Default | Emits | Notes |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `v-model` | `number` | `0` | `update:modelValue` | Index of the selected quote, passed through to `quote-tabs`; left unbound the band starts on the first quote. A click, an arrow key or the timer writes the new index back. |

## States

- Visual states: `default`
- Empty: when no item carries `text` the band renders nothing; an item without `text` is skipped, and the `v-model` index counts the quotes that remain
- The active quote, its progress and the tab keyboard model belong to `quote-tabs`; `autoplay`, `autoplayInterval` and `v-model` are passed straight through to it

## Accessibility

- Visible focus: owned by the composed components.
- Keyboard map: `Tab` reaches each link or button; `Enter` follows it.
- Contrast ≥4.5:1 (text) / ≥3:1 (large + icons): owned by the composed components.
- Touch target ≥40×40 px — every affordance is a large `button` or spans its cell.
- ARIA: the band is a plain `div`; the carousel is `quote-tabs`' own labelled region (`ariaLabel`), so no second landmark is added.
- `motion-reduce:transition-none motion-reduce:transform-none` — the tab transition belongs to `quote-tabs`; this band adds no motion.

## Examples

### Default

<!-- components/client-story-columns-section.md -->

# Client Story Columns Section

> Three featured success stories side by side: each column is a client's mark on a face painted in the client's own brand colour, over a one-line story that links to the case. With `reveal="hover"` each column is one card instead: the client's reversed logo on its brand face, which opens while hovered or focused to reveal the story line and a "Read story" cue, the whole card linking to the case; below the large breakpoint those cards stack and stay open. It is a page section — the site places it from a `{ _id: 'client-story-columns-section', props }` entry and binds `props` straight onto it — and it composes `section-module`, `registration-frame` and `logo-wall`. The retired `'customer-spotlight'` `_id` resolves here with `reveal: 'hover'`.

- Import: `import ClientStoryColumnsSection from '@aziontech/webkit/client-story-columns-section'`
- Category: section · Surface: site · Structure: monolithic

## When to use

- Use ClientStoryColumnsSection for three or four client cards in brand colours linking to case studies; `reveal="hover"` shows the story on hover.

## Do

- Three cells for `reveal="always"` (the grid is three columns from the large breakpoint); three or four for `reveal="hover"`, which shares the row between them.
- With `reveal="hover"` use the client's reversed (white) logo and one sentence per story; `logoWidth` sizes the mark as a share of the card (default `35%`).
- Leave `brand` out for a client the client registry knows (`clientBrand(name)` from `@aziontech/webkit/assets/client-registry`): the face reads its colours, and its ink, by `name`. Add a missing client to the registry rather than writing its hex colours into the page.
- Pass `brand` only for a one-off client the registry does not carry: `base` and `glow` may be any CSS colour, and `ink` is `'dark'` when the face is light enough that the link text must be dark. A cell with neither paints a black face.

## Don’t

- For a bento of client stories with photos and logos → use `client-mosaic-section`.
- For a quotation → use `client-quotes-section` or `logo-wall-section` `variant="quote"`.

## Related

- `client-mosaic-section` — the bento sibling.
- `logo-wall` — the single-mark wall each column's face is rendered with.

## Props

| Prop | Type | Default | Required | Description |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `anchor` | `string` | `''` | false | In-page id set on the band so a hash link can target it. |
| `ariaLabel` | `string` | `''` | false | Accessible name of the list of stories. |
| `linkLabel` | `string` | `''` | false | Label the mark face reveals on hover and the link's accessible name. |
| `cells` | `ClientStoryColumnsSectionCell[]` | `[]` | false | The stories, in order; each is `{ name, logo, href, story, brand?, logoTreatment?, logoWidth? }` where `brand` is `{ base, glow, ink? }` (CSS colours painting the face, `ink` `'dark'` or `'light'`) and defaults to the client registry's colours for `name`, `logoTreatment` is `'color'`, `'monochrome'` or `'knockout'` (the shared `LogoTreatment`), and `logoWidth` (read by `reveal="hover"`) sizes the mark as a share of the card. |
| `reveal` | `ClientStoryColumnsSectionReveal` | `'always'` | false | When the story shows: `always` under each face, or `hover`, where each card opens to reveal it while hovered or focused and stays open below the large breakpoint. |

## States

- Visual states: `default`, `hover` on each column (the face reveals the link line, the story fill rises)
- The face colours are bound as the `--wall-face` and `--wall-ink` custom properties from `brand`, or from the client registry by `name` when `brand` is left out; `data-treatment` on every mark drives its ink
- `reveal="hover"`: each list item carries `data-state=open|closed`; one card is open at a time on large screens, every card is open below the large breakpoint
- Empty: when `cells` is empty the band renders nothing

## Accessibility

- Visible focus: the mark link is `logo-wall`'s, with its ring; the story panel is a duplicate link hidden from the tab order and from assistive technology.
- Keyboard map: `Tab` moves between the three mark links; `Enter` follows.
- ARIA: the band is a `<section>`; the grid is a list named by `ariaLabel` whose items are the columns; each mark carries the client `name` as `alt`; the story panel is `aria-hidden` with `tabindex="-1"` so the case is reachable once.
- Contrast ≥4.5:1 (text) / ≥3:1 (large + icons): the link line ink follows `brand.ink`; the story text is `var(--text-default)` on `var(--bg-canvas)`.
- `motion-reduce:before:transition-none` on the hover fill.
- Touch target ≥40×40 px — each mark face spans its column.
- `reveal="hover"`: the list carries the one accessible name (`ariaLabel`, else the built-in "Customer stories"); each card is one link named "<link label>, <client>: <story>", so its logo is decorative (`alt=""`); focus opens the card the same way hover does and the ring is drawn inside the card; `az-arrow-up-right` mirrors under right-to-left.

## Examples

### Default

```vue
<script setup>
  import dafitiExtendedReversed from '@aziontech/webkit/assets/dafiti-extended-reversed.svg'
  import madeiramadeiraExtendedReversed from '@aziontech/webkit/assets/madeiramadeira-extended-reversed.svg'
  import magaluExtendedColor from '@aziontech/webkit/assets/magalu-extended-color.svg'
  import ClientStoryColumnsSection from '@aziontech/webkit/client-story-columns-section'
</script>

<template>
  <ClientStoryColumnsSection
    aria-label="Featured success stories"
    link-label="Read story"
    :cells="[
      {
        name: 'Magalu',
        logo: magaluExtendedColor,
        href: 'https://www.azion.com/en/success-case/magalu/',
        story:
          'Explore how Magalu enhances cybersecurity with Azion\'s Firewall and WAF, improving threat intelligence and bot management in retail.',
        logoTreatment: 'monochrome'
      },
      {
        name: 'Dafiti',
        logo: dafitiExtendedReversed,
        href: 'https://www.azion.com/en/success-case/dafiti/dafiti-accelerates-its-e-commerce-by-86-and-saves-45-on-data-transfer-costs-using-azion-edge-application/',
```

<!-- components/closing-call-to-action-section.md -->

# Closing Call To Action Section

> The last ask at the end of a landing page, followed by a hatched closing frame with no rules of its own, because the site footer under it opens with one. It is a page section — the site places it from a `{ _id: 'closing-call-to-action-section', props }` entry and binds `props` straight onto it — and it composes `section-module`, `call-to-action`, `button`, `registration-frame` and `texture`. `variant` picks the layout only: `panel` (the default) sets the copy and its actions on one surface, over a short closing frame; `primary` is the loud close, one centred ask on the canvas under a pixelated texture whose waves keep travelling, over a tall closing frame. Both take the same copy, the same two actions and an optional copyable shell `command` set before the actions. `frame` draws the closing frame alone, with no ask: the close of a page whose last band already carries its own action. `hatch` turns the closing frame off when another closing band stacks directly below and draws the shared one (`site-page` sets it for stacked closings). In `primary`, `audiences` turns the ask into a switch between ways in (a person or an agent): each audience swaps in its own copyable line and actions, and a typed line (`typedLead` + `typedWords`) loops under them; the switch block loads on demand, only when `audiences` is set.

- Import: `import ClosingCallToActionSection from '@aziontech/webkit/closing-call-to-action-section'`
- Category: section · Surface: site · Structure: monolithic

## When to use

- Use ClosingCallToActionSection for the last band before the site footer that asks for one action, with links or a command.
- Rendering page JSON, or you need a whole band with no custom content → this section. Building an SFC by hand, needing slots, or nesting inside another band → the `call-to-action` primitive.
- In `primary` with `audiences`, to close a page whose reader may be a person or an agent, each with its own way in.
- To close a solution or product page on its one or two asks, or a page such as Careers on one action (`panel`), or a page that ends on its one loud ask (`primary`).

## Do

- Place it last on the page, right above `site-footer-section`, after a hatched `section-gap`. It draws the hatched closing frame itself, so nothing follows it inside the page column: no trailing `SectionGap` and no closing `RegistrationFrame` + `Texture` (the `shell/site` close is for a page that ends on any other band). `webkit/no-gap-after-closing-section` flags the doubled close.
- Set `anchor: 'contact'` so the page's contact links scroll to the close.
- Use `variant: 'frame'` (no copy) to close a page that ends on a band with its own actions (a listing, a legal page); `site-page` draws no gap before it.
- Turn `hatch` off only on a closing band that another closing band follows directly; the last one keeps the frame.
- Give `command` a short shell line (`npm create azion@latest`); it is copied as written and reads as a command, with the `$` prompt.
- Give `audiences` two or more entries so the switch renders; set `audiencesAriaLabel` and `lineAriaLabel` so the switch and the copyable field are named. With `audiences` set, `description` is the closing sentence under the typed line, and `command` and the two actions are not drawn.
- Mark a command line with `copy.shell` so it draws the `$` prompt; leave a prompt meant for an agent bare.
- Keep `title` to one short line; `primary` does not draw `eyebrow`.

## Don’t

- For a newsletter sign-up close → use `newsletter-section`.
- For a close that lists deployable templates → use `template-gallery-section` with `closing`.
- For an ask in the middle of a page → compose `call-to-action` directly.

## Related

- `call-to-action` — the framed band this section renders.
- `newsletter-section` — the closing band whose ask is a subscribe form.
- `template-gallery-section` — with `closing`, the closing band that lists deployable templates.

## Props

| Prop | Type | Default | Required | Description |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `variant` | `ClosingCallToActionSectionVariant` | `'panel'` | false | Layout of the band: `panel` is one surface over a short closing frame, `primary` a centred ask under a pixelated texture over a tall closing frame, `frame` the tall closing frame alone. |
| `anchor` | `string` | `''` | false | In-page id set on the band so a `#hash` link can scroll to it. |
| `eyebrow` | `string` | `''` | false | Short overline above the headline; not drawn in `primary`. |
| `title` | `string` | `''` | false | Headline of the band, rendered by the call to action as its `h2`; the band renders nothing when empty. |
| `description` | `string` | `''` | false | Supporting sentence under the headline. |
| `primaryAction` | `SectionTitleAction` | `undefined` | false | The white button of the band, under the headline; `{ label, href, trailing? }` where `trailing` adds the chevron. Omitted when absent. |
| `secondaryAction` | `SectionTitleAction` | `undefined` | false | The outlined button of the band, beside the primary one; `{ label, href, trailing? }`. Omitted when absent. |
| `command` | `string` | `''` | false | Shell command on a copyable line before the actions, copied as written; drawn in `panel` and `primary`. |
| `hatch` | `boolean` | `true` | false | Draws the hatched closing frame under the band; turn it off when another closing band stacks directly below and draws the shared frame. |
| `audiences` | `ClosingCallToActionSectionAudience[]` | `[]` | false | The ways in of the `primary` close, in switch order; each is `{ value, label, copy?, actions? }` (`copy` is `{ label, text, shell? }`, `shell` drawing the `$` prompt; an action is `{ label, href, variant?, trailing? }`) and the first one opens. Replaces the command and the two actions; the switch renders only with two or more. |
| `typedLead` | `string` | `''` | false | Fixed opening of the typed line under the audiences, before the typed word. |
| `typedWords` | `string[]` | `[]` | false | Words typed, held, erased and typed again in order after `typedLead`; the typed line renders only with `audiences` and words. |
| `audiencesAriaLabel` | `string` | `''` | false | Accessible name of the audience switch. |
| `lineAriaLabel` | `string` | `''` | false | Accessible name of the read-only field that shows an audience's copyable line. |

## States

- Visual states: `default`
- `data-variant` mirrors the `variant` prop and drives the call-to-action layout and the closing frame height
- `primary`: the call to action takes its `primary` register with a pixelated `texture` in its `background` slot; its waves stop under reduced motion (owned by `texture`)
- `frame`: only the tall closing frame renders, whatever the copy; `hatch` does not apply
- `hatch` off: the band renders without its closing frame
- Empty: when `title` is empty the band renders nothing but its root (except in `frame`)
- Each action and the command line render only when given
- `audiences` (in `primary`): picking an audience swaps the copyable line and the actions; the swapped row sits in a polite live region and fades in. After mount the typed words are typed (90 ms a character), held (1.8 s), erased (45 ms a character) and the next one typed (after 270 ms), in a loop; the timer is cleared on scope dispose. Under reduced motion every word shows at once, joined by commas, and the cursor is hidden

## Accessibility

- Visible focus: owned by the composed buttons.
- Keyboard map: `Tab` reaches the command line and its copy button, then the primary and the secondary action; `Enter` follows an action. With `audiences`, `Tab` reaches the switch (arrow keys move it, owned by `toggle-group`), the copy button and each action.
- With `audiences`: the switch is a `radiogroup` named by `audiencesAriaLabel`; the swapped row is `aria-live="polite"`; the read-only field is named by `lineAriaLabel` and the copy button by the audience's `copy.label`. The typed line reads its whole sentence from a visually hidden copy while the animated letters are `aria-hidden`.
- ARIA: the `call-to-action` renders the band's `<section>` named by its title; the root is a plain `div` so the band has one landmark. The closing frame is decorative.
- Contrast ≥4.5:1 (text) / ≥3:1 (large + icons): owned by `call-to-action` and `button`.
- `motion-reduce:transition-none motion-reduce:transform-none` — see the Motion table; under reduced motion nothing types or blinks, and the texture and the buttons carry their own fallback.
- Touch target ≥40×40 px — the actions are large buttons.

## Examples

### Default

```vue
<script setup>
  import ClosingCallToActionSection from '@aziontech/webkit/closing-call-to-action-section'

  const primaryAction = {
    label: 'Start for free',
    href: 'https://console.azion.com/signup?tck=build_cta'
  }

  const secondaryAction = {
    label: 'Talk to sales',
    href: 'https://www.azion.com/en/contact/',
    trailing: true
  }
</script>

<template>
  <ClosingCallToActionSection
    variant="panel"
    anchor="contact"
    eyebrow="Build"
    title="Build once. Run everywhere."
    description="Get a faster path to launch, lower latency, and less infrastructure overhead."
    :primary-action="primaryAction"
    :secondary-action="secondaryAction"
  />
</template>
```

2 more example(s) in [components/closing-call-to-action-section.md](components/closing-call-to-action-section.md).

<!-- components/competitor-comparison-section.md -->

# Competitor Comparison Section

> The capability-by-capability comparison of Azion with one competitor on a migration landing page: a left section title over a framed table whose header row (the capability column, the Azion brand and the competitor logo) stays pinned while the page scrolls. Each category opens with a heading row; each cell is a support glyph — full, partial or none — named by a tooltip and by screen-reader text, and a capability with a definition carries an info button that opens it in a popover. It is a page section — the site places it from a `{ _id: 'competitor-comparison-section', props }` entry and binds `props` straight onto it — and it composes `section-module`, `section-title`, `registration-frame`, `brand`, `popover`, `icon-button` and `tooltip`.

- Import: `import CompetitorComparisonSection from '@aziontech/webkit/competitor-comparison-section'`
- Category: section · Surface: site · Structure: monolithic

## When to use

- Use CompetitorComparisonSection to compare Azion with one competitor, on an "Azion vs X" page, across a few categories of capabilities.

## Do

- Give every row a `definition`; the info popover is where a reader learns what the capability means.
- Pass the competitor's entry from `@aziontech/webkit/assets/competitor-registry` (`competitor('Akamai')`): its `logo` and `logoLight` render on first paint, server render included.
- With only a `name`, the band looks the name up in the competitor registry once the page is interactive (the registry loads on demand, outside the band's bundle) and shows the name as text until then, or for a platform the registry does not hold.
- For a platform outside the registry, ship both `competitor.logo` (dark theme) and `competitor.logoLight` (light theme), or one file with its `artwork`.
- Set `anchor` when a page links to the table (`#compare`).
- Keep `caption` equal to the title; it is the table's accessible name.

## Don’t

- To compare Azion's own plans or support tiers → use `plan-comparison-section`.
- For price cards with a billing-period toggle → use `pricing-plans-section`.

## Related

- `plan-comparison-section` — the plan-by-plan matrix of Pricing.
- `tooltip` — names each support glyph on hover and focus.
- `logo` — draws the competitor's mark right on both themes.

## Props

| Prop | Type | Default | Required | Description |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `anchor` | `string` | `''` | false | In-page id set on the band so a hash link can target it. |
| `eyebrow` | `string` | `''` | false | Eyebrow above the section title. |
| `title` | `string` | `''` | false | Headline of the band, rendered by the section title as its `h2` and naming the band; no header renders when empty. |
| `caption` | `string` | `''` | false | Screen-reader caption naming the table. |
| `columnLabel` | `string` | `''` | false | Heading of the capability column. |
| `fullLabel` | `string` | `''` | false | Tooltip and screen-reader text of a full-support cell. |
| `partialLabel` | `string` | `''` | false | Tooltip and screen-reader text of a partial-support cell. |
| `noneLabel` | `string` | `''` | false | Tooltip and screen-reader text of an unsupported cell. |
| `competitor` | `CompetitorComparisonSectionCompetitor` | `—` | true | The platform compared with Azion; `{ name, logo?, logoLight?, artwork? }` where `logo` is the dark-theme file and `logoLight` the light-theme one. With only a `name`, the logo is taken from the competitor registry once the page is interactive, and the name is the column head until then or when the registry has no entry. |
| `groups` | `CompetitorComparisonSectionGroup[]` | `[]` | false | The categories, in order; each is `{ title, rows }` and a row is `{ capability, definition?, azion?, competitor? }` with each level `full`, `partial` or `none` (missing reads as `none`). |

## States

- Visual states: `default`
- A support cell is one of `full`, `partial` or `none`, mirrored on the glyph's `data-support`
- The competitor head is a `Logo` (`data-testid="competitor-comparison-section__competitor-logo"`) whose `data-route` is `pair` (light/dark files), `single` (one file) or `wordmark` (the name as text: no logo and no registry entry, or before the registry loads)
- Not applicable: `loading`, `error`, `disabled`, `readonly`, `invalid`, `open`/`closed` (the popovers own their open state)
- Empty: when `groups` is empty the band renders nothing but its root — no header, no table

## Accessibility

- Visible focus: owned by the composed `icon-button` and `tooltip` triggers.
- Keyboard map: `Tab` reaches each definition's info button; `Enter` / `Space` opens its popover and `Esc` closes it (owned by `popover`).
- ARIA: the band is a `<section>` named by its title (`aria-label`) when one is set; the table carries a `caption`, `scope="col"` heads, a `scope="colgroup"` head per category and a `scope="row"` head per capability, so each value is announced with its row and column. The glyphs are `aria-hidden` and every cell carries its level as `sr-only` text; the competitor logo is a `Logo` named by the platform, inside a head cell that carries the same name.
- Contrast ≥4.5:1 (text) / ≥3:1 (large + icons): text and glyph colours are the tokens above.
- `motion-reduce:transition-none motion-reduce:transform-none` — the popover and tooltip carry their own reduced-motion fallback; this band adds no motion.
- Touch target ≥40×40 px — the info button is a small `icon-button`, a secondary affordance next to the capability name.

## Examples

### Default

<!-- components/compliance-logos-section.md -->

# Compliance Logos Section

> The compliance band: a framed media split whose copy states the certifications and whose media is a frame grid of badges, each a picture over a check-marked pill, closed by a "see more" cell. It is a page section — the site places it from a `{ _id: 'compliance-logos-section', props }` entry and binds `props` straight onto it — and it composes `section-module`, `media-split` and `card-grid`.

- Import: `import ComplianceLogosSection from '@aziontech/webkit/compliance-logos-section'`
- Category: section · Surface: site · Structure: monolithic

## When to use

- On security, enterprise or pricing pages, to list the certifications and link to the compliance page.

## Do

- Four or five certifications plus the link cell fill two rows of three.
- Give every badge an `alt` naming the certification; the pill repeats it for sighted readers.

## Don’t

- For client logos → use `logo-wall-section` or `client-mosaic-section`.

## Related

- `media-split` — the copy-beside-media band this section renders through.

## Props

| Prop | Type | Default | Required | Description |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `eyebrow` | `string` | `''` | false | Eyebrow above the headline. |
| `title` | `string` | `''` | false | Headline of the band, rendered by the media split as its `h2`; the band renders nothing when empty. |
| `description` | `string` | `''` | false | Supporting sentence under the headline. |
| `ariaLabel` | `string` | `''` | false | Accessible name of the badge list. |
| `certifications` | `ComplianceLogosSectionCertification[]` | `[]` | false | The badges, in order; each is `{ label, badge, alt }` where `badge` is the picture URL and `label` the pill text. |
| `primaryAction` | `SectionTitleAction` | `undefined` | false | The closing "see more" cell; `{ label, href }`. Omitted when absent. |
| `anchor` | `string` | `''` | false | In-page id set on the band so a `#hash` link can scroll to it. |

## States

- Visual states: `default`, `hover` and `focus-visible` on the see-more cell
- Empty: when `title` is empty the band renders nothing; when `certifications` is empty the media holds only the see-more cell, if any

## Accessibility

- Visible focus: the see-more cell carries `focus-visible:ring-2 focus-visible:ring-(--ring-color) focus-visible:ring-inset`.
- Keyboard map: `Tab` reaches the see-more link; `Enter` follows it.
- ARIA: the band is a plain `div`; the named landmark is `media-split`'s own section (labelled by `title`), so no second landmark is added; the badge grid is a list (`role="list"` named by `ariaLabel`) of list items; each badge image carries its `alt`; the check bubble and chevron are `aria-hidden`.
- Contrast ≥4.5:1 (text) / ≥3:1 (large + icons): pill `var(--text-default)` on `var(--bg-surface-raised)`; see-more `var(--text-muted)` rising to `var(--text-default)` on hover.
- `motion-reduce:transition-none motion-reduce:transform-none` on both transitions above.
- Touch target ≥40×40 px — the see-more cell is a full grid cell.

## Examples

### Default

```vue
<script setup>
  import ccpaSymbolColor from '@aziontech/webkit/assets/ccpa-symbol-color.svg'
  import gdprSymbolColor from '@aziontech/webkit/assets/gdpr-symbol-color.svg'
  import lgpdSymbolColor from '@aziontech/webkit/assets/lgpd-symbol-color.svg'
  import pciDssSymbolColor from '@aziontech/webkit/assets/pci-dss-symbol-color.svg'
  import socSymbolColor from '@aziontech/webkit/assets/soc-symbol-color.svg'
  import ComplianceLogosSection from '@aziontech/webkit/compliance-logos-section'
</script>

<template>
  <ComplianceLogosSection
    eyebrow="Compliance"
    title="Meet your compliance requirements"
    description="Azion's certifications help customers and global partners meet a wide range of compliance requirements."
    aria-label="Compliance certifications"
    :certifications="[
      {
        label: 'SOC 2 & 3',
        badge: socSymbolColor,
        alt: 'AICPA SOC 2 Type 2 and SOC 3 badge'
      },
      {
        label: 'PCI DSS',
        badge: pciDssSymbolColor,
        alt: 'PCI DSS badge'
      },
      {
        label: 'LGPD',
        badge: lgpdSymbolColor,
        alt: 'LGPD badge'
      },
      {
        label: 'GDPR',
        badge: gdprSymbolColor,
        alt: 'GDPR badge'
      },
      {
        label: 'CCPA',
        badge: ccpaSymbolColor,
        alt: 'CCPA badge'
      }
    ]"
    :primary-action="{ label: 'See more', href: '/compliance' }"
  />
</template>
```
