<!-- components/console-showcase-section.md -->

# Console Showcase Section

> A full-width framed panel that presents a product scene (a console mock or a diagram) under a section title, on a texture, with optional tabs that each play one beat of the scene. The scene is a slot: the page passes a `ConsoleMockup` or a diagram and reads the selected tab from the slot scope. In `console` variant the scene keeps a 1.9:1 aspect and can be cropped and faded at the bottom. From the design sample's site `ConsoleShowcaseSection`.

- Import: `import ConsoleShowcaseSection from '@aziontech/webkit/console-showcase-section'`
- Category: section · Surface: site · Structure: monolithic

## When to use

- Use ConsoleShowcaseSection for a marketing band that plays a product scene under a section title.
- Showing the product itself on a landing page.
- A tour of three or four views of one screen.

## Do

- Two to four tabs, each a different view, labelled with one word.
- Crop the scene only to remove empty space at its bottom (0.1–0.3).

## Don’t

- For a screenshot with copy beside it — use `MediaSplitSection`.
- For code — use `FeatureScrollSection` (a step with `code`) or `CliTerminalSection`.

## Related

- `ConsoleMockup` — the console scenes it frames.
- `ToggleGroup` — the tab control.
- `Texture`, `RegistrationFrame`, `SectionTitle`.

## Props

| Prop | Type | Default | Required | Description |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `variant` | `'console' \| 'diagram'` | `'console'` | false | What the panel frames: `console` crops and fades a console mock at a fixed aspect, `diagram` lets a scene keep its own height. |
| `titleAlign` | `SectionTitleAlign` | `'center'` | false | Layout of the headline block. |
| `titlePadding` | `SectionTitlePadding` | `'medium'` | false | Vertical padding of the headline block. |
| `size` | `SectionTitleSize` | `'medium'` | false | Step of the headline on the heading scale. |
| `anchor` | `string` | `''` | false | In-page id set on the band so a hash link can target it. |
| `eyebrow` | `string` | `''` | false | Eyebrow above the title. |
| `title` | `string` | `''` | false | Headline of the band, rendered as its `h2`. |
| `description` | `string` | `''` | false | One or two sentences under the headline. |
| `texture` | `TextureVariant` | `'pixelate'` | false | Texture the panel is grounded with. |
| `textureFade` | `TextureFade` | `'top'` | false | Axis the texture fades out along. |
| `tabs` | `ConsoleShowcaseSectionTab[]` | `[]` | false | Tabs over the frame, each `{ value, title }`; each plays its own beat of the scene. |
| `tabsAriaLabel` | `string` | `''` | false | Accessible name of the tab control; empty uses the built-in "Console views". |
| `crop` | `number` | `0` | false | Share of the scene height clipped off its bottom, from 0 to 0.5, from the medium breakpoint up. |
| `primaryAction` | `ConsoleShowcaseSectionAction` | `undefined` | false | First button under the headline, `secondary`; `{ label, href, trailing?, target? }`. Shown only with a label and a link. |
| `secondaryAction` | `ConsoleShowcaseSectionAction` | `undefined` | false | Second button under the headline, `outline`; same shape. Shown only with a label and a link. |

## Slots

| Slot | Scope | Notes |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `scene` | `{ tab: string | undefined }` \| The scene the band presents; `tab` is the selected tab value (undefined without tabs). |

## States

- `data-variant` mirrors `variant`.
- The first tab is selected on render.
- `--console-mockup-fade-start` / `--console-mockup-fade-end` are set for a `ConsoleMockup` scene when cropped.

## Accessibility

- A `section` named by its title.
- Tabs are a `ToggleGroup` (radio group) named by `tabsAriaLabel` or "Console views"; arrow keys move between them.
- The scene supplies its own accessible content (a mock is usually `aria-hidden` with a description in the copy).

## Examples

### Default

```vue
<script setup>
  import ConsoleMockup from '@aziontech/webkit/console-mockup'
  import ConsoleShowcaseSection from '@aziontech/webkit/console-showcase-section'

  const tabs = [
    { value: 'workloads-list', title: 'Workloads' },
    { value: 'workload-deployments', title: 'Deployments' },
    { value: 'workload-deploy-logs', title: 'Logs' }
  ]
</script>

<template>
  <ConsoleShowcaseSection
    eyebrow="Console"
    title="One console for every workload"
    description="Deploy, observe and secure from the same place."
    :tabs="tabs"
    :crop="0.2"
  >
    <template #scene="{ tab }">
      <ConsoleMockup :name="tab ?? 'workloads-list'" />
    </template>
  </ConsoleShowcaseSection>
</template>
```

1 more example(s) in [components/console-showcase-section.md](components/console-showcase-section.md).

<!-- components/contact-hub-section.md -->

# Contact Hub Section

> The band under the Contact title band: the team lanes side by side (Sales and Support), each with its scope in one sentence and the action that reaches that team, over a row of self-serve channels. It is a page section — the site places it from a `{ _id: 'contact-hub-section', props }` entry and binds `props` straight onto it — and it composes `section-module`, `registration-frame`, `card-grid`, `feature-item`, `button` and `status-indicator`. Lanes and channels share one frame, the channels in the rows under the lanes. A lane can carry a second button and a link to the system status page, so a reader can check for an incident before opening a ticket.

- Import: `import ContactHubSection from '@aziontech/webkit/contact-hub-section'`
- Category: section · Surface: site · Structure: monolithic

## When to use

- On the Contact page, to route a reader to the team or channel that answers them.

## Do

- Give it as many lanes and channels as the page has: both rows balance themselves (`card-grid` `layout: 'balanced'`), so two channels split the row in halves, three in thirds, four into 2 × 2 and five into 3 + 2, with no empty cell.
- Don't pad `channels` to three or drop one to make the row fit.
- Keep each lane's description to one sentence that states what that team handles.
- Set `target: '_blank'` on a channel or status link that leaves the site.
- Lead each lane with its `action`; add a `secondaryAction` only for a second route to the same team (an outlined button after the first).
- Give the band an `anchor` when a page links to it with a `#hash`.

## Don’t

- For a closing ask at the end of a page → use `closing-call-to-action-section`.
- For a grid of product use cases → use `customer-use-cases-section`.

## Related

- `feature-item` — each self-serve channel is a linked topic.
- `customer-use-cases-section` — the sibling band of icon-led topics.

## Props

| Prop | Type | Default | Required | Description |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `anchor` | `string` | `''` | false | In-page id set on the band so a `#hash` link can scroll to it. |
| `lanes` | `ContactHubSectionLane[]` | `[]` | false | The team lanes side by side, in order; each is `{ icon, title, description, action?, secondaryAction?, statusLink? }` where `action` is the lane's leading button, `secondaryAction` an outlined button after it, and `statusLink` the system status link beside them. |
| `channels` | `ContactHubSectionChannel[]` | `[]` | false | The self-serve channels in the rows under the lanes, in order; each is `{ icon, title, description, href, target? }`, rendered as a linked topic. |

## States

- Visual states: `default`
- Empty: with neither `lanes` nor `channels` the band renders nothing but its root
- Empty frame: with neither lanes nor channels the frame is left out; lanes alone or channels alone still draw one frame
- A lane's action, secondary action and status link render only when given, in that order
- Status link: the pill carries a `status-indicator` (success dot and the link's label)
- One frame: lanes fill rows of up to two and the channels the rows under them, up to three per row, each group balanced so every row is full
- Anchor: the root carries `id` = `anchor` when set, with a scroll margin so a `#hash` jump clears a sticky header

## Accessibility

- Visible focus: the status link carries `focus-visible:ring-2 focus-visible:ring-(--ring-color) focus-visible:ring-offset-2 focus-visible:ring-offset-(--bg-canvas)`; buttons and topics own theirs.
- Keyboard map: `Tab` reaches each lane action, its secondary action, the status link and each channel; `Enter` follows it.
- ARIA: the band is an unnamed `<section>`; each lane heading and each channel is an `h2`, so the outline reads one entry per route. Glyphs are `aria-hidden`; the status pill's dot belongs to `status-indicator`, which hides it and keeps the label as the link's name. A link with `target: '_blank'` carries `rel="noopener noreferrer"`.
- Contrast ≥4.5:1 (text) / ≥3:1 (large + icons): lane heading `var(--text-muted)`, description `var(--text-default)`.
- `motion-reduce:transition-none` on the status link's colour transition.
- Touch target ≥40×40 px — the status link is at least `h-10` (`min-h-10`), buttons are large, channels are whole-cell links.
- Reflow: the status link has `min-h-10` and `max-w-full`, and the lane floor wraps its buttons and the status link onto new lines, so nothing overflows a narrow column or a 200% zoom.

## Examples

### Default

```vue
<script setup>
  import ContactHubSection from '@aziontech/webkit/contact-hub-section'
</script>

<template>
  <ContactHubSection
    :lanes="[
      {
        icon: 'az az-briefcase',
        title: 'Sales',
        description:
          'Discuss your requirements, get custom pricing, or see the platform running on your own workload.',
        action: { label: 'Talk to sales', href: '/contact/sales' }
      },
      {
        icon: 'az az-headphones',
        title: 'Support',
        description:
          'Log in with your Azion account to open a ticket. Our engineers pick it up 24/7 on paid support plans.',
        action: {
          label: 'Log in to open a ticket',
          href: 'https://console.azion.com/login',
          trailing: true
        },
        statusLink: { label: 'System status', href: 'https://status.azion.com/', target: '_blank' }
      }
    ]"
    :channels="[
      {
        icon: 'az az-shield',
        title: 'Under a cyberattack?',
        description:
          'Get emergency mitigation for an attack in progress, even before you become a customer.',
        href: 'https://www.azion.com/en/lp/under-attack-mitigation/',
        target: '_blank'
      },
      {
        icon: 'az az-discord',
        title: 'Join the community',
        description: 'Ask developers and the Azion team on Discord. Open to everyone, on any plan.',
        href: 'https://discord.com/invite/pM8ANzztuB',
        target: '_blank'
      },
      {
        icon: 'az az-book',
        title: 'Read the docs',
        description: 'Guides, API reference, and troubleshooting for every Azion product.',
        href: 'https://www.azion.com/en/documentation/',
        target: '_blank'
      }
    ]"
  />
</template>
```

<!-- components/copy-command-section.md -->

# Copy Command Section

> A one-line command or agent prompt with a copy button, set in a large `InputGroup` under hero or section copy ("npm create azion", "Ask your agent to …"). With `shell` a `$` prompt leads the line. From the design sample's site `CopyCommandSection`.

- Import: `import CopyCommandSection from '@aziontech/webkit/copy-command-section'`
- Category: section · Surface: site · Structure: monolithic

## When to use

- Use CopyCommandSection for a one-line command or agent prompt with a copy button on a marketing page.
- The one command that starts a product, beside or instead of a call to action.
- A prompt to paste into an AI agent (`shell` off).

## Do

- One line that works when pasted as is.
- Place it in a hero or section-title actions slot.

## Don’t

- For several lines or output — use `CodeBlock` or `CliTerminalSection`.
- For an editable field — use `Input`.

## Related

- `CopyButton`, `InputGroup` — the parts.
- `CliTerminalSection` — a full terminal session.

## Props

| Prop | Type | Default | Required | Description |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `value` | `string` | `—` | true | Text on the line, copied as written. |
| `shell` | `boolean` | `true` | false | Draws the shell prompt before the text; turn off for a prompt meant for an agent. |

## States

- Read-only input; the copy button shows its copied state (from `CopyButton`).

## Accessibility

- The read-only input is named "Command" or "Prompt"; the copy button is named "Copy command" or "Copy prompt".
- The `$` is decorative (`aria-hidden`).
- Keyboard: `Tab` reaches the field (text can be selected) and the copy button.

## Examples

### Default

```vue
<script setup>
  import CopyCommandSection from '@aziontech/webkit/copy-command-section'
</script>

<template>
  <CopyCommandSection value="npm create azion@latest" />
</template>
```

1 more example(s) in [components/copy-command-section.md](components/copy-command-section.md).

<!-- components/customer-use-cases-section.md -->

# Customer Use Cases Section

> The "what teams use it for" band of a product page: a three-column frame whose first cell carries the band's overline, headline, lead and one call to action, followed by one cell per use case. It is a page section — the site places it from a `{ _id: 'customer-use-cases-section', props }` entry and binds `props` straight onto it — so its whole API is the props below, and it composes `card-grid`, `registration-frame`, `eyebrow`, `feature-item` and `button` rather than owning any anatomy of its own.

- Import: `import CustomerUseCasesSection from '@aziontech/webkit/customer-use-cases-section'`
- Category: section · Surface: site · Structure: monolithic

## When to use

- Use CustomerUseCasesSection when the band's title sits in the first grid cell with one action.
- On a product or solution page, right after the opening band, to name the concrete jobs the product is bought for. It is the one use-case band of a product page.
- Whenever a page needs three to six short, icon-led use cases with one link out to the documentation.

## Do

- On a product page give exactly four `items`; the first cell spans two rows at `lg`, so four cases fill the grid exactly. Elsewhere keep to four or so.
- Write `title` as the heading the page's outline needs; it is the band's `h2`.
- Give `primaryAction` an `href` to the documentation and set `trailing` so the chevron reads as "go".
- Leave `eyebrow` as the product name; it is the overline the reader scans first.

## Don’t

- For a grid of features with artwork → use `feature-tiles-section`.
- For use cases that each link to their own page, or any grid of claims with no single action → use `capability-grid-section`.
- Inside another band's cell → compose `feature-item` directly.

## Related

- `feature-item` — the icon, title and description cell each use case is rendered with.
- `card-grid` — the frame grid this band lays its cells in.

## Props

| Prop | Type | Default | Required | Description |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `eyebrow` | `string` | `''` | false | Eyebrow above the headline in the title cell, closed by the blinking cursor. |
| `title` | `string` | `''` | false | Headline of the band, rendered as its `h2` in the title cell. |
| `description` | `string` | `''` | false | One-line lead under the headline. |
| `primaryAction` | `SectionTitleAction` | `undefined` | false | Button pinned to the foot of the title cell; `{ label, href, trailing? }` where `trailing` adds the chevron. Omitted when absent. |
| `items` | `CustomerUseCasesSectionItem[]` | `[]` | false | The use cases rendered as frame cells, in order; each item is `{ icon, title, description }`. |
| `anchor` | `string` | `''` | false | In-page id set on the band so a hash link can target it. |

## States

- Visual states: `default`
- Empty: when `items` is empty the band renders nothing — not the title cell, not the frame — because a use-case band with no use cases is absent from the page, not an `empty-state`
- The title cell spans two rows from `lg`, so four items fill a three-column grid exactly
- The eyebrow is plain text with no `//` prefix: that prefix belongs to the hero and the pricing sections only.

## Accessibility

- Visible focus: owned by the composed `button`, whose anchor carries the `focus-visible:ring-2 focus-visible:ring-(--ring-color) focus-visible:ring-offset-2 focus-visible:ring-offset-(--bg-canvas)` ring.
- Keyboard map: `Tab` reaches the action link; `Enter` follows it. Nothing else in the band is interactive.
- ARIA: the band is a `<section>` labelled by its `h2` (`aria-labelledby`); each use case is a `feature-item` with an `h3`, so the outline reads band → use cases. The overline is decorative text.
- Contrast ≥4.5:1 (text) / ≥3:1 (large + icons): headline `var(--text-default)`, lead `var(--text-muted)`; icon colours are `feature-item`'s.
- `motion-reduce:transition-none motion-reduce:transform-none` — the overline cursor and the button's hover belong to `eyebrow` and `button`, which carry their own reduced-motion fallback; this band adds no motion.
- Touch target ≥40×40 px — the action is a large `button`.

## Examples

### Default

```vue
<script setup>
  import CustomerUseCasesSection from '@aziontech/webkit/customer-use-cases-section'
</script>

<template>
  <CustomerUseCasesSection
    eyebrow="SQL Database"
    title="What teams use SQL Database for"
    description="Teams use it to:"
    :primary-action="{ label: 'Docs', href: '/site/docs', trailing: true }"
    :items="[
      {
        icon: 'az az-comments',
        title: 'RAG Applications',
        description: 'Build topic-specific chatbots with the topic data stored in SQL Database.'
      },
      {
        icon: 'az az-search',
        title: 'Search Applications',
        description: 'Power search with vector similarity queries on stored embeddings.'
      },
      {
        icon: 'az az-sync',
        title: 'AI Context Injection',
        description: 'Give AI applications relevant context from vector embeddings.'
      },
      {
        icon: 'az az-globe',
        title: 'AI Workflows',
        description: 'Pair stored embeddings with AI Inference to run RAG applications on Azion.'
      }
    ]"
  />
</template>
```

<!-- components/faq-section.md -->

# Faq Section

> The frequently asked questions band near the end of a landing page: the heading in the left column and the questions on the right, each answer closed until the reader opens it, one at a time. It is a page section — the site places it from a `{ _id: 'faq-section', props }` entry and binds `props` straight onto it — and it composes `section-module` and `faq`. An answer is a markdown string, so it can run to several paragraphs (split by a blank line) and `- ` bullet lists, and a sentence can carry a link, bold or italic text, all without a slot or `v-html`.

- Import: `import FaqSection from '@aziontech/webkit/faq-section'`
- Category: section · Surface: site · Structure: monolithic

## When to use

- Use FaqSection for the questions band of a marketing page placed from page JSON (Markdown answers).
- Rendering page JSON, or you need a whole band with no custom content → the section (`faq-section`). Building an SFC by hand, needing slots, or nesting inside another band → the primitive (`faq`).
- To answer the handful of questions a reader still has before the closing call to action.

## Do

- Use one FAQ section per page and give it `anchor: 'faq'` so a page can link straight to it.
- Keep an answer short; links use `[label](https://github.com/aziontech/webkit/blob/main/.specs/href)`, bold `**text**` and italic `*text*`.
- Separate paragraphs with a blank line; start each bullet with `- ` and keep the list in its own block.
- Absolute `https://` links open in a new tab; site-relative links stay in the tab.

## Don’t

- For answers that need content beyond paragraphs, bullet lists and inline links (tables, images, code) → compose `faq` with its `answer` slot.
- For a single highlighted question with an action → use `call-to-action`.

## Related

- `faq` — the framed accordion band this section renders.
- `closing-call-to-action-section` — the band that usually follows it.

## Props

| Prop | Type | Default | Required | Description |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `anchor` | `string` | `''` | false | In-page id set on the band so a `#hash` link can scroll to it. |
| `title` | `string` | `''` | false | Headline of the band, rendered by the FAQ as its `h2` in the left column. |
| `items` | `FaqSectionItem[]` | `[]` | false | The questions in display order; each is `{ question, answer }` where `answer` is markdown carrying paragraphs, bullet lists, links, bold and italic text. |

## States

- Visual states: `default`, `open` (one answer at a time, owned by the composed accordion)
- Empty: when `items` is empty the band renders nothing but its root
- An answer renders `[label](https://github.com/aziontech/webkit/blob/main/.specs/href)` as a link, `**text**` as bold and `*text*` as italic; any other text is shown as written
- An answer with a blank line or `- ` lines renders as stacked paragraphs and an ARIA list (`role="list"` / `listitem`, inside the accordion's paragraph); a single-paragraph answer renders as inline text, as before

## Accessibility

- Visible focus: owned by the composed accordion triggers and the answer links.
- Keyboard map: `Tab` moves between the questions and the links of an open answer; `Enter` / `Space` toggles a question.
- ARIA: the `faq` renders the band's `<section>` labelled by its `h2`; the root is a plain `div` so the band has one landmark. A link that opens a new tab carries `rel="noopener noreferrer"`.
- Contrast ≥4.5:1 (text) / ≥3:1 (large + icons): owned by `faq`.
- `motion-reduce:transition-none motion-reduce:transform-none` — the band adds no motion; the accordion carries its own fallback.
- Touch target ≥40×40 px — owned by the accordion triggers.

## Examples

### Default

```vue
<script setup>
  import FaqSection from '@aziontech/webkit/faq-section'
</script>

<template>
  <FaqSection
    anchor="faq"
    title="Frequently asked questions"
    :items="[
      {
        question: 'What types of storefronts can I build and deploy on Azion?',
        answer:
          'You can build and deploy storefronts, marketplaces, campaign pages, and supporting retail apps. Any web app or website runs on Azion. The platform also covers much of the backend, for static and dynamic workloads on a distributed architecture.'
      },
      {
        question: 'Which frameworks are compatible?',
        answer:
          'Azion is compatible with modern frameworks such as Next.js, React, Vue, Astro, Angular, Nuxt, Svelte, and others, so you can reuse your current stack without a full rewrite. See the full [frameworks compatibility list](/en/documentation/products/devtools/azion-edge-runtime/frameworks-compatibility/).'
      },
      {
        question: 'How do preview deployments work?',
        answer:
          'You can validate storefront changes in preview environments before release, share results across teams, and promote approved versions to production with a controlled workflow.'
      }
    ]"
  />
</template>
```

<!-- components/feature-scroll-section.md -->

# Feature Scroll Section

> The walkthrough band of a product page: a large section title over one screen per feature. From the large breakpoint up the features scroll through a sticky stack — the copy advances while the feature's live scene or illustration stays pinned in a framed, textured stage with one small action under each step (a step may pin a one-file code sample instead, for a code-led walkthrough); below it the same features are a three-column grid of media tiles. It is a page section — the site places it from a `{ _id: 'feature-scroll-section', props }` entry and binds `props` straight onto it — and it composes `section-module`, `section-title`, `registration-frame`, `card-grid`, `media-tile`, `illustration`, `code-block`, `sticky-stack`, `texture`, `button` and the webkit scene assets (a `ConsoleMockup` console fragment, most often).

- Import: `import FeatureScrollSection from '@aziontech/webkit/feature-scroll-section'`
- Category: section · Surface: site · Structure: monolithic

## When to use

- Use FeatureScrollSection for the product-page walkthrough, one pinned screen (scene or code) per feature.
- To walk through four to six capabilities of one product, each with a picture of its own.
- To walk through capabilities each shown by the file you would write: give each item a `code` sample. It is the code slot of a marketing page: media bands show no code.

## Do

- Four to six items; the stack needs enough steps to earn the scroll.
- Give every item a `scene`, a `code` sample or an `illustration`; it is the pinned picture on large screens and the tile media on small ones. On a product page prefer a `scene` naming a `ConsoleMockup` (`{ component: 'ConsoleMockup', props: { name } }`): it shows the console itself.
- Keep `action` to a short label such as "Docs" — it sits under the step copy at the small size.

## Don’t

- For three reasons with no scroll story → use `feature-tiles-section`.
- In a hand-built SFC that needs its own media slot → use `sticky-stack` directly.

## Related

- `sticky-stack` — the scroll engine the large layout runs on.
- `media-tile` — the cell the small layout renders each feature with.

## Props

| Prop | Type | Default | Required | Description |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `anchor` | `string` | `''` | false | In-page id set on the band so a `#hash` link can scroll to it. |
| `eyebrow` | `string` | `''` | false | Eyebrow above the section title. |
| `title` | `string` | `''` | false | Headline of the band, rendered by the large section title as its `h2`; the band renders no header when empty. |
| `description` | `string` | `''` | false | Supporting sentence under the headline. |
| `items` | `FeatureScrollSectionItem[]` | `[]` | false | The features, in order; each is `{ title, description, scene?, code?, illustration?, action? }` where `scene` is `{ component, props? }` naming a webkit scene asset (drawn live in the stack, still in the grid), `code` is a one-file sample `{ fileName, code, language?, fileIcon?, copyAriaLabel? }` drawn when no scene is set, `illustration` is a registry name drawn when neither is set, and `action` is `{ label, href, trailing?, target?, variant? }` rendered as a small button under the step, secondary unless `variant` says otherwise. |

## Assets

- Illustrations: every valid name is in illustrations.md (MCP `list_assets({ kind: "illustrations" })`). An unknown name renders a "PLACE DESIGN ASSET" placeholder and warns in the console.
- Diagrams: every valid name is in diagrams.md (MCP `list_assets({ kind: "diagrams" })`). An unknown name draws nothing.

## States

- Visual states: `default`
- Below the large breakpoint the features render as a three-column grid of media tiles; from it up they render as a sticky stack whose active step is `sticky-stack`'s own
- Empty: when `items` is empty the band renders nothing — not the header, not the frames
- Media: an item's `scene` wins over its `code`, which wins over its `illustration`; a `code` sample is a one-file `code-block` with line numbers, typed in once in the stack and loaded on demand; in the stack the scene receives `active` for the open step only, so a console fragment plays its beat while its step is open and rests on its settled frame otherwise; in the grid every scene is drawn still (`active: false`) in a 20rem window. A scene name that is not registered draws nothing.

## Accessibility

- Visible focus: owned by the composed `button`.
- Keyboard map: `Tab` reaches each step's action and a code sample's copy button; `Enter` follows it; the stack's own progression is scroll-driven and never traps focus.
- ARIA: the band is a `<section>` named by its title (`aria-label`) when one is set; the step headings are `sticky-stack`'s and `media-tile`'s; the texture layer and the pinned illustration are decorative; a scene carries its own semantics (a `ConsoleMockup` is a decorative console picture).
- Contrast ≥4.5:1 (text) / ≥3:1 (large + icons): owned by the composed components.
- `motion-reduce:transition-none motion-reduce:transform-none` — the step transition belongs to `sticky-stack`, which carries its own fallback; this band adds no motion.
- Touch target ≥40×40 px — the per-step action is a small `button` on the large layout only, where pointer precision is available.

## Examples

### Default

```vue
<script setup>
  import FeatureScrollSection from '@aziontech/webkit/feature-scroll-section'
</script>

<template>
  <FeatureScrollSection
    eyebrow="Workloads"
    title="Everything a workload does, one screen at a time"
    description="Scroll through the console a workload is run from: the list, the topology, the domains, the deploy and the rollout. Each screen is the one you get, not a picture of it."
    :items="[
      {
        title: 'Every workload on one list',
        description:
          'See each workload with its domain, status and last deployment, so what is live is never a question.',
        illustration: 'traffic-and-cost-bar-chart',
        action: {
          label: 'Docs',
          href: 'https://www.azion.com/en/documentation/products/workloads/',
          trailing: true
        }
      },
      {
        title: 'Read the deployment topology',
        description:
          'The domains, the application and the firewall a workload serves, drawn as one chain.',
        illustration: 'global-network',
        action: {
          label: 'Docs',
          href: 'https://www.azion.com/en/documentation/products/workloads/',
          trailing: true
        }
      },
      {
        title: 'Domains and TLS, handled',
        description:
          'Add a domain, get its certificate issued and renewed, and set the TLS floor without leaving the page.',
        illustration: 'dns-protection',
        action: {
          label: 'Docs',
          href: 'https://www.azion.com/en/documentation/products/workloads/',
          trailing: true
        }
      },
      {
        title: 'Ship a version in seconds',
        description:
          'Build, upload, propagate and warm the cache, every step streamed as it happens until the version is live everywhere.',
        illustration: 'fastest-path-to-live-website',
        action: {
          label: 'Docs',

        }
      },
      {

        illustration: 'preview',
        action: {
          label: 'Docs',

          trailing: true
        }

  />
```

<!-- components/feature-tabs-section.md -->

# Feature Tabs Section

> The feature walkthrough band of a product page: a horizontal section title, with its button beside the headline, over up to three tabs on one side and the active tab's media on the other. A tab's media is its own code file (a `code-block` with line numbers whose lines rise in while the tab is open, on a pixelate texture in a taller stage), the band's live scene (handed the open tab's `value`), or the tab's illustration. It is a page section — the site places it from a `{ _id: 'feature-tabs-section', props }` entry and binds `props` straight onto it — and it composes `section-module`, `section-title`, `registration-frame`, `media-tabs`, `code-block`, `illustration`, `button`, `link` and the webkit scene assets. The tabs advance on a timer that pauses under the pointer or keyboard focus and never runs under reduced motion; that state is owned by `media-tabs`.

- Import: `import FeatureTabsSection from '@aziontech/webkit/feature-tabs-section'`
- Category: section · Surface: site · Structure: monolithic

## When to use

- Use FeatureTabsSection for up to three tabs beside a media on a product page.
- To walk through the two or three features behind one claim, one picture at a time, like Functions' Observability.

## Do

- Keep to three tabs; tabs past the limit are not rendered.
- Give every tab an `illustrationLabel` saying what its picture shows.
- Use tab `code` when the feature is an API a developer reads; keep each file short enough to read in the stage. Set `copyAriaLabel` to say what the copy button copies.
- Use the band `scene` when one console widget plays a different beat per tab (`{ component: 'ConsoleMockup', props: { name: 'functions-start' } }` with tab `value`s naming its tabs); give every tab a `value` then.

## Don’t

- For architectures drawn as diagrams across the full width → use `architecture-tabs-section`.
- For a scroll-driven walkthrough of more features → use `feature-scroll-section`.

## Related

- `media-tabs` — the timed tabs this section renders through.
- `illustration` — the picture a tab shows when it has no code and the band has no scene.
- `code-block` — the code file a tab opens as its media.

## Props

| Prop | Type | Default | Required | Description |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `anchor` | `string` | `''` | false | In-page id set on the band so a `#hash` link can scroll to it. |
| `eyebrow` | `string` | `''` | false | Eyebrow above the section title. |
| `title` | `string` | `''` | false | Headline of the band, rendered by the horizontal section title as its `h2`; the band renders no header when empty. |
| `description` | `string` | `''` | false | Supporting sentence under the headline. |
| `tabs` | `FeatureTabsSectionTab[]` | `[]` | false | The features, in order, at most three; each is `{ title, description, value?, code?, illustration?, illustrationLabel? }` where `value` is the stable key handed to the band scene as its `tab`, `code` is the `{ label, value, code, language?, fileName?, fileIcon? }` file the tab opens as its media, `illustration` is a registry name drawn when the tab has no code and the band no scene, and `illustrationLabel` its accessible name. |
| `scene` | `FeatureTabsSectionScene` | `undefined` | false | Live scene drawn as the media of every tab without code, `{ component, props? }` naming a webkit scene asset; it receives the tab's `value` as `tab` and whether the tab is open as `active`. Wins over the illustrations. |
| `autoplay` | `boolean` | `true` | false | Opens the next tab on a timer, with the composed tabs' visible pause control. |
| `autoplayInterval` | `number` | `5200` | false | Milliseconds each tab holds before the next one opens. |
| `copyAriaLabel` | `string` | `''` | false | Accessible name of the copy button on each tab's code file; empty keeps the code block's own. |
| `primaryAction` | `SectionTitleAction` | `undefined` | false | Button in the section title, beside the headline (under the frame when the band has no title); `{ label, href, trailing?, target? }` where `trailing` adds the chevron. Omitted when absent. |

## Assets

- Illustrations: every valid name is in illustrations.md (MCP `list_assets({ kind: "illustrations" })`). An unknown name renders a "PLACE DESIGN ASSET" placeholder and warns in the console.
- Diagrams: every valid name is in diagrams.md (MCP `list_assets({ kind: "diagrams" })`). An unknown name draws nothing.

## States

- Visual states: `default`; the active tab, its progress and the timer are owned by `media-tabs`
- Empty: when `tabs` is empty the band renders nothing — not the header, not the frame
- The frame draws no rule of its own under the tabs: the tab cells close the band with their own bottom rules, so the frame adds its bottom rule only when the under-frame link follows them
- The header renders only when `title` is set; the action renders only when `primaryAction` is set — as a secondary `button` in the header, or as a `link` under the frame when there is no header
- Media per tab: its `code` wins, then the band `scene`, then its `illustration`; when any tab carries `code` the stage grows from 24rem to 30rem and draws the pixelate texture, and an open tab's code lines rise in (owned by `code-block`, never under reduced motion). A scene name that is not registered draws nothing.

## Accessibility

- Visible focus: owned by `media-tabs` (the tabs), `code-block` (the copy button), `button` and `link`.
- Keyboard map: `Tab` reaches the action, the tabs and the open file's copy button; the tabs' keys are owned by `media-tabs`.
- ARIA: the band is a `<section>` named by its title (`aria-label`) when one is set; the headline is the section title's `h2`; each illustration is named by its tab's `illustrationLabel`, or decorative when empty; a code file is real text a screen reader reads, with a named copy button.
- Contrast ≥4.5:1 (text) / ≥3:1 (large + icons): owned by `section-title`, `media-tabs`, `code-block`, `button` and `link`.
- `motion-reduce:transition-none motion-reduce:transform-none` — the band adds no motion; the timer never runs under reduced motion (owned by `media-tabs`).
- Touch target ≥40×40 px: the tab rows and the large header button; the under-frame link is supplementary to them.

## Examples

### Default

```vue
<script setup>
  import FeatureTabsSection from '@aziontech/webkit/feature-tabs-section'

  const tabs = [
    {
      title: 'Trace',
      description:
        'The run as a waterfall: each step placed against the invocation it belongs to, nested where it nested.',
      illustration: 'live-debugging',
      illustrationLabel: 'A trace waterfall of one invocation'
    },
    {
      title: 'Logs',
      description:
        'Structured output on the same clock as the trace, filtered by level, searchable in place.',
      illustration: 'traffic-and-cost-bar-chart',
      illustrationLabel: 'Structured logs filtered by level'
    },
    {
      title: 'Request path',
      description:
        'The workload, application, function and connector it travelled, each captioned with the time spent there.',
      illustration: 'distributed-apis',
      illustrationLabel: 'The path a request took through the platform'
    }
  ]

  const primaryAction = {
    label: 'Open an invocation',
    href: '/product-preview',
    trailing: true
  }
</script>

<template>
  <FeatureTabsSection
    eyebrow="Observability"
    title="Inspect every run end-to-end"
    description="Every invocation of a function is traced on the platform itself — spans, logs and the path the request took. Nothing to configure, no storage to attach. Pick a document to open it."
    :tabs="tabs"
    :primary-action="primaryAction"
  />
</template>
```

2 more example(s) in [components/feature-tabs-section.md](components/feature-tabs-section.md).

<!-- components/feature-tiles-section.md -->

# Feature Tiles Section

> The reasons band of a marketing page: an optional centered section title over a frame grid of media tiles, each an image or an illustration above a short heading and a sentence. It is a page section — the site places it from a `{ _id: 'feature-tiles-section', props }` entry and binds `props` straight onto it — and it composes `section-module`, `section-title`, `registration-frame`, `card-grid`, `media-tile` and `illustration`. `variant` picks the layout only: `artwork` is four columns with marks on every corner, `illustrated` is three columns with marks on the bottom rule; the tiles keep the same shape in both.

- Import: `import FeatureTilesSection from '@aziontech/webkit/feature-tiles-section'`
- Category: section · Surface: site · Structure: monolithic

## When to use

- Use FeatureTilesSection for a static set of picture tiles.
- To state three or four reasons to buy, each with a picture, under a product or pricing argument.

## Do

- Match the tile count to the variant: four tiles for `artwork`, three for `illustrated`.
- Give a tile either `image` (a URL) or `illustration` (a registry name), not both; `scale` only matters with `illustration`.
- Leave `title` empty to drop the header when the band sits under a hero that already states it; set `anchor` when a link above scrolls to the band.

## Don’t

- For linked use cases or a grid of claims without pictures → use `capability-grid-section`.
- For a scroll-driven walkthrough with one screen per feature → use `feature-scroll-section`.

## Related

- `media-tile` — the picture-over-copy cell each tile is rendered with.
- `feature-widgets-section` — the sibling band whose cells carry an action each.

## Props

| Prop | Type | Default | Required | Description |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `variant` | `FeatureTilesSectionVariant` | `'artwork'` | false | Layout of the grid: `artwork` is four columns with marks on every corner, `illustrated` three columns with marks on the bottom rule. |
| `anchor` | `string` | `''` | false | In-page id set on the band so a `#hash` link can scroll to it. |
| `eyebrow` | `string` | `''` | false | Eyebrow above the section title. |
| `title` | `string` | `''` | false | Headline of the band, rendered by the centered section title as its `h2`; the band renders no header when empty. |
| `description` | `string` | `''` | false | Supporting sentence under the headline. |
| `tiles` | `FeatureTilesSectionTile[]` | `[]` | false | The tiles rendered as frame cells, in order; each is `{ title, description, image?, alt?, illustration?, scale? }` where `image` is a picture URL, `alt` its alternative text (empty keeps the picture decorative), `illustration` a registry name drawn fluid, and `scale` its media scale. |

## Assets

- Illustrations: every valid name is in illustrations.md (MCP `list_assets({ kind: "illustrations" })`). An unknown name renders a "PLACE DESIGN ASSET" placeholder and warns in the console.

## States

- Visual states: `default`
- `data-variant` mirrors the `variant` prop and drives the column count and the frame marks
- Empty: when `tiles` is empty the band renders nothing — not the header, not the frame
- The header renders only when `title` is set
- A tile picture carries its `alt`; without one it is decorative (`alt=""`, `aria-hidden`)

## Accessibility

- Visible focus: nothing in the band is interactive.
- Keyboard map: none.
- ARIA: the band is a `<section>` named by its title (`aria-label`) when one is set; the headline is the section title's `h2` and each tile's heading is `media-tile`'s; illustrations are decorative and named by the tile's heading; a tile picture is named by its `alt`, or hidden from assistive technology when `alt` is empty.
- Contrast ≥4.5:1 (text) / ≥3:1 (large + icons): owned by `section-title` and `media-tile`.
- `motion-reduce:transition-none motion-reduce:transform-none` — the band adds no motion.
- Touch target ≥40×40 px — not applicable, nothing is interactive.

## Examples

### Default

```vue
<script setup>
  import branches from '@aziontech/webkit/assets/branches.svg'
  import personalTokens from '@aziontech/webkit/assets/personal-tokens.svg'
  import FeatureTilesSection from '@aziontech/webkit/feature-tiles-section'
</script>

<template>
  <FeatureTilesSection
    variant="artwork"
    eyebrow="Why Azion"
    title="From commit to live traffic, on one platform"
    description="Build, ship, and run an application on the same platform that serves it — one place to deploy from, one place to watch it from."
    :tiles="[
      {
        image: branches,
        title: 'Build at AI speed.',
        description:
          'Go from commit to a live URL in minutes. Test on a preview, promote when you are ready, and roll back without a rebuild.'
      },
      {
        image: personalTokens,
        title: 'Secure by default.',
        description:
          'Every workload runs in its own isolated environment, with WAF, rate limits, and leading compliance practices. No configuration required.'
      }
    ]"
  />
</template>
```

1 more example(s) in [components/feature-tiles-section.md](components/feature-tiles-section.md).

<!-- components/feature-widgets-section.md -->

# Feature Widgets Section

> The "three ways to start" band of a product page: a centered section title with up to two calls to action, over a bento grid where every cell is a live console scene (or an illustration) above a bold-led sentence and its affordances — a link button, a copy button that puts a prompt on the clipboard and confirms it with "Copied" for two seconds, or both. A cell's scene plays its beat while the pointer is over the cell. It is a page section — the site places it from a `{ _id: 'feature-widgets-section', props }` entry and binds `props` straight onto it — and it composes `section-module`, `section-title`, `button`, `registration-frame`, `bento-grid`, `illustration`, `highlight-button` and the webkit scene assets.

- Import: `import FeatureWidgetsSection from '@aziontech/webkit/feature-widgets-section'`
- Category: section · Surface: site · Structure: monolithic

## When to use

- Use FeatureWidgetsSection when each cell has its own action or copy button.
- To offer the same outcome through two or three entry points (agent, console, terminal), each with its own next step.

## Do

- Give each item an `action` (a link), a `copy` (text to put on the clipboard), or both when the reader may want the docs and the prompt.
- On a product page give each item a `scene` naming a `ConsoleMockup` fragment (`{ component: 'ConsoleMockup', props: { name } }`); keep `illustration` for cells with no console to show.
- Keep `copy.text` self-contained; it is what the reader pastes into their agent.
- Set `anchor` when a hero button scrolls to this band.

## Don’t

- For reasons without an action per cell → use `feature-tiles-section` (with pictures) or `capability-grid-section` (without).
- For a long argument per feature → use `feature-scroll-section`.

## Related

- `bento-grid` — the grid the cells are laid in.
- `highlight-button` — the copy affordance a cell renders when it carries `copy`.

## Props

| Prop | Type | Default | Required | Description |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `anchor` | `string` | `''` | false | In-page id set on the band so a `#hash` link can scroll to it. |
| `eyebrow` | `string` | `''` | false | Eyebrow above the section title. |
| `title` | `string` | `''` | false | Headline of the band, rendered by the section title as its `h2`, and the accessible name of the band. |
| `description` | `string` | `''` | false | Supporting sentence under the headline. |
| `primaryAction` | `SectionTitleAction` | `undefined` | false | First button under the headline; `{ label, href, trailing? }`. Omitted when absent. |
| `secondaryAction` | `SectionTitleAction` | `undefined` | false | Second, outlined button under the headline; `{ label, href, trailing? }` where `trailing` adds the chevron. Omitted when absent. |
| `titleAlign` | `SectionTitleAlign` | `'center'` | false | Alignment of the section title over the grid: `center`, `start` or `split`. |
| `columns` | `FeatureWidgetsSectionColumns` | `2` | false | Column count of the grid from the medium breakpoint up: `2`, `3` or `4`. |
| `copiedLabel` | `string` | `''` | false | Label a cell's copy button shows for two seconds after a copy; empty uses the catalog's `Copied`. |
| `items` | `FeatureWidgetsSectionItem[]` | `[]` | false | The cells, in order; each is `{ title, description, scene?, illustration?, action?, copy? }` where `scene` is `{ component, props? }` naming a webkit scene asset drawn in a 20rem window and handed `hover` while the pointer is over the cell, `illustration` is drawn when no scene is set, `action` is `{ label, href }` rendered as an outlined link button and `copy` is `{ label, text }` rendered as a copy button that puts `text` on the clipboard; a cell may carry both. |

## Assets

- Illustrations: every valid name is in illustrations.md (MCP `list_assets({ kind: "illustrations" })`). An unknown name renders a "PLACE DESIGN ASSET" placeholder and warns in the console.
- Diagrams: every valid name is in diagrams.md (MCP `list_assets({ kind: "diagrams" })`). An unknown name draws nothing.

## States

- Visual states: `default`
- Empty: when `items` is empty the band renders nothing — not the header, not the frame
- The header renders only when `title` is set; the two actions ride with it
- A cell's copy button writes `copy.text` to the clipboard on activation, then reads `copiedLabel` (or the catalog's `Copied`) with a check icon for two seconds before it returns to its label; the timer is cleared when the band unmounts. A failed write leaves the label as it was
- A cell with both `action` and `copy` renders the link button first, then the copy button
- Media per cell: `scene` wins over `illustration`; the scene receives `hover: true` while the pointer is over its cell (also on `pointermove`, so a pointer left still while the page scrolls under it counts). A scene name that is not registered draws nothing

## Accessibility

- Visible focus: owned by the composed `button` and `highlight-button`, which carry the `focus-visible:ring-2 focus-visible:ring-(--ring-color) focus-visible:ring-offset-2 focus-visible:ring-offset-(--bg-canvas)` ring.
- Keyboard map: `Tab` moves between the header actions and each cell's buttons; `Enter` / `Space` activates. The scene's hover beat is decorative, so keyboard users lose nothing without it.
- ARIA: the band is a `<section>` named by its title (`aria-label`) when one is set; the grid is an unnamed `bento-grid` inside that landmark, so the name is not duplicated; the cell lead is a `strong` inside the sentence, not a heading, so the outline stays the band's `h2`; the action row is a polite live region, so the copy button's "Copied" is announced.
- Contrast ≥4.5:1 (text) / ≥3:1 (large + icons): lead `var(--text-default)`, copy `var(--text-muted)`.
- `motion-reduce:transition-none motion-reduce:transform-none` — the buttons' motion carries its own fallback; this band adds no motion.
- Touch target ≥40×40 px — the cell buttons are medium `button` / `highlight-button`.

## Examples

### Default

```vue
<script setup>
  import FeatureWidgetsSection from '@aziontech/webkit/feature-widgets-section'
</script>

<template>
  <FeatureWidgetsSection
    anchor="get-started"
    eyebrow="Get started"
    title="Start where you already work"
    description="The same function, three ways to ship it. Hand it to a coding agent, build it in the Console, or deploy it from the terminal."
    :primary-action="{ label: 'Start for free', href: '/signup' }"
    :secondary-action="{ label: 'Docs', href: '/site/docs', trailing: true }"
    :columns="3"
    :items="[
      {
        title: 'Ask your coding agent',
        description:
          'Connect the Azion MCP servers to Claude Code, Cursor or ChatGPT, and the agent creates, instances and routes the function, with a dry run before every write.',
        illustration: 'deploy-secure-mcp-server',
        copy: {
          label: 'Copy prompt',
          text: 'Create a function that answers 401 when a request has no Authorization header.'
        }
      },
      {
        title: 'Build it in the Console',
        description:
          'Choose the application it runs on, write the handler in the editor, name it, and create it. The rule that runs it is drafted for you.',
        illustration: 'build-applications',
        action: { label: 'Docs', href: '/site/docs' }
      },
      {
        title: 'Ship it from the terminal',
        description:
          'azion init scaffolds from 25 presets, azion dev serves it on localhost:3333, and azion deploy puts it on a live domain.',
        illustration: 'quick-start-with-templates',
        action: { label: 'Docs', href: '/site/docs' }
      }
    ]"
  />
</template>
```

1 more example(s) in [components/feature-widgets-section.md](components/feature-widgets-section.md).

<!-- components/hero-form-section.md -->

# Hero Form Section

> The opening band of a lead-capture page, where the form is the reason for the page: the hero copy and a list of highlights on one side, the form on the other, above the fold, with the client strip on the floor. It is a page section — the site places it from a `{ _id: 'hero-form-section', props }` entry and binds `props` straight onto it — and it composes `hero`, `hero-title`, `feature-item`, `field`, `input`, `select`, `phone-input`, `textarea`, `button` and `alert`. The fields are data: each entry of `fields` picks its control by `type`, so the Contact page and the Under attack page are the same block with different `fields`. The block never sends: it validates the fields locally (required fields and the email format, with the messages from `messages`) and on a valid submit emits `submit` with the event and the values keyed by field `name`; the site posts them and reports the outcome back through `loading`, `submitted` and `failed`.

- Import: `import HeroFormSection from '@aziontech/webkit/hero-form-section'`
- Category: section · Surface: site · Structure: monolithic

## When to use

- Use HeroFormSection when the page exists to capture a lead and the form sits above the fold.
- To open a page whose purpose is a lead form, such as Contact sales or Under attack.

## Do

- Give every field a unique `name`; it is the key of the submitted value.
- Mark half-width fields in pairs so each row stays full.
- Set `visuallyHiddenLabel` on a field the page labels by its placeholder alone (azion.com's phone field): the label stays the control's accessible name for assistive tech but is not drawn, so give that field a `placeholder` that says what to type.
- Keep the headline to a short phrase; the band sets it on a narrower measure than the description (`--container-md`), so "Grow as an Azion Partner" breaks over two lines as on the site. There is no width prop to tune.
- Set `loading` while the values are sent, then `submitted` or `failed` with the outcome.

## Don’t

- For a newsletter sign-up with one email field → use `newsletter-section`.
- For an opening band with calls to action and no form → use `hero-section`, which takes every other marketing page opening.

## Related

- `newsletter-section` — the other form section; same `submit` and state contract.
- `alert` — renders the success and failure messages.

## Props

| Prop | Type | Default | Required | Description |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `messages` | `HeroFormSectionMessages` | `—` | true | Copy of the form outcomes: `{ missing, invalidEmail, success, error }`. |
| `eyebrow` | `string` | `''` | false | Short overline above the headline. |
| `titleHighlight` | `string` | `''` | false | Opening words of the headline, painted in the brand accent. |
| `title` | `string` | `''` | false | Page headline, rendered as the page `h1`; the band renders nothing when empty. |
| `description` | `string` | `''` | false | Sentence under the headline. |
| `descriptionLink` | `HeroFormSectionLink` | `undefined` | false | A link continuing the description inline, such as a phone line; `{ label, href }`. Omitted when absent. |
| `descriptionAfter` | `string` | `''` | false | Copy after the inline link, closing the description's sentence. |
| `primaryAction` | `SectionTitleAction` | `undefined` | false | First button under the copy; `{ label, href, trailing?, target? }`. Shown only with a label and a link. |
| `secondaryAction` | `SectionTitleAction` | `undefined` | false | Second, outlined button beside the first; `{ label, href, trailing?, target? }`. Shown only with a label and a link. |
| `anchor` | `string` | `''` | false | In-page id set on the band so a `#hash` link can scroll to it. |
| `items` | `HeroFormSectionItem[]` | `[]` | false | The highlights under the description, in order; each is `{ icon, title, description }`. The list renders only when there are items. |
| `formAriaLabel` | `string` | `''` | false | Accessible name of the field group, read as its legend. |
| `fields` | `HeroFormSectionField[]` | `[]` | false | The form fields in order; each is `{ name, type, label, placeholder?, required?, half?, options?, country?, visuallyHiddenLabel? }`; `visuallyHiddenLabel` keeps the label as the accessible name but does not draw it. |
| `submitLabel` | `string` | `''` | false | Label of the submit button. |
| `marqueeLabel` | `string` | `''` | false | Eyebrow above the client marquee. |
| `marqueeMarks` | `string[]` | `[]` | false | Registry names of the client marks, in order; the marquee renders only when there are marks. |
| `loading` | `boolean` | `false` | false | The site is sending the values: the button shows its loading state and a submit is ignored. |
| `submitted` | `boolean` | `false` | false | The site sent the values: the form gives way to `messages.success`. |
| `failed` | `boolean` | `false` | false | The site could not send the values: `messages.error` shows under the form. |

## Events

| Event | Payload | Notes |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `submit` | `(event: SubmitEvent, values: Record<string, string>)` | Fired on a valid submit, not while `loading`; `values` holds every field by `name`, trimmed, with a phone number prefixed by its dial code. The block never sends it. |

## Assets

- Brand marks: every valid name is in brand-marks.md (MCP `list_assets({ kind: "brandMarks" })`). An unknown name is not drawn as a mark: `Marquee` prints it as a typographic wordmark, the quote components show no mark.

## States

- Visual states: `default`, `invalid`, `loading`, `submitted`, `failed`
- `invalid`: on submit, an empty required field shows `messages.missing` and a malformed email `messages.invalidEmail` under the field; focus moves to the first field in error; editing the field clears it; nothing is emitted
- `loading` (prop): the button shows its loading state, every field is disabled (the scope locks off the one flag) and a submit emits nothing; `data-loading` on the root
- `submitted` (prop): the form is replaced by a success `Alert` with `messages.success`, which takes focus when focus was in the form; `data-submitted` on the root
- `failed` (prop): a danger `Alert` with `messages.error` shows under the button; the form stays editable; `data-failed` on the root
- A field with `visuallyHiddenLabel` renders its `Field.Label` visually hidden (`sr-only`), so the control keeps its accessible name and the page shows only the placeholder
- The headline caps at `--container-md` while the description and highlights keep the `lg` column
- `descriptionLink` renders inline after `description`, underlined, followed by `descriptionAfter`, so the copy reads as one sentence
- Without `items`, the copy block is sticky from `lg` (it stays in view while the taller form scrolls past); with `items` it scrolls with the highlights
- `loading` also sets `disabled` on the `fieldset`, so the browser locks every control in it
- Empty: when `title` is empty the band renders nothing but its root; when `items` is empty the list is omitted; when `marqueeMarks` is empty the strip is omitted

## Accessibility

- Visible focus: owned by the field controls and `button`.
- Keyboard map: `Tab` reaches the inline description link and the actions under the copy, then walks the fields in order and the button; `Enter` in a text field or on the button submits.
- ARIA: the band is the `hero` `<section>` named by the title (`aria-label`); the root is a plain `div`. The fields sit in a `fieldset` whose visually hidden `legend` is `formAriaLabel`; each control is labelled through its own `Field.Label` with an id from `useId`; each label is a `Field.Label` rendered by the band, carrying the persistent Required tag of a `required` field from first render. An `email` field is an `<input type="email">` with `autocomplete="email"` and `spellcheck="false"`. Validation runs on submit only: a required field carries `required` and `aria-required` from the first render, and a submit that finds it empty or malformed sets the red `invalid` state (`aria-invalid`); the message is the field's helper text, and editing the field clears it. The success message is a `status` region and the failure message an `alert`.
- Contrast ≥4.5:1 (text) / ≥3:1 (large + icons): owned by the composed components.
- `motion-reduce:transition-none` on the inline description link's colour transition; the composed components carry their own fallback.
- Touch target ≥40×40 px — the text fields and the button are large.

## Examples

<!-- components/hero-section.md -->

# Hero Section

> The opening band of a marketing page: the page `h1` with its overline, accent phrase, sentence and up to two actions, full width above the page column, with an optional client strip on the floor. It is a page section — the site places it from a `{ _id: 'hero-section', props }` entry and binds `props` straight onto it — and it composes `hero`, `hero-title`, `button` and `illustration`, plus `flow-diagram` and `tag` for the topology and `section-container`, `section-module`, `registration-frame`, `card-grid`, `feature-item` and `section-gap` for the argument grid. `variant` picks the layout only: `center` fills a screen over a dot field with the copy centered, `title-band` is a padded band with the copy centered, `copy-beside-art` sets an illustration (or an `image`) beside the copy, sized from that illustration's own drawn bounds so it bleeds past the column edge and trims its empty top and bottom, and `copy-on-top-art` fills a screen with the copy centered over a scene in its `top` or `bottom` slot, unpadded so the scene reaches the band's edges, and draws a `FlowDiagram` topology from `levels` in the bottom window when neither slot is given, so page JSON can carry the scene as data; every layout takes the same copy, actions and client strip, and any layout can set a framed grid of arguments (`items`, three to a row, each a `FeatureItem`) under the band. The client strip's overline falls back to the locale catalog (`Proven on mission-critical workloads`).

- Import: `import HeroSection from '@aziontech/webkit/hero-section'`
- Category: section · Surface: site · Structure: monolithic

## When to use

- Use HeroSection for every marketing page opening; pick the layout with `variant`.
- To open a home, solution, product, listing or contact page with its headline and main actions; add `items` for a grid of arguments under it, or `levels` with `variant="copy-on-top-art"` for a topology under the copy.
- Rendering page JSON, or you need a whole band with no custom content → the section (`hero-section`). Building an SFC by hand, needing slots, or nesting inside another band → the primitive (`hero`).
- Pick the layout by page type (azion.com):
- Home and the product index page: `center` with the client marquee (`marqueeMarks`). No other page type opens with it.
- Every product page: `title-band` with `size="large"`.
- Compliance, Recognitions, Support, Careers and Resource Hub: `center-band`.
- Blog: `title-band` with `size="medium"`, followed by a `small` `section-gap`.
- Any other page: `title-band`.

## Do

- Use `title-band` with `size="large"` for a product page and `size="medium"` for a page whose bands start right under the headline.
- Give `illustration` and `illustrationAriaLabel` only to the `copy-beside-art` layout; the other layouts ignore them.
- Leave `titleLength` at `short` for a headline of up to about 50 characters (about seven words): like azion.com, the `center`, `title-band` and `center-band` layouts set it on a narrower measure than the sentence under it, so "Certifications and Compliance" breaks over two lines. Use `long` for a headline of about 51 to 90 characters, which would run to three lines on the short measure. Rewrite a headline longer than about 90 characters; count the string as rendered, in each locale.
- Leave `headerOffset` at its default under `SiteHeaderSection`: it takes the bar's `--layout-header-height` out of a band that fills a screen, so the band fills exactly the first screen with it. Set it empty only on a page with no header above the band; never a literal length.

## Don’t

- When the page exists to capture a lead and the form sits above the fold → use `hero-form-section`.
- `center` with the client marquee on any page other than Home and the product index page → use `title-band` (or `center-band` on the pages listed under When to use).
- In a hand-built SFC that needs slots HeroSection does not offer → use the `hero` primitive.
- For a band inside the page column → use `section-module` or a sibling section.

## Related

- `hero` — the band primitive every layout is rendered with.
- `hero-title` — the headline block rendered inside it.
- `hero-form-section` — the opening band with a lead form beside the copy.
- `flow-diagram` — the diagram primitive the `levels` topology is drawn with.
- `feature-item` — the icon, heading and sentence each argument of `items` is rendered with.

## Props

| Prop | Type | Default | Required | Description |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `variant` | `HeroSectionVariant` | `'title-band'` | false | Layout of the band: `center` fills a screen over a dot field, `title-band` is a padded band, `center-band` is a large site-wide band with the copy centered, `compact-band` is a short site-wide band with the copy at the start, `copy-beside-art` sets an illustration or an image beside the copy, `copy-on-top-art` fills a screen with the copy centered over a scene in the `top` or `bottom` slot. |
| `size` | `HeroSectionSize` | `'medium'` | false | Vertical rhythm of the band; `large` is the opening a product page leads with. |
| `eyebrow` | `string` | `''` | false | Short overline above the headline. |
| `titleHighlight` | `string` | `''` | false | Opening words of the headline, painted in the brand accent; reads as one sentence with `title`. |
| `title` | `string` | `''` | false | Page headline, rendered as the page `h1`; the band renders nothing when empty. |
| `description` | `string` | `''` | false | Sentence under the headline. |
| `primaryAction` | `SectionTitleAction` | `undefined` | false | First button under the headline; `{ label, href, trailing?, target? }`. Shown only with a label and a link. |
| `secondaryAction` | `SectionTitleAction` | `undefined` | false | Second, outlined button beside the first; `{ label, href, trailing?, target? }`. Shown only with a label and a link. |
| `marqueeLabel` | `string` | `''` | false | Eyebrow above the client marquee; empty uses the locale catalog (`Proven on mission-critical workloads`). |
| `marqueeMarks` | `string[]` | `[]` | false | Registry names of the client marks on the band floor, in order; the marquee renders only when it has marks. |
| `illustration` | `string` | `''` | false | Registry name of the illustration set beside the copy in the `copy-beside-art` layout. |
| `illustrationAriaLabel` | `string` | `''` | false | Accessible description of the illustration. |
| `image` | `HeroSectionImage` | `undefined` | false | Image set beside the copy in the `copy-beside-art` layout when no `illustration` is given; `{ src, alt, width, height }`. A portrait image (taller than wide) is drawn at half width, centered, below `md`. |
| `eyebrowPrefix` | `HeroTitleEyebrowPrefix` | `'//'` | false | Mark set before the eyebrow (`//`, `<>`, `</>`, or empty for none); drawn only with an eyebrow. |
| `anchor` | `string` | `''` | false | In-page id set on the band so a `#hash` link can scroll to it. |
| `headerOffset` | `string` | `'var(--layout-header-height)'` | false | Height of the site header above the band, taken out of a band that fills a screen; defaults to the `SiteHeaderSection` bar's token, any CSS length, empty for a page with no header above it. |
| `titleLength` | `HeroSectionTitleLength` | `'short'` | false | Length of the headline: `short` sets it on the site's narrow headline measure in the centered layouts, so a four-to-seven-word headline breaks over two balanced lines while the sentence under it keeps the full column; `long` lets the headline take the whole column. |
| `plain` | `boolean` | `false` | false | Drops the dot texture of the `center` layout. |
| `levels` | `HeroSectionLevel[]` | `[]` | false | Columns of the topology the `copy-on-top-art` layout draws under the copy when neither scene slot is given, left to right; each is `{ nodes }`, the cards top to bottom, each `{ eyebrow, icon, title, label, severity, lit? }`. A card without `title` is skipped. |
| `items` | `HeroSectionItem[]` | `[]` | false | Arguments in a framed grid under the band, three to a row; each is `{ icon, title, description }`. The grid renders only when there are items. |

## Slots

| Slot | Scope | Notes |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `top` | — | A scene in the window above the copy (`ConsoleMockup`, a diagram). The design sample's hero scenes render here. |
| `bottom` | — | A scene in the window under the copy; it enters after the copy (`animate-content-enter`, off under reduced motion) and fades at the inline edges on narrow containers. |

## Assets

- Illustrations: every valid name is in illustrations.md (MCP `list_assets({ kind: "illustrations" })`). An unknown name renders a "PLACE DESIGN ASSET" placeholder and warns in the console.
- Brand marks: every valid name is in brand-marks.md (MCP `list_assets({ kind: "brandMarks" })`). An unknown name is not drawn as a mark: `Marquee` prints it as a typographic wordmark, the quote components show no mark.

## States

- Visual states: `default`
- `data-variant` mirrors the `variant` prop and drives the hero's height, width, backdrop and the copy alignment
- `data-title-measure="narrow"` on the root when the headline is set on the narrow measure (`titleLength: 'short'` in the `center`, `title-band` and `center-band` layouts); the headline then caps at `--container-lg` while the description keeps the column
- Empty: when `title` is empty the band renders nothing but its root
- The client strip renders only when `marqueeMarks` has marks; each action renders only with a label and a link; the art renders only in the `copy-beside-art` layout with `illustration` or `image` set (`illustration` wins when both are given)
- `copy-beside-art` art bounds: a registered illustration whose drawn area is known (the `azion-to-*` comparisons and the solution illustrations) takes a width, a bleed past the column's end edge and a top and bottom trim computed from where it is drawn on its canvas, set as `--hero-section-art-width`, `--hero-section-art-bleed`, `--hero-section-art-trim-top` and `--hero-section-art-trim-bottom` on the art box from `md` up; any other illustration keeps the default bounds (161.45 % wide, 35.73 % bleed, no trim); an `image` fills the media column. Below `md` the art takes the column width
- `data-orientation="portrait"` on an `image` taller than it is wide
- Topology: in `copy-on-top-art` with no `top` or `bottom` slot, `levels` draws a `FlowDiagram` in the bottom window, one column per level and one card per node with a title; a level left with no card is dropped, and with none left the window is not drawn. A lit node carries `data-lit` and a brand glow. Other layouts ignore `levels`, and a slotted scene wins over it
- Argument grid: `items` renders a framed three-column `CardGrid` of `FeatureItem`s (headings at level 2) inside a site-wide `SectionContainer` under the hero, closed by a hatched `SectionGap`; only with a title and at least one item

## Accessibility

- Visible focus: owned by `button`.
- Keyboard map: `Tab` moves through the actions; the client strip is not focusable.
- ARIA: the hero `<section>` is named by the full headline (`aria-label`); the headline is the page `h1`; the illustration is named by `illustrationAriaLabel`, decorative when empty; an `image` carries its `alt`. The topology cards are static text (eyebrow, title, status tag), not focusable; each argument heading is an `h2` under the page `h1`.
- Contrast ≥4.5:1 (text) / ≥3:1 (large + icons): owned by `hero-title` and `button`.
- `motion-reduce:transition-none motion-reduce:transform-none` — the band adds no motion; the client strip stops under reduced motion in `marquee`.
- Touch target ≥40×40 px — the large buttons meet it.

## Examples

### Default

```vue
<script setup>
  import HeroSection from '@aziontech/webkit/hero-section'
</script>

<template>
  <HeroSection
    variant="title-band"
    size="large"
    eyebrow="Cache"
    title="Deliver content fast, worldwide"
    description="Serve cached content close to users. Cut origin load and keep applications fast during traffic spikes."
    :primary-action="{ label: 'Start for free', href: 'https://console.azion.com/signup/' }"
    :secondary-action="{
      label: 'Docs',
      href: 'https://www.azion.com/en/documentation/',
      trailing: true
    }"
  />
</template>
```

# Investor Marks Section

> The investors band: a centered section title over a two-column bento grid where each cell is an investor's mark, linked to its site, that lifts on hover to reveal a "visit website" line. It is a page section — the site places it from a `{ _id: 'investor-marks-section', props }` entry and binds `props` straight onto it — and it composes `section-module`, `section-title`, `registration-frame` and `bento-grid`.

- Import: `import InvestorMarksSection from '@aziontech/webkit/investor-marks-section'`
- Category: section · Surface: site · Structure: monolithic

## When to use

- On the company page, to name the investors with their marks.

## Do

- Give each item a `logoLight` when its mark needs a different file on the light theme; otherwise one `logo` serves both.
- Keep `linkLabel` short; it is revealed under the mark on hover and read before the investor name.

## Don’t

- For client marks → use `client-mosaic-section` or `logo-wall-section`.

## Related

- `bento-grid` — the grid the marks are laid in.

## Props

| Prop | Type | Default | Required | Description |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `eyebrow` | `string` | `''` | false | Eyebrow above the section title. |
| `title` | `string` | `''` | false | Headline of the band, rendered by the centered section title as its `h2`; the band renders no header when empty. |
| `linkLabel` | `string` | `''` | false | Line revealed under a mark on hover, and the start of each link's accessible name. |
| `items` | `InvestorMarksSectionItem[]` | `[]` | false | The investors, in order; each is `{ name, logo, logoLight?, href }` where `logo` is the mark on the dark theme and `logoLight` the one on the light theme. |
| `anchor` | `string` | `''` | false | In-page id set on the band so a `#hash` link can scroll to it. |

## States

- Visual states: `default`, `hover` and `focus-visible` on each cell (fill rises, mark lifts, the link line appears)
- Empty: when `items` is empty the band renders nothing

## Accessibility

- Visible focus: each cell link carries `focus-visible:ring-2 focus-visible:ring-(--ring-color) focus-visible:ring-inset` and shows the same reveal as hover.
- Keyboard map: `Tab` moves between investor links; `Enter` follows one (opens in a new tab).
- ARIA: the band is a `<section>` named by its title (`aria-label`); each link's accessible name is "`linkLabel`, `name` (opens in a new tab)"; the revealed line is `aria-hidden` since the name already carries it; the two theme marks share the investor's name as `alt`.
- Contrast ≥4.5:1 (text) / ≥3:1 (large + icons): link line `var(--text-default)` on `var(--bg-hover)`.
- `motion-reduce:transition-none motion-reduce:transform-none` on every transition above.
- Touch target ≥40×40 px — each link is a full 12rem-tall cell.

## Examples

### Default

```vue
<script setup>
  import monasheesExtendedMono from '@aziontech/webkit/assets/monashees-extended-mono.svg'
  import monasheesExtendedReversed from '@aziontech/webkit/assets/monashees-extended-reversed.svg'
  import qualcommExtendedMono from '@aziontech/webkit/assets/qualcomm-extended-mono.svg'
  import qualcommExtendedReversed from '@aziontech/webkit/assets/qualcomm-extended-reversed.svg'
  import InvestorMarksSection from '@aziontech/webkit/investor-marks-section'
</script>

<template>
  <InvestorMarksSection
    eyebrow="Investors"
    title="Backed by visionary investors"
    link-label="Visit website"
    :items="[
      {
        name: 'monashees+',
        logo: monasheesExtendedReversed,
        logoLight: monasheesExtendedMono,
        href: 'https://www.monashees.com'
      },
      {
        name: 'Qualcomm Ventures',
        logo: qualcommExtendedReversed,
        logoLight: qualcommExtendedMono,
        href: 'https://www.qualcommventures.com'
      }
    ]"
  />
</template>
```
