# Job Posting Section

> The body of a careers posting page, under the posting's title band: the description in rows, one per section of the posting (a heading, then its paragraphs and bullet lists), beside a sticky rail that restates the posting (its area, its title and its facets such as department, location and contract) with the link to the application; then the application form, and a dialog that confirms a sent application with a way back to the open positions. From the design sample's careers job page. It is a page section — the site places it from a `{ _id: 'job-posting-section', props }` entry and binds `props` straight onto it — and it composes `section-module`, `registration-frame`, `section-title`, `eyebrow`, `button`, `field`, `label`, `input`, `phone-input`, `file-dropzone`, `file-token-group`, `alert`, `dialog` and `panel`. It collects data, so it follows the forms exception of the page-section contract: it validates on submit only and emits `submit`; the site sends the application and hands the outcome back through `loading`, `submitted` and `failed`.

- Import: `import JobPostingSection from '@aziontech/webkit/job-posting-section'`
- Category: section · Surface: site · Structure: monolithic

## When to use

- On the page of one open position, under a `hero-section` (`variant: 'title-band'`) that carries the posting's title and its apply link.

## Do

- Write each section as a short heading and its `blocks`: a `text` block per paragraph, a `list` block per bullet list, in the posting's order.
- Keep `facets` to the four the listing states (department, location, work arrangement, contract), each with a glyph.
- Set `form.anchor` (for example `apply`) and point the title band's action at `#apply`; the rail's apply button links there too.
- Put the format and size limits of a `file` field in its `helper`, and the accepted types in `accept`.
- Mark only what the team needs as `required`; say "Optional." in the `helper` of the rest.
- Send the application from the `submit` handler, read the file from `new FormData(event.target)`, and set `loading`, then `submitted` or `failed`.

## Don’t

- For the list of open positions → use `listing-index-section`.
- For a lead or contact form at the top of a landing page → use `hero-form-section`.
- For a newsletter sign-up at the end of a page → use `newsletter-section`.

## Related

- `hero-section` — the title band above the posting; its action links to `#` plus `form.anchor`.
- `hero-form-section` — the sibling page section that also emits `submit` and takes `loading`, `submitted` and `failed`.

## Props

| Prop | Type | Default | Required | Description |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `form` | `JobPostingSectionForm` | `—` | true | The application form: `{ title, description?, submitLabel, anchor?, ariaLabel?, fields }`, where `anchor` is the id of the apply section the rail button links to and a field is `{ name, type, label, required?, helper?, placeholder?, accept?, autocomplete?, country? }`. |
| `messages` | `JobPostingSectionMessages` | `—` | true | Copy of the form and its outcomes: `{ missing, invalidEmail, invalidUrl, error, successTitle, successDescription, done, browse }`. |
| `anchor` | `string` | `''` | false | In-page id set on the band so a `#hash` link can scroll to it. |
| `area` | `string` | `''` | false | Area the posting is filed under, the overline of the rail. |
| `title` | `string` | `''` | false | Title of the posting, restated at the top of the rail; the band renders nothing when empty. |
| `facets` | `JobPostingSectionFacet[]` | `[]` | false | Facts about the posting listed in the rail, in order; each is `{ label, icon? }`. |
| `applyLabel` | `string` | `''` | false | Label of the rail button that links to the application form; the button is left out when empty. |
| `sections` | `JobPostingSectionSection[]` | `[]` | false | The description, one row per section in order; each is `{ title, blocks }` where a block is `{ variant: 'text', text }` or `{ type: 'list', items }`. |
| `secondaryAction` | `SectionTitleAction` | `undefined` | false | Link in the confirmation dialog back to the open positions; `{ label, href }`. Omitted when absent. |
| `loading` | `boolean` | `false` | false | The site is sending the application: the submit button shows its loading state, every control is disabled and a submit is ignored. |
| `submitted` | `boolean` | `false` | false | The site sent the application: the confirmation dialog opens. |
| `failed` | `boolean` | `false` | false | The site could not send the application: `messages.error` shows under the submit button. |

## Events

| Event | Payload | Notes |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `submit` | `(event: SubmitEvent, values: Record<string, string>)` |  |

## States

- Visual states: `default`, `invalid`, `loading`, `submitted`, `failed`
- `invalid`: on submit, an empty required field shows `messages.missing`, a malformed email `messages.invalidEmail` and a URL that does not start with `http://` or `https://` `messages.invalidUrl` under the field; focus moves to the first field in error; editing the field (or picking a file) clears its message; nothing is emitted
- `loading` (prop): the submit button shows its loading state, the `fieldset` and every control are disabled and a submit emits nothing; `data-loading` on the root
- `submitted` (prop): when it turns on, the confirmation dialog opens with `messages.successTitle`, `messages.successDescription`, the `secondaryAction` link and a `messages.done` button; closing the dialog (Done, the close button, `Escape` or the overlay) clears the form; `data-submitted` on the root
- `failed` (prop): a danger `Alert` with `messages.error` shows under the submit button; the form stays editable; `data-failed` on the root
- File field: an inline `file-dropzone` with a browse button takes one file; once picked, the file shows in a `file-token-group` whose remove button clears it and brings the dropzone back
- Description: each section row after the first draws a rule above it (`data-ruled`); a `list` block is a bulleted list
- Rail: shown from the large breakpoint up, sticky under the site header while the description scrolls; the apply button renders only when `applyLabel` is set
- Disabled, readonly: not states of the band; `loading` is the only lock
- Empty: when `title` is empty the band renders nothing but its root; with no `sections` the description column is empty and the rail still renders; with no `facets` the facet list is left out

## Accessibility

- Visible focus: owned by the composed `button`, `input`, `phone-input`, `file-token-group` and `dialog` controls.
- Keyboard map: `Tab` walks the rail's apply link, then the fields in order (the file field's browse button, or its remove button once a file is picked), then the submit button; `Enter` in a text field or on the submit button submits; `Enter` or `Space` on the browse button opens the file picker. In the confirmation dialog `Tab` cycles the secondary action, Done and the close button (focus is trapped), and `Escape` closes it and returns focus (`dialog`'s Dialog (Modal) pattern).
- ARIA: the root is a plain `div` (the posting's title is the title band's heading); each description section heads with an `h2` through `section-title`; the rail restates the title as a paragraph, not a heading, so the outline lists the posting once. The fields sit in a `fieldset` whose visually hidden `legend` is `form.ariaLabel`, or `form.title`; each control is labelled through its own `Field.Label` with an id from `useId`, carrying the persistent Required tag of a `required` field from first render. An `email` field is an `<input type="email">` with `autocomplete="email"` and `spellcheck="false"`, a `url` field a text input with `inputmode="url"`. The file field's dropzone is a named `group`; its browse button is named `messages.browse` followed by the field label. Validation runs on submit only: a required field carries `required` and `aria-required` from the first render, and a submit that finds it empty or malformed sets the red `invalid` state (`aria-invalid`); the message is the field's helper text. The failure message is an `alert`; the confirmation is a modal `dialog` named by its title and described by its description.
- Contrast ≥4.5:1 (text) / ≥3:1 (large + icons): owned by the composed components; copy uses the text tokens above.
- `motion-reduce:transition-none motion-reduce:transform-none` — the band adds no motion; the composed button, dropzone and dialog carry their own fallback.
- Touch target ≥40×40 px — the text fields, the buttons and the dialog actions are large or medium controls at least 40 px tall.

## Examples

### Default

```vue
<script setup>
  import JobPostingSection from '@aziontech/webkit/job-posting-section'
  import { nextTick, ref } from 'vue'

  const facets = [
    {
      label: 'Engineering',
      icon: 'az az-sitemap'
    },
    {
      label: 'San Francisco, California',
      icon: 'az az-map-pin'
    },
    {
      label: 'Hybrid',
      icon: 'az az-building'
    },
    {
      label: 'Full-time',
      icon: 'az az-clock'
    }
  ]

  const sections = [
    {
      title: 'About Acme',
      blocks: [
        {
          type: 'text',
          text: 'Acme builds the platform teams use to ship and run their applications worldwide. We are a small team with a big footprint, and we care about tools that get out of the way.'
        }
      ]
    },
    {
      title: 'About the role',
      blocks: [
        {
          type: 'text',
          text: 'As a Senior Platform Engineer you will turn quality into a built-in capability of our engineering platform: automation, internal tools and standards that remove manual steps from the delivery cycle.'
        },
        {
          type: 'text',
          text: 'You will work with the development, platform and infrastructure teams on self-service tools, standard pipelines and automated release checks.'
        }
      ]
    },
    {
      title: 'What you will do',
      blocks: [
        {
          type: 'list',
          items: [
            'Build and evolve standard CI/CD pipelines for tests, checks and releases.',
            'Write internal frameworks and tools for test automation.',
            'Automate functional, integration and end-to-end tests.',
            'Guide engineers in testing and automation practices.'
          ]
        }
      ]
    },
    {
      title: 'What you bring',
      blocks: [
        {
          type: 'list',
          items: [
            'Experience with test automation and quality engineering.',
            'Solid experience with CI/CD and pipeline automation.',
            'Tooling written in TypeScript, Python or Go.',
            'Working knowledge of Linux and containers.'
          ]
        }
      ]
    }
  ]

  const form = {
    anchor: 'apply',
    title: 'Do you want to build with us?',
    description: 'Fill in the form to apply for this position.',
    submitLabel: 'Submit application',
    fields: [
      {
        name: 'name',
        type: 'text',
        label: 'Name',
        required: true,
        autocomplete: 'name'
      },
      {
        name: 'email',
        type: 'email',
        label: 'Email address',
        required: true,
        helper: 'We answer every application at this address.'
      },
      {
        name: 'phone',
        type: 'phone',
        label: 'Contact number',
        required: true
      },
      {
        name: 'linkedin',
        type: 'url',
        label: 'LinkedIn profile URL',
        helper: 'Optional.'
      },
      {
        name: 'resume',
        type: 'file',
        label: 'Resume',
        required: true,
        accept: 'application/pdf,.docx,.doc',
        helper: '.pdf, .docx or .doc, up to 5 MB'
      }
    ]
  }

  const messages = {
    missing: 'This field is required.',
    invalidEmail: 'Enter an email address like name@example.com.',
    invalidUrl: 'Enter the full URL, starting with https://.',
    error: 'We could not send your application. Try again in a few minutes.',
    successTitle: 'Application sent',
    successDescription:
      'Thank you for applying. We read every application and will reply by email.',
    done: 'Done',
    browse: 'Browse files'
  }

  const submitted = ref(false)

  const send = async () => {
    submitted.value = false
    await nextTick()
    submitted.value = true
  }

    area="Engineering"

  />
</template>
```

<!-- components/listing-index-section.md -->

# Listing Index Section

> The full, browsable listing of a page such as the Careers jobs list: a headline over a toolbar (a search field, one select per facet and one for the order) and every row under its group, as a table from the small breakpoint up and as a list of items below it, with an empty state when nothing matches. A row may carry its client's mark, drawn before the name on the table and above it on phones; a pointer click anywhere on a table row follows the row link; the other columns are metadata in the small overline, each cut to one line with its full text as a tooltip; group headings stick under the site header while their rows scroll. Below the large breakpoint the toolbar folds to the search field and a filter button that reveals the selects, with each applied facet listed under it as a removable chip. A select with more than seven options gains an option search. Search, facets and order are local state over the rows the page carries; a facet is seeded from `?<id>=` in the URL when the value is one the page offers. It is a page section — the site places it from a `{ _id: 'listing-index-section', props }` entry and binds `props` straight onto it — so its whole API is the props below, and it composes `section-module`, `section-title`, `registration-frame`, `input`, `select`, `table`, `item`, `chip`, `empty-state` and `button` rather than owning any anatomy of its own.

- Import: `import ListingIndexSection from '@aziontech/webkit/listing-index-section'`
- Category: section · Surface: site · Structure: monolithic

## When to use

- Use ListingIndexSection for a filterable list of non-article rows (jobs): a listing a reader narrows by text and by a few facets, such as open positions grouped by department.
- When every row links to its own page and the listing fits in the page JSON.

## Do

- Name the first column after what the row is (`Role`); it is the row's link, and the second column describes the row on phones.
- Give every row the facet values it carries under the facet's `id`, so the selects narrow it.
- Keep `groups` in the order the reader scans; rows of a group not listed there are not shown.
- Make the first entry of `sorts` the order the page is authored in, with an empty `by`.

## Don’t

- For a short, unfiltered preview of the same rows → use `listing-table-section`.
- For a data table with sorting, selection and pagination inside the product → use `table`.
- For a grid of linked topic cards → use `subject-grid-section`.
- For the full filterable list of articles or resources → use `article-index-section`.
- For a card grid of customer stories with search and facets → use `story-index-section`.

## Related

- `table` — the composed rows and cells the listing renders from the small breakpoint up.
- `item` — the list rows the listing renders below the small breakpoint.
- `empty-state` — the state shown when the search and facets match nothing.

## Props

| Prop | Type | Default | Required | Description |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `anchor` | `string` | `''` | false | In-page `id` of the band, the target of a `#hash` link. |
| `title` | `string` | `''` | false | Headline over the listing, rendered by the section title as its `h2` and naming the band. |
| `columns` | `ListingIndexSectionColumn[]` | `[]` | false | The columns, left to right; each is `{ key, label, grow?, align? }`. The first names the row and links it, the second describes it on phones. |
| `data` | `ListingIndexSectionRow[]` | `[]` | false | Every row, in source order; each is `{ href, group, values, facets, mark? }` where `values` and `facets` are keyed by column and facet and `mark` is the client's `{ name, logo }`. |
| `groups` | `string[]` | `[]` | false | The groups the rows are listed under, in order; a group with no matching row is not shown. |
| `facets` | `ListingIndexSectionFacet[]` | `[]` | false | The facet selects; each is `{ id, label, allLabel, options }` where `id` matches a row facet and seeds it from `?id=` in the URL, and every option is `{ value, label }`. |
| `sorts` | `ListingIndexSectionSort[]` | `[]` | false | The orders, the first the default; each is `{ value, label, by? }` where `by` is the column it sorts on and an empty `by` keeps the source order. |
| `sortAriaLabel` | `string` | `''` | false | Accessible name of the order select. |
| `searchPlaceholder` | `string` | `''` | false | Placeholder of the search field from the large breakpoint up; the field is omitted when this and `searchAriaLabel` are both empty. |
| `searchShortPlaceholder` | `string` | `''` | false | Shorter placeholder of the search field below the large breakpoint; empty uses `searchPlaceholder`. |
| `searchAriaLabel` | `string` | `''` | false | Accessible name of the search field. |
| `emptyTitle` | `string` | `''` | false | Headline of the state shown when nothing matches; the state renders only with it. |
| `emptyDescription` | `string` | `''` | false | Sentence under the empty-state headline. |
| `emptyActionLabel` | `string` | `''` | false | Label of the empty-state button that clears the search and every facet; no button renders without it. |
| `filterLabel` | `string` | `''` | false | Label of the filter button below the large breakpoint; empty uses the locale catalog (`Filters`). |

## States

- Visual states: `default`, `filtered`, `empty`
- Filtered: the search matches a row when any of its values contains the query, ignoring case; every facet set to a value keeps only the rows carrying it; the order select sorts on its column or keeps the source order
- Empty: when the search and facets match no row, `empty-state` replaces the table and the list, and its `emptyActionLabel` button clears the search and every facet; without `emptyTitle`, nothing is shown in their place
- Seeded: on mount a facet takes the value of `?<id>=` in the URL when it is one of its options
- Absent: when `data` is empty the band renders nothing — not the header, not the toolbar
- The table renders from the small breakpoint up, the list below it; below the large breakpoint the search field shows `searchShortPlaceholder`
- Folded toolbar (below the large breakpoint): a filter button (`aria-expanded`, `aria-controls`) beside the search field shows or hides the facet and order selects, stacked (the panel carries `data-state="open|closed"`); its label carries the number of applied facets; every facet set to a value renders a removable `chip` (`<facet label>: <option label>`) whose remove clears that facet. From the large breakpoint the selects sit on the toolbar row and the button and chips are hidden (CSS only, so server and client markup match)
- Option search: a facet or order select with more than seven options renders a search field above its options and a `status` line when nothing matches
- Marked: a row with `mark` draws the client's logo (flattened to one ink that inverts in the dark theme) before the name in the table and above the title in the list
- Whole-row navigation: a click on a table row outside its link is forwarded to the link, keeping `ctrl`/`meta`/`shift`/`alt`; a click that ends a text selection is not forwarded
- Metadata cells (every column after the first) render `text-overline-sm`, truncated to one line, with their full value as `title`; the list's description renders `text-overline-sm`
- Sticky groups: each group heading row sticks under `var(--layout-header-height)` while its rows scroll
- Server render and hydration: the large-breakpoint check runs only after mount, so the server HTML and the first client render both use `shortPlaceholder` and match; the full placeholder applies on mount.

## Accessibility

- Visible focus: owned by the composed `input`, `select` and `button`; each row link draws `focus-visible:ring-2 focus-visible:ring-(--ring-color)`.
- Keyboard map: `Tab` moves through the filter button (below the large breakpoint), the search field, the selects, the applied-filter chips and the row links in reading order; a select opens on `Enter`, `Space` or `ArrowDown`, moves with the arrows and closes on `Escape` (all `select`'s own); `Enter` follows a row link.
- ARIA: the band is a `<section>` named by its title (`aria-label`) when one is set; the toolbar is a `role="search"` region; the search field is named by `searchAriaLabel`, each facet select by its `label` and the order select by `sortAriaLabel`; the filter button reports `aria-expanded` and controls the selects; each applied chip's remove button is named by the chip; a client mark is named by the client; each group is an `h3`; the table is `table`'s `role="table"`, and the phone list is `item`'s `role="list"` of `role="listitem"` rows.
- Contrast ≥4.5:1 (text) / ≥3:1 (large + icons): row links and group headings `var(--text-default)`, headers and meta `var(--text-muted)`.
- `motion-reduce:transition-none motion-reduce:transform-none` — the field, select and button transitions belong to the composed components, which carry their own reduced-motion fallback; this band adds no motion.
- Touch target ≥40×40 px — the search field, selects and empty-state button are large; each phone row link stretches over its whole item and each table row is a whole-row target.

## Examples

### Default

```vue

</script>

  <ListingIndexSection
    title="Open positions"
    :columns="[
      { key: 'role', label: 'Role', grow: 2 },
      { key: 'team', label: 'Team and location', grow: 2 },
      { key: 'type', label: 'Work type', align: 'end' }
    ]"
    :data="[
      {
        href: '/careers/software-engineer-go',
        group: 'Engineering',
        values: {
          role: 'Software Engineer (Go)',
          team: 'Engineering · San Francisco',
          type: 'Hybrid · Full-time'
        },
        facets: { area: ['Engineering'], location: ['San Francisco'] }
      },
      {
        href: '/careers/grc-analyst',
        group: 'Security',
        values: {
          role: 'GRC Analyst (Governance, Risk & Compliance)',
          team: 'Compliance and Corporate IT · San Francisco / New York',
          type: 'Hybrid · Full-time'
        },
        facets: { area: ['Security'], location: ['San Francisco', 'New York'] }
      }
    ]"
    :groups="['Engineering', 'Security']"
    :facets="[
      {
        id: 'area',
        label: 'Department',
        allLabel: 'All departments',
        options: [
          { value: 'Engineering', label: 'Engineering' },
          { value: 'Security', label: 'Security' }
        ]
      },
      {
        id: 'location',
        label: 'Location',
        allLabel: 'All locations',
        options: [
          { value: 'New York', label: 'New York, United States' },
          { value: 'San Francisco', label: 'San Francisco, United States' }
        ]
      }
    ]"
    :sorts="[
      { value: 'newest', label: 'Newest posted', by: '' },
      { value: 'title', label: 'Title (A–Z)', by: 'role' }
    ]"
    sort-aria-label="Sort positions"
    search-placeholder="Search by title, team, or location"
    search-short-placeholder="Search positions"
    search-aria-label="Search positions"
    empty-title="Nothing here yet"
    empty-description="No open positions match your search."
    empty-action-label="View all open positions"
  />
```

<!-- components/listing-table-section.md -->

# Listing Table Section

> A titled preview of a longer listing: a few linked rows in columns, and one outlined button into the full list. It is a page section — the site places it from a `{ _id: 'listing-table-section', props }` entry and binds `props` straight onto it — and it composes `section-module`, `section-title`, `registration-frame`, `table` and, below `sm`, `item`. Each row is one link named by its first column; a pointer click anywhere on the row follows that link (with the same modifier keys), and the link stays the keyboard and screen-reader path. The other columns are metadata in the small overline, each cut to one line with its full text as a tooltip. Below `sm` the same rows become a list of items, the first column as the title and the second as the description in the small overline, with the button going full width.

- Import: `import ListingTableSection from '@aziontech/webkit/listing-table-section'`
- Category: section · Surface: site · Structure: monolithic

## When to use

- To preview the latest entries of a listing (open roles, releases, events) with a way into the full list.

## Do

- Keep three columns at most; the first names the row and the second describes it on phones.
- Give the wide columns `grow: 2` and align a short trailing column to `end`.
- Keep the preview to a handful of rows and point `primaryAction` at the full listing.

## Don’t

- For a filterable or paginated listing → use `listing-index-section`.
- For a comparison of plans or features → use the pricing matrix.

## Related

- `table` — the composition table the wide layout renders.
- `listing-index-section` — the full listing this band links into.

## Props

| Prop | Type | Default | Required | Description |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `anchor` | `string` | `''` | false | In-page id set on the band so a `#hash` link can scroll to it. |
| `title` | `string` | `''` | false | Headline of the band, rendered by the left-aligned section title as its `h2`; the band renders no header when empty. |
| `columns` | `ListingTableSectionColumn[]` | `[]` | false | The columns left to right; each is `{ key, label, grow?, align? }` where `key` picks the row value, `grow` is the flex weight (1, 2 or 3) and `align` is `start` or `end`. The first column names the row and the second describes it on phones. |
| `data` | `ListingTableSectionRow[]` | `[]` | false | The linked rows, in order; each is `{ href, values }` where `values` maps a column key to its text. |
| `primaryAction` | `SectionTitleAction` | `undefined` | false | Outlined button into the full listing, in the table footer; `{ label, href, trailing? }` where `trailing` adds the chevron. Omitted when absent. |

## States

- Visual states: `default`, `hover` (row fill `var(--bg-hover)` in both the table and the list)
- Whole-row navigation: a click on a row outside its link is forwarded to the link, keeping `ctrl`/`meta`/`shift`/`alt`; a click that ends a text selection is not forwarded
- Metadata cells (every column after the first) render `text-overline-sm`, truncated to one line, with their full value as `title`
- Empty: when `data` or `columns` is empty the band renders nothing — not the header, not the frame
- The header renders only when `title` is set; the footer button only when `primaryAction` is set
- Below `sm` the table is hidden and the rows render as an item list

## Accessibility

- Visible focus: owned by the row links and the composed `button`.
- Keyboard map: `Tab` reaches each row's link and then the button; `Enter` follows it.
- ARIA: the band is a `<section>` named by its title (`aria-label`) when one is set; the table keeps its native column headers; the phone list is the same rows as list items.
- Contrast ≥4.5:1 (text) / ≥3:1 (large + icons): row titles `var(--text-default)`, values `var(--text-muted)`.
- `motion-reduce:transition-none motion-reduce:transform-none` — the band adds no motion; the button carries its own fallback.
- Touch target ≥40×40 px — each phone row is a whole-item link; the button is large.

## Examples

### Default

```vue
    title="Latest roles"

      { key: 'role', label: 'Role', grow: 2 },
      { key: 'team', label: 'Team and location', grow: 2 },
      { key: 'type', label: 'Work type', align: 'end' }
    ]"
    :data="[
      {
        href: '/careers/senior-platform-engineer',
        values: {
          role: 'Senior Platform Engineer',
          team: 'Delivery Engineering · San Francisco',
          type: 'Hybrid · Full-time'
        }
      },
      {
```

<!-- components/logo-wall-section.md -->

# Logo Wall Section

> The customers band: a section title over a framed logo wall of linked client marks with one signed quote in its aside, closed by a call to action. It is a page section — the site places it from a `{ _id: 'logo-wall-section', props }` entry and binds `props` straight onto it — and it composes `section-module`, `section-title`, `registration-frame`, `logo-wall`, `quote` and `button`.

- Import: `import LogoWallSection from '@aziontech/webkit/logo-wall-section'`
- Category: section · Surface: site · Structure: monolithic

## When to use

- Use LogoWallSection for customer logos plus one featured quote.
- To prove adoption with marks and let one customer speak, early on a home or solution page.

## Do

- Eight or nine logos fill the rectangle wall beside the aside.
- Pass client registry entries (`@aziontech/webkit/assets/client-registry`) as they stand: each carries `name`, `logo` and either `logoLight` (a light-theme drawing) or `artwork` (which theme a single file must be inverted on), and every mark renders through `Logo`.
- Without a registry entry, set `logoTreatment` per mark: `monochrome` for a mark drawn in one ink that must follow the theme, `knockout` for a black mark on a coloured fill, `color` for a colour mark, and `artwork: 'light'` for a reversed mark that must flip on the light theme; give a `logoLight` file instead when the light theme needs a different drawing.
- Give the quote's company mark a `logoLight` (or an `artwork`) the same way, so it stays legible on both themes.
- Keep to one or two actions under the quote: the primary (`secondary` variant) and an optional outlined `secondaryAction`.

## Don’t

- For several quotes in turn → use `client-quotes-section`.
- For marks with photos and stories in a mosaic → use `client-mosaic-section`.

## Related

- `logo-wall` — the wall the marks are laid in, with the quote in its aside.
- `logo` — draws every mark (the wall's and the quote's) right on both themes.

## Props

| Prop | Type | Default | Required | Description |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `variant` | `LogoWallSectionVariant` | `'wall'` | false | Layout of the band: `wall` sets the client marks with the quote in their aside, `quote` sets the quote alone as a full-width band (the design sample's QuoteBand) and needs no logos. |
| `quoteVariant` | `LogoWallSectionQuoteVariant` | `'signed'` | false | Register of the quote in the `quote` layout: the featured `highlight` or the band-sized `signed` sentence. |
| `anchor` | `string` | `''` | false | In-page id set on the band so a hash link can target it. |
| `eyebrow` | `string` | `''` | false | Eyebrow above the section title. |
| `title` | `string` | `''` | false | Headline of the band, rendered by the section title as its `h2`; the band renders no header when empty. |
| `ariaLabel` | `string` | `''` | false | Accessible name of the logo wall. |
| `logos` | `LogoWallSectionLogo[]` | `[]` | false | The client marks, in order; each is `{ name, logo, logoLight?, logoTreatment?, artwork?, href? }` where `logoTreatment` is the shared `LogoTreatment` (`'color'`, `'monochrome'` or `'knockout'`, the `Logo` looks) and `artwork` is the registry's `'color'`, `'light'` or `'dark'`. |
| `quote` | `LogoWallSectionQuote` | `undefined` | false | The signed quote in the wall's aside; `{ text, name, jobTitle, logo, logoAlt, logoLight?, artwork? }`. Omitted when absent. |
| `primaryAction` | `SectionTitleAction` | `undefined` | false | Button under the quote; `{ label, href, trailing?, target? }`. Omitted when absent. |
| `secondaryAction` | `SectionTitleAction` | `undefined` | false | Outlined button beside the primary one under the quote; `{ label, href, trailing?, target? }`. Omitted when absent. |

## States

- Visual states: `default`
- Empty: when `logos` is empty the band renders nothing
- Each mark is a `Logo` carrying `data-treatment` (the `logoTreatment`, default `color`), `data-variant`, `data-artwork` and `data-route` (`pair`, `single` or `wordmark`), so the theme-following inks are driven by data attributes
- Not applicable: `loading`, `error`, `disabled`, `readonly`, `invalid`, `open`/`closed`

## Accessibility

- Visible focus: owned by `logo-wall` (each mark link) and `button`.
- Keyboard map: `Tab` moves between mark links and the action; `Enter` follows.
- ARIA: the band is a `<section>` named by its title (`aria-label`); the wall is `logo-wall`'s own list named by `ariaLabel`; every mark is a `Logo` (`role="img"`) named by the client `name`, announced once even when a light/dark pair renders; the quote is a `blockquote` signed by its author.
- Contrast ≥4.5:1 (text) / ≥3:1 (large + icons): owned by the composed components; marks follow the theme through their treatment.
- `motion-reduce:transition-none motion-reduce:transform-none` — the band adds no motion.
- Touch target ≥40×40 px — each mark link spans its wall cell.

## Examples

<!-- components/media-split-section.md -->

# Media Split Section

> One claim beside its picture, on its own frame: the copy cell with its overline, headline and sentence on one side, and the media on a raised, pixel-textured surface on the other. It is a page section — the site places it from a `{ _id: 'media-split-section', props }` entry and binds `props` straight onto it — and it composes `section-module`, a framed `media-split`, `button`, `illustration`, `console-mockup` and the webkit scene assets. The media is required and is a drawn asset: an illustration, an image (cover or framed) or a registered scene, of which a product console widget (`widget`) is one; request-flow diagrams and code do not go in a media band; with one action the whole band links to it (unless `linked` is off), with two the band stays unlinked. The media sits beside the copy, or under it when `orientation` is `vertical`.

- Import: `import MediaSplitSection from '@aziontech/webkit/media-split-section'`
- Category: section · Surface: site · Structure: monolithic

## When to use

- Rendering page JSON, or you need a whole band with no custom content → the section (`media-split-section`). Building an SFC by hand, needing slots, or nesting inside another band → the primitive (`media-split`).
- Use MediaSplitSection for one marketing claim beside its picture or scene.
- To make one point beside one picture and move on (a values statement, a single product claim).

## Do

- Give the band exactly one media: `scene`, `widget` (a `ConsoleMockup` name), `image` or `illustration`; when several are set the first of that order wins. A band with a title and no media logs a development warning, and the site-composition check (`test/standards/site-composition.test.mjs`) fails it in the shipped page JSON. A `Diagram` scene counts as no media.
- Set `image.framed` for a picture that is an object rather than a scene (a badge, a logo, a screenshot): it sits whole on a rounded, bordered plate inset in the cell instead of covering it.
- Use `size="large"` for a band that carries only its headline and description.
- Use `orientation="vertical"` for a wide scene that needs the full width of the frame under the copy.
- Turn `linked` off when the media holds controls of its own, so a click in it is not taken as a click on the band.
- Set `anchor` when another part of the page links to the band.
- Give an `image` its `width` and `height` so the page reserves its box before it loads.

## Don’t

- For several claims stacked one after the other → use `media-split-stack-section`.
- For a claim beside the code that delivers it → use `feature-scroll-section` (each item's `code`); a media band shows no code.
- For a request-flow diagram → use `console-showcase-section` with `variant: 'diagram'` (the diagram as its `scene`) or `architecture-tabs-section`; a media band shows no diagram.
- For a full-width terminal session with no copy column → use `cli-terminal-section`.
- For copy beside one piece of media in a hand-built SFC that needs the `media` slot → use `media-split`.

## Related

- `media-split` — the band primitive the section is rendered with.
- `media-split-stack-section` — the stacked sibling for a run of claims.

## Props

| Prop | Type | Default | Required | Description |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `anchor` | `string` | `''` | false | In-page id set on the band so a `#hash` link can scroll to it. |
| `eyebrow` | `string` | `''` | false | Short overline above the headline. |
| `title` | `string` | `''` | false | Headline of the band, rendered by the media split as its `h2` and naming its region; the band renders nothing when empty. |
| `description` | `string` | `''` | false | Paragraph under the headline. |
| `size` | `MediaSplitSectionSize` | `'medium'` | false | Type scale of the copy; `large` sets the headline at heading-xl and centres the copy beside the media, `medium` aligns it to the top of the media. |
| `orientation` | `MediaSplitSectionOrientation` | `'horizontal'` | false | Axis the band splits on; `vertical` stacks the media under the copy for a wide scene. |
| `texture` | `TextureVariant` | `'pixelate'` | false | Texture painted under the media: `dots`, `grid`, `lines`, `dither`, `pixelate` or `none`. |
| `linked` | `boolean` | `true` | false | Links the whole band to a lone action; turn it off when the media holds controls of its own. |
| `scene` | `MediaSplitSectionScene` | `undefined` | false | Scene drawn in the media cell, `{ component, props? }` naming a webkit scene asset other than `Diagram` and the props bound onto it; wins over every other media. |
| `widget` | `ConsoleMockupName` | `undefined` | false | Name of the product console widget drawn in the media cell when no scene is set. |
| `image` | `MediaSplitSectionImage` | `undefined` | false | Picture drawn in the media cell when neither a scene nor a widget is set; `{ src, alt?, width?, height?, framed? }`, where `framed` sets it whole on a rounded plate instead of covering the cell. |
| `illustration` | `string` | `''` | false | Registry name of the illustration drawn in the media cell when no other media is set. |
| `illustrationAriaLabel` | `string` | `''` | false | Accessible name of the illustration; empty keeps it decorative. |
| `primaryAction` | `MediaSplitSectionAction` | `undefined` | false | First button under the copy; `{ label, href, trailing?, icon?, target?, variant? }`. When it is the only action it renders outlined with a trailing chevron, and the whole band links to it; `variant` overrides the drawn variant. |
| `secondaryAction` | `MediaSplitSectionAction` | `undefined` | false | Second, outlined button beside the first; `{ label, href, trailing?, icon?, target?, variant? }`. With two actions the band stays unlinked. |

## Assets

- Illustrations: every valid name is in illustrations.md (MCP `list_assets({ kind: "illustrations" })`). An unknown name renders a "PLACE DESIGN ASSET" placeholder and warns in the console.
- Diagrams: every valid name is in diagrams.md (MCP `list_assets({ kind: "diagrams" })`). An unknown name draws nothing.

## States

- Visual states: `default`
- `size` is forwarded to the media split and drives its type scale and copy alignment
- Empty: when `title` is empty the band renders nothing but its root; with no media set (or only a `Diagram` scene) the media cell is omitted and one development warning names the band
- With exactly one action the band is a link to that action's `href` (unless `linked` is `false`), and that lone action renders as an outlined button closed by the trailing chevron; with two the first is secondary, the second outlined, and the band is not a link
- `size="medium"` aligns the copy to the top of the media, `size="large"` centres it
- `orientation` and `texture` are forwarded to the media split
- Media precedence: `scene`, `widget`, `image`, `illustration`. A widget and a framed image are inset from the cell's edges (`media-split`'s `mediaPadded`); a scene, a plain image (which covers the cell) and an illustration run flush
- A widget plays its beat while the pointer is over the band, and shows its finished state on touch and under reduced motion (owned by `console-mockup`); it draws no collaborator cursor (`cursor` off), so the band reads as the product screen
- Scenes and the widget load on demand (`defineAsyncComponent`)
- An action's `variant` (`secondary`, `outline`, `ghost`) wins over the drawn default; an action's `icon` is drawn before the label when it has no trailing chevron

## Accessibility

- Visible focus: owned by `button` and `media-split`.
- Keyboard map: `Tab` moves through the actions, and through the media link when the band links.
- ARIA: the media split's `<section>` is the band's named region (`aria-label` from `title`), so the root is a plain `div`; the headline is its `h2`; an image is named by `alt`, an illustration by `illustrationAriaLabel`, and both are decorative when empty; a widget is an inert, `aria-hidden` picture of the console, so the copy carries its meaning.
- Contrast ≥4.5:1 (text) / ≥3:1 (large + icons): owned by `media-split` and `button`.
- `motion-reduce:transition-none motion-reduce:transform-none` — the band adds no motion; the scenes stop under reduced motion on their own.
- Touch target ≥40×40 px — the medium buttons meet it.

## Examples

### Default

```vue
<script setup>
  import MediaSplitSection from '@aziontech/webkit/media-split-section'
</script>

<template>
  <MediaSplitSection
    eyebrow="Our values"
    title="Four values shape every role at Azion"
    description="Quality, Drive, Accountability, and Innovation. They guide how we work every day: we set high standards and measure what we deliver, take on hard problems with persistence, own our commitments and mistakes, and use AI and new technology to solve problems in new ways. Together, they help us earn and keep the trust of our customers."
    size="large"
    :scene="{ component: 'BrandGlobeScene' }"
  />
</template>
```

2 more example(s) in [components/media-split-section.md](components/media-split-section.md).

<!-- components/media-split-stack-section.md -->

# Media Split Stack Section

> A run of large media-split bands that pile up under the site header as the page scrolls, each a centered claim with an outlined "learn more" button beside its picture, the whole band linking through. An optional centered section title at the medium size opens the run, as the run headers of the live product pages do. It is a page section — the site places it from a `{ _id: 'media-split-stack-section', props }` entry and binds `props` straight onto it — and it composes `section-module`, `section-title`, `section-stack`, `media-split`, `illustration`, `console-mockup` and `button`. Every band has a picture, and it is a drawn asset: a registered `scene`, a `ConsoleMockup` console mock (`widget`, itself a registered scene), a registry `illustration`, or an `image` URL, plain or `imageFramed` on its own plate. The band type is a union that requires one of them; request-flow diagrams and code do not go in a media band. Every band sets its picture at the closing end, so the run reads down one edge.

- Import: `import MediaSplitStackSection from '@aziontech/webkit/media-split-stack-section'`
- Category: section · Surface: site · Structure: monolithic

## When to use

- Use MediaSplitStackSection for a run of linked claims that pile up as the reader scrolls.
- To list the solution areas or the claims of a page, one large band each, with a link through to its own page.

## Do

- Give every band one picture: `scene`, `widget` (a `ConsoleMockup` name — the product's console screen), `illustration` (a registry name, with `illustrationLabel`) or `image` (a URL, with `imageAlt` when it says something). The type requires one; when several are set the first in that order wins. Page JSON is untyped, so a band with none renders without its media cell, logs a development warning, and fails the site-composition check (`test/standards/site-composition.test.mjs`). A `Diagram` scene counts as none.
- Set `imageFramed` for a picture that is an object rather than a scene — a badge, a logo, a screenshot: it sits whole on a rounded, bordered plate inset in the cell. A plain `image` is scaled to fit the cell and never overflows it.
- Give every band an `action`; it is both the button and the link the whole band follows.

## Don’t

- For a single claim beside its picture or scene → use `media-split-section`.
- For a request-flow diagram → use `console-showcase-section` with `variant: 'diagram'`; for code → use `feature-scroll-section`.
- For small reasons in a grid → use `feature-tiles-section`.

## Related

- `section-stack` — the sticky run the bands pile up in.
- `media-split` — the band each entry renders through.

## Props

| Prop | Type | Default | Required | Description |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `anchor` | `string` | `''` | false | In-page id set on the run so a `#hash` link can scroll to it. |
| `eyebrow` | `string` | `''` | false | Eyebrow above the section title. |
| `title` | `string` | `''` | false | Headline of the run, rendered by the medium centered section title as its `h2`; the run renders no header when empty, and with a header each band's headline drops to an `h3`. |
| `description` | `string` | `''` | false | Supporting sentence under the section title. |
| `bands` | `MediaSplitStackSectionBand[]` | `[]` | false | The bands, in order; each is `{ title, description, eyebrow?, href?, action? }` plus its required picture, one of `scene`, `widget`, `illustration` (with `illustrationLabel?`) or `image` (with `imageAlt?`, `imageFramed?`), where `eyebrow` is the band's overline, `href` the link the whole band follows (falling back to the action's), `scene` a webkit scene asset other than `Diagram`, `widget` a `ConsoleMockup` name, `image` a picture URL, `illustration` a registry name, and `action` `{ label, href }` the outlined button. |

## Assets

- Illustrations: every valid name is in illustrations.md (MCP `list_assets({ kind: "illustrations" })`). An unknown name renders a "PLACE DESIGN ASSET" placeholder and warns in the console.
- Diagrams: every valid name is in diagrams.md (MCP `list_assets({ kind: "diagrams" })`). An unknown name draws nothing.

## States

- Visual states: `default`; hovering a linked band reveals its link affordance (owned by `media-split`)
- Empty: when `bands` is empty the run renders nothing — not the header, not the stack
- The header renders only when `title` is set; a band renders its button only when it has an `action`, links as a whole to its `href` (or its action's when it has none), and renders its media cell only when it has a picture; a band's `eyebrow` sits above its headline
- Band headlines are `h2` when the run has no header and `h3` under the section title, so the outline never repeats a level
- Media precedence per band: `scene`, then `widget`, then `illustration`, then `image`. A widget is inset from the cell's edges (`media-split`'s `mediaPadded`), and so is a framed image; an illustration and a plain image run flush
- A widget plays its beat while the pointer is over its band (`hover`), and shows its finished state on touch and under reduced motion (owned by `console-mockup`); it draws no collaborator cursor (`cursor` off), so the band reads as the product screen
- The widget loads on demand (`defineAsyncComponent`): a run that uses none never downloads it

## Accessibility

- Visible focus: owned by `button` and `media-split`'s media link.
- Keyboard map: `Tab` reaches each band's media link and button; `Enter` follows them.
- ARIA: the run is a `<section>` named by its title (`aria-label`) when one is set; each band is `media-split`'s own section named by the band's title, with its headline as an `h3` under the run's `h2` (an `h2` when the run has no title); illustrations are named by `illustrationLabel` and images by `imageAlt`, or decorative when empty; a widget is an inert, `aria-hidden` picture of the console, so the band's copy carries its meaning.
- Contrast ≥4.5:1 (text) / ≥3:1 (large + icons): owned by `section-title`, `media-split` and `button`.
- `motion-reduce:transition-none motion-reduce:transform-none` — the run adds no motion.
- Touch target ≥40×40 px: the medium buttons and the band links.

## Examples

### Default

```vue

    {
      image: quickStartWithTemplates,
      eyebrow: 'Web applications',

      description: 'Build static sites, run serverless applications, or modernize legacy systems.',
```

<!-- components/network-section.md -->

# Network Section

> The infrastructure band of a marketing page: a tall framed panel with the world network map drawn behind a small start-aligned title, a claims eyebrow and a row of claim chips, closed by a floor of client outcomes (a quote with its highlights and, when given, the client's mark), benefits (a topic each) or stats (headline figures). It is a page section — the site places it from a `{ _id: 'network-section', props }` entry and binds `props` straight onto it — and it composes `section-module`, `registration-frame`, `network-map`, `section-title`, `card-grid`, `quote`, `feature-item` and `stats`. `variant` picks the floor only: `outcomes` renders the quotes on one column per entry — two at least, four at most; `benefits` renders the topics on up to three columns, balanced so every row is full; `stats` renders the figures as a `stats` row. The band above is the same.

- Import: `import NetworkSection from '@aziontech/webkit/network-section'`
- Category: section · Surface: site · Structure: monolithic

## When to use

- To state the network's reach with figures and back it with client results, product benefits or headline figures.

## Do

- Four short `claims`; they are chips, not sentences. Give a claim as `{ label, mobile: false }` to keep it to the medium breakpoint and up when the phone band has room for fewer chips.
- Two to four `outcomes`: the floor sets one column per entry, so it closes the grid; a fifth entry wraps to a second row.
- Three `benefits` (or six), each a short `title`, a `description` and a required `icon` (an Azion icon class) that leads the topic; each renders as a topic with its title as an `h3`.
- Four `stats` on the `stats` floor, each a `value` and a `label`, with an optional `prefix` and `suffix`.
- Give the band an `anchor` when a page links to it with a `#hash`.
- Leave `logo` out of an outcome when the site shows the figure alone; the cell then renders no mark row.
- Use `logoTreatment: 'monochrome'` for a dark mark that must invert in the dark theme; leave `color` for a colour mark.

## Don’t

- For figures alone, without the map band → use `stats-section`.
- For a logo wall with one quote → use `logo-wall-section`.

## Related

- `network-map` — the animated map behind the copy.
- `quote` / `feature-item` — the cell types of the `outcomes` and `benefits` floors.
- `stats` — the row the `stats` floor renders.

## Props

| Prop | Type | Default | Required | Description |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `variant` | `NetworkSectionVariant` | `'outcomes'` | false | Which floor closes the band: `outcomes` renders the client quotes, `benefits` the benefit topics on up to three columns, `stats` the headline figures. |
| `anchor` | `string` | `''` | false | In-page id set on the band so a `#hash` link can scroll to it. |
| `eyebrow` | `string` | `''` | false | Eyebrow above the headline. |
| `title` | `string` | `''` | false | Headline of the band, rendered by the small section title as its `h2`; the band renders nothing when empty. |
| `claimsEyebrow` | `string` | `''` | false | Eyebrow-set line above the claims. |
| `claims` | `(string \| NetworkSectionClaim)[]` | `[]` | false | Short figures rendered as chips under the claims eyebrow, in order; each is a string or `{ label, mobile? }`, where `mobile: false` hides the chip below the medium breakpoint. |
| `outcomes` | `NetworkSectionOutcome[]` | `[]` | false | Client results for the `outcomes` floor, in order, one column each up to four; each is `{ logo?, logoAlt?, logoTreatment?, text, highlights? }` where `logo` is the mark URL (the cell renders no mark without it), `logoAlt` names the client, `logoTreatment` is `'monochrome'` or `'color'` (`LogoTreatment` without `knockout`), and `highlights` are the phrases of `text` set in the accent. |
| `benefits` | `NetworkSectionBenefit[]` | `[]` | false | Topics for the `benefits` floor, in order, on up to three columns balanced so every row is full; each is `{ title, description, icon }`, rendered as a topic led by its glyph whose title is an `h3`. |
| `stats` | `NetworkSectionStat[]` | `[]` | false | Figures for the `stats` floor, in order, rendered by `stats`; each is `{ value, label, prefix?, suffix? }`. |

## States

- Visual states: `default`
- `data-variant` mirrors the `variant` prop and selects the floor (`outcomes`, `benefits` or `stats`)
- Empty: when `title` is empty the band renders nothing; when the selected floor's array is empty the band renders without a floor
- An outcome without `logo` renders its quote with no mark row
- The `benefits` floor is `card-grid`'s `balanced` layout with `columns: 3`, so three benefits fill one row and four split 2 × 2; every benefit is a `feature-item` with an `h3`, led by its `icon` glyph
- The `stats` floor is a `stats` row under a rule; `stats` picks its own column count so no cell is left empty
- Claims: a chip is `h-6` with `text-label-sm` below the small breakpoint and `h-7` with `text-label-md` from it; a claim with `mobile: false` carries `data-mobile="hidden"` and is hidden below the medium breakpoint
- Anchor: the root carries `id` = `anchor` when set, with a scroll margin so a `#hash` jump clears a sticky header
- The map renders twice: a faded top-right world from the medium breakpoint up, a centered masked world below it, framed close on the network (`scale` 3.3); both are `network-map`'s own animation

## Accessibility

- Visible focus: nothing in the band is interactive.
- Keyboard map: none.
- ARIA: the band is a `<section>` named by its title (`aria-label`); the headline is the section title's `h2`; the claims are a real list; the maps are decorative and hidden from assistive technology by `network-map`; each outcome's mark carries the client name as `alt`; benefit topics are `h3`s; the `stats` floor is `stats`, one `figure` per stat with its label as the caption. A claim hidden below the medium breakpoint is `display: none` there, so assistive technology skips it too.
- Contrast ≥4.5:1 (text) / ≥3:1 (large + icons): claims eyebrow `var(--text-muted)`, claims `var(--text-default)` on a tinted canvas; the map sits at low opacity behind the copy and never under the text column on large screens.
- `motion-reduce:transition-none motion-reduce:transform-none` — the map animation belongs to `network-map`, which carries its own reduced-motion fallback; this band adds no motion.
- Touch target ≥40×40 px — not applicable, nothing is interactive.

## Examples

<!-- components/newsletter-section.md -->

# Newsletter Section

> The closing band whose ask is a newsletter sign-up: the overline and a large headline on the lead side, a subscribe form labelled by the ask on the other, over a strip lit by the pixelated primary wave, then the hatched closing frame. It is a page section — the site places it from a `{ _id: 'newsletter-section', props }` entry and binds `props` straight onto it — and it composes `section-module`, `registration-frame`, `eyebrow`, `field`, `input`, `button` and `texture`. The block never sends: it validates the address locally (an empty field and a malformed address each show their message under the field) and on a valid submit emits `submit` with the event and `{ email }`; the site posts the address and reports the outcome back through `loading`, `submitted` and `failed`, which the band renders with the messages from `form`.

- Import: `import NewsletterSection from '@aziontech/webkit/newsletter-section'`
- Category: section · Surface: site · Structure: monolithic

## When to use

- Use NewsletterSection when the closing ask is an email field.
- To close the Blog or the Resource Hub on a newsletter subscription.

## Do

- Set `loading` while the address is sent, then `submitted` or `failed` with the outcome.
- Keep `description` to one sentence; it is set as the large headline.
- Write `form.invalidMessage` with an example address so the fix is obvious.
- Place it last on the page, right above `site-footer-section`, after a hatched `section-gap`. It draws the hatched closing frame itself, so nothing follows it inside the page column: no trailing `SectionGap` and no closing `RegistrationFrame` + `Texture` (the `shell/site` close is for a page that ends on any other band). `webkit/no-gap-after-closing-section` flags the doubled close.

## Don’t

- For a close on one or two link actions or a command → use `closing-call-to-action-section`.
- For a close that lists deployable templates → use `template-gallery-section` with `closing`.
- For a lead form with several fields → use `hero-form-section`.

## Related

- `closing-call-to-action-section` — the closing band whose ask is a link action.
- `alert` — renders the success and failure messages.

## Props

| Prop | Type | Default | Required | Description |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `form` | `NewsletterSectionForm` | `—` | true | Copy of the subscribe form: `{ placeholder, submitLabel, successMessage, errorMessage, requiredMessage, invalidMessage }`. |
| `anchor` | `string` | `''` | false | In-page id set on the band so a `#hash` link can scroll to it. |
| `eyebrow` | `string` | `''` | false | Eyebrow on the lead side, closed by the blinking cursor. |
| `title` | `string` | `''` | false | The ask, rendered as the label of the email field; it also names the band. Empty, the field keeps a visually hidden built-in label. |
| `description` | `string` | `''` | false | The pitch, rendered as the band's large `h2` on the lead side. |
| `loading` | `boolean` | `false` | false | The site is sending the address: the field is disabled, the button shows its loading state and a submit is ignored. |
| `submitted` | `boolean` | `false` | false | The site sent the address: the form gives way to `form.successMessage`. |
| `failed` | `boolean` | `false` | false | The site could not send the address: `form.errorMessage` shows under the form. |
| `hatch` | `boolean` | `true` | false | Draws the hatched closing frame under the band; turn it off when another closing band stacks directly below and draws the shared frame. |

## Events

| Event | Payload | Notes |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `submit` | `(event: SubmitEvent, values: Record<string, string>)` | Fired on a valid submit, not while `loading`; `values` is `{ email }` with the trimmed address. The block never sends it. |

## States

- Visual states: `default`, `invalid`, `loading`, `submitted`, `failed`
- `invalid`: the field is `invalid` with `form.requiredMessage` when empty or `form.invalidMessage` when malformed, under the field; the field is `required` from the first render, so only a failed submit turns it red; focus returns to the field; typing clears it; nothing is emitted
- `loading` (prop): the field is disabled, the button shows its loading state and a submit emits nothing
- `submitted` (prop): the form is replaced by a success `Alert` with `form.successMessage`, which takes focus when focus was in the form
- `failed` (prop): a danger `Alert` with `form.errorMessage` shows under the form, which stays editable
- `hatch` off: the band renders without its closing frame, so the closing band stacked below draws the page's one close
- The eyebrow is plain text with no `//` prefix: that prefix belongs to the hero and the pricing sections only.

## Accessibility

- Visible focus: owned by `input` and `button`.
- Keyboard map: `Tab` reaches the field then the button; `Enter` in the field or on the button submits.
- ARIA: the band is a `<section>` named by its title (`aria-label`); the ask is a `<label for>` bound to the field with `useId`, and without a title a visually hidden label ("Email address") keeps the field named; the field is an `<input type="email">` with `autocomplete="email"` and `spellcheck="false"`; the validation message is the field's helper text, the field carries `aria-required` from the first render, and an empty or malformed submit sets `aria-invalid`. The success message is a `status` region and the failure message an `alert`. The texture strip is `aria-hidden`.
- Contrast ≥4.5:1 (text) / ≥3:1 (large + icons): headline and ask `var(--text-default)`.
- `motion-reduce:transition-none motion-reduce:transform-none` — the band adds no motion; the texture and the button carry their own fallback.
- Touch target ≥40×40 px — the field and the button are large.

## Examples

### Default

```vue
<script setup>
  import NewsletterSection from '@aziontech/webkit/newsletter-section'
  import { ref } from 'vue'

  const form = {
    placeholder: 'Your email',
    submitLabel: 'Subscribe',
    successMessage: 'Thanks for subscribing to the Azion newsletter',
    errorMessage: 'We could not subscribe you. Try again.',
    requiredMessage: 'Enter your email address.',
    invalidMessage: 'Enter an email address like name@example.com.'
  }

  const subscribedEmail = ref('')

  const onSubmit = (event, values) => {
    subscribedEmail.value = values.email
  }
</script>

<template>
  <NewsletterSection
    anchor="contact"
    eyebrow="Stay up to date"
    title="Subscribe to our newsletter"
    description="Product updates, event highlights, and industry insights, delivered to your inbox."
    :form="form"
    :submitted="subscribedEmail !== ''"
    @submit="onSubmit"
  />
</template>
```

2 more example(s) in [components/newsletter-section.md](components/newsletter-section.md).

# Photo Marquee Section

> The office band of the careers page: a horizontal section title over a framed strip of photographs of varied aspect ratios that scrolls continuously and pauses on hover, on focus or from its pause control; the loop is the one `marquee` runs on. It is a page section — the site places it from a `{ _id: 'photo-marquee-section', props }` entry and binds `props` straight onto it — and it composes `section-module`, `section-title` and `registration-frame`.

- Import: `import PhotoMarqueeSection from '@aziontech/webkit/photo-marquee-section'`
- Category: section · Surface: site · Structure: monolithic

## When to use

- Use PhotoMarqueeSection for a decorative moving strip of photos.
- To show a place or a team in pictures without a grid.

## Do

- Give every photo its `width` and `height` so the strip reserves each slot before the picture loads.
- Leave `duration` at `0` to let the strip pace itself (ten seconds per photo).

## Don’t

- For quotes → use `recognition-marquee-section`.

## Related

- `recognition-marquee-section` — the sibling strip of quotes with the same loop.

## Props

| Prop | Type | Default | Required | Description |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `title` | `string` | `''` | false | Headline of the band, rendered by the horizontal section title as its `h2`; the band renders no header when empty. |
| `description` | `string` | `''` | false | Paragraph beside the headline. |
| `descriptionWidth` | `PhotoMarqueeSectionDescriptionWidth` | `'narrow'` | false | Width the description is capped at: `narrow` or `wide`. |
| `ariaLabel` | `string` | `''` | false | Accessible name of the scrolling region. |
| `duration` | `number` | `0` | false | Seconds one loop of the strip takes; `0` paces it at ten seconds per photo. |
| `photos` | `PhotoMarqueeSectionPhoto[]` | `[]` | false | The photographs, in order; each is `{ src, alt, width, height }`. |
| `anchor` | `string` | `''` | false | In-page id set on the band so a `#hash` link can scroll to it. |

## States

- Visual states: `default`, paused while the strip is hovered or holds focus, or while its pause control is pressed (the track carries `data-paused`)
- Empty: when `photos` is empty the band renders nothing
- The list is rendered twice for a seamless loop; the duplicate is `aria-hidden`, its photos carry an empty `alt`, and it is hidden under reduced motion, where the region scrolls by hand instead

## Accessibility

- Visible focus: the scrolling region is focusable and carries `focus-visible:ring-2 focus-visible:ring-(--ring-color) focus-visible:ring-inset`.
- Keyboard map: `Tab` reaches the region, which pauses the loop, then the pause control (`Enter` / `Space` toggles it, WCAG 2.2.2); under reduced motion the control is hidden and arrow keys scroll the region.
- ARIA: the band is a `<section>` named by its title (`aria-label`); the strip is a `region` named by `ariaLabel`; every photo carries its `alt` once — the duplicate copy is `aria-hidden` with empty `alt`.
- Contrast ≥4.5:1 (text) / ≥3:1 (large + icons): owned by `section-title`.
- `motion-reduce:animate-none` on the strip; the duplicate list is hidden so nothing moves and nothing repeats.
- Touch target ≥40×40 px — not applicable beyond the focusable region.

## Examples

### Default

```vue

    description-width="wide"
    aria-label="Azion offices"
    :photos="[
      {
        src: placeholderImage + '#office-desks',

      },
      {
        src: placeholderImage + '#office-lounge',
```

<!-- components/plan-comparison-section.md -->

# Plan Comparison Section

> The feature-by-feature matrix under the plan cards on Pricing: one column per plan with its name and action button pinned in the header while the page scrolls, one band per product with an overline, a title and a lead, and one row per feature saying what each plan includes — an included mark, a not-included mark, or the allowance as text. The recommended plan carries an accent bar, and below the large breakpoint the table folds to one plan picked from a searchable select. It is a page section — the site places it from a `{ _id: 'plan-comparison-section', props }` entry and binds `props` straight onto it — and it composes `section-module`, `select`, `input`, `button`, `icon-button`, `popover`, `tooltip`, `eyebrow`, `tag` and `link`.

- Import: `import PlanComparisonSection from '@aziontech/webkit/plan-comparison-section'`
- Category: section · Surface: site · Structure: monolithic

## When to use

- Use PlanComparisonSection for a feature matrix across plans or support tiers: on Pricing under the plan cards, product by product, and on Support with one column per support tier.

## Do

- Give every plan a stable `id`; the narrow-screen picker selects by it.
- Mark one plan `highlighted`; it gets the accent bar, the secondary button and opens the picker.
- Give every row's `values` one entry per plan, in plan order.
- Use a row's `hint` to define the term its label names; it opens in a popover titled with the label, so it can run to a sentence or two.
- Set `includedLabel` and `excludedLabel` to the page's words; left empty, the marks read the locale's Included and Not included.

## Don’t

- To compare Azion with one competitor → use `competitor-comparison-section`.
- For price cards with a billing-period toggle → use `pricing-plans-section`.

## Related

- `pricing-plans-section` — the plan cards this matrix sits under.
- `competitor-comparison-section` — Azion against one competitor.

## Props

| Prop | Type | Default | Required | Description |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `anchor` | `string` | `''` | false | In-page id set on the band so a hash link can target it. |
| `caption` | `string` | `''` | false | Screen-reader caption naming the table. |
| `columnLabel` | `string` | `''` | false | Heading of the feature column, shown from the large breakpoint up. |
| `pickerAriaLabel` | `string` | `''` | false | Accessible name of the plan picker shown below the large breakpoint. |
| `includedLabel` | `string` | `''` | false | Tooltip and screen-reader text of an included cell; empty uses the locale catalog (`Included`). |
| `excludedLabel` | `string` | `''` | false | Tooltip and screen-reader text of a not-included cell; empty uses the locale catalog (`Not included`). |
| `plans` | `PlanComparisonSectionPlan[]` | `[]` | false | The plan columns, in order; each is `{ id, name, description?, highlighted?, action }` with `action` as `{ label, href }`; a plan without a name is dropped. |
| `sections` | `PlanComparisonSectionSection[]` | `[]` | false | The product bands, in order; each is `{ eyebrow, title, description, rows, action? }` (`action` closes the band, `{ label, href }`) and a row is `{ label, values, group?, hint?, tag? }` with one value per plan: `true` or `✓` is included, `—` is not included, other text shows as written, empty is blank; `hint` defines the label in a popover. |
| `primaryAction` | `SectionTitleAction` | `undefined` | false | Closing link under the table, opened in a new tab; `{ label, href }`. Omitted when absent. |

## States

- Visual states: `default`
- The highlighted plan carries the accent bar and the secondary button; the others carry an outlined button
- Below the large breakpoint only the picked plan's column shows (`data-folded` on the others); the picker opens on the highlighted plan, or the first
- Picker search: the picker's list carries a search field that narrows the plans by name; with no match it shows a `role="status"` line from the locale catalog (`No results found.`); the query clears each time the list opens or closes
- Cells: an included cell is a `az az-circle-check` mark and a not-included cell an `az az-circle-minus` mark, each with a tooltip of its label on hover and the same label as `sr-only` text
- Hint: a row with `hint` ends its label with an info icon button that opens a popover titled with the label and described by the hint
- Closing rule: when neither the last band nor the table carries a closing link, the last row draws a bottom rule (`data-closing`) so the table never ends open
- Below the large breakpoint the plan button's label wraps (auto height, at least 40 px) instead of widening the table, so the folded matrix fits a 320 px screen whatever the font metrics.
- A group row (`group: true`) reads in the default text colour and draws a top rule unless it opens its band
- Category row: a group row whose values are all empty is a category heading. It opens its own `tbody` with one `th scope="rowgroup"` spanning every column (label, hint and tag included) and renders no value cells; the rows after it belong to that `tbody` until the next category. A group row that carries values stays a feature row.
- The feature column head (`columnLabel`) is start-aligned, like the row labels under it.
- Empty: when no plan has a name the band renders nothing but its root

## Accessibility

- Visible focus: owned by the composed `select`, `input`, `button`, `icon-button` and `link`.
- Keyboard map: `Tab` reaches the picker (below `lg`), each plan button, each hint button and the closing link; the picker's keyboard map is `select`'s and its search field takes typing while the list is open; `Enter` or `Space` on a hint button opens its popover and `Escape` closes it, returning focus to the button (`popover`'s map).
- ARIA: the table carries a `caption`, `scope="col"` plan heads, a `scope="rowgroup"` head opening each band `tbody` and each category `tbody`, and a `scope="row"` head per feature, so each value is announced with its row and plan. The included and not-included marks are `aria-hidden` and carry their label as `sr-only` text (the tooltip repeats it for pointer users); each hint button is named `About <label>` from the locale catalog and its popover is titled by the label; the picker's search field is named `Search <picker name>` and its no-match line is a `status`; the accent bar is decorative; the plan names stay readable to screen readers below `lg`.
- Contrast ≥4.5:1 (text) / ≥3:1 (large + icons): text colours are the tokens above.
- `motion-reduce:transition-none motion-reduce:transform-none` — the composed controls carry their own reduced-motion fallback; this band adds no motion.
- Touch target ≥40×40 px — the plan buttons and the picker are `large`.
- Reflow: below `lg` (one plan column beside the row labels) the header cells, the section headings and the row labels use `var(--spacing-sm)` side padding instead of `var(--spacing-lg)`, so the two columns fit a 640px box at 200% zoom; from `lg` the padding is unchanged.

## Examples

### Band links

```vue
<script setup>
  import PlanComparisonSection from '@aziontech/webkit/plan-comparison-section'

  const plans = [
    {
      id: 'hobby',
      name: 'Hobby',
      description: 'For side projects.',
      action: { label: 'Start for free', href: '/signup' }
    },
    {
      id: 'pro',
      name: 'Pro',
      description: 'For teams in production.',
      highlighted: true,
      action: { label: 'Start trial', href: '/signup?plan=pro' }
    }
  ]

  const sections = [
    {
      eyebrow: 'Compute',
      title: 'Functions',
      description: 'Run code close to users.',
      rows: [
        { label: 'Requests per month', values: ['1M', '10M'] },
        { label: 'Custom domains', values: [true, true] },
        { label: 'Usage alerts', values: ['—', true] }
      ],
      action: { label: 'See compute rates', href: '/pricing/compute' }
    },
    {
      eyebrow: 'Storage',
      title: 'Object Storage',
      rows: [
        { label: 'Storage included', values: ['5 GB', '100 GB'] },
        { label: 'Versioning', values: ['—', true] }
      ],
      action: { label: 'See storage rates', href: '/pricing/storage' }
    }
  ]
</script>

<template>
  <PlanComparisonSection
    caption="Hobby and Pro compared feature by feature"
    column-label="Features"
    picker-aria-label="Plan being compared"
    included-label="Included"
    excluded-label="Not included"
    :plans="plans"
    :sections="sections"
  />
</template>
```

<!-- components/platform-directory-section.md -->

# Platform Directory Section

> A directory of Azion's products under a section title: one column per product group (Build, Store, Secure, Observe), each row an icon, a product name and one line about it, linking to that product. It is a page section — the site places it from a `{ _id: 'platform-directory-section', props }` entry and binds `props` straight onto it — so its whole API is the props below, and it composes `section-module`, `section-title`, `registration-frame` and `link-directory` rather than owning any anatomy of its own.

- Import: `import PlatformDirectorySection from '@aziontech/webkit/platform-directory-section'`
- Category: section · Surface: site · Structure: monolithic

## When to use

- Use PlatformDirectorySection for the Azion products directory band.
- On Home, right under the hero, to show every product the platform offers at a glance.
- On a solution, product or pricing page that repeats the full product map.

## Do

- Keep to four groups or fewer; the directory fans out to one column per group at the large breakpoint, up to four, so one, two or three groups never leave an empty cell.
- Give each item an `href`; an item without a title or a link is skipped, and a group left with no items is skipped too.
- Set `ariaLabel` so the directory's navigation landmark is told apart from the site navigation; left empty, the landmark takes the `title`.
- Use `eyebrow` for the short label above the headline (the page's name for the product map) and `description` for one sentence under it.
- Give the band an `anchor` when a page links to it with a `#hash`.

## Don’t

- For a handful of icon-led use cases → use `customer-use-cases-section`.
- For a site-wide navigation menu → use `navigation-menu`.
- Inside another band's cell → compose `link-directory` directly.

## Related

- `link-directory` — the named navigation landmark whose columns and rows this band fills.
- `section-title` — the headline over the directory.

## Props

| Prop | Type | Default | Required | Description |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `anchor` | `string` | `''` | false | In-page id set on the band so a `#hash` link can scroll to it. |
| `eyebrow` | `string` | `''` | false | Short label above the headline; shown only with a title. |
| `title` | `string` | `''` | false | Headline over the directory, rendered by the section title as its `h2`; the band renders no header, eyebrow or description when empty. |
| `description` | `string` | `''` | false | One sentence under the headline; shown only with a title. |
| `ariaLabel` | `string` | `''` | false | Accessible name of the directory's navigation landmark; empty uses the title. |
| `groups` | `PlatformDirectorySectionGroup[]` | `[]` | false | The columns, in order; each is `{ title, items }` where every item is `{ icon, title, description, href }`. Items without a title or an `href` are skipped, and so are groups left empty. |

## States

- Visual states: `default`
- One column per group from the large breakpoint, up to four (`link-directory`'s `columns` is 2, 3 or 4), one column below the small one; the column layout is `link-directory`'s own
- No empty cell: a single group spans the whole row (`data-span="full"`), and with three groups the third spans both columns between the small and the large breakpoints (`data-span="pair"`), so the rule colour never shows through a missing column
- Header: with a `title`, the section title renders the eyebrow and the description that are set around the headline; without one, no header renders
- Anchor: the root carries `id` = `anchor` when set, with a scroll margin so a `#hash` jump clears a sticky header
- Empty: when no group carries a linked item the band renders nothing — not the header, not the frame

## Accessibility

- Visible focus: owned by the composed `link-directory` item links.
- Keyboard map: `Tab` moves through the product links in reading order; `Enter` follows one. Nothing else in the band is interactive.
- ARIA: the band is a `<section>` named by its title (`aria-label`) when one is set; the directory is `link-directory`'s `nav`, named by `ariaLabel` (or the title when `ariaLabel` is empty), and each column's overline heading names its list of rows.
- Contrast ≥4.5:1 (text) / ≥3:1 (large + icons): owned by the composed components.
- `motion-reduce:transition-none motion-reduce:transform-none` — the row hover belongs to `link-directory`, which carries its own reduced-motion fallback; this band adds no motion.
- Touch target ≥40×40 px — each row is a full-width link taller than 40 px.

## Examples

### Default

```vue
<script setup>
  import PlatformDirectorySection from '@aziontech/webkit/platform-directory-section'
</script>

<template>
  <PlatformDirectorySection
    title="Everything you need to build and run applications"
    aria-label="Azion products"
    :groups="[
      {
        title: 'Build',
        items: [
          {

            href: '/products/functions'
          },
          {
            icon: 'az az-ai-inference',
            title: 'AI Inference',
```
