<!-- components/pricing-plans-section.md -->

# Pricing Plans Section

> The plan cards at the top of Pricing: a billing-period toggle over a framed row of pricing cards, each with its plan name, its price for the picked period, what it includes and one button. The recommended plan sits on a raised surface and carries a tag. It is a page section — the site places it from a `{ _id: 'pricing-plans-section', props }` entry and binds `props` straight onto it — and it composes `registration-frame`, `toggle-group`, `card-grid`, `pricing-card` and `button`; the picked period is local state. The band runs full bleed (the page's inline inset only, closed by a bottom rule) and centres its frame at the site measure, with rules on its sides.

- Import: `import PricingPlansSection from '@aziontech/webkit/pricing-plans-section'`
- Category: section · Surface: site · Structure: monolithic

## When to use

- Use PricingPlansSection for price cards with a billing-period toggle on the public pricing page, the plans side by side at the top of Pricing.

## Do

- Give every plan one price per period, matched by `period`; a price without a `period` applies to every period.
- Highlight one plan; it is raised and is the only card that shows its tag.
- Put terms that hold for every period in the plan's `description`; a price's own `details` replaces it for that period.
- Keep `features` to five or so short labels led by an icon.

## Don’t

- To compare plans or support tiers feature by feature → use `plan-comparison-section`.
- To compare Azion with one competitor → use `competitor-comparison-section`.

## Related

- `pricing-card` — the card each plan renders with.
- `plan-comparison-section` — the matrix that sits under these cards.

## Props

| Prop | Type | Default | Required | Description |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `anchor` | `string` | `''` | false | In-page id set on the band so a hash link can target it. |
| `periods` | `PricingPlansSectionPeriod[]` | `[]` | false | The billing periods of the toggle, in order; each is `{ label, value }`. The toggle shows with two or more and opens on the first. |
| `periodsAriaLabel` | `string` | `''` | false | Accessible name of the billing-period toggle. |
| `plans` | `PricingPlansSectionPlan[]` | `[]` | false | The plan cards, in order; each is `{ name, description?, highlighted?, tagLabel?, prices, featuresTitle?, features?, action? }` where a price is `{ period?, value, prefix?, suffix?, details? }`, a feature is `{ icon, label }` and `action` is `{ label, href }`; `description` stands in for a price without `details`, a plan without a name is dropped, and an action without a label or href is dropped. |

## States

- Visual states: `default`
- The picked period selects each plan's price; a plan with no price for it falls back to its period-less price
- Price change: every card is `animated`, so a period switch fades the old price out and the new one in (the card's reduced-motion fallback swaps it in place)
- Terms: the line under the price is the price's `details`, else the plan's `description`, else empty
- Layout: a full-width band (`__band`) on `var(--bg-canvas)` with the page's inline inset `var(--layout-boundary-inline)` and a bottom rule, and inside it a `registration-frame` with side rules and bottom marks capped at `var(--layout-measure-site)` and centred
- The highlighted plan sits on `var(--bg-surface)`, shows its tag and carries the secondary button; the others sit on `var(--bg-canvas)` with an outlined button
- Empty: when no plan has a name the band renders nothing but its root

## Accessibility

- Visible focus: owned by the composed `toggle-group` and `button`.
- Keyboard map: `Tab` reaches the toggle and each card's button; the toggle's arrow-key map is `toggle-group`'s.
- ARIA: the toggle is named by `periodsAriaLabel`; each plan is a `pricing-card` article with its name as `h3`; the features are a list and their glyphs are `aria-hidden`.
- Contrast ≥4.5:1 (text) / ≥3:1 (large + icons): text colours are the tokens above.
- `motion-reduce:transition-none motion-reduce:transform-none` — the toggle indicator belongs to `toggle-group` and the price fade to `pricing-card`, each with its own reduced-motion fallback; this band adds no motion of its own.
- Touch target ≥40×40 px — the toggle and the buttons are `large`.

## Examples

### Default

<!-- components/process-scroll-section.md -->

# Process Scroll Section

> The stages of a process as a row of numbered dark cards on a pixel texture, under a section title. On narrow screens the row scrolls sideways with snap; from the large breakpoint up the cards share the width, or, with `pinned`, keep a 26rem card width while the band pins under the site header and the page scroll slides the row sideways until its last card is in view (azion.com's careers hiring process). The retired `'sticky-carousel'` `_id` resolves here with `pinned: true` and `titleAlign: 'center'`. From the design sample's site `ProcessScrollSection`.

- Import: `import ProcessScrollSection from '@aziontech/webkit/process-scroll-section'`
- Category: section · Surface: site · Structure: monolithic

## When to use

- Use ProcessScrollSection for numbered stage cards; set `pinned` to pin them under the header while they slide.
- How something works in three to five ordered stages (build, deploy, observe), or five to eight short numbered points pinned on scroll (`pinned`, `title-align="center"`).

## Do

- Name each stage with a verb.
- Keep descriptions to two lines.
- Set `pinned` when the stages run wider than the page column (five tall cards, as the careers hiring process): the band stays on screen while the page scroll walks the row. Do not nest a pinned band in a container that clips overflow; sticky positioning needs the page scroll.

## Don’t

- For more than five stages or stages with art — use `FeatureScrollSection`.

## Related

- `Texture`, `RegistrationFrame`, `SectionTitle`.

## Props

| Prop | Type | Default | Required | Description |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `anchor` | `string` | `''` | false | In-page id set on the band so a hash link can target it. |
| `eyebrow` | `string` | `''` | false | Eyebrow above the title. |
| `titleAlign` | `SectionTitleAlign` | `'split'` | false | Alignment of the section title over the card row. |
| `title` | `string` | `''` | false | Headline of the band, rendered as its `h2`; the band renders nothing when empty. |
| `description` | `string` | `''` | false | Paragraph beside the headline. |
| `descriptionWidth` | `SectionTitleDescriptionWidth` | `'narrow'` | false | Column span of the paragraph: `wide` runs it across the middle column too. |
| `steps` | `ProcessScrollSectionStep[]` | `[]` | false | The stages, in order; each is `{ title, description }` and gets its own numbered card. |
| `ariaLabel` | `string` | `''` | false | Accessible name of the scrollable card row; defaults to the title. |
| `pinned` | `boolean` | `false` | false | From the large breakpoint, pins the band under the site header while the page scrolls and slides the card row sideways until its last card is in view; below it, or under reduced motion, the row scrolls sideways on its own. |
| `headerOffset` | `string` | `'var(--layout-header-height)'` | false | Height of the sticky site header a pinned band sticks under; defaults to the SiteHeaderSection bar's token, any CSS length. |

## States

- Renders nothing without a title or steps.
- Cards are `data-theme="dark"` in both themes (the sample's design).
- `data-pinned` on the root mirrors `pinned`. From `lg` with motion allowed, the band is `sticky` under `headerOffset` and one screen tall, the root (the runway) grows by the row's overflow, and the row's `translate` follows the page scroll (mirrored in right-to-left); below `lg` or under `prefers-reduced-motion` the band does not pin and the row scrolls inside itself. The runway is measured on mount and on scroll/resize, so the server HTML is the unpinned band.

## Accessibility

- The band root is a plain container; the scroll row is the one focusable `region`, named by `ariaLabel` or the title, so keyboard users can scroll it with the arrow keys and the page has one landmark per band.
- Stages are an ordered list; the big number is `aria-hidden` because the list already orders them.
- Each stage heading is an `h3`.

## Examples

### Default

```vue
<script setup>
  import ProcessScrollSection from '@aziontech/webkit/process-scroll-section'

  const steps = [
    { title: 'Push', description: 'Every push to the main branch builds a new version.' },
    { title: 'Build', description: 'The build runs in an isolated worker with your settings.' },
    {
      title: 'Deploy',
      description: 'The version goes live everywhere at once, with no regions to pick.'
    },
    { title: 'Observe', description: 'Metrics and logs for the new version arrive in seconds.' }
  ]
</script>

<template>
  <ProcessScrollSection
    eyebrow="How it works"
    title="From commit to production"
    description="Four stages, all automatic once the repository is connected."
    :steps="steps"
  />
</template>
```

1 more example(s) in [components/process-scroll-section.md](components/process-scroll-section.md).

# Recognition Marquee Section

> The analyst-recognition band: a section title over a framed strip of signed quotes — one per report, marked by the firm's logo — that scrolls continuously and pauses on hover, on focus or from its pause control; the loop is the one `marquee` runs on. It is a page section — the site places it from a `{ _id: 'recognition-marquee-section', props }` entry and binds `props` straight onto it — and it composes `section-module`, `section-title`, `registration-frame` and `quote`.

- Import: `import RecognitionMarqueeSection from '@aziontech/webkit/recognition-marquee-section'`
- Category: section · Surface: site · Structure: monolithic

## When to use

- Use RecognitionMarqueeSection only for a looping row of analyst or press quotes.
- To list analyst reports and awards on a page that needs third-party proof.

## Do

- Five to eight items; the loop needs enough width to read as continuous.
- Leave `duration` at `0` to let the strip pace itself (twelve seconds per item).

## Don’t

- For office photos → use `photo-marquee-section`.
- For customer quotes → use `client-quotes-section`.

## Related

- `quote` — the signed card each recognition renders with.
- `photo-marquee-section` — the sibling strip of pictures with the same loop.

## Props

| Prop | Type | Default | Required | Description |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `eyebrow` | `string` | `''` | false | Eyebrow above the section title. |
| `title` | `string` | `''` | false | Headline of the band, rendered by the section title as its `h2`; the band renders no header when empty. |
| `ariaLabel` | `string` | `''` | false | Accessible name of the scrolling region. |
| `duration` | `number` | `0` | false | Seconds one loop of the strip takes; `0` paces it at twelve seconds per item. |
| `items` | `RecognitionMarqueeSectionItem[]` | `[]` | false | The recognitions, in order; each is `{ text, reportName, date?, firmName, logo, logoLight? }` where `logo` is the firm's mark on the dark theme and `logoLight` the one on the light theme. |
| `anchor` | `string` | `''` | false | In-page id set on the band so a `#hash` link can scroll to it. |

## States

- Visual states: `default`, paused while the strip is hovered or holds focus, or while its pause control is pressed (the track carries `data-paused`)
- Empty: when `items` is empty the band renders nothing
- The list is rendered twice for a seamless loop; the duplicate is `aria-hidden` and hidden under reduced motion, where the region scrolls by hand instead

## Accessibility

- Visible focus: the scrolling region is focusable and carries `focus-visible:ring-2 focus-visible:ring-(--ring-color) focus-visible:ring-inset`.
- Keyboard map: `Tab` reaches the region, which pauses the loop, then the pause control (`Enter` / `Space` toggles it, WCAG 2.2.2); under reduced motion the control is hidden and arrow keys scroll the region.
- ARIA: the band is a `<section>` named by its title (`aria-label`); the strip is a `region` named by `ariaLabel`; the second copy of the list is `aria-hidden`; each firm mark carries `firmName` as `alt`; each recognition is a `blockquote` signed by its report and date.
- Contrast ≥4.5:1 (text) / ≥3:1 (large + icons): owned by `quote` on `var(--bg-surface)`.
- `motion-reduce:animate-none` on the strip; the duplicate list is hidden so nothing moves and nothing repeats.
- Touch target ≥40×40 px — not applicable beyond the focusable region.

## Examples

### Default


<!-- components/site-footer-section.md -->

# Site Footer Section

> The footer under every marketing page: the link columns, then the social row beside the status indicator and the language select. It is a page section — the site places it from a `{ _id: 'site-footer-section', props }` entry and binds `props` straight onto it — and it composes `footer`, `brand`, `icon-button`, `status-indicator` and `select`. The bottom bar is one row, as azion.com draws it: the Azion mark (the footer's `brand` slot) and the small social icon buttons at the start, the status indicator (`status`) and the language select with its globe (`language`) at the end; below `md` it stacks, the mark and icons over the status and language. In the `site` placement the footer spans the page like `site-header-section`, opening on a full-width rule with a corner mark at each end, with no side rules. The layout is CSS only, so the server HTML and the hydrated page are the same tree. The language select keeps its own value unless the page binds `v-model:language`; its options are the labels the reader sees.

- Import: `import SiteFooterSection from '@aziontech/webkit/site-footer-section'`
- Category: section · Surface: site · Structure: monolithic

## When to use

- Use SiteFooterSection for the marketing site footer from page JSON.
- Rendering page JSON, or you need a whole band with no custom content → the section (`site-footer-section`). Building an SFC by hand, needing slots, or nesting inside another band → the primitive (`footer`).
- As the last block of every marketing page.

## Do

- **Do** render it as the last child of the page shell, as is: in `site` it draws its own full-width top rule with its corner marks.
- **Don't** wrap it in a bordered or padded `div` (`border-t`, `py-*`): the rules double.
- Use `variant: 'site'` under a marketing page, so the footer spans the page like its header and opens on its own rule; the page above must not draw a bottom rule of its own.
- Keep four link columns so the grid fills at `md`.
- Set `target: '_blank'` on a link that leaves azion.com (Community, Status): it opens in a new tab, draws the external-link glyph after the label and tells assistive tech it opens a new tab. Never add the glyph yourself.
- Give every social link a `label` that names the network, since the button shows only the glyph.

## Don’t

- Inside the product console or the docs → compose `footer` directly.
- For a closing ask above the footer → use `closing-call-to-action-section`.

## Related

- `footer` — the composition footer this section fills.
- `closing-call-to-action-section` — the band that sits right above it.

## Props

| Prop | Type | Default | Required | Description |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `variant` | `SiteFooterSectionVariant` | `'site'` | false | Where the footer sits: `site` spans the page like the site header under its own top rule, `content` runs the bands full bleed under a hairline. |
| `ariaLabel` | `string` | `''` | false | Accessible name of the footer landmark. Empty uses the locale catalog (`Footer`). |
| `home` | `string` | `''` | false | Destination of the Azion mark; the mark is not rendered when empty. |
| `homeAriaLabel` | `string` | `''` | false | Accessible name of the Azion mark link. Empty uses the locale catalog (`Azion home`). |
| `columns` | `SiteFooterSectionColumn[]` | `[]` | false | The link columns, in order; each is `{ title, links }` where every link is `{ label, href, target? }`; a `_blank` link opens in a new tab and closes on the external-link glyph. |
| `socials` | `SiteFooterSectionSocial[]` | `[]` | false | The profile icon buttons, in order; each is `{ icon, label, href }` where `label` names the button for assistive tech. They open in a new tab. |
| `statusLabel` | `string` | `''` | false | Line of the system status indicator; the indicator is not rendered when empty. |
| `languages` | `string[]` | `[]` | false | Options of the language select, as the reader sees them; the first one is selected until `v-model:language` holds another. The select is not rendered when empty. |
| `languageAriaLabel` | `string` | `''` | false | Accessible name of the language select, also its placeholder. Empty uses the locale catalog (`Language`). |
| `language` | `string` | `''` | false | Two-way value (`v-model:language`). |

## v-model

| Model | Type | Default | Emits | Notes |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `v-model:language` | `string` | `''` | `update:language` | The chosen option of the language select, one of `languages`. Empty shows the first option; choosing one emits it, so the page can switch its locale. Unbound, the select keeps its value locally. |

## States

- Visual states: `default`
- `data-variant` mirrors the `variant` prop on the footer
- The Azion mark opens the bottom bar at every width, in the footer's `brand` slot
- The status indicator sits in the footer's `status` slot and the language select in its `language` slot; below `md` they share the bottom bar at its two ends, from `md` they sit side by side at its end
- The social buttons are small icon buttons
- The status indicator, the language select and the Azion mark each render only when their prop is set
- A link whose `target` is `_blank` sets `rel="noopener noreferrer"` and `data-external`, draws the `az az-external-link` glyph after its label in the label's own colour (hidden from assistive tech) and appends the catalog's “(opens in a new tab)” as screen-reader text
- Server render and hydration: one mark, placed by CSS only, so the server HTML, the first client render and every later render are the same tree — nothing moves on mount.

## Accessibility

- Visible focus: the mark link carries `focus-visible:ring-2 focus-visible:ring-(--ring-color) focus-visible:ring-offset-2 focus-visible:ring-offset-(--bg-canvas)`; links, icon buttons and the select own theirs.
- Keyboard map: `Tab` moves through the column links, the mark, the social buttons and the language select; the select opens with `Enter` / `Space` and moves with the arrows, owned by `select`.
- ARIA: the `footer` is the `contentinfo` landmark named by `ariaLabel`; each column is titled; the mark link is named by `homeAriaLabel`, each social button by its `label`, the select by `languageAriaLabel`; an external link's name ends with “(opens in a new tab)” from the locale catalog.
- Contrast ≥4.5:1 (text) / ≥3:1 (large + icons): owned by the composed components.
- `motion-reduce:transition-none` on the mark link's opacity transition.
- Touch target ≥24×24 px — the social buttons are small `icon-button`s (28 px, above the 24 px WCAG 2.5.8 minimum; the design keeps the social row quiet), the select follows `select`; the column links and the Azion mark link rely on the WCAG 2.5.8 spacing exception (see Responsive).

## Examples

<!-- components/site-header-section.md -->

# Site Header Section

> The marketing site's own navigation: a sticky bar with the Azion mark, the mega-menu triggers and plain links, and the contact, login and sign-up actions, plus the sheet that carries the same tree below `xl`. It is a page section — the site places it from a `{ _id: 'site-header-section', props }` entry and binds `props` straight onto it — and it composes `app-header`, `brand`, `navigation-menu`, `icon-button`, `search-trigger`, `button`, `drawer`, `panel-header`, `panel-footer`, `scroll-area` and `sidebar-menu`. Each mega-menu lists its panel as columns of groups, and the sheet's drill-down tree is projected from the same menus, so an entry added to a panel is in both. The open state of the sheet, its drill path and the bar's inline inset are local state. Search is the folded `search-trigger` last in the bar: the section owns no palette, it emits `search` (on a click and on ⌘K / Ctrl K) and the page opens its own `command`, as every header does under `rules/webkit-header-search.md`.

- Import: `import SiteHeaderSection from '@aziontech/webkit/site-header-section'`
- Category: section · Surface: site · Structure: monolithic

## When to use

- Use SiteHeaderSection for the marketing site's top bar (mega-menus and mobile sheet).
- At the top of every marketing page, once.

## Do

- Keep a panel to one, two or four columns; only the four-column panel is stretched to the page column.
- Stack a short group under another in the same column (Platform under Observe) instead of adding a fifth column.
- Give every entry an `href`; an entry without one links to `#`.
- Listen to `search` and open the page's `Command` from it (`@search="searchOpen = true"` beside `<Command v-model:open="searchOpen">`); the trigger already carries ⌘K / Ctrl K.
- Leave `secondaryActionOnPhones` off: on a phone the bar keeps the menu button, the mark, the call to action and search, as azion.com does, and Login stays in the sheet footer.
- The bar is `--layout-header-height` tall: `HeroSection` already takes it out of a screen-filling band; a bare `Hero variant="screen"` takes `offset="var(--layout-header-height)"`, never a literal length.
- Don't wrap the bar, add a second search button beside it, or bind ⌘K yourself.

## Don’t

- For the Console's application header → use `app-header` directly.
- For an in-page list of links → use `navigation-menu` or `sidebar-menu` directly.

## Related

- `app-header` — the bar primitive the navigation is rendered in.
- `navigation-menu` — the mega-menu the bar opens from `xl` up.
- `sidebar-menu` — the drill-down tree the sheet carries below `xl`.

## Props

| Prop | Type | Default | Required | Description |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `home` | `string` | `'/'` | false | Where the Azion mark links. |
| `menus` | `SiteHeaderSectionMenu[]` | `—` | true | The mega-menus, in bar order; each is `{ value, label, columns }` where `columns` is an array of columns, each holding groups of `{ label, href?, items }` and each item `{ label, description?, href?, icon? }`. |
| `links` | `SiteHeaderSectionLink[]` | `[]` | false | Bar entries that open no panel, after the menus; each is `{ label, href }`. |
| `contact` | `SiteHeaderSectionLink` | `undefined` | false | Contact link set in the bar from `xl` up and last in the sheet's tree; `{ label, href }`. |
| `primaryAction` | `SiteHeaderSectionLink` | `undefined` | false | The sign-up button, filled, in the bar and in the sheet footer; `{ label, href }`. |
| `secondaryAction` | `SiteHeaderSectionLink` | `undefined` | false | The login button beside it, in the bar from `sm` up and in the sheet footer; `{ label, href }`. |
| `secondaryActionOnPhones` | `boolean` | `false` | false | Keeps the login button in the bar below `sm` too; off, it steps out on phones, as on azion.com, and stays in the sheet footer. |

## Events

| Event | Payload | Notes |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `search` | `(event: MouseEvent \| KeyboardEvent)` |  |

## States

- Visual states: `default`; the sheet is `open` or closed (local state, owned by `drawer`)
- Closing the sheet resets its drill path; following a leaf of the tree closes the sheet
- The sheet closes on its own when the viewport reaches `xl` (80rem), where the bar shows every entry and the menu button is gone
- The bar reads its own inline inset on mount and on resize, so the four-column panel's collision padding lands it on the page column's leading edge
- Each panel's column count is mirrored on `data-columns` and drives its track count; only a four-column panel is stretched to the page column
- An absent `contact`, `primaryAction` or `secondaryAction` is omitted from the bar and the sheet
- The secondary action steps out of the bar below `sm` unless `secondaryActionOnPhones`; the sheet footer always carries it

## Accessibility

- Visible focus: the mark link draws the ring token; triggers, items and buttons own theirs.
- Keyboard map: `Tab` moves through the bar; ⌘K / Ctrl K fires `search` (a mounted `Command` claims the shortcut first); the mega-menu and the sheet own their keyboard models (arrows, `Enter`, `Esc` closes and restores focus to the trigger).
- ARIA: the bar is the `app-header` landmark named "Azion", so the root is a plain `div`; the mark link is named "Azion home"; the menu button "Open navigation" is an icon button with a name; the search trigger is named "Search" and announces `aria-keyshortcuts` (`Meta+K Control+K`); the sheet is titled "Menu".
- Contrast ≥4.5:1 (text) / ≥3:1 (large + icons): owned by the composed components.
- `motion-reduce:transition-none` on the mark link; the sheet and the panels own their reduced-motion fallbacks.
- Touch target ≥40×40 px — the medium buttons and icon buttons meet it.

## Usage

```vue
<script setup>
  import Command from '@aziontech/webkit/command'
  import SiteHeaderSection from '@aziontech/webkit/site-header-section'
  import { ref } from 'vue'

  const searchOpen = ref(false)
</script>

<template>
  <SiteHeaderSection
    home="/"
    :menus="[
      {
        value: 'developer',
        label: 'Developer',
        columns: [
          [
            {
              label: 'Docs',
              items: [
                {
                  label: 'Azion Docs',
                  description: 'Documentation and technical references',
                  href: '/docs'
                }
              ]
            }
          ]
        ]
      }
    ]"
    :links="[{ label: 'Pricing', href: '/pricing' }]"
    :contact="{ label: 'Contact', href: '/contact' }"
    :primary-action="{ label: 'Start for free', href: '/signup' }"
    :secondary-action="{ label: 'Login', href: '/login' }"
    @search="searchOpen = true"
  />
  <Command v-model:open="searchOpen"></Command>
</template>
```

<!-- components/stats-section.md -->

# Stats Section

> The proof-by-numbers band of a marketing page: a framed row of big figures, each with its caption and optional prefix or unit. It is a page section — the site places it from a `{ _id: 'stats-section', props }` entry and binds `props` straight onto it — and it composes `section-module`, `registration-frame` and `stats`, which owns the figures' scale and layout.

- Import: `import StatsSection from '@aziontech/webkit/stats-section'`
- Category: section · Surface: site · Structure: monolithic

## When to use

- Use StatsSection for a band of proof figures placed from page JSON.
- Rendering page JSON, or you need a whole band with no custom content → the section (`stats-section`). Building an SFC by hand, needing slots, or nesting inside another band → the primitive (`stats`).
- To state reach, scale or results in three to five figures right after a hero or a claim.

## Do

- Keep `value` short and the unit in `suffix`, so the figure reads at a glance.
- Three to five items; more than that and the figures stop reading as a band.

## Don’t

- For figures that need a quote or a logo beside them → use `network-section` or `logo-wall-section`.
- For a single figure inside another band → use `stats` directly.

## Related

- `stats` — the figure row this band frames.
- `network-section` — the band that pairs claims with client outcomes.

## Props

| Prop | Type | Default | Required | Description |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `items` | `StatsSectionItem[]` | `[]` | false | The figures rendered as cells, in order; each item is `{ value, label, prefix?, suffix? }` where `value` is the figure, `label` its caption, and `prefix` / `suffix` the qualifier and unit set beside it. |
| `anchor` | `string` | `''` | false | In-page id set on the band so a `#hash` link can scroll to it. |

## States

- Visual states: `default`
- Empty: when `items` is empty the band renders nothing — a proof band with nothing to prove is absent from the page

## Accessibility

- Visible focus: nothing in the band is interactive.
- Keyboard map: none.
- ARIA: the band is a plain `div`; the figures' semantics (each figure paired with its caption) belong to `stats`.
- Contrast ≥4.5:1 (text) / ≥3:1 (large + icons): owned by `stats`.
- `motion-reduce:transition-none motion-reduce:transform-none` — the band adds no motion.
- Touch target ≥40×40 px — not applicable, nothing is interactive.

## Examples

### Default

```vue
<script setup>
  import StatsSection from '@aziontech/webkit/stats-section'
</script>

<template>
  <StatsSection
    :items="[
      { value: '100', suffix: 'ms', label: 'Median response, worldwide' },
      { value: '24', suffix: '/7', label: 'Support on every plan' },
      { value: '99.99', suffix: '%', label: 'Availability, contractual' }
    ]"
  />
</template>
```

<!-- components/story-index-section.md -->

# Story Index Section

> Every success story of a company as one filterable library. A left-aligned title sits over a toolbar — a search field and one select per facet (industry, solution, product) on one row, each starting with an option that clears it — then a grid of story cards and a load-more button that reveals the next page. Below `lg` the toolbar folds to the search field (with its shorter placeholder) and a filter button that reveals the selects, with each applied facet listed under it as a removable chip. A select with more than seven options gains an option search. Each card is one link named by its company: the client mark in a single ink, the story's category as an overline, its description and a read cue whose arrow nudges on hover. The grid runs one column on phones, two from `sm` and three from `lg`. Search and facets combine; when nothing matches, an empty state offers to clear every filter. It is a page section — the site places it from a `{ _id: 'story-index-section', props }` entry and binds `props` straight onto it — and it composes `section-module`, `section-title`, `registration-frame`, `input`, `select`, `chip`, `button` and `empty-state`; the search, the facet selections, each select's own option search and the page count are local state, and facets can be seeded from the URL query.

- Import: `import StoryIndexSection from '@aziontech/webkit/story-index-section'`
- Category: section · Surface: site · Structure: monolithic

## When to use

- Use StoryIndexSection for a card grid of customer stories with search and facets.

## Do

- Derive each facet's `options` from the values the items carry, so every option matches at least one story.
- Give every item a `facets` entry per facet `id`; an item without one never matches a selection on that facet.
- Pass `client.logo` as any image URL or imported asset (`import gpaExtendedReversed from '@aziontech/webkit/assets/gpa-extended-reversed.svg'`); the card fits a mark of any intrinsic size, so do not pre-scale or crop it.

## Don’t

- For a closing row of three related stories → use `article-cards-section`.
- For the full filterable list of articles or resources → use `article-index-section`.
- For a filterable list of non-article rows (jobs) → use `listing-index-section`.

## Related

- `article-index-section` — the filterable list of articles or resources.
- `empty-state` — the no-match state.

## Props

| Prop | Type | Default | Required | Description |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `anchor` | `string` | `''` | false | In-page id set on the band so a `#hash` link can scroll to it. |
| `title` | `string` | `''` | false | Headline of the band, rendered left-aligned as its `h2`; the band renders no header when empty. |
| `items` | `StoryIndexSectionItem[]` | `[]` | false | The stories, in order; each is `{ id, href, client, category, description, facets?, target? }`; a story whose `target` is `_blank` opens in a new tab. |
| `facets` | `StoryIndexSectionFacet[]` | `[]` | false | The facet selects, in order; each is `{ id, label, allLabel, options }`. |
| `searchPlaceholder` | `string` | `''` | false | Placeholder of the search field; the field is omitted when this and `searchAriaLabel` are both empty. |
| `searchShortPlaceholder` | `string` | `''` | false | Shorter placeholder of the search field below `lg`; empty uses `searchPlaceholder`. |
| `searchAriaLabel` | `string` | `''` | false | Accessible name of the search field. |
| `emptyTitle` | `string` | `''` | false | Message of the no-match state; the state renders only with it. |
| `emptyActionLabel` | `string` | `''` | false | Label of the no-match state's button that clears every filter; no button renders without it. |
| `linkLabel` | `string` | `''` | false | Read cue at the foot of each card, also the lead of its accessible name. |
| `moreLabel` | `string` | `''` | false | Label of the load-more button under the grid; without it every match is listed and no button renders. |
| `pageSize` | `number` | `12` | false | Stories listed per page when `moreLabel` is set; the load-more button reveals the next page. |
| `filterLabel` | `string` | `''` | false | Label of the filter button below `lg`; empty uses the locale catalog (`Filters`). |
| `seedFromQuery` | `boolean` | `false` | false | Seeds each facet on mount from the URL query parameter named by its `id` when the value is one of its options. |
| `ariaLabel` | `string` | `''` | false | Accessible name of the list of stories. |

## States

- Visual states: `default`, `hover` (a card lifts to the raised surface, the read cue brightens and its arrow nudges), `focus-visible` (inset ring)
- Filtered: the search matches the client name and the description; each facet keeps the stories whose `facets[id]` holds the selected value; all filters combine
- Paged: with `moreLabel` set, the first `pageSize` matches render and the load-more button, shown while more remain, adds the next `pageSize`; changing the search or a facet returns to the first page
- Clearing one facet: every select starts with an option labelled by its `allLabel` that clears that facet
- Folded toolbar (below `lg`): a filter button (`aria-expanded`, `aria-controls`) beside the search field shows or hides the selects, stacked (the panel carries `data-state="open|closed"`); its label carries the number of applied facets; every facet set to a value renders a removable `chip` (`<facet label>: <option label>`) whose remove clears it; the field shows `searchShortPlaceholder` when set. From `lg` the selects sit on the toolbar row and the button and chips are hidden (CSS only for the layout; the placeholder switches after mount, so server and client markup match)
- Option search: a select with more than seven options renders a search field above its options and a `status` line when nothing matches
- Seeded: with `seedFromQuery`, on mount (client only) a facet takes the value of `?<id>=` in the URL when it is one of its options
- New tab: a story whose `target` is `_blank` links with `rel="noopener"`
- Empty: when no story matches, the grid and the load-more button give way to `empty-state`, whose action clears the search and every facet; it renders only when `emptyTitle` is set
- `data-last-row-sm` / `data-last-row-lg` mark the cards on the grid's last row so it drops its bottom rule
- Client mark: drawn at the mark row's height, at most `9rem` wide, keeping its aspect ratio; when the card is narrower than that (two columns at 200% zoom) the mark shrinks to the card's width (`min-w-0`) and stays start-aligned, so a mark of any size or aspect ratio never pushes the card past its column

## Accessibility

- Visible focus: every card shows an inset `focus-visible` ring; the field, selects and buttons own theirs.
- Keyboard map: `Tab` moves through the filter button (below `lg`), the search field, each select, the applied-filter chips, the cards and the load-more button; the selects own their keyboard map.
- ARIA: the band is a `<section>` named by its title (`aria-label`) when one is set; the toolbar is a `search` landmark; the field is named by `searchAriaLabel` and each select trigger by its facet `label`; the list is named by `ariaLabel`; the filter button reports `aria-expanded` and controls the selects; each applied chip's remove button is named by the chip; each card link is named `linkLabel: client name`, followed by `(opens in a new tab)` when its `target` is `_blank`, its mark decorative (`alt=""`) and its read cue `aria-hidden`; an option search with no match announces itself as a `status`.
- Contrast ≥4.5:1 (text) / ≥3:1 (large + icons): text uses `--text-default` / `--text-muted`.
- `motion-reduce:transition-none` on every transition.
- Touch target ≥40×40 px — every card is a full-cell link.

## Examples

### Default

```vue
<script setup>
  import axurExtendedReversed from '@aziontech/webkit/assets/axur-extended-reversed.svg'
  import exameExtendedReversed from '@aziontech/webkit/assets/exame-extended-reversed.svg'
  import gpaExtendedReversed from '@aziontech/webkit/assets/gpa-extended-reversed.svg'
  import StoryIndexSection from '@aziontech/webkit/story-index-section'
</script>

<template>
  <StoryIndexSection
    anchor="cases"
    title="Companies building with Azion"
    aria-label="Success stories"
    link-label="Read story"
    more-label="Load more"
    :items="[
      {
        id: 'case-gpa',
        href: '/site/success-cases/gpa',
        client: { name: 'GPA', logo: gpaExtendedReversed },
        category: 'Retail',
        description:
          'Grupo Pão de Açúcar (GPA) solved a targeted DNS attack, secured 100+ critical applications in 15 days, and reduced costs by 30%.',
```

<!-- components/subject-grid-section.md -->

# Subject Grid Section

> A library of learning subjects in a divider grid: each subject is one framed card with its name, what it covers and a list of its article links, and an optional closing call to action spans the grid's last two cells so the grid ends without an empty cell. It is a page section — the site places it from a `{ _id: 'subject-grid-section', props }` entry and binds `props` straight onto it — so its whole API is the props below, and it composes `section-module`, `registration-frame`, `card-grid`, `call-to-action` and `button` rather than owning any anatomy of its own.

- Import: `import SubjectGridSection from '@aziontech/webkit/subject-grid-section'`
- Category: section · Surface: site · Structure: monolithic

## When to use

- On the Learning Center index, to list every subject with the articles that teach it.
- Whenever a page needs a grid of topic cards that each carry a short list of links, closed by one call to action.

## Do

- Give every subject a unique `slug`; it is the card's `id`, so a `#slug` link lands on it.
- Keep the subject count at a multiple of three plus one when `closing` is set, so the closing cell fills the last row exactly.
- Keep each subject's articles to a handful; the card grows with its list.

## Don’t

- For a grid of features with artwork → use `feature-tiles-section`.
- For a filterable list of rows → use `listing-index-section`.
- Inside another band's cell → compose `registration-frame` and `call-to-action` directly.

## Related

- `call-to-action` — the `lead` band drawn in the closing cell.
- `card-grid` — the divider grid the subject cards sit in.

## Props

| Prop | Type | Default | Required | Description |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `anchor` | `string` | `''` | false | In-page `id` of the band, the target of a `#hash` link. |
| `subjects` | `SubjectGridSectionSubject[]` | `[]` | false | The subject cards, in order; each is `{ slug, title, description, articles }` where every article is `{ label, href }`. Subjects without a title or a linked article are skipped. |
| `closing` | `SubjectGridSectionClosing` | `undefined` | false | Call to action across the grid's last two cells; `{ eyebrow, title, titleMuted, description, primaryAction, secondaryAction }` where each action is `{ label, href, trailing?, target? }`. Omitted when absent. |
| `ariaLabel` | `string` | `''` | false | Accessible name of the band; empty leaves the section unnamed. Set it so the band is a named region landmark. |

## States

- Visual states: `default`
- Three columns from the large breakpoint, one below the small one, on `card-grid`'s `fixed` layout (equal tracks whatever the count), so the closing cell really spans two columns from the small breakpoint up and takes two thirds of the last row on desktop, as on azion.com's learning page
- Empty: when no subject carries a title and a linked article the band renders nothing — not the grid, not the closing cell

## Accessibility

- Visible focus: each article link draws `focus-visible:outline-2 focus-visible:outline-offset-2 focus-visible:outline-(--ring-color)`; the closing buttons own theirs.
- Keyboard map: `Tab` moves through the article links in reading order, then the closing actions; `Enter` follows one. Nothing else in the band is interactive.
- ARIA: the band is a `<section>` carrying `anchor` as its `id` and named by `ariaLabel` when set, so it becomes a region landmark; each subject is an `h3` over a `role="list"` of links; the closing cell is `call-to-action`'s own section, named by its title.
- Contrast ≥4.5:1 (text) / ≥3:1 (large + icons): titles `var(--text-default)`, descriptions and links `var(--text-muted)`.
- `motion-reduce:transition-none motion-reduce:transform-none` — the article link colour transition carries `motion-reduce:transition-none`.
- Touch target ≥40×40 px — the closing actions are large `button`s; article links are inline text links inside a list.

## Examples

<!-- components/template-gallery-section.md -->

# Template Gallery Section

> The templates band of a solution page: a framed media split whose copy side invites the reader to start from a template and whose media side is a scrolling two-column wall of deployable template cards, closed by a marquee of the frameworks and clouds it works with. Each card is a mark, a title, one line and a deploy action. It is a page section — the site places it from a `{ _id: 'template-gallery-section', props }` entry and binds `props` straight onto it — so its whole API is the props below, and it composes `section-module`, `media-split`, `scroll-area`, `card-grid`, `button`, `registration-frame` and `marquee` rather than owning any anatomy of its own. With `closing` set it is the page's closing band: the hatched closing frame follows it, with no rules of its own, because the site footer under it opens with one (`hatch` turns that frame off when another closing band stacks directly below and draws the shared one).

- Import: `import TemplateGallerySection from '@aziontech/webkit/template-gallery-section'`
- Category: section · Surface: site · Structure: monolithic

## When to use

- Use TemplateGallerySection on marketing pages to show deployable templates; set `closing` when it is the last band.
- On a solution page (Retail, Web Apps, AI Workloads, Security, Performance, Streaming) to show the templates a reader can deploy right away.
- Whenever a band pairs one pitch with a long, browsable list of deployable starters.

## Do

- Keep the wall to an even number of templates so the two columns end level.
- Turn on `invertInDark` only for a mark whose artwork ships in dark ink, so it stays legible on the dark theme.
- Set `templatesAriaLabel` so the scrolling wall is announced by name.
- Set `closing` only on the last band of the page, right above `site-footer-section`: it draws the hatched closing frame itself, so nothing follows it inside the page column (no trailing `SectionGap`, no closing `RegistrationFrame`).
- Turn `hatch` off only on a closing band that another closing band follows directly; the last one keeps the frame.

## Don’t

- For a grid of features with artwork → use `feature-tiles-section`.
- For a marquee of marks alone → compose `marquee` directly.
- For a handful of icon-led use cases → use `customer-use-cases-section`.
- For a closing ask with links or a command and no templates → use `closing-call-to-action-section`; for an email sign-up close → use `newsletter-section`.

## Related

- `media-split` — the framed copy-and-media split the band is built on; it owns the band's named region.
- `marquee` — the row of framework and cloud marks that closes the band.

## Props

| Prop | Type | Default | Required | Description |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `anchor` | `string` | `''` | false | In-page id set on the band so a `#hash` link can scroll to it. |
| `eyebrow` | `string` | `''` | false | Eyebrow above the headline. |
| `title` | `string` | `''` | false | Headline of the band, rendered by the media split as its `h2` and naming its region. |
| `description` | `string` | `''` | false | Paragraph under the headline. |
| `primaryAction` | `SectionTitleAction` | `undefined` | false | First button under the copy, drawn as the large secondary button; `{ label, href, trailing?, target? }`. Omitted when absent. |
| `secondaryAction` | `SectionTitleAction` | `undefined` | false | Second button under the copy, drawn as the large outlined button; `{ label, href, trailing?, target? }`. Omitted when absent. |
| `templates` | `TemplateGallerySectionTemplate[]` | `[]` | false | The template cards of the scrolling wall, in order; each is `{ title, description?, icon?, invertInDark?, href? }` where `href` makes the whole card a link to the template. Templates without a title are skipped. |
| `deployLabel` | `string` | `''` | false | Label of the deploy button on each card; a card renders its button only when both this and its `href` are set. |
| `templatesAriaLabel` | `string` | `''` | false | Accessible name of the scrolling wall of templates. |
| `marqueeLabel` | `string` | `''` | false | Eyebrow above the row of marks. |
| `marqueeMarks` | `string[]` | `[]` | false | Registry names of the frameworks and clouds in the row of marks, in order; the marquee is omitted when empty. |
| `closing` | `boolean` | `false` | false | Makes the band the page's closing band: the hatched closing frame follows it, as the closing call to action draws it. |
| `hatch` | `boolean` | `true` | false | Draws the hatched closing frame under a `closing` band; turn it off when another closing band stacks directly below and draws the shared frame. Ignored without `closing`. |

## Assets

- Brand marks: every valid name is in brand-marks.md (MCP `list_assets({ kind: "brandMarks" })`). An unknown name is not drawn as a mark: `Marquee` prints it as a typographic wordmark, the quote components show no mark.

## States

- Visual states: `default`
- A card with an `href` is a link target as a whole: a click anywhere on it follows the `href` (and a modified or middle click opens it in a new tab, as any link does), the pointer turns to the hand over it, and hovering it lights its deploy button. With a deploy button the card keeps one tab stop — the button — and the card-wide link is a pointer-only target hidden from assistive technology; without a deploy button the card-wide link is the card's one tab stop, named by the template title
- A card without an `href` is not interactive: no hand pointer, no hover light
- Empty: when no template carries a title the band renders nothing — not the split, not the marquee, not the closing frame
- `closing`: the tall hatched closing frame (`data-hatch="true"`, decorative) follows the band; `hatch` off drops it

## Accessibility

- Visible focus: owned by the composed `button`s and the `scroll-area`; a card-wide link that is a card's only tab stop draws `focus-visible:ring-2 focus-visible:ring-(--ring-color) focus-visible:ring-inset` over the card.
- Keyboard map: `Tab` reaches the copy's actions, the scrolling wall and each card's one link (its deploy button, or the card itself when it has none) in reading order; `Enter` follows a link. Nothing else in the band is interactive.
- ARIA: the band's root is a plain `div`, because `media-split` already renders the band's region named by its title; the wall is `scroll-area`'s region named by `templatesAriaLabel`; the template marks and the closing frame are decorative (`aria-hidden`, or no content); a card-wide link duplicated by a deploy button is `aria-hidden` with `tabindex="-1"`, so each card is announced once.
- Contrast ≥4.5:1 (text) / ≥3:1 (large + icons): card titles `var(--text-default)`, card lines `var(--text-muted)`; marks flagged `invertInDark` invert under the dark theme.
- `motion-reduce:transition-none motion-reduce:transform-none` — the button hover and the marquee scroll belong to `button` and `marquee`, which carry their own reduced-motion fallback; this band adds no motion.
- Touch target ≥40×40 px — the deploy buttons are medium `button`s, the copy's actions are large.
